# Learnings archive: 2026-11

Entries dated 2026-11, moved verbatim from `docs/learnings.md` on 2026-11-22, newest first (the original order). Append new entries for the current month at the top of this file (below this note). Promote an entry to the digest in `docs/learnings.md` only if it is durable.

## 2026-11-22: Identifiers renamed to match display names (rules 52, `rename-identifiers`)

- **What changed (owner, 2026-11-22, before the public launch).** 23 enum variants left from the sci-fi theme now carry the PascalCase of their display name: units `Soldier`→`Spearman`, `Ranger`→`Archer`, `Trooper`→`Pikeman`, `Marksman`→`Crossbowman`, `AssaultTrooper`→`Musketman`, `Heir`→`HeirToTheThrone`, `Drone`→`CombatDrone`, `Landship`→`EarlyTank`; buildings `BroodHall`→`RiceStorehouse`, `Laboratory`→`Library`, `RelayUplink`→`GreatLibrary`, `IterReactor`→`IterFusionReactor`, `Hydroponics`→`Mill`, `MachineShop`→`Forge`, `WarShrine`→`Agoge`, `College`→`University`, `Clearinghouse`→`Bank`, `Autoworks`→`Factory`, `PlanetaryFoundry`→`RoyalArsenal`, `WorldExchange`→`GrandBazaar`, `InferenceCore`→`ResearchLab`, `CourierHall`→`YamRelayStation`; improvement `SaltpeterWorks`→`SaltpetreWorks`. No rule, number, display name or variant order changed. `eras_tests::identifiers_renamed_in_rules_52_load_from_their_old_names` now fails for any unit or building whose identifier is not its display name in PascalCase.
- **Old names load through `#[serde(alias = "Old")]`** on each variant (`crates/core/src/types.rs`), beside the older aliases (`Monument`, `Arcology`, `Citadel`). The alias also works where the kind is a JSON map key. The engine never writes an old name. Tests: the table in `eras_tests`, the rules-49 state in `crates/sim/tests/rules49_upgrade.rs`, and a recorded tape and state in `crates/wasm/tests/renamed_identifiers.rs`.
- **The state hash is FNV-1a over the JSON text (`GameState::hash`), so a variant's name is part of every hash.** A rename with no rule change still moves every pinned tape hash and scenario hash, and an older build of the same rules version cannot read the new names (online guests, the `v/<id>/` builds). So it is a `RULES_VERSION` bump (51 to 52) with the usual plumbing: `refresh-save-version-fixtures.py 51 52`, `rehash_scenarios`, `UPDATE_UI_RULES=1 cargo test -p legatum-ai --test ui_rules`, `tools/sim/repin.sh`. The aliases therefore serve records that are read without the version check (tapes, archived games, the rules-49 upgrade, fixtures whose version is only re-stamped), not players' rules-51 saves, which are refused like any other version.
- **A glob import turns a renamed variant into a catch-all binding.** `use UnitKind::*; match k { Soldier | Trooper => ... }` still compiles after `Soldier` is gone: the bare name becomes a new variable that matches everything. Only the "unused variable" and "unreachable pattern" warnings show it, so run clippy with `-D warnings` after any variant rename (`crates/ai/src/proxy*.rs`, `openings.rs`, `data.rs` and three core test files import the kinds this way).
- **Quoted old names in TypeScript are not all identifiers.** `'Soldier'` in `forecastWarnings.test.ts` is a display-name argument (`attackerRisk(f, unitName)`), `'Heir'` is also a `GoalRule`, and the Guide's World Ages paragraph still says "Laboratory, College, Clearinghouse or Inference Core" as player text (stale since the historical re-theme; left for a text fix, because this change must not alter player text). A scripted rename needs word boundaries plus a review of every hit that is not a qualified path.
- **`rules.generated.json` is a sorted-key JSON object, so renamed keys move.** Display fields are identical once old ids are mapped to new; only `saveVersion` differs. App code that takes the first match while walking `Object.entries(UNIT_NAME)` (`unlockArt.ts::UNIT_BY_PLURAL`) depends on that order among names of equal length; no text names two such units today.
- **Asset files follow the identifier** (`unit-<kind>`, `building-<kind>`, `improvement-<kind>`, lower case): 170 tracked asset and prompt files renamed with `git mv` (97 PNGs, 24 SVGs and 49 prompt files; the images are byte-identical, and 30 construction prompts changed only the line naming a renamed sibling). `building-relay-uplink.svg` became `building-greatlibrary.svg`, which removes the one special case in `data.ts::buildingIconName`, and `milestone-relay-uplink` became `milestone-great-library`. Not renamed: the unit art slug `landship` (`app/src/assets/units/landship-*`), because its master `landship-source.png` is stored outside git under that path (`tools/art/sources-manifest.json`); renaming it needs the archive re-packed. The unlock-art masters under `docs/design/unlock-art/masters/*.png` are untracked local files: `tools/art/wonder-art.py` and `landmark-atlas-edit.py` now look for the new stems, so rename the local masters before running them.
- **Left with their old names on purpose:** `TechId::Cannonry` (displayed "Ballistics"; the name `Ballistics` in an old save means `Feudalism`), `IndustryProject::{Gold, Science}`, secondary names that are not enum variants (`PlayerView::courier_halls`, `CourierHallView`, the `war_shrine_drive` gene, test function names), sim report labels ("First Soldier completed"), and every dated log.


## 2026-11-22: The leftover retina map lag: hover still redrew the whole map, and the rest is the headless browser (`perf-retina-map-draw`)

- **Measured after the lag fix (`152080f0`), on a dense turn-131 battle (`tools/perf/dense_battle_save.py --mine Ranthambore --theirs Shangdu` on seed 7000001, `tools/perf/battle-hover.cjs`, headless Chromium, 1440x900, zoom 2):** at DPR 1 the sweep is clean (no long tasks, pointer-move lag p95 31 ms). At DPR 2: long tasks 5.0 s, frame p95 67 ms, move lag p95 110 ms.
- **One real cause, fixed:** each new tile under the pointer still redrew the whole map (56 full draws in the sweep), only to move the hover outline and the selected unit's route. A frame where nothing but `hover`, `hoverSelect`, `pathInfo` or `pathAttack` changed now repaints the old and new boxes of those marks (`hoverClipBoxes`, `hoverOnlyChange`), the same clipped frame the selection pulse and smoke use. DPR 2 after: long tasks 5.0 → 2.3 s, frame p95 67 → 33 ms, move lag p95 110 → 78 ms, slow frames 65 → 28; DPR 1 unchanged.
- **The look is the same:** with map animations off, 120 hover positions (Soldier, Settler and nothing selected) compared against a forced full redraw. No mark is ever left behind; the only differences are anti-aliasing on stroke edges inside the repainted boxes (1 to 3 pixels over 40/255 per frame), which the pulse and smoke frames have always had (a clip made of several rectangles rasterises edges slightly differently from no clip).
- **What is left at DPR 2 in the cloud is the headless browser, not our code.** A trace of the same sweep: 14.9 s of 22 s is the renderer's `Commit` (15 ms per frame), with `SoftwareRenderer::DoDrawQuad` and `Canvas2DResourceProvider::ProduceCanvasResource` behind it: without a GPU, any change to the canvas copies and composites all 5.2 million pixels, clipped draw or not. JavaScript is under 10% of the time. The remaining full frames are clicks (a selection pans the camera) and the new terrain chunks the pan needs: one 512 px chunk takes 40 to 68 ms to draw here, almost all of it `drawImage` rasterising in software.
- **Confirmed on a real GPU (Sam's Mac, M3 Max, headless Chromium 153 with `--use-angle=metal --ignore-gpu-blocklist`, renderer "ANGLE Metal Renderer: Apple M3 Max", machine busy at load 40 to 60):** the same sweep at DPR 2 holds 60 fps before and after this change: frame p95 16.8 ms, no slow frames, no long tasks, pointer-move lag p95 18 ms, map draw mean 1.4 ms and max under 4 ms, three runs a side within 1 ms of each other. Full map draws fall from 40 to 5 per sweep. The retina lag left after the first fix was the headless browser's software rendering; the saving here is work removed for slower GPUs, not something this Mac can show.
- **To get the GPU in headless Chromium on a Mac** pass `--use-angle=metal --ignore-gpu-blocklist` (the default is SwiftShader) and check the WebGL `UNMASKED_RENDERER` string. `vite preview` listens on `[::1]` only there: add `--host 127.0.0.1` for scripts that call 127.0.0.1.
- **So headless DPR 2 numbers cannot say whether a real retina screen still lags.** Use them to count work (full draws against clipped draws, chunk renders), not to judge smoothness. The judge is `frame_times` from real games (`map_draw_max_ms`, `long_task_ms`, `slow_frames` against `canvas_mp10`), recorded from `152080f0` on.
- **Gotcha for tests:** `MapCanvas.test.ts` mocks `draw` and happy-dom gives canvases no context, so a clipped frame (which clips before calling `draw`) needs the test's stub context; the mocked `draw` must also return `ambient`.
## 2026-11-22: The stronger unit takes a tile contested at Peace (rules 51, `claude/project-thread-s7depg`)

- **A peaceful contest has to be settled before the advance pass, and the losers must leave the rival list.** Marking the losers in `decided` was not enough: the winner then met them again in `alive_claimants` and bounced as "friendly units contested the tile", so nobody moved. `settle_peaceful_contest` runs after the city-captor pass, and the advance pass drops its losers (`outmatched`) from the rivals. Only that set is dropped, so the city rules, where a force tie leaves `decided` units in the list on purpose, are untouched.
- **Sum per side, like a fallen city.** The first cut compared each side's strongest unit. Sam pointed out the rule that units contesting a fallen city combine their force (`settle_by_force`, 2026-11-11), so the open-ground contest now sums each team's claimants too. Before adding a contest rule, look for the existing one it should mirror. Sam then made the city's exact force tie a seeded draw as well (it bounced everyone), so both contests break ties the same way.
- **A rules bump is mostly fixture plumbing.** `tools/sim/refresh-save-version-fixtures.py 50 51` moved 79 current-rule fixtures and scenario states, and `rehash_scenarios` re-encoded the scenario hashes. `wasm::json` had a test that spelled out `"save_version":50`; it now uses `RULES_VERSION`. The step that is easy to miss is `app/src/rules.generated.json` (`UPDATE_UI_RULES=1 cargo test -p legatum-ai --test ui_rules`): the app checks saves against its `saveVersion`, so a stale 50 made the build refuse its own saves. The UI reviewer caught it, not the core tests.

## 2026-11-22: Late-game map lag was the whole map redrawing for things that touch a few pixels (`claude/project-thread-6jvf4u`)

- **Symptom (Sam, Chrome, Hard Rome, from about turn 126):** hover and clicks jumpy in a crowded battle. The engine was not the cause: the map's draw loop did full-canvas redraws for almost everything.
- **Causes, measured on a dense turn-131 battle (`tools/perf/dense_battle_save.py` + `tools/perf/battle-hover.cjs`):** (1) every pointer move set `dirty` in `stepGesture` even when the camera did not move, so each mouse twitch redrew the whole map; (2) every pixel of hover re-rendered `MapCanvas` (the 2 px rule existed only for the banner tooltip); (3) a damaged city's smoke (20 fps), an alarmed city's ring (30 fps) and idle life (8 fps) each forced full redraws, so a battle with a burning city never let the map rest; (4) each battle forecast rebuilt the whole `PlayerView` in WASM (5 to 7 ms; a hover over an enemy city asked for two or three); (5) the biggest one in his own game: the forecast card's aftermath line asked the engine per enemy unit whether it could reach the tile, sending the whole view as JSON each time (`unitCanReach`), so a card over a fight with a dozen enemies cost 100 ms+ and re-ran on every pixel of hover.
- **Fixes:** redraw only when the camera moved or a drawn input changed (`frameChanged`); hover state changes only with the tile or banner under the pointer (the banner tooltip moves its element directly); smoke, siege rings, the selection pulse and idle life repaint only their boxes (`ambientClipRects`, `idleClipBoxes`) with the rest of the map's clock held (siege rings moved to the pulse clock, idle life has its own `idleT`); WASM keeps the forecast view until the state changes (`Game::forecast_view`, invalidated with the JSON view cache); the aftermath asks once for all enemies and remembers the answer per view (`unitsCanReach`, `Game.units_can_reach`).
- **Result on his own game (turn 126 rebuilt from the uploaded tape with its engine, `extract_state`; headless Chromium, 1440x900, zoom 2, 600-move sweep over the densest fight):** DPR 1: long tasks 22.4 s → 0, pointer-move lag p95 738 → 27 ms, frame p95 267 → 17 ms; DPR 2: long tasks 57 s → 4.4 s, move lag p95 809 → 92 ms. Loading a rules-49 state in the rules-50 app needed only `save_version` set.
- **Result on a synthetic dense battle:** at DPR 2 long tasks 41.9 s → 4.2 s, pointer-move lag p50 116 → 23 ms, frames over 50 ms 556 → 40, worst click 291 → 124 ms; at DPR 1 move lag p50 30 → 22 ms and frame p95 33 → 17 ms; forecast 6.6 → 0.5 ms. Headless software rendering exaggerates canvas fill cost, so the DPR 2 numbers overstate the gain on a GPU, but the work removed is real everywhere.
- **Telemetry so the next report comes with data:** each turn's frame gaps, input lag, long tasks and map draw cost go into the archive as `frame_times` (`frameTelemetry.ts`, `TurnFrameTime`), upload with the game, and print as a Smoothness line in `tools/games` facts. Nothing that identifies a player or device (no browser, window or screen size); only the canvas's megapixels.
- **Gotcha:** headless profiles of this map are dominated by `(program)`/`Commit` (software raster), not JavaScript; compare runs on the same box and DPR, and read map draw counts (full vs clipped) as well as times.
## 2026-11-22: The research screen's era heading still promised "three VP objectives" (`claude/project-thread-qrkycu`)

- **Under rules 50 entering an era opens nothing for you:** an Age's one card is revealed to everyone when the first empire reaches its era, and a laggard can score it. `TechPanel`'s era heading said "finish them all to enter the next era and open its three VP objectives" whenever an Age two eras on was revealed; the clause is gone. The tour had no shot that showed it because no research fixture staged an open Age; `research-unlocks` now shoots the World Age box before any Age and with Ages open (`90a`, `90b`).

## 2026-11-22: Costs around End Turn that grow with the game (`perf-late-game`)

- **The upload's renamed copy replayed the whole game on the main thread every five turns.** `Recording::anonymized` recomputes every hash when a player typed a name, by resolving every recorded turn again: 40 ms at turn 20, 951 ms at turn 220 natively (`growth_profile`, seed 7000001, one thread; WASM is slower). `anonymized_resuming` keeps the renamed state and hashes from the last call and replays only the turns since; it trusts them only while the recording still has that turn with the same original hash, so a loaded or forked game falls back to a full replay. Same run after: 180 ms at turn 220 with 20 turns between calls (the page calls every 5).
- **Every turn made the engine save twice**: once for the autosave and again for the "last good state" kept for recovering a failed End Turn. The save is 0.3 MB at turn 20 and 1.7 MB at turn 220 (the archive of every order is 1.1 MB of that), 47 ms natively. `writeAutosave` now returns the save it made and `remember` reuses it.
- **No benchmark saw either**, because `sim_bench` and `ai_budget` time the turn, not the page's work around it. `crates/wasm/examples/growth_profile.rs` prints those costs every N turns of one game, so growth shows as a column. Measure a cost at turn 20 and turn 220 before calling it small.
- **A wasm-bindgen method that returns `Result<_, JsError>` can be called from a native example as long as it succeeds**: only building the error touches JavaScript.
## 2026-11-22: eleven-faction census on rules 50: best/worst 9.1, games end at a median of turn 220

- **Result (2,112 Hard 8-empire games, explicit cyclic rosters, 1,536 seats per faction):** per-seat win rate Egypt 21.4%, Mongols 18.7%, Athens 14.5%, Germany 14.3%, Celts 14.1%, Aztecs 12.8%, Sparta 12.0%, Switzerland 10.0%, Byzantium 9.0%, India 8.3%, Rome 2.3%. Best/worst 9.1; 2.6 among the nine finished factions. The 1.5x aim is not met. Table and intervals: `docs/sim-results/2026-11-22-census-eleven-factions-rules50.md`.
- **Length:** median end turn 220 (10th to 90th percentile 181 to 270), 98.9% VP holds, 1.1% capitals victories, none at the turn limit; 99.2% of games had an empire reach the World War era and 55% reached the Future. One game (seed 9811057) hit the 1,200-turn cap.
- **Method:** with 11 factions and 8 seats, a seeded draw gives uneven appearances. Use the 11 cyclic 8-faction windows with `--rotate-factions` (`--games` a multiple of 8) so each faction plays equally many seats. The census summary now reports median end turn, ending shares and the World War share; records carry `max_era` (absent in older reports: unknown, not Ancient).
- **Gotcha:** `pkill -f "legatum-sim --players 6"` in a shared container also kills other agents' sims. Kill by PID.

## 2026-11-22: The servers moved behind playlegatum.com (security review, decision 2)

- **A `workers.dev` address cannot have an edge rule.** Rate-limiting rules belong to a zone, so both Workers now have a custom domain (`relay.playlegatum.com`, `games.playlegatum.com`) and one zone rule (40 requests per 10 seconds per address, block 10 seconds; the free plan allows one rule with a 10-second window). Requests the rule refuses never reach the Worker, so they do not use up the free plan's daily requests.
- **`routes` in wrangler turns `workers.dev` off unless `workers_dev: true` is set.** It is set on purpose: builds kept playable by `keep-builds` have the old `workers.dev` relay baked in. Turn it off once no kept build uses it; until then the old address is outside the rule.
- **The site's Content-Security-Policy covers the kept builds too.** One `_headers` file serves every `v/<id>/` page, so it must still allow the old `workers.dev` addresses or an older build could no longer reach its relay or upload its game.
- **Deploy order matters:** Workers first (the custom domains must answer), then the site, whose Content-Security-Policy now names the new addresses.

## 2026-11-22: Tour fixtures that stopped matching the game (`claude/project-thread-qrkycu`)

- **The new game's autosave is in IndexedDB, not `localStorage['legatum.autosave.v1']`.** That key is now only the legacy slot and the no-database fallback, so an e2e that seeds it and reads it back after starting a game gets the seeded save, never the new one (`lobby-difficulty.spec.ts`). Read the live game's view (`window.legatum.getState()`) instead.
- **A rival's era banner (`EraNotice`) never shows a `first: true` entry**: the first empire into an era opens the Age, and the Age's score check names it. A fixture that stages `EraEntered { first: true }` for a rival gets no banner at all.
- **A capture's wonder card needs the wonder's landmark in that city, and is skipped for the Courier Hall's city.** Without the landmark, `GreatWorkCompleted { again }` tells itself as "A world wonder retaken" placed at your capital, so the map label lands beside an unrelated city. Stage wonder captures on a non-Hall city with a `landmark` tile (`wonderCapture` in `e2e/tour/ui-review-1103.spec.ts`).

## 2026-11-22: Join code, one seat per connection and Remove player (`join-code`)

- **`game_take` never bound the claim to the connection.** It only issued a token; `s.connection` is set later by `join`, so one socket could `game_take` every open seat and never `join`. The one-seat rule therefore tracks the claim on the connection (`Connection.claim` = seat + token, valid while that seat still holds the token), and a room seat counts too. In a room, `Room::take` already left the first seat, so no change there.
- **The host's close reason reaches a guest only as a terminal `error` message.** The tunnel's close frame has no payload, so a close code or reason never crosses the relay. `Sender::close(code, message)` queues a final `{"type":"error","code":...}`, which the guest client must act on (`removed`, `too_many_codes`, plus `join_code_needed` and `wrong_join_code`, stop its automatic reconnect) or it would loop on a seat claim it cannot make.
- **"Closed" in a game is a fresh token nobody holds.** A seat is open when `token` is `None` (summary's `open_seats`) and reserved otherwise; Remove swaps the token for a new random one and sets `joined = true`, so the seat is neither open to strangers nor blocking `can_resolve` (a seat without a connection is skipped once every other living seat is ready). In a room the same thing is a disconnected reservation with a new token; `staging_release` opens it.
- **The five-strike lockout is per connection, so it does not stop a script that reconnects.** Only the relay's per-address socket and rate caps bound that; a 4-character code is weak against it. The code is not case sensitive (stored upper-case) to spare typing mistakes.
- **A join code must stay out of everything the host serializes.** It is a field of `Host` only, and a test asserts it is in no status, summary, save, history or message text. The relay is told only `locked: true` (strict boolean).

## 2026-11-22: Hard picks city sites by the whole ring's terrain Production (gene `site_terrain_weight` 20)

- **The finding.** Every owned ring-one tile is worked from founding, so a new city's early Production is the terrain of its whole ring, but Hard's site multiplier counted only the centre and the best two ring-one tiles. In the designer's recorded games his new cities made more Production over their first ten turns than Hard's, and most of the gap was more Hills and Forest in ring one: site choice, not tile management. `site_terrain_weight` N adds N% of site value per point of terrain Production on the explored ring-one tiles that are unowned or ours (fog and foreign tiles count nothing). Default 20, which makes a ring Production point worth what the existing best-two term already pays.
- **Evidence (rules 50).** One seat at 20 against five at 0, fresh seeds, sequential design with three looks: stopped at the first look (667 seeds) on VP share at the end, +0.72 points [+0.33, +1.11]; VP at the end +0.16 [+0.06, +0.26], win +1.75 points [+0.78, +2.72], cities at T100 +0.07, at the end +0.21; no faction significantly worse. With all five Hard seats at 20 the scripted rusher captures a Hard city by T60 in 33.6% of 220 seeds against 32.3% (not significant). On rules 49 a 10/20/40/80 sweep picked 20; Science at T60 dips slightly (Hills and Forest sites carry less early Food and Science) and cities founded by T60 hold at every weight.
- **Winner's curse.** A value picked as best on the sweep seeds is biased up on those seeds; a sequential test stopped at a boundary is biased up too. Re-measure on fresh seeds before landing. On rules 49 this gene gave +0.55 VP share on the sweep seeds and +0.21 (not significant) on fresh seeds, and an earlier gene looked +0.53 on sweep seeds and +0.05 on 2,000 fresh ones. Expect the true rules-50 effect below +0.72.
- **VP share at T100 overstates small edges.** A seat holds about 1.2 VP at T100, so +0.09 VP reads as +1.2 share points; by the end the table holds several times more VP and the same lead is a fraction of a point. Judge on end-of-game VP share, VP and wins; T100 share is a side note (here +1.28 at T100 against +0.72 at the end).
- **Pinned Hard behaviour moves.** The `hard_vs_human` benchmark pins and two recorded-decision tests (India settler fixtures, archived Mongols settler parity) changed with the gene; the recorded ones run with it off.


## 2026-11-22: Host hardening against hostile guests (`claude/project-thread-xw5vhs`)

- **A repeat `join` rebuilt and sent the full view and re-saved every time.** A seated guest could loop `join` on one socket and make the host build a ~1 MB view and serialize the save each time. A `join` from the connection that already joined this seat in the same planning turn (same name) now gets no reply, and the save is set only when the name changed (`Seat.joined_at`, Join handler in `crates/wasm/src/host/mod.rs`). A reconnect on a new connection still gets welcome and view.
- **The raw guest name (up to ~64 KiB) went into `history.metadata.seats[..].name`.** Only the seat copy was trimmed. One `clean_name` helper (`crates/wasm/src/host/staging.rs`) strips Cc/Cf characters (bidi overrides, zero-width marks), collapses whitespace and cuts to 80 characters; the game join, the saved history and staging all store its result.
- **Junk orders were stored and recorded even when validation failed.** The UI relies on staged orders that carry a validation error, so that stays. Instead one seat may stage at most 2,000 orders per turn (an over-limit message is not stored and gets one validation error) and a reply lists at most 100 errors (`MAX_STAGED_ORDERS`, `MAX_VALIDATION_ERRORS` in `host/mod.rs`).
- **The guest reassembled up to 64M characters per message.** The cap is now 16 MiB of characters (`app/src/relaySocket.ts`); the host's own per-delivery queue budget is 8 MiB, so a real view has clear headroom.

## 2026-11-22: Upload cap per address (security review)

- **The games Worker now caps NEW games at 50 per client address per UTC day** (`Quota` Durable Object, var `MAX_NEW_GAMES_PER_DAY`; 429 with `Retry-After`). Re-uploading a stored game is never counted. The address goes through the same /64 cut as the relay's `addressKey` (copied into `infra/games/src/quota.ts`) and is kept only as a salted hash for the current day.
- **Why a per-request rate limit was not enough:** R2 storage is the only unbounded bill, and even one request per minute stores gigabytes a day at 4 MiB each. The cap bounds storage per address, not request speed. Many addresses can still add up; that is accepted (SECURITY.md).
- **Privacy wording changed:** "keeps no IP addresses" was no longer true. The README now says the upload server remembers a scrambled form of the address until the end of the UTC day and keeps no request logs. SECURITY.md, `infra/games/README.md`, the Worker header and `wrangler.jsonc` comments say the same. No player-facing app text states the old claim.

## 2026-11-22: Every game finishes on the version it started on (`claude/project-thread-jcqy76`)

- **Problem:** with a deploy on every merge, a deploy that bumps `RULES_VERSION` stranded any game in progress: the new build refuses an older-rules save, and Pages serves only the newest deployment. Pages does keep every deployment at its own `<hash>.legatum-game.pages.dev` address, but that is another origin, so it cannot see the player's autosaves (browser storage is per origin).
- **Fix:** each build is also published on the same site at `v/<id>/` (`tools/release/keep-builds.mjs`), and `versions.json` lists them; a save opens in the build that made it (`app/src/platform/versions.ts`). The kept page is the build's own `index.html` with `<base href="../../">`: Vite's relative base (`./`) makes scripts load art relative to the script (in `assets/`), but `BASE_URL`-relative fetches (terrain, audio, the licence file) and `history.replaceState` resolve against the document's base, so without the tag they would look under `v/<id>/`. One page per build instead of a full copy keeps the site far under Pages' 20,000-file limit at several deploys a day.
- **Kept builds live in the newest build's title, never as a banner:** the UI review found a separate "new version" note pushed the title menu off the screen at 1000x600, so the news rides on Single Player's sub-line instead.
- **Gotchas:** the autosave database must stay at version 1 and keep its stores: every kept build opens the same database, and a build that opens it at a lower version than a newer build set gets a `VersionError`. A page another build opened with a save never sends it on again, so a wrong list cannot bounce a save between builds. `_headers` is site-wide (the newest build's), so a kept build whose relay or upload Worker address changed is blocked by the newest CSP.
## 2026-11-22: The Mac could not build master: two files differed only in case (`claude/project-thread-7n64r8`)

- **`celebrate/ScoreCheck.tsx` and `celebrate/scoreCheck.ts` (#34) are one file on a Mac's default case-insensitive disk**, so `tsc -b` failed there while Linux CI passed; the production deploy had to be built on a case-sensitive disk image. The model is now `scoreCheckCards.ts`, and `app/scripts/case-clash.test.mjs` fails on any two tracked paths that differ only in case (`.ts`/`.tsx` count as the same module, since imports leave the extension off).
- **`check-dist-refs.mjs` flagged `404.html -> /`**: a link to a folder is served by its `index.html`, which the check now accepts.

## 2026-11-22: One Playwright-unloadable import hid the whole screen tour (`claude/project-thread-qrkycu`)

- `celebrate/scoreCheck.ts` and `vpModel.ts` imported `roman` from `brand/ornaments.tsx`, which imports `logo-legatum.svg?url`. Playwright's Node loader can't import `?url`, and `e2e/tour/helpers.ts` reaches `scoreCheck.ts` through `moments.ts`, so every tour spec reported "No tests found". `roman` now lives in `brand/roman.ts` (no asset imports); `ornaments.tsx` re-exports it.
- A plain-language rewrite ("You have 2 Iron, and 4 are in use") put "in use" above the supply, which can't happen: idle users are the shortfall. The line now states the gap ("You are already 2 Iron short"). Check a rewrite of a number sentence against the screen's own numbers.

## 2026-11-22: Security review of the online surface (`claude/project-thread-xw5vhs`)

- **Per-address limits were per IPv6 address, so they limited nothing.** `CF-Connecting-IP` for an IPv6 client is one of the 2^64 addresses its line owns, and a script can use a new one per request. The relay's 2 rooms, 6 creations per 10 minutes and 4 guest sockets per address were each counted per full address, so one home line could fill all 64 lobby slots. `addressKey` in `infra/relay/src/shared.ts` now cuts an IPv6 address to its /64 before hashing. Any future per-address limit (the games Worker included) must go through the same cut.
- **The build label was a second free-text field in the lobby.** Room names were cleaned, but `build` only refused control characters, and the Join screen prints it ("This game is on version X"). It now accepts only what a real label is made of (letters, digits, spaces and `. · ( ) _ + -`).
- **`/api/status` served whatever the host stored, up to 1 MiB per room.** The Room now drops a `status` frame over 64 KiB (a real one is a few KiB) and every relay answer says `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`.
- **One guest order could make the host's save unloadable.** `SetCityAutomation` was validated by city ownership only, and its `picked` item was written to `City.last_auto_pick` as sent. A pick naming a tile off the map passed validation, and from then on every save failed `check_state` ("a city's production names a tile off the map"): a host reload silently started an empty game, and Save to file wrote a file nothing loads. Found by fuzzing the real `NetHost` with about 0.4M hostile orders, which found no panic and only this. `check_order` (behind `validate_city_orders`) now refuses such a pick. The general rule: anything an order stores on the state unchanged must be checked against what `check_state` demands.
- **Everything pushed becomes public with the repository:** all 37 branches and every `refs/pull/*/head` were scanned blob by blob for keys, tokens, bearer strings, home paths and addresses before launch. Nothing was found beyond the two commits carrying the maintainer's own email, which he accepted.
- **The free Workers plan fails closed, which is the right default.** About 100,000 requests in a day (one small script) use up the account's daily allowance and take the relay and the upload Worker offline until 00:00 UTC, but nothing is billed. The only open-ended bill is R2 storage. A Worker cannot rate-limit its way out of this, since a refused request still counts; the fix is a custom domain with a Cloudflare rate-limiting rule in front.



## 2026-11-22: Merged-tour fixes: two Returns, a stale engine string, a cut warning (`ui-tour/fix`)

- A moment card owns Return in a capture-phase key handler, so the dock's Return never fired, but the dock still drew a filled primary with its own Return chip: two primaries on screen. The dock now reads `useCelebration()` and `pendingAlarms` and steps back (`SeatBar.tsx` `returnOwned`).
- A player-visible string in `crates/core` (the barbarian `MoveBlocked` reason) reaches the turn report, so a copy-lint rule that bans it fails `textArtifacts.test.tsx` until the WASM engine is rebuilt: copying `wasm-pkg` from another worktree tests the old string. Scenario briefings in `scenarios/lab/*.json` are in the same corpus.
- The city panel's red production warning appears at Ready, after the panel already scrolled to the build list, so it sat half under the sticky head; scrolling is re-checked when the warning's text changes (`revealBelowStickyHead`). The research queue's third empty slot was scrolled out of the 1000x600 band because every slot took `minmax(244px, 1fr)`; empty slots now size to their content.
- Six lanes merged cleanly by file but left stale tests behind: each lane ran only its own test files, and a lane's vitest run against a stub `wasm-pkg` hid WASM-backed failures (`Levy.test.tsx`). Run the full `npm test` once on the merged head before review.

## 2026-11-22: A hover tooltip stuck over a scrolled list (`ui-tour/moments`)

- `Tip`'s capture-phase scroll listener stays attached until React re-renders. When the browser sends the pointer's leave and the list's scroll in the same frame, the leave closed the tip and the still-attached listener (seeing a non-hover mode) took its "pinned: follow the trigger" branch and set a position again, so the bubble came back and stuck. Scroll and resize handlers now do nothing once the tip is closed, and the bubble is hidden whenever its mode is null. Reproduced only in a real browser (a wheel over a scrolling list); `Tip.scroll.test.tsx` replays the same-frame order.
- Timed moment cards now own Return (their primary) and L (the turn report they hold back) while shown; e2e helpers that clicked the card's old "Turn report" button press L instead.

## 2026-11-22: A token on the hex above a city hid its banner (`ui-tour/map`)

- **Own banners drew under own units unless a foreign unit stood beside the city** (`render.ts::bannerAboveUnits`, now gone), so a unit on the NE or NW neighbour covered the name, population and meters (tour 2026-11-22, `unit-north/*`). Zoomed in, every banner now draws after the units; zoomed out the seal still draws under them.
- **Drawing the banner on top was not enough at the smallest near zooms (0.61-1.0):** the plate plus meters is about 40 px tall against a 44 px hex, so the token on the hex above vanished almost entirely. `bannerLift` raises such a token to the banner's top edge (at most half a hex), and its head and owner disc show. The raised token pokes out of its own hex, so `draw` returns `liftedHits` and the map routes clicks there to the token's hex.
- **Map clicks were hex-only:** a banner drawn over a token would have sent its clicks to the unit behind it. `bannerCityAt` (`bannerTooltip.ts`) routes click, tap, right-click and hover on a banner to its city, using the drawn parts (plate, and the meter/siege strip at its own width), not the cover box: an alarmed banner's cover box reaches the neighbouring hex's centre, which broke `occupation-lock`'s right-click. Tour helper `aim` now aims at a raised token's head when a banner covers the hex centre.

## 2026-11-22: Whole-game tour layout fixes at 1000x600 (`ui-tour/layout`)

- The city panel's idle reveal (`revealIdleProductionPicker`) scrolls to the build list under the sticky head, so any row between the head and the list (the yields) vanished on open. Rows that must stay in view belong inside `.cp-head`; `--cp-head-h` and the reveal follow its height on their own.
- The left dock's bottom is `--lens-height` + the lens gap: when the lens tool shrinks to a chip placed beside the dock, publish `--lens-height: 0` or the panel keeps the old bar's gap.
- Playwright clears `test-results/` at the start of every run, so re-shooting one domain deletes another domain's tour shots; run every domain you need to compare in one command. The research tour's `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` on the details centred them and cut the card names, masking what the panel itself does.

## 2026-11-22: Chrome consistency: one form per concept (`ui-tour/chrome`)

- **All-caps key hints came from `.btn { text-transform: uppercase }` reaching the `<kbd>` inside it.** `.kbd` and `:is(.btn,.act,.cta,.prod,.link) kbd` now set `text-transform: none`, so "Return" and "Esc" print as typed in every button.
- **Renaming a visible label means renaming its `aria-label` and every e2e `getByRole` name with it.** The Score ledger link kept `aria-label="All objectives"` and about 25 specs matched on it; the link now has no aria-label (its text is its name). Specs matching "Diplomacy inbox" now match `/^Diplomacy/`.

## 2026-11-22: Tour copy and icons: sheets, report medallions, Age word (`ui-tour/copyb`)

- **Faction sheets read `FACTION_GOAL[f].sheet` (one sentence), not `detail`.** `detail` keeps the fine print for the Guide, `vpModel` and the picker blurb; a goal without a `sheet` falls back to `detail`. VP amounts on the sheet go through `Amounts` so they wear the VP icon.
- **Report medallions take two pseudo-icon names:** `era-numeral:III` (the era's numeral seal) and `wonder:<Wonder>` (a natural wonder's painting), both handled by `Glyph` in `ringMedallion.tsx`. `EraEntered` was unreachable at its second `case` (the tech case came first), so era lines drew the tech emblem.
- **One word per concept is enforced by `app/src/test/copyRules.ts`** (HP not health, "No Walls", "Not growing", "Military units 3 / 2", "strength" not "melee" for a figure, "65 ranged · range 2", "Needs orders", "Needs X" for a missing tech). The Iron shortage line is built in `improvementReason` from supply data ("Siege needs 1 Iron. You have 2 Iron for 4 needs: ..."); when Upgrade and Siege are both blocked it is one line, because two lines push Disband out of the 1000x600 panel.

## 2026-11-22: A deploy broke the art in games already open in a tab (`claude/project-thread-7n64r8`)

- **Symptom:** after the WebP deploy (e9d1e50), Sam's open game showed broken images on late-era research cards (Stock Exchange, Tank, Fighter, Bomber, Research Lab) and missing city sprites on the map. The live build itself was fine: all 897 `new URL(...)` art references in its main script answered 200.
- **Cause:** Vite names built files by content (`assets/<name>-<hash>.<ext>`) and Cloudflare Pages serves only the newest deployment. A tab opened before a deploy keeps the old code, which loads art lazily by the old names; the WebP switch renamed every picture (`x-HASH.png` became `x-HASH.webp`, same hash), so every picture the tab had not yet shown answered 404. Pictures already shown were cached, which is why only later content broke. Any art change does this to the files it touches, not only the WebP switch.
- **Reloading was not the fix:** a reload runs the new build, and a save on an older `RULES_VERSION` couldn't continue there (`saveErrors.ts`). Since keep-builds (entry above) it opens in its own build instead, while the site still serves one with its rules (30 days).
- **Fix:** `tools/release/carry-assets.mjs` keeps every file a deployed build used in the last 30 days (its `asset-history.json` records the last day a build used each file, not the first: dating by first deploy would drop long-lived art at the very deploy that stops using it; a crawl of the live pages the first time) in the next deploy; production (`deploy-web.yml`) and previews (`deploy-preview.sh`) set `LEGATUM_CARRY_ASSETS_FROM`. A build that is already gone from the site can be rebuilt: the asset hash depends only on the file's content, so `LEGATUM_WEBP=0 npx vite build` at an older commit gives the exact PNG names (`--from-dir`). `tools/release/check-dist-refs.mjs` fails a build whose JS, CSS or HTML points to a file it lacks (web build and CI app build).

## 2026-11-22: A shared CARGO_TARGET_DIR ran another branch's tests (CI on many worktrees)
- **What happened.** Serial CI runs for several branches, each in its own git worktree, pointed at one `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`. A run printed "Finished release in 0.05s" and ran the previous branch's test binaries; an engine PR "passed" tests that never compiled its code.
- **Cause.** Cargo hashes path packages relative to the workspace root, so every worktree maps to the same fingerprints, and it judges freshness by file mtime. A second worktree whose files are older than the last build counts as up to date.
- **Rule.** Never share one target dir between worktrees, even serially. For serial CI on many branches use one worktree and `git checkout --detach origin/<branch>` per run (checkout bumps the mtime of every changed file); for concurrent agents, one private target dir per worktree. A CI log with no "Compiling" lines for a branch that changes Rust is a red flag. Recorded in `legatum-workflow`.

## 2026-11-22: Uploaded games are analysed on the Mac (`tools/games`)

- **Uploads are hostile input, and two paths reached code execution on the Mac (fixed before install).** (1) The job built whatever `engine_commit` a game named; GitHub serves `refs/pull/*/head` from the same repo, so a fake upload could point at a stranger's PR commit and get it built and run. Now only commits that are ancestors of `origin/master` are built, and only master is fetched. (2) The notification put seat faction names inside AppleScript source; a quote broke out into `do shell script`. Now the text is an argv item and the Worker accepts only plain-word faction/difficulty names. Also added: per-run and per-day analysis caps and skipping AI-only games, so junk uploads can't spend the model budget; timeouts on every command (a stuck run held the lock forever); and the reviewer now gets facts.md pasted in with no tools (`claude -p --tools ""`), because `--allowedTools Read,Grep,Glob` still let injected coach text read any file on the Mac. The Worker now caps coach-answer keys and lengths, so the app clips answers to 2000 characters: a longer one would have had the whole game refused.
- **Every new uploaded field the job reads needs the same hostile-input care.** `frame_summary` (added for the lag work) indexed `frame_times` rows directly, so a crafted upload with a missing key crashed facts.md, and a string in `units` went straight into the reviewer's prompt. Rows now pass only if every field is a whole number in range. Check this for any new field the analysis job reads.
- **Not every uploaded game is evidence (designer, 2026-11-22).** Sam abandoned an India game after two misclicks (an Archer bought in the wrong city, walls cancelled): learning from actions he did not intend teaches the AI nothing. `analyse_games.py exclude <id> --reason` keeps such a game out of analysis and lists it under "Excluded" in `reports/INDEX.md`; anything that reads R2 directly (hill-climb) should check the ledger's `excluded` field too.
- **Squashing master to one root commit orphans every engine commit games were played on.** After the open-source squash (new root 5718ac4), no earlier `engine_commit` is an ancestor of master, so the master-only build rule would refuse every game played before the site is redeployed from the new root (cached builds still work). The job now also trusts refs pinned by hand under `refs/trusted/` in its own clone; pin the pre-squash master there. Any future history rewrite needs the same pin.
- **A reviewer outage must not count as the game's failure.** While the Mac's `claude` login was expired every review failed, each failure used one of the game's 3 tries, and the finished games were locked out until re-run by hand with `--force`. Now a failed review is followed by a trivial canary call; if that fails too, it raises `ReviewerDown`: the game keeps its tries, the run stops, and the owner gets one notification (`ledger["reviewer_down"]`, cleared by the next success).
- **First real run (2026-11-22): open wars crashed facts.md, and some engine commits are refused by design.** Telemetry wars still open at the end have `end=None`; `sorted()` compared it with an int when two wars started on the same turn (Sam's Switzerland game). Sorted with an explicit key now. Games played on pre-cut engines (legatum-private `1695f6e`) or on a PR preview build (`1fc5322`, reachable only through `refs/pull`) are refused by the master-only rule: they still get telemetry facts, just no event replay. Also: the job's reviews silently fail when the Mac's `claude` login expires ("OAuth session expired"); the fix is signing in again, and each upload gets 3 tries.
- **A prompt's data markers and a "longest first" queue are attack surface too (security review).** A coach answer could contain the literal `=== FACTS END ===` line and pose as instructions after the data block, so `clip()` thins runs of `=`. Picking due games longest-first let junk uploads claiming 10,000 turns take every daily slot, so the queue now goes finished-first, then newest upload. `security add-generic-password -w TOKEN` shows the token in `ps`; pipe the command into `security -i` instead.
- **A site deploy can strand a game in progress.** Deploying PR #33 also shipped rules 50 (#31), and the app refuses a rules-49 autosave (`json.rs`: only the sim upgrades 49 saves). Telling the player to reload to pick up a fix was wrong: a tab started on older rules must finish where it is. Before advising a reload, compare `RULES_VERSION` between the build the player is on and the one being deployed. The analysis job is unaffected: it replays each game at its own engine commit.
- **Uploads were never read.** Since the web-only change every game reached R2, but nothing looked at it, and coach answers were not saved at all (fixed in the same branch). `tools/games/analyse_games.py` polls the games Worker's private read API (`/v1/read/games`, bearer `READ_TOKEN`; 404 without it, so the API's existence is not advertised), keeps `~/LegatumData/game-analysis/ledger.json` (one row per game, analysed once per upload etag), and writes `reports/<game id>/facts.md` + `review.md`.
- **Replay needs the game's own engine.** A record only replays on the commit it was played on, so the job builds `game_dump` and `legatum-analyze` at `metadata.engine_commit` and caches them per commit. `game_dump` replays from the tape's setup: on a partial recording (`initial_state` set) it reports every turn as a hash mismatch, which is not a bug; the job skips it there and trusts `legatum-analyze`, which replays from the saved state.
- **`claude -p` with `--add-dir DIR "prompt"` loses the prompt:** `--add-dir` is variadic and swallows it ("was read as a value of --add-dir"). Send the prompt on stdin.
- **Coach answers are not engine data.** The upload carries them as a top-level `coach_answers` key; the Rust `Recording` denies unknown fields, so anything that feeds a record back to the engine (export, `legatum-analyze`) must strip it first.

## 2026-11-22: Coach answers were silently dropped after the web-only change (`claude/coach-answers-saved`)

- **Cause:** `writeAnswer` only appended to a module-level array (`unwrittenAnswers`); the desktop app that used to write it to disk was removed, so answers died with the tab while the designer was answering in a real game.
- **Fix:** answers now live in `WhyMemory.answers` (last 500, so the save file and localStorage autosave carry them; old saves load with none) and the hosted build's upload copy carries them as top-level `coach_answers` (`historyToRecord`). The engine's `Recording` has `deny_unknown_fields`, so the key exists only in the upload copy: `exportGameHistory` strips it and `client.archive()` never has it. The Worker accepts an array of at most 2000 objects. README Privacy says so; the title notice does not (a third line overflowed the footer at 1000x600).

## 2026-11-22: A new order kind is dropped silently unless the app's decoder knows it (PR 31 UI review)

- **`ORDER_KINDS` in `app/src/orderDecoding.ts` is a hand-kept copy of `core::orders::Order`.** `decodeOrders` runs on every order list that comes from storage or the relay (`remote.ts`). It drops a kind it doesn't know, with only a console warning, and `describeOrder` shows "Unknown order (GiveGold)". `GiveGold` was added to the core, the TypeScript union and `describeOrder`, but not to the set. So a queued gift showed as unknown, and an online guest's gift would never have reached the host. tsc can't catch this, because the set is typed `Set<Order['type']>` and a missing member is legal. A test now checks that a `GiveGold` order survives `decodeOrders`, and the give-gold tour asserts the pending-orders line.
- **`withThe` gave "the Athens".** It added "the" to any name ending in "s". It now adds it only to a people's name (`PEOPLE_NAME`: the Aztecs, the Mongols, the Celts), never to Athens or Switzerland.

## 2026-11-22: Rules-49 states upgrade for sims; co-op Gold gifts (rules 50, `claude/review-world-ages`)

- **A rules-49 state can be upgraded faithfully, with two known differences.** `wasm::json::upgrade_from_49` drops each player's `capitals_taken`. A Celtic entry whose city no longer exists becomes `capitals_sacked`, which is exactly the rules-50 exception. Held-capital VP is derived live from current holders. Every scored Age card's `slot` is set to 0, since only one card per Age exists now. The two differences: the slot rename (display only), and a non-Celtic capital razed by order loses its point, which is what rules 50 intends. The app still refuses 49. Only sims opt in (`hill_suite --accept-rules 49`). The checked-in fixture is the owner's "India: strike the leader" T148 state (`crates/sim/tests/fixtures-rules49/`), which plays 10 all-Hard turns after the upgrade.
- **Tapes cannot be upgraded this way.** A tape replays from turn 1 under new rules, so a 49 tape still fails on its first hash mismatch. Only states carry over.
- **Gold gifts move after purchases, not at the turn's start.** If a gift moved first, the giver could promise Gold and then spend it on a purchase in the same turn, and both would be checked against the same treasury. Moving the gift after `apply_actions`, capped at the Gold left, means purchases always win and a gift never overdraws. Validation still checks all of a turn's gifts against the starting treasury, so the forecast the player sees is an upper bound ("before income and purchases"). The AI never gives Gold. Its fixture panics on a `GiveGold`, so a future AI path has to be added on purpose.
- **Wonder prices had no curve.** All seven world wonders pay the same 2 held VP, but the Ancient Pyramids cost 240 while the Classical Great Library cost 600, more than the Medieval Hagia Sophia (400). A geometric fit by era index (about x1.13 per era) put the Pyramids 35% under and the Great Library 45% over. Every other wonder was within 15%. Changed only those two: Pyramids 360, Great Library 400 (equal to the Hagia Sophia, so a later wonder is never cheaper). The Star Fort moved from Military Engineering to Machinery, because Industrial units with built-in Siege arrive one era after Military Engineering and left the third wall tier almost no time to matter. Both are a first pass the owner may veto. On the final rules-50 tree a two-player Hard duel ended by the capitals victory in 22 of 24 games (VP hold 2 of 24; 1 of 8 before the wonder change). So `hold_metrics` now samples 16 games, not 8. The per-era fit is in the rules skill (§3 "Wonder price curve").

## 2026-11-22: World Ages becomes the only era scoring; three peaceful cards per Age (rules 50, `claude/review-world-ages`)

- **What was removed.** The `objectives_deck` switch and the `objectives=era` arm, plus everything only that arm used. On the card side: the Techs, Captures, CapturesFromAhead, CapturesFromLeader, Kills and BreakLeaderHold kinds, `era_pool`, and the slot-per-family draw. Counters went too: `FightStats` captures, kills and leader counters, `Levels::techs`, and the `CaptureVpSnapshot` leader. The AI lost its arms for those kinds (`objective_pursuit`), and the app lost every `rule_experiments?.objectives_deck` branch plus the lobby's Rule experiments section. The setup and staging types the lobby used are gone with it.
- **Old files are refused, not misread.** Saves and tapes are refused by `save_version` (49 against 50) with the usual version message. Removed struct fields are ignored when an old JSON carries them, because no core type uses `deny_unknown_fields`. So a version-50 file written before this change, with `objectives_deck: true` or the old `FightStats` counters, still loads. A version-50 save from the era arm keeps its records but reads them as Age records. That is accepted: no such save shipped, because rules 50 came out together with this change. The WASM staging room keeps an empty `rule_experiments` struct, so an older room JSON still parses.
- **Peaceful cards.** These are Patrons (Library/University/Bank/Research Lab line), Prosperity and Frontier, on the old era objectives' Build/Expand counts and lobby-scaled. They exist because a drawn Fight card left some factions with no path to the Age. Still one card per Age (the 2026-11-11 ruling), so a peaceful card comes up 3 times in 6 (3 in 7 from the Renaissance). The draw index is taken over the scaled pool, which has the same length as `age_pool`, so the lobby size never changes which card a seed draws.
- **Removing an enum variant breaks historical AI fixture views, not saves.** `PlayerView.eras[].objectives` carries `Objective` values, so 29 gzipped views under `crates/ai/src/fixtures` and `crates/ai/tests/fixtures` stopped parsing with "unknown variant `Kills`". Saves were unaffected, because `EraRecord` stores no `Objective`. The fix was to drop the cards of removed kinds from those views and leave everything else alone. A related fragility: `settler_routes::archived_detour_*` assumed a persistent brain orders the same as a fresh brain on a reloaded state. Under the new cards, the persistent brain picks the Pyramids on T153 where a fresh one picks Walls. The test now checks the real invariant: fresh on the state equals fresh on the reloaded copy.
- **The app's render fixture (`withV2`) now reveals only begun Ages**, which are Ages up to the later of your era and the rival's era 3. It keeps three cards per Age so list and sheet tests still see several lines, although a real game has one. Tests that assumed a preview of an unentered era or a "next era" block were rewritten for Ages. The e2e specs that used those surfaces were edited but not run here (no Playwright browser in the container).

## 2026-11-22: Fable review batch: held capital VP, Alliance waiver, buy-under-threat experiment, seeded ties (rules 50, `claude/review-rules-batch`)

- **Held capital VP needs no new save field.** The point is computed live (`scoring::capitals_held`: owner holds an `original_capital` whose founder is on another team, barbarians excluded), like a world wonder. `Player::capitals_taken` is gone. `CapitalTaken`/`CapitalLost` events come from diffing `CaptureVpSnapshot::capitals` (pre-turn holders) against the post-turn holders in `scoring::update_with_capture_snapshot`, so a city that changes hands twice in a turn reports only the net change. A hold breaks the same turn the city is lost, through the ordinary `vp_holds` check.
- **The Celtic sack keeps its capital point (corrected the same day).** The first cut let a held-only rule silently take the Celts' decided sack point away, because a burned city can't be held. That weakened a faction's signature (pillar 8). It is now an explicit exception: `Player::capitals_sacked`, written only by the sack path, and `VpSource::CapitalSacked`. When a general rule changes, check each faction's exceptions to it.
- **Alliance waiver reads "either side".** `diplomacy::upkeep_waived_by` waives an Alliance's upkeep when either allied team is at War with a third team that `scoring::near_victory` (alive, and on a hold or within `NEAR_VICTORY_VP` = 2 of its target). The Gold breakdown shows two lines (the upkeep, then the waiver as a minus) because `Breakdown::add` drops zero lines. The AI prices an Alliance at the waived upkeep from public data (`PlayerView::alliance_upkeep_waived_by`, `ai::diplomacy::treaty_upkeep`).
- **Same-turn ties use `rng::seeded_order`.** Keys are drawn in sorted item order, then items are sorted by (key, item), so the result does not depend on the order of the input. Tile buys: losing empires' first `BuyTile` for a contested tile is deferred until after every empire's city orders, then fails as "already owned". Eras: `eras::draw_same_turn_firsts` redraws only eras first claimed this turn. Wonders: `cities::wonder_race_order` puts the bigger overflow first, then draws, and only reorders the cities that finish a Great Work this turn.
- **A shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` across worktrees of the same repo is unsafe.** Workspace path crates hash to the same `-C metadata` in every worktree, so one worktree's `legatum-core` rlib silently replaces another's. Clippy failed on a field that existed in this worktree's source. Use one target directory per worktree when several agents build at once.

## 2026-11-22: The new-era card reopened the research screen after research was picked (`claude/project-thread-fuxj51`)

- **A held card's "Choose research" was frozen when the turn's story was planned.** `eraUpCardFor` and `unlockCardFor` read `research.queue`/`current` from the view after the turn; a research pick is a pending `SetResearchQueue` order, not in the view until the next turn resolves. The held card also waits behind any open dialog (`useDialogOpen` in `MomentLane.tsx`), so a player who opened the research screen quickly, picked, then met the era takeover still saw "Choose research", and Return reopened the screen. `celebrate/researchSettled.ts` now re-checks `effectiveResearchQueue` over the pending orders when the card shows and turns that primary into Continue (or keeps Show for an unlock).

## 2026-11-22: Engine robustness review: save references, aircraft upgrade Gold, save/hash allocations (`claude/review-engine-robustness`)

- **Save validation may only refuse what would index out of bounds: player ids (into `players`) and tiles (into the map).** Team, city and unit references are stale in ordinary play (a razed Hall city, pruned camp rosters, memories, supply records, relations of a team whose empires are gone) and every reader uses `.get` or a member filter; no code indexes by a team id. A first version also refused stale team references and stale city/unit ids (those named by Hall records, camp rosters, memories and the like) and was wrong: it refused the app's Hall and Broadcast fixtures and an analysis test (`hall.wasm.test.ts`, `actions.wasm.test.ts`, `analysis/tests/rules.rs`). `save_fuzz.rs` now round-trips three full all-Hard games through `parse_state` every 5 turns.
- **A `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` shared between worktrees gave false green runs:** cargo treated another worktree's artifacts as fresh, so the tests never ran against this branch's code. Verify in a target dir no other worktree uses, and check the log shows `Compiling legatum-core`.
- **The review's barbarian capture panic is not reachable:** movement never puts a barbarian into `captures_city` (`movement.rs`, "Barbarians besiege cities but never take them"). `capture_city` now returns early for a barbarian captor anyway.
- **Aircraft upgrades are paid in the ranged phase, after unit upgrades and city purchases, in aircraft-id order** (not list order). Validation now checks them against the city validator's projected state (`cities::validate_city_orders_projected`) in that same order, applying each.
- **A random-number save fuzzer found no panics even with the new reference checks switched off** (3,000 cases, 40-turn game): the id fields that panic are mostly absent (`None`) in ordinary saves, so only the targeted mutation test (`validation::tests`) exercises them.

## 2026-11-22: Web resilience: a crash card, a loading plate and a narrow-window plate (`claude/review-web-resilience`)

- **A render error anywhere blanked the page.** The only error boundary was SeatBar's order list, so a throw in any panel unmounted the React root mid-game. `ui/CrashScreen.tsx` adds a root boundary (wired in `main.tsx`) and window `error`/`unhandledrejection` handlers. Window errors go to the card only from an allowlist (`isFatal`: a `WebAssembly.RuntimeError`, which means the engine trapped, and a failed download of a lazy chunk); everything else is only logged, so a stray background rejection never covers a working game.
- **The crash card imports nothing from the game.** It reads `legatum.autosave.v1` straight from localStorage (a copy of `store.ts::AUTOSAVE_KEY`) and names the file like `platform/files.ts`. When autosaves move to IndexedDB, `readAutosaveText` must read there as well (keeping the localStorage fallback for older saves).
- **The engine is ~7 MB and the page was empty until it arrived.** `index.html` now holds a static plate in `#root` (React replaces it on mount). The hosted CSP forbids inline scripts but allows inline styles, so the "Still loading?" line is revealed by a CSS animation delay, not a timer. A module that throws during import never reaches React, and the plate stays with that line, which is better than a blank page.
- **An engine that is in the build but fails to download used to boot the app anyway** (`main.tsx` swallowed the rejection), and the failure only surfaced on New Game. Now it shows the crash card's engine form with Reload. A build without the engine keeps its own "missing from this build" screen.
- **happy-dom's `navigator.webdriver` is always true, and Playwright's is too.** The narrow-window plate skips automation so the phone-layout e2e specs keep testing the layout behind it; its unit test mocks the getter to false.
- **This container has no `wasm-pack`, so the WASM package could not be built here.** The new e2e checks ran against a hosted build made with a stub engine package (a 5 KB valid `.wasm` fetched like the real loader); the full hosted play-through was not run.

## 2026-11-22: Egypt's Settler counted Production; a worktree build gave a stale sim binary (`claude/project-thread-h0s4wd`)

- **Site score.** `settlement.rs` scored Egypt's sites with plain terrain yields (Food ×4 + Production ×3), so its Settler chased Hills and Forest it gets nothing from. `site_tile_yields` now applies the Nile (double Food, no Production) in both `site_potential` and the memoised `SiteBasics::inputs`, which must agree, as the test checks. Over 400 Egypt seats the win rate held at 18.8% and eliminations fell from 9.0% to 5.8% (`docs/sim-results/egypt-settle-nile-2026-11-22.md`). This is view and AI only, so no `RULES_VERSION` bump.
- **Stale binary.** I built master in a git worktree, then the branch in the main checkout, with the same `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`. The branch binary came out after about a minute, and 400 games matched master's game for game, down to every field but `wall_ms`. A `touch` and rebuild (3.5 min) gave a binary that plays differently. Before trusting an A/B, diff a few games' JSON between the arms: if every game is identical, suspect the build before the change.

## 2026-11-22: Dropping the desktop app: what the browser build had been leaning on (`web-only`)

- **Most "desktop-only" code was a second copy of a browser path, not a feature.** Autosave, game history, training logs, why-answers, scenario runs and haptics each had a Tauri branch that wrote to `~/LegatumData` and a browser branch (localStorage, IndexedDB, download, Vibration API). Removing Tauri meant deleting the native branch and making the browser branch the only one, plus making `readAutosave` synchronous (the title screen no longer waits on "Checking autosaves…").
- **Native training ran the AI search on threads in Rust; the browser runs it in slices on the main thread.** `TrainingDriver` lost its native mode; the sim keeps the parallel `search_turn` feature for itself.
- **A fake-timer test hung vitest for 12 minutes.** `vi.useFakeTimers()` freezes `performance.now()`, so the training slice loop (`do { … } while (performance.now() < deadline)`) never ended. Time-capped loops need real timers in tests (a 100 ms cap is enough).
- **The online host could not stay native.** The Mac app embedded `legatum-server`, which dialed the Cloudflare relay. In the browser the authoritative host (game, staging, AI) runs in the host's tab as WASM and a JS tunnel speaks the relay's frame format; the LAN server is gone, since the relay replaces it and the designer's firewall blocked LAN anyway.
- **Scenario folder drop-ins existed only because the app could read a folder.** On the web a scenario ships by merging to master (the Pages deploy), and playtests are Pages previews (`scripts/deploy-preview.sh <label>`).
- **`npm run e2e:hosted` is not in `tools/ci/local-ci.sh`,** so its title assertions went stale when co-op moved into the browser (it still expected a disabled Host Game). Run it by hand (`scripts/build-web.sh --skip-wasm && cd app && npm run e2e:hosted`) whenever the title or multiplayer menu changes.

## 2026-11-22: Art audit pass: map grade on card art, floating unlock pieces, converging faces (`claude/art-pass-1122`)

- **The washed-out wonder cards were the map's palette grade.** `landmark-atlas-edit.py` grades wonders and Great Works so they sit beside the towns on the map, and the same 256/128/64 files served the research cards. The map now reads `assets/landmarks/<slug>-256.png` (`landmarkArtUrl`) and the cards get the ungraded copy (`tools/art/card-grade.py`), no regeneration needed.
- **Five unlock pieces had no ground** (the three Siege-line effects reused the floating siege props; India's two buildings were cut out without a plot), and the Supermarket read as clip art. Repainted with Codex image generation on the Mac (Remote Control, 4 at a time), two variants each, 28 images in about 6 minutes, none refused.
- **The stock magenta key erases thin parts of a small subject:** chhatri columns and a sling's ropes vanished, leaving domes and the pouch floating. `tools/art/unlock-art.py` crops and enlarges the subject before using `unit-art.py`'s edge key.
- **Portrait edits that change only one face work first time** with an edit prompt that pins everything else ("the woman must not change at all").

## 2026-11-22: Art gets its own reviewer; the lookalike check never judged style or staleness (`claude/art-reviewer`)

- **The only automated art check was "too similar to another piece"** (`tools/art/distinctness.py`, run by `app/src/artDistinct.test.ts`). It passed with 0 failures while the launch build still showed unlock paintings with no ground under them, faded wonder tiles beside vivid neighbours, a unit copied from a commercial robot and five portraits whose men share one face. Style, set fit, subject correctness and stale art need a reviewer that looks at the pictures.
- **A numeric style check is too noisy on its own.** Mean saturation, coverage and ground width per unlock flagged the faded wonders but also correct grey stone and concrete (ITER, the Swiss redoubt, the Machine Shop), and missed groundless pieces whose wheels or barrels span the bottom rows. `tools/art/art-review.py sheet` instead draws each changed piece beside its old version and its unchanged set neighbours at display size, in colour and greyscale, for the reviewer to judge.
- **Art goes stale the way docs do.** `art-review.py impact` lists renames and new names in `data.rs`/`eras.rs`, new art-carrying enum variants, art slugs wired in `app/src` with no file, deleted art still named in `distinctness.json`, and art changed with no prompt or README beside it. The gate now requires `.progress/art-review/<branch>.ok` from `legatum-art-reviewer` when art or that code changes (`ART_IMPACT` in `tools/gates/check.py`). Terrain sprites (`app/public/terrain/`) now count as art too.
- **The first review run (the 2026-11-22 art pass) shaped the tool:** one sheet per size made five identical sheets per repaint (now one per set at the judged size); folder neighbours put the Hunter-Killer beside the Archer instead of the tanks (`--with=`); copies need `git diff -C`; and it found what the lead had missed: two floating shrub crowns, a trebuchet pouch cut from its ropes, a Mechanised Infantry icon still tracked after a wheeled repaint, stale sibling prompts and PROVENANCE dates, all now `impact` lines.
- **The UI reviewer judged clarity but never simplicity** (user, 2026-11-22: "question every requirement"): each screen now gets "what can we remove or merge" findings, and a whole-game consistency table (one icon, word, colour and component per concept) runs before each release, since branch reviews only see changed screens. Art quality moved to the art reviewer; layout of art stays with the UI reviewer.

## 2026-11-22: Online co-op runs in the host's browser tab; what moving the server into WASM taught (`claude/browser-host`)

- **The old server's host logic needed no transport to work.** `host.rs`, staging, protocol, events and admission moved into `crates/wasm/src/host` almost unchanged once the async outbox became a synchronous queue drained by the caller (`NetHost::drain`). The 29 server protocol tests still pass, driven synchronously in `crates/wasm/tests/net_host.rs`. `crates/server` (axum/tokio, CLI, LAN, embedded relay client) is deleted.
- **A browser cannot send WebSocket pings.** The relay closes a host tunnel that sends text frames, and ignores unknown frame kinds. So the host sends an empty binary `ping` frame (kind 6) every 20 s. Staging clients now send a `{type:"ping"}` every 20 s too, because the host's silence timeout applies to them.
- **A browser cannot see why a WebSocket upgrade failed.** It gets only `onclose`, with no HTTP status. A host tunnel that never opens therefore asks `GET /rooms/<id>/api/status`; a 404 means the relay forgot the room, and the host announces a new one.
- **Background tabs throttle timers to about once a minute.** A 60 s silence timeout would have closed guests whose tab was in the background. Guests now get 120 s; the host's own in-tab connection is never timed out.
- **`catch_unwind` does nothing in WASM (panic=abort).** A host panic ends the game for everyone. The server's per-request panic guard has no equivalent, so host bugs must be fixed rather than contained.
- **Randomness and time in a crate that builds for both targets:** seat credentials use `crypto.getRandomValues` through a `wasm_bindgen` extern on wasm32 and `getrandom` natively; `now` is `js_sys::Date` on wasm32. `getrandom` on wasm32 would need the `wasm_js` backend and a cfg flag in every build.
- **The title screen should not load a 15 MB engine to learn the rules version.** `vite.config.ts` reads `RULES_VERSION` from `crates/core/src/lib.rs` into a define, and `online.test.ts` checks it against the engine's `online_rules_version()`.
- **Reload resume lives in `sessionStorage`, per tab.** The host keeps its room key and latest save (`legatum.online.hosting`, `legatum.online.save`); a guest keeps its room (`legatum.online.room`). A new tab never takes over a game. A save too large for `sessionStorage` silently loses only the reload resume.
- **Vitest's `runAllTimersAsync` never ends while an interval runs.** The staging keepalive made one lobby test loop 10,000 timers; that test now advances a fixed 60 s.
- **The e2e suite now runs on the static build and the real relay.** `e2e/fixtures.ts` serves `app/dist` with its own `_headers` (CSP included), and the `relay` fixture starts `infra/relay` under `wrangler dev` on 127.0.0.1:8787. The build must be made for that relay (`LEGATUM_RELAY_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8787 scripts/build-web.sh`), because the relay URL and CSP are baked in. wrangler runs workerd as a child process, so the fixture kills the whole process group.

## 2026-11-22: Relay lobby limits (`claude/review-relay-hardening`)

- **A Room cannot see WebSocket protocol pings.** The Workers runtime answers them without waking the Durable Object, so a host liveness check needs app-level frames (the browser host's kind-6 `PING`). The 60 s heartbeat applies only to a host that has sent at least one.
- **Awaiting an RPC inside `webSocketMessage` lets the next frame overtake the current one.** Heartbeat reports to the Lobby are fire-and-forget (`ctx.waitUntil`).
- **Limits live in the Worker, not the dashboard:** 2 live rooms and 6 creations per 10 minutes per hashed IP, per-socket message and byte buckets sized from a real turn (8 guests, chunked multi-MiB views), `LOBBY_OPEN=0` as a kill switch. Raw IPs are never stored.
- **Not done:** the client still shows one generic message for a refused room and ignores guest close codes 1013 and 4029 (`app/src/net/browserHost.ts`, `app/src/remote.ts`).

## 2026-11-22: Egypt's tiles still showed Production: the Nile lives on city totals, not tiles (`claude/project-thread-h0s4wd`)

- **Cause.** Core applies the Nile in `economy::nile` to a city's totals, so every per-tile number (`economy::owner_tile_yields`, the view's `next_claim.yields`, `improvement_preview.gain`) is the plain tile yield. The app's map yield icons, tile card, "Next tile" claim and improvement cards read those and showed Egypt Production it never gets, and Food at half its real value.
- **Fix (display only, no rules change, saves keep loading).** `resources.ts::nileYields` (double Food, no Production for Egypt) wraps `tileYields`, `resourceBenefitLines`, `cityProgress::claimSummary` and the city panel's improvement gains. The core view leaves the Quarry out of an Egyptian city's options (`view.rs`, test `egypt_is_not_offered_a_quarry_but_may_still_order_one`); the order itself stays legal, so no `RULES_VERSION` bump. Core per-tile numbers are left raw because the AI reads them.
- **Dormant prompts follow the offer.** `improvements.ts::isDormant` didn't know the Quarry was gone, so Egypt's Stone kept a red "build the Quarry" note, a dashed dormant ring and a place in the unimproved count. It now takes the faction (`offersImprovement`).
- **Settle lens (done later the same day).** See the entry above: the site score now applies the Nile too.
## 2026-11-22: Web games upload to R2; player names are inside the state hash

- **The hosted web build kept every game only in the browser.** Histories queue in IndexedDB (`legatum-game-archive-v1`) and `deliver()` returned false on the hosted build, so they sat there until the player used Export game history. A designer's Hard game (Athens, 145 turns) was still recoverable from that queue after he left it, but a cleared browser would have lost it. Now `deliver()` uploads to the `infra/games` Worker when the build has `LEGATUM_GAMES_URL`, and a success acknowledges (deletes) the local copy.
- **Removing names from a recording needs a replay.** `GameState::hash` hashes the serialized state, which includes `players[].name` and `setup.players[].name`, so renaming seats breaks every tape hash. `Recording::anonymized` renames to the defaults ("Player N", "AI N") and re-resolves each turn's recorded orders to recompute the hashes (no AI search, so it is fast). A recording already on default names skips the replay. Orders carry no free text (diplomacy explanations are AI-generated), so names are the only typed text in a tape.
- **The hosted page's CSP allows `connect-src 'self'` only**, so any off-site endpoint must be added to `app/hosted/_headers`; `scripts/build-web.sh` adds the upload Worker there when the variable is set.
## 2026-11-22: A tech that gives a faction nothing is skipped, not shown as a "No benefit" card (rules 49, `claude/project-thread-gux8ae`)

- **Audit (master b7fa587, every faction × tech, state-aware for the Mongols' era-gated Walls):** the only empty pair was Egypt × Bronze Working (the Nile removes Production, so the Workshop is unavailable). But `tech_has_benefit` counted any card line, and six lines only add Production, which Egypt never has (Deep Quarry, +1 Production per Workshop family, Railways' Mines, Factories +2, Fusion's +5, Recursive AI's +20%): Egypt's cards listed bonuses it can't use. They are now `CardEffect::Production` and hidden for Egypt; every affected tech still gives Egypt something else.
- **Skipping by putting the tech in the known set at game start is the smallest change that keeps every rule honest.** `eras::current_era` already walks the known prefix, so Egypt finishes the Ancient era after four techs with no era code touched. What had to change: diffusion (`tech_known_by` would otherwise give every rival 10% off Bronze Working from turn 1), the view's era counts, and every app screen built on `research.catalog` (`researchCatalog`), including the "N of 45 known" header and the after-the-last-tech check, which compare known against the catalog.
- **Balance: no measurable shift.** Eight-seat scripted Hard, both Egypt pools of `docs/sim-results/egypt-nile-2026-11-21.md` (seeds 7000-7199 and 9000-9199, `--rotate-factions`, 400 Egypt seats an arm): Egypt wins 17.2% on master and 17.8% on the branch (CI about ±3.7 pp), eliminated 40 against 33 times, 4.77 against 4.85 cities at T100.
- **The gate:** `factions.rs::no_faction_ever_researches_a_tech_that_gives_it_nothing` pins the skip list and requires every other pair to give something in its own era. A faction change that empties a tech fails it; prefer giving the tech something real, and skip only on purpose.

## 2026-11-22: First weekly lean pass: 55 dead examples deleted (`perf-dead-examples`)

- **55 of 134 example programs (10,775 lines, 3.9% of the Rust) were named by nothing**: no script, tool, CI step, test group, Cargo.toml entry or current doc. They were one-off investigation scripts for finished AI and balance studies. Historical docs (learnings, sim-results, playtests) still name them, so treat those names as gone; the old history keeps the files (legatum-private). Before deleting tooling, ask the threads that run long jobs (the hill climb uses only `duel`, `hc_duel` and `hill_suite`).
- **Deleting examples did not speed up CI here**: cold `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` took 50 s with them and 54 s without, because sccache serves most of the work and the test groups never built examples. The gain is code to maintain, not CI minutes; measure before claiming a speedup.

## 2026-11-22: Performance checks lapsed inside the ops supervisor; a separate nightly supervisor (`perf-supervisor`)

- **The old performance watcher was a few sections of `legatum-ops-supervisor`, and they quietly stopped.** The ops check kept running every 6 hours on the Mac, but its runs grew into fleet, disk and budget checks; the last sim-speed baseline is 2026-10-31, the AI turn budget 2026-10-27, app size 2026-10-23, and the `ledger.csv` it was told to compare against never existed. A check buried in a long checklist with heavier duties gets dropped first.
