Performance and tech debt now belong to `legatum-perf-supervisor`, which only measures, flags and makes one improvement a week.
- **Cloud-container timings are too noisy for night-to-night comparison.** Two single runs of the same `sim_bench` binary on a 4-core container differed by about 6% (duel 2.29 vs 2.14 CPU s/game on a docs-only range). `tools/perf/lean_check.py` therefore builds base and head side by side and runs them interleaved, best of three; only sizes and counts, which are exact, are compared with the previous ledger row.
- **A fresh container can corrupt the pinned toolchain if two cargo commands race its first install** ("the 'cargo' binary ... is not applicable to the '1.98.1' toolchain"). `rustup toolchain uninstall 1.98.1` and one cargo command reinstall it cleanly.
- **The repo tracks about 332 MB in 5,039 files (66 MB under docs/), and the text sessions may load (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, skills, agents, learnings) is about 3 MB.** Both are now ledger metrics, so growth shows up the night it lands.

## 2026-11-21: Border-army watch (Hard reads a stronger neighbour's army parked at a city in peace) held Geneva on rules 47, not on rules 48: dropped

- **Tried:** gene `border_army_watch` (branches `claude/hc-border-army-2..4`). A peaceful empire with at least twice our public army whose visible units stand within 3 hexes of a city added their declaration-weighted threat power; while twice that exceeded local defence the city built Walls or a defender (melee first, kept against routine production), and one defender was bought while the pressure itself exceeded local defence. Cause it answered (designer's Mongol game 2026-11-21, traced): 3-7 Keshigs 2-3 hexes from Geneva T52-T61 at peace, public army 301-409 against 65, every Hard defence term 0 (threat score under the top-threat bar, border-army term capped, early warning off, door and city threat count only units at war).
- **Rules 47 (hybrid seat, 96 paired):** M51-geneva 0% -> 75.0 (defend on, 72/0) and 70.8 (defend off, 68/0); the trace showed the two extra Swiss units (+1 Soldier, +1 Ranger by T61) slowed the bombardment (Geneva 75 HP at T65 against 48) and kept a body inside.
- **Rules 48 (Egypt's Nile, master 50b497f7f):** the Mongol tape is the only one with an Egypt seat and drifts from T1. The gene still fires identically (the same two extra units by T61), but Geneva's HP now falls on master's schedule in both arms (51 at T65, 0 at T67) and the city is taken on T67: M51 0 -> 6.2 on both defend columns. M72, S67, S120, M90, S131 and the three Celts cells: 0 discordant pairs.
- **Fresh games (200 all-Hard, 6 players, seeds 9311003+, gene on vs off):** the trigger holds about 10 times a game (2,094 episodes against 2,106); a watched city is captured within 20 turns in 15.9% against 16.9% (about 0.9 standard errors); city captures per game 13.47 against 14.14; 0 panics. No reliable effect.
- **Lesson:** a gain carried by one old position must show its mechanism on a second position before it counts. Two Ancient-era defenders are too small an answer once the siege timing moves a turn. A next attempt should weigh Walls and defenders against the attacker's forecast siege clock (the turns this army needs to breach and take the city) rather than a fixed 2x pressure bar.

## 2026-11-21: Pricing the home a campaign leaves exposed kept armies home without saving home cities (campaign_home_exposure, dropped)

- **What was tried:** gene `campaign_home_exposure` (Hard). A Campaign's failure branch, (100 - odds)% of outcomes, was charged with the home cities its departing force leaves exposed (visible strength of teams at war or able to declare, within 6 tiles, less the city's defence and troops staying within 2), each priced at what the capture is worth. Cause it targeted: on his Switzerland 8.9.72 hold (S131) at tech parity, the Celts (1.2% win chance) campaigned on Emain Macha at a forecast 20% and lost three cities to his hold. Pricing that loss in the Celts' own win chance gave only -27 to -36, because a weak team's chance cannot fall below its small total while a capital capture was priced at +1,115.
- **Rules 48 (master 50b497f7f), hybrid seat, 96 paired runs, lower of hybrid-defend on and off:** S131 5.2 -> 13.5, S131 at tech parity 2.1 -> 18.8, A47-dyrrachium-137 (held out) 32.3 -> 44.8, M07 100 -> 100, M72 65.6 -> 61.5. The M72 cost was the gene's own (identical with and without the allied/guaranteed-peace fix; +4/-12 of 192 pairs, p = 0.077). New wars on his seat were unchanged.
- **Fresh-game census (200 all-Hard games, gene on vs off):** the gene withheld 95% of the campaigns it decided against (24% without it), yet the home cities it protected were lost within 20 turns as often (18.0% against 18.8%); captures fell from 14.48 to 13.91 a game, and the share of started campaigns ending in a capture from 28.3% to 27.2%.
- **Lesson:** the home-exposure price kept armies home without saving home cities. The S131 failure was an over-optimistic forecast: the Celts' "capture at 20%" never succeeded in 16 traced runs. Fix the forecast (calibrate capture odds against realized outcomes), not the price of leaving.
## 2026-11-21: Cutting the public release: scans, notarization and the Mac's permission guard (`launch-export`)

- **The personal-data scan flagged test fixtures, not leaks.** `tools/release/export-public.sh` stopped on Windows paths in `crates/archive/src/lib.rs` tests (they read as a home folder with a user name) and on the private network ranges `app/src-tauri/src/online.rs` names to recognise a LAN address. The fixtures now use `C:/Profiles/...` and the private-ip rule allows that one file. Check a hit before adding an allowance: the owner-specific patterns stay in the untracked `.private/scrub-patterns.txt`.
- **gitleaks is not installed in a cloud container, so the export exits 1 there even when the tree is clean.** The personal-data scan still runs first and prints its own verdict. Run the export on the Mac for the final cut, or read the scan lines rather than the exit code.
- **`notarytool` cannot read its keychain profile while the Mac's screen is locked** ("No Keychain password item found for profile"), although building and signing with the .p12 work. Notarization needs the user at the Mac, or the screen left unlocked.
- **A Remote Control session on the Mac will not publish to the public internet on a relayed instruction.** Creating the Cloudflare Pages project was refused until the user says yes himself in the thread. Ask him for that sentence up front instead of quoting earlier approval.

## 2026-11-21: Pre-launch code fixes: the archive never blocks start-up, data folders per OS, offline unit tests (`code-fixes`)

- **The "archive outside git" guard locked out anyone whose home folder is a git repository** (dotfiles in `~/.git`): `outside_repo` refused any path with a `.git` in an ancestor, `archive_root` defaults to `~/LegatumData/archive`, and both the Tauri `setup` and `legatum-server` returned that error, so the app exited with no window and the server exited 1. The guard exists because archives are multi-MB tapes that were once committed by accident (see the 16 MB of tapes entry), so it now refuses only a Legatum source checkout (`.git` beside `crates/archive/src/lib.rs`, worktrees included) or a path inside a `.git` folder. And the archive is now optional everywhere: the server's `Host` holds `Option<Archive>` (`record_in` is a no-op without one), the Tauri shell manages `LocalArchive(Option<Archive>)` and `archive_stage` returns `null`, which `platform/archive.ts` reads as "keep the history on this device". One log line says the archive is off.
- **`HOME` is not a data directory on every OS.** The server defaulted saves to `$HOME/Library/Application Support/Legatum/saves` on Linux too and exited on Windows (no `HOME`). `legatum_archive::data_dir()` now gives the platform folder (macOS Application Support, `%APPDATA%\Legatum`, `$XDG_DATA_HOME/legatum` or `~/.local/share/legatum`); `~/LegatumData` stays the default where `HOME` is set, because the owner's tools read it, and falls back to `data_dir()` otherwise. The mapping takes the OS and an env lookup as arguments so every platform is unit-tested on Linux.
- **happy-dom resolves a relative `fetch` against `http://localhost:3000`.** Any component test that did not stub `fetch` made a real connection (the game-history upload checks `/api/status`): `GameOver.test.tsx` printed 54 `ECONNREFUSED`, `TitleScreen.test.tsx` 2, and `LobbyScreen.test.ts` leaked requests after `vi.unstubAllGlobals()` once a test outlived its 5 s limit under load. `src/test/setupOffline.ts` (first setup file) makes `fetch` fail at once in the happy-dom environment, as an unreachable host would; tests that need answers still stub it. `vite.config.ts` now sets `testTimeout: 30_000` and `hookTimeout: 60_000`: WASM-backed tests need 4 to 10 s on a loaded box.
- **Python tool tests had drifted from the code** (none run in CI): `tools/evolve/genome.py`'s `DECISION_RANGES` lacked 176 of the 407 genes in `crates/ai/src/params.rs` and kept 5 deleted ones (regenerated from the Rust table in Rust order); the probe telemetry still names the pre-rename factions (`summarize.py` maps Tollkeepers, Choir and Remnant to Switzerland, Japan and Byzantium); `ablate_report.py` lacked Egypt and Rome, which have no faction goal (`GoalRule::None`); 12 Lab cards were missing from the scenario-gate split (assigned with `scenario_gate_split.py --add`, which never moves a card).
- **`npm audit fix` with npm 10 rewrote the whole lockfile** (it drops the `libc` fields npm 11 writes) without fixing the advisory, which needs happy-dom 21. The bump to `^21.0.4` was hand-applied to the two lockfile entries; all 64 happy-dom test files pass on it.
- **Removed before the public release:** the 46 raw decision-diff JSONs (54 MB; the Markdown reports stay, `patterns.py` now stops rather than writing an empty ledger when they are absent) and the unreferenced faction-card screenshots folder under docs/playtests (16 MB, one of them failing `hygiene.py --all`).
## 2026-11-21: Pre-launch UX fixes (`ux-fixes`)

From the pre-launch UX audit of a fresh clone.

- **More city names move a pinned tape, so they were not added.** Eight peoples have six names, then "<Faction> Colony N" (`rules/cities.rs::next_city_name`). Lengthening a list would move the seeded start (`range(0, len - 1)`) and the wrap order, so the attempt kept the six-name walk unchanged and put 16 extra names per people in a separate list taken only after the six are used. Core (901 tests) and the AI crate stayed green, but `legatum-sim`'s `runner::tests::scripted_hard_city_fire_tape_is_pinned` moved (7059942646238742188 against the pinned 3287317370864719011) and passes again with the change reverted: in that all-AI game an empire founds a seventh city, and its name is in the state JSON that the FNV hash reads. Adding names needs a deliberate repin (`tools/sim/repin.sh`) in its own change.
- **Scenario text had two readers.** The Lab prints `title`, `lab.question`, `lab.blurb`, `lab.ask`, `lab.opponent`, `briefing`, `objective.summary` and `predictions` (the result card's "What we expected"); `source`, `tests` and `human` are for the simulator only, but they are public once the repository is. Rewriting them is safe: no test hashes the file text (`state.hash` covers the state blob only), and `scenario_gate.rs` dedupes on (state hash, source, seat, summary), so a uniform scrub cannot merge two different cards. The copy lint (`copyRules.ts`) now refuses "your <People> game", "tests whether", "the designer" and "should be easy for a human" in any audited text, and it already runs over every Lab card's fields including `tests`. The war copy of Hold Biel lost its Lab card (same position and words as `scenarios/lab/swiss-hold-biel.json`); the simulator still runs it.
- **A title footer of "DEV (sha)" came from having no `playtest-*` tags,** which every clone lacks. Production builds now fall back to app/package.json's version (`formatBuildLabel(label, describe, version)`, version passed only when `import.meta.env.PROD`).
- **`scripts/build-wasm.sh` wrote to the home folder on every build** through `tools/ops/timed.py`. It now records timings only when the timings folder already exists or `LEGATUM_TIMINGS=1`.
- **The map's keyboard focus ring was the shared 3px accent outline** drawn inside the whole viewport, so a keyboard game wore a red alarm frame every turn. The canvas now has a 1px faint accent hairline.
- **Fixed by CSS at 1000x600:** a picture moment card (a ruin's archive) shrinks its picture to 76px and its title to 24px under 700px tall, so the quote shows whole; the scenario bar puts its title on its own row under 1140px wide instead of cutting it to "TWO …"; the title screen drops its tagline under 660px when Resume shows, so the menu clears the footer.
- **The red screen tour was fixtures and tests behind the rules, plus one real copy bug.** Rules that moved under them: empires start with their capital founded and no Settler, so `roadsFixture` (Roadworks and road-art shots) founded nothing and crashed on `f.cities[0].owner`; Flight unlocks the Biplane and the Fighter needs Air Power, so the "Fighter ready" fixture produced nothing; the Fighter upgrades to the Jet Fighter (Jet Engines), not straight to the Stealth Fighter, so the queued-upgrade fixture had no aircraft upgrade row; India's Chivalry unit is the War Elephant, not a Knight; Aztecs' "Landships" are "Early Tanks"; Electricity unlocks the Stock Exchange; Stealth aircraft upgrade from the jets. The defeat game-over fixture removed the fallen empire's cities but left tiles naming them, and core's save validation refuses that, so the tour saw "This save file is damaged" rather than the Defeat screen; the fixture now clears that territory, and `gameOverFixture.wasm.test.ts` and `roadsFixture.wasm.test.ts` load the fixtures in core so the next drift fails in vitest, not only in the tour. The real bug: the World Age detail in the score ledger said the deck rule twice ("this card ... it never expires. Every empire sees these cards ... they never expire."); the fine print now repeats it only after you score.

## 2026-11-21: Training is no longer a level players can pick (`hide-training`)

The README says Easy, Normal and Hard read only the seat's own view. The lobby also listed a fourth level, Training, whose battle study cuts each arena from the true state, and its description did not say so.

- **What changed:** `app/src/lobby.ts` offers `PLAYER_LEVELS` (Easy, Normal, Hard). Training is listed only when `trainingSwitch` is on: a development build (`import.meta.env.DEV`) opened with `?training=1`, remembered under `legatum.dev.training`. A production build returns false before it reads the URL or storage. The core, the save shape and the hashes are untouched.
- **One function carries the fallback.** `levelOf` returns Training only with the switch on, so every caller (the pickers, `buildSetup`, the staging config both ways, the remembered preference) reads a stale `search` flag as Hard without its own check. A stored `Training` or `HardSearch` preference is rewritten as Hard the first time the lobby opens.
- **`Array.map(levelOf)` is wrong once `levelOf` has an optional second parameter.** `map` passes the index as that argument, so seat 0 would read as "switch off" and the others as "switch on" (caught in review of `staging.ts`, before any test ran). Call it through an arrow function.
- **A dev flag that must not ship is gated on the build, not only on a URL.** `?borders=` and `?perf=1` work in production builds because they are harmless there; this one is not, so the build check comes first and has its own test (`trainingSwitch(false, '?training=1', ...)` is false).
- **Left as it is:** a save or tape recorded with Training still loads, keeps studying its battles and is labelled Training in Diplomacy and the race; its tooltip and report line now say it uses the true positions of nearby units. The server still relays a `search` flag a client sends.
- **Not run here:** the Playwright specs (`training-lobby`, `lobby-seats`, `tour/title-lobby`) were rewritten for three levels but need a built `app/dist` and a browser. `tour/training.spec.ts` stages a Training seat through a fixture, so it does not need the lobby.

## 2026-11-21: The public copy had drifted from the code; a gate now checks the counts

An audit before the repository went public found the README written against an older build. Nothing checked it, because the docs reviewer covered the rules reference, the skills and the Guide, but not the front door.

- **What had drifted (each checked in code):** "nine peoples" (`Faction::ALL` has eleven; Egypt and Rome have `GoalRule::None`); "seven eras, 35 technologies" (`ERA_COUNT` is 9, `TECHS` has 45 rows); the unit triangle "up to the Modern era" (it ends at the World War era, `TRIANGLE_END_ERA_TIER`); aircraft "in the Modern era" (the Biplane comes with Flight, Machine Age); "a tax on runaway empires" (`DEFAULT_DECADENCE_SLOPE` is 0); a "Hard + Search" level (replaced on 2026-11-07); "it picks one whole-game strategy" (`plan.rs`: one objective at a time); a "3 to 6 hour game" and a games-per-second figure with no measurement behind them; "the phone is not a target" beside a documented iPhone simulator build. `ARCHITECTURE.md` still said turn 1 has no cities (false since rules 45). In the app, the Guide said each era unlocks a world wonder (seven wonders, nine eras) and the lobby said Hard expands further and attacks at closer odds (`difficulty.rs` raises only the military knob).
- **"An AI that never cheats" needed a scope.** It is true of Easy, Normal and Hard: every brain plans from a `PlayerView` and `crates/core` has no branch on difficulty or `is_human`. It is not true of two research tools that read the true state (`training.rs` and the simulator's `SearchConfig::omniscient`). The README now says exactly that. The banner image and the teaser still carry the unscoped line and an eight-people caption; they are re-recorded separately.
- **Universal claims invite a counter-example.** "Every number has a breakdown" and "every battle is forecast" became statements of what opens into a breakdown and what shows a forecast.
- **The guard:** `tools/gates/public-claims.py` runs on every branch in `tools/gates/check.py` and fails when the README's peoples count or names, era count or technology count disagree with the code (it would have failed on the old README with four findings). The README lines it reads carry `<!-- peoples-count -->` and `<!-- eras-count -->`. The docs reviewer has a mandatory public-claims step with a table of each countable claim and where it is defined, and refuses its marker while one is stale.
- **Also changed:** `docs/design/sfx/old/` (35 effects with no source record) is deleted; the Civilization strategy catalogue keeps its summaries and links but no quoted text or collection method; `docs/README.md` indexes every entry and marks the working notes as unedited history with links to files that are not public (about 690 across 90 files, left as written).
## 2026-11-21: Distribution: what a static host and a signed Mac build each needed
- **The web UI asked a missing server three times.** `fetchStatus` (boot and the Multiplayer menu's poll), the game-history upload and the scenario-run save each fetch `/api/status`. The code already treated a 404 or an HTML answer as "no server", but on a static host the browser still logs every 404 as a console error, and a host with a single-page fallback (Cloudflare Pages serves `index.html` for unknown paths when there is no `404.html`) answers 200 with HTML. The hosted build now sets `VITE_LEGATUM_HOSTED=1` (`app/src/platform/hosted.ts`) and makes none of those requests; `app/hosted/404.html` gives Pages a real 404.
- **Evidence it works with no backend:** `npm run e2e:hosted` plays `app/dist` on `app/e2e-hosted/serve.mjs` (static files, the `_headers` file applied, 404 for everything else): Athens on a Small map, turns until a second city is founded, Save to file, reload and Continue, Load Game from the file. Zero console errors, zero failed requests, no request to another origin or to `/api` or `/ws`, under the same Content-Security-Policy as the desktop app minus the IPC sources. About 70 seconds.
- **First load is about 7 MB compressed** (84 requests to the title screen, 15.7 MB raw): the engine is 9.5 MB of WebAssembly (3.1 MB gzipped), the script bundle 3.9 MB (1.8 MB gzipped), the title painting 0.6 MB. Reaching turn 1 adds about 1 MB of art. The whole `dist` is 136 MB in 1,020 files because every painting is a separate hashed file fetched on demand; none is over Pages' 25 MiB file limit (`scripts/build-web.sh` checks).
- **A Settler has no legal site until someone explores.** With only the capital's surroundings seen, `settlementSites` returns nothing, so a scripted game that never moves its Scout can build a Settler and never found. The smoke test turns on Auto-explore. The e2e opening helper (`e2e/opening.ts`) also assumes the first call to action is "Choose production", which is false for Athens (its goal action leads).
- **`wasm-pack` needs two downloads this sandbox blocks.** Without a prebuilt `wasm-bindgen` it compiles `wasm-bindgen-cli` from source (6.5 minutes), and its `wasm-opt` download from GitHub releases fails outright. `LEGATUM_WASM_NO_OPT=1` builds a 12.2 MB engine; `wasm-opt -O3` from the npm `binaryen` package gives the shipped 9.5 MB but takes 5 minutes there. GitHub's runners download both normally.
- **Licence notices: 16 crates in the desktop shell ship no licence file** (the `objc2` family, `block2`, `dispatch2`, `tauri-plugin`, `selectors`, `alloc-stdlib`, `defmt-parser`). `tools/release/third-party-licenses.mjs` gives them the standard SPDX text from `tools/release/license-texts/` and fails by name when it has none. Desktop scope: 269 crates and 10 npm packages, 164 distinct texts, 530 KB. Web scope: 53 crates, 170 KB.
- **Mac signing, from the Tauri 2.12 sources and docs, not yet run:** Tauri imports `APPLE_CERTIFICATE` into its own temporary keychain, so the workflow deletes the keychain it used to read the identity (the same identity in two keychains makes `codesign` call it ambiguous). Tauri notarizes and staples the `.app` but only signs the `.dmg`, so the workflow notarizes and staples the image itself. The entitlements file is empty on purpose: WebAssembly runs in WKWebView's own process, which carries Apple's JIT entitlement. See `docs/ops/releasing.md` for what the first real run still has to prove.
## 2026-11-21: A scrub list must not contain what it scrubs
- **What happened:** the public export's personal-data scan was one regex in `tools/release/export-public.sh` that listed, next to generic shapes, the exact values it was meant to keep out: a cloud account id, a machine's network address, an email address and a few personal terms. Each was written with a one-character class so the script would not match itself. The scan passed, and the script published every value to anyone who read it. An earlier learnings entry even quoted part of the pattern as a tip. A pre-launch audit found it; the values appeared nowhere else in the tree.
- **Why the scan could not catch it:** a deny-list of literals can only be checked by something that knows the literals, and the obfuscation that stops the self-match is also what hides them from every other scanner. The check was correct and the file holding it was the leak.
- **Changed:** tracked files hold only generic patterns (`tools/release/scrub-patterns.txt`: any home path, any 12-digit account id in an ARN or bucket name, RFC 1918 addresses outside tests, relationship words, emails outside an allow-list). Owner-specific values are read from the git-ignored `.private/scrub-patterns.txt` when it exists (`tools/release/scrub.py`; shape in `.private.example/`). The export, `referenced-excluded.py` and the hygiene gate share that one loader. `tools/release/test-scrub.sh`, also a CI step, fails when the tracked tree matches any pattern, and the merge-gate tests build their probe lines from samples in the pattern file. The same idea moved account-specific cloud names and spend limits out of `tools/cloud`, `tools/evolve` and `infra/` into `.private/cloud.env` and `terraform.tfvars`.
- **Rules:** generic patterns in the repository, specific values outside it. A value to hide is never written in a tracked file, a test, a comment, a commit message or a note about the scrub itself, whole, in part or obfuscated. When a scrub replaces a word, read the sentence afterwards: mechanical substitution left "A is A" headings, bracketed insertions inside quotes and notes that named what had been removed.
- **Still open:** a tree fix does not remove the old line from git history; that needs a fresh export or a history rewrite before the repository is public.
## 2026-11-21: Egypt's Nile: the specified 1:1 rule won 30%, and the yields cache keyed on the wrong thing
- **Rule (designer):** double Food, no Production, surplus Food builds everything, a building city does not grow, the Grow project grows it (`economy.rs::nile`, rules 48). Doing it all inside the city's yields meant resolution, forecasts and the AI needed no special cases.
- **Balance:** at 1 progress per surplus Food Egypt won 30% of eight-seat Hard games (even 12.5%); 90% gave 18%, 85% 14.5%, 75% 7.5%. Lowering the Food instead was worse than lowering the conversion (1.75x Food at 1:1: 5.5%), because doubled Food is what makes Grow worth taking. 85% was chosen on those numbers, but they came from an AI misreading its panels (next bullet): after the fix 1:1 measured 17.5%, 90% 9.2%, 85% 10.5%, and the designer went back to 1:1. `docs/sim-results/egypt-nile-2026-11-21.md`.
- **Bug it exposed:** `yields_cache::CityCache::totals` keyed a city's totals on population, resistance and the project only ("totals depend on the build only through Germany's projects"). On the Nile they also depend on whether anything is being built, so a forecast for "nothing" was reused for a Soldier. Release builds skip the `debug_assert`, so the first sims fed the AI stale turns; only the dev WASM build panicked. The slot now carries `economy::egypt_nile_builds(item)`. Any rule that makes yields depend on the build needs that key widened; `nile_surplus_food_builds_everything...` in `tests/factions.rs` covers it.
- **Second bug, older than the Nile:** the player view (`view.rs::city_detail`) derived each build's yields by running `economy::city_yields_for_item` on the cached yields *with nothing in production*, which had already run the build-dependent part once. Germany's projects were harmless (nothing in production adds none), but everything else in `yields_for_item` ran twice in every city panel and every AI decision view: the Nile doubled Food twice (×4 on the panel), and decadence and the post-research Science conversion were taken twice. Resolution was right; only views and the AI's reading of them were wrong. The view now starts from `yields_cache::CityCache::base` (`economy::city_yields_base_mode`). Caught by `capital_science_is_visible_unscaled_and_paid_on_the_founding_turn`, which compares the panel with `city_yields` for every faction. The sims were rerun after the fix (report updated): a view bug that only the AI reads can move a faction's measured win rate by half, so rerun balance sweeps after any view fix.
- **The city panel still ran the old Egypt rule, and Grow had no button (follow-up, 2026-11-22):** `CityPanel.tsx` drew project buttons only for Germany, so Egypt's Grow option existed in the view but never appeared (Sam: "how do i grow?"). The panel also still computed old-rule numbers (`surplus × 2` for Buildings only, "Production is unused"). Under the Nile the yields already hold the conversion: Production is the progress from Food and the converted Food is the `The Nile: surplus Food builds` source line, so the panel now reads those. A rule change that ships with a UI must be checked in the city panel itself, not only the faction text; reviews passed because no reviewer opened an Egyptian city. `ui/EgyptGrow.wasm.test.tsx` covers the button and the Food prices.
- **Prices on cards were hard-coded to Production (follow-up, same day):** the piece card (`premium.tsx::pieceStats`) and the unique-piece lines (`data.ts::uniquePieceText`/`uniquePieceBare`) printed "80 Production" for Egypt, which has none. Prices now go through `data.ts::costYield`/`costInFactionYield` (Food for Egypt, at its conversion rate). A faction that loses a yield needs every place that names that yield checked, not only the yields code.
- **A tech's benefit counts its resource reveal:** `data::tech_has_benefit` now counts a strategic resource reveal, so Egypt's Industrialization (Coal, which its Industrial-era buildings need) is not a stepping stone; only Bronze Working is.
## 2026-11-21: Tapes recorded before rules 40 load again; the hill-climb suite is checked in (`claude/hill-climb-suite-2`)
- **Tape load:** `Tape::from_json` (`crates/wasm/src/tape.rs`) now drops an `Action` order whose action is `ClaimObjective` (the Demobilise card's order, removed in rules 40) the way it already drops retired building items, so a tape recorded on the old card loads instead of failing. Nothing replaces the order: it paid units for a card that no longer exists, so a replay from that turn can differ from the recorded game. Test: `retired_claim_objective_action_does_not_prevent_tape_load`. No rule, save-shape or `RULES_VERSION` change.
- **Suite:** the hill-climb measurement harness (`crates/sim/examples/hill_suite.rs`, `tools/hill-climb/suite.py`, `pillar8.py`, `all_arms.py`, `README.md`) is in the repo. Real tapes, state streams and cell lists stay outside Git (`cells.example.json` shows the schema); the two tables in `tools/hill-climb/results/` are sanitized historical measurements. Held-out cells must never be traced or tuned on.
## 2026-11-21: Online play (lobby + relay), and why LAN co-op failed on the designer's Mac

- **The LAN failures were the firewall.** The Mac's firewall was in "Block all incoming connections" mode with stealth on, and old `legatum-server` binaries were listed as blocked: `/api/status` answered on localhost and timed out on the LAN address, even from the same Mac. Every new unsigned server build also re-prompts. Other traps found in the code: the server trusts only the IPv4 addresses it had at startup (a changed IP or a `.local` name gets 403 until restart), and Chrome on macOS 15+ needs the Local Network permission.
- **Don't run the engine in a Durable Object.** The co-op host is authoritative (rules, six Hard AIs, saves, archive). Workers Free allows 10 ms CPU per HTTP request, a late Hard turn is 125 to 220 ms of WASM on the Mac, and the archive would leave `~/LegatumData`. A relay that only pipes frames costs nothing and changes no game code (`plans/online-play.md`).
- **The contract held on the first join.** Four agents built the server tunnel, the Worker, the app shell and the lobby screen in parallel against `plans/online-play.md` (about 35 minutes for the slowest); a real `legatum-server --relay` and `wrangler dev` played 10 two-player turns in 0.9 s with no changes to any part.
- **Server:** a trait with a `send` method implemented directly on axum's `WebSocket` clashes with `futures_util::SinkExt::send`; wrap it in a newtype. `rwlock.read().map(..)` keeps a non-Send `PoisonError<Guard>` across an `.await`; use `.read().ok()`. Build the rustls `ClientConfig` with an explicit ring provider and hand it to both tokio-tungstenite and reqwest, or the process default can panic when another crate in the app enables aws-lc-rs. A 128x128 starting view is over 256 KiB, so it tests chunking with no hook; a 48x32 view is about 210 KiB and is not chunked.
- **Tauri 2.12:** `AssetResolver::get` returns `index.html` for any missing path and guesses `text/html` for unknown extensions, so the embedded server compares bytes against `index.html` to return 404. App commands need an `AppManifest` in `build.rs` before a capability can grant them to a remote origin, and from then on the bundled UI is ACL-checked too: every new command goes in both `build.rs` and `capabilities/default.json` (a test checks). The CSP is only a response header from the app's own scheme, so the page on `127.0.0.1` has none. On the 127.0.0.1 host page `__TAURI_INTERNALS__` exists but only `online_host_stop` and `online_info` are allowed, so server mode must win over every desktop path.
- **Cloudflare (checked 2026-11-21):** Durable Object WebSockets take 32 MiB messages; incoming messages bill 20:1 as requests and hibernating objects bill no duration; SQLite-backed classes run on the free plan. `@cloudflare/vitest-pool-workers` is now `@cloudflare/vitest-plugin`; it does not reset Durable Object storage between tests, and test sockets deliver binary as `Blob` unless `binaryType = "arraybuffer"`.
- **The cloud container can compile the Tauri crate** after `apt-get install libwebkit2gtk-4.1-dev libsoup-3.0-dev libjavascriptcoregtk-4.1-dev` and a placeholder `app/dist/index.html`. Verified on a Mac: the self-test (`LEGATUM_ONLINE_SELFTEST`, `app/src-tauri/src/selftest.rs`) passed 7/7 steps on macOS 26.7.3 in a release build, covering the window navigating from `tauri://localhost` to `http://127.0.0.1:<port>`, IPC from that page and the way back. Tauri 2.12's `eval_with_callback` returns a script's value as JSON, so a remote page can report without an IPC command; on macOS wry drops the callback while a page is still loading, so poll only after page-load-finished.
- Multiplayer menu button emphasis (launch review, 2026-11-21): a disabled button must never carry `primary`, and when a game is open Resume is the only primary. A desktop build with no relay URL has no lobby, so the menu branches on `online?.relay`, not `online`, and falls back to the network-game buttons instead of offering Host Game online.

## 2026-11-21: A coalition cannot break the Mongol 8.9.73.1 hold with the armies it has; the per-unit risk gates are not the blocker
- From his T96 position, an all-out local probe sent every coalition unit within 8 hexes at one of his cities each turn, with no risk gates. Celt Soldiers hit Geneva 6 times and took nothing. Bibracte took 15 hits and nobody captured it. Only Egypt's AssaultTroopers and Rangers plus the Celts, thrown at Bukhara, stopped his win: 38 hits, one of his cities lost, 8 VP and no win by T126 (one run).
- A joint-assault pass (gene `contest_vp_city` in `contest.rs`) targets the VP leader's Great Work and held-goal cities from 2 VP short of his target and sizes the force by summed damage. It does issue orders, but it changed nothing (4 of 4 wins, even with 12 units, a 100% loss limit and 6 extra turns). Era-2 Soldiers march 7 to 10 turns to a walled era-4 city and do about 10 HP a round. The hill-climb thread found the same with the forecast levers (16 of 16 wins under every variant).
- So the hold is lost earlier, in army modernization: era-4 empires still field Soldiers at T100. The pass is parked off by default in `mongol-8.9.73.1/contest-vp-city-parked.patch` in the project files. Revisit it once AI armies keep up.
- Gotcha: once his hold starts, a force that needs 7+ turns can never land inside the 10-turn window. Any hold-break has to start before he reaches the target.
- Hill-climb follow-up: even a free, fully modern army plus Gold for every AI at T90 left him winning 16 of 16, so this position is lost by T90 and fixes must prevent the hold earlier. Capital VP is permanent (`CapitalTaken`), so retaking Geneva or Bibracte cannot break a hold. Only Great Work and held-goal cities can, which is what `capture_goal_swing` counts.

## 2026-11-21: A Hard Scout on sentry duty from T7 kept the Swiss on one city for 71 turns (Mongols 8.9.73.1 review)
- **Cause.** `civilians.rs::plan_scouts` runs `watch_approaches` and `post_sentry` before exploring. The Mongols are rush-capable by their public rules, so the Swiss sent their only Scout to stand beside his capital on T7. It stayed there until T63. No other Swiss unit explores, so the Swiss fogged view never held a good site (`count_good_sites` 0, settler gate `sites_ok` false every turn): 0 Settlers in 71 turns. That one city held the Pyramids, and its capture gave him 3 VP and an elimination.
- **Fix.** Gene `sentry_needs_sites` (default 1): a Hard empire with fewer than four cities, an unexplored frontier and no known good site skips both sentry passes, so its Scouts explore. Once a site is known, the sentry goes back on duty. Hard Mongols in his seat from T2 (16 reseeded runs): Swiss cities at T150 went from 1.06 to 2.19, and eliminations from 4 to 1. In 256 AI-only Hard games it was neutral (cities 3.08 against 3.07 at T50, 4.10 against 4.11 at T100).
- **Tool gotcha.** His recorded orders cannot measure an opening change. Any change to a rival's early moves makes his T15 founding misfire, and his seat collapses to one city. Use `neighbour_alarm --hard-seat` instead. `neighbour_alarm` now also reports `at100`, `winner`, `vp` and `seat_curve`.
- **Also found: a coalition at war never contests a hold.** From his T96 position his orders win 8 of 8 on the newest AI and under seven existing settings. The plan layer gates every Campaign on his VP cities with "funded forecast unavailable", the open-city strike sees them as "not currently visible" or "walls still fire", and `contest.rs` handles only Celtic camps. Not fixed here; see the review.
## 2026-11-21: Coach card: Esc skipped the question for good, because the card stole focus
- **Cause:** `WhyCard` focuses its textarea when a question arrives and mapped Esc to Skip. So the first Escape he pressed for the map (deselect, cancel targeting) skipped the question, logged `skipped`, raised the category's threshold, and left no way back.
- **Fix:** Esc hides the card behind a "Coach question" button (`whyHidden` in the store, draft kept); Skip is button-only; the latest turn report shows a Coach question line (`CoachLine` in `TurnReport.tsx`) that reopens a waiting question or restores the last skipped one (its skip is taken back and a late answer is a second JSONL line for the same question; readers should take the later line).
- **Also fixed:** `afterLoad` now clears `why` and the coach question, so a coach game's toggle and waiting question no longer carry into the next game started from the lobby.
## 2026-11-21: "Possible" is not "recommended": the dock told new Celts to raise warbands on turn 1

The dock's ready-goal offer (`goalDecision.ts::readyGoalAction`) offers each faction's goal action once, the first turn it is available. For one-time acts (New Fire, Share the Learning) that is the right moment; for Raise the Warbands it is turn 1, the worst moment: the camp's tile yields nothing, it draws rivals, and the next city founded breaks the goal again. The AI already knew this (calling as soon as possible cut AI Celts from 9.0% to 3.7%; Hard calls when the 2 VP leave at most 1 VP to the target). Sam saw "Raise the Warbands, counts toward +2 VP" as the primary button on his first Celtic turn. Fix: offer it only in the Hard AI's window (`WILD_OFFER_SLACK` 1); the sidebar button stays. The same bug hit Athens (Share the Learning on turn 1 hands every rival free technologies for 2 VP): now offered only when its VP would reach the target, the AI's `rite_pays` crossing (`BROADCAST_OFFER_SLACK` 0). Swarm, Sakoku and Retire still use first availability. Lesson: a primary call to action must reuse the AI's judgement of when an action pays, not just whether it is legal.
## 2026-11-21: Moving to GitHub: the curated export dropped files the code still loads
- **What happened:** the first `master` built from `tools/release/export-public.sh` failed CI at clippy, because `crates/sim/examples/swiss_playbook_probe.rs` uses `include_str!` on a `docs/sim-results` file the export excludes. Eight vitest files also failed: `runtime-assets.py` dropped unit and portrait art that only tests name, by folder.
- **Fix (PR #3):** the export now brings back any excluded file the exported tree references (`tools/release/referenced-excluded.py`), and keeps a whole asset folder when a test names the folder. Files over 10 MB or matching the personal-data pattern still stay out, with a warning.
- **Lesson:** an exporter that filters a tree must be checked by building and testing its output, not by scanning it. CI on the exported branch caught in minutes what the scans could not.
- **Actions limit (same day, ~19:52 UTC):** every Actions job in the private repo then failed within seconds with no runner (runner id 0, no steps, no logs) on every branch at once: the account's Actions minutes or spending limit, not the workflow. Until the repo is public (hosted runners are free there), `tools/ci/local-ci.sh` runs the same steps in a Claude cloud container (4 cores: about 15 minutes warm) and prints a table for the PR comment. The cloud proxy blocks wasm-pack's binaryen download, so run it there with `LEGATUM_WASM_NO_OPT=1`; a fresh container also needs `cargo install wasm-pack --version 0.15.0 --locked` (the script does it).
- **CI speed (same day):** one CI job took about 75 minutes on GitHub's 2-core runner for private repos (the same build takes 20 minutes on 4 cores): 49 for the release build behind `cargo test --workspace --release`, 10 for the fat-LTO WASM build, 8 for the app tests. `cargo --timings` showed 42% of the build was the 125 examples with no tests: `cargo test` compiles every example, though clippy `--all-targets` already proves they compile. The rust-cache only holds third-party crates, and almost every PR touches `legatum-core` or `legatum-ai`, so caching barely helps. CI now builds only `--tests` targets, split into groups that each build and run on their own runner (`tools/ci/rust_test_groups.py`, read from `cargo metadata` so new tests are never skipped), shares one thin-LTO WASM build across three app test shards, and builds pushes only on `master`. Dead end: a single `cargo nextest archive` fanned out to shards still waited 49 minutes for the one build, and its tests failed because `env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_*")` points at the build runner's path.
- **Then:** GitHub became the source of truth the same day. The Codex runner now branches lanes from a fetched `origin/master` and pushes finished `astra/<name>` branches for PRs. CI gained a static `gates` job (`tools/gates/check.py --checks-only --skip art`).
## 2026-11-21: A held shot can outlive its city
- The hill-climb suite (M07-early, stand-in seat, rules 47) panicked at `movement.rs` `reserves`: a shot held for a fallen city was still pending when a Celtic capture razed that city in an earlier movement round, and a later round's relief into another of the defender's cities indexed `state.cities[&h.city]`. Anything that reads a `HeldShot`'s city during movement must use `cities.get` (the `retain` and `release_held_shots` already did). Test: `held_shot_tests::a_held_shot_whose_city_was_razed_does_not_stop_a_later_relief`.

## 2026-11-21: Two "the AI cheated" reports were resolution-order gaps: shots on a fallen empty city, and a charge whose target died first
- **Geneva, turn 67 (Sam's Mongol game, tape replayed with `game_dump`, 0 hash mismatches):** Geneva was at 0 HP and empty. Ranged fire resolves in phase 2, before movement, so five Keshig volleys (51 damage) hit an empty fallen city, which does nothing. In movement round 1 the Swiss Soldier stepped in under "Reinforcing your own city goes first", became the garrison, and met only the Keshig charge (52 HP left). The AI did nothing illegal; the rule order let any defender dodge a volley by arriving after it.
- **Fix (Sam's order: slip in, then ranged, then melee; rules 47):** a ground unit's shot at a hostile city with 0 HP and no garrison is held (`battle::HeldShot`) and fires at the head of the first movement round in which a defender stands on the tile, shared with supporters, before the melee. A held shot also counts as an attack for the reinforcement priority. Unfired held shots land on the empty city after movement, as before. Replayed at rules 47, his volleys kill the Soldier and the Keshig takes Geneva.
- **Settler, turn 75:** not a bounce. A move onto a tile holding a Horseman and a Settler is an attack on the Horseman (`explicit_attack_target` picks military first). Another Keshig's shot killed the Horseman in phase 2, and `start_pursuits` voided the charge silently, so the Settler walked away. Now the attack passes to the next visible hostile unit on that tile (`movement::next_target_on`); with none left it ends with a `MoveBlocked` the report shows ("didn't attack: its target was destroyed first").
- **Lesson:** any "the AI slipped past my attack" report: check phase order first (ranged phase 2 runs on the turn-start snapshot; anything that arrives or changes in movement is invisible to it), and look for orders that end silently. Still open: the forecast card does not describe a held shot, and the garrison a volley kills still gives that round's melee its counterattack (one snapshot per stage).

## 2026-11-21: Hard Rome walked away from a falling city once its siege promoted the attackers (gene `roman_finish_siege`)
- **The owner's game:** Hard Rome had Laurion nearly down (T32-34, Walls at 19), then all three units, each just promoted, walked back to Capua.
- **Cause, reproduced (240 rotated all-Hard games on master, each order tagged with the pass that issued it):** of 186 one-city Roman sieges with the target below half HP and a unit adjacent, 76 were abandoned (no unit adjacent for 3 turns, no capture within 7). The orders that took the units away were mostly `roman_veteran_retreat` (69): city attacks promote the attackers, a promoted unit is a wounded veteran, and the retreat runs in `plan_settlers` before any military pass, with an exception only for a threatened city's last defender. Founding was minor (5 orders). The PR #21 gate made it worse: `roman_early_war` opened the one-city conquest gate only while Rome had no veteran, so the first promotion dropped the conquest city in 49 of 58 abandoned sieges with a veteran present.
- **Second cause, found by measuring the first fix:** at 0 HP the siege forecasts (`campaign_can_take`, `grind_forecast`) stop pricing the city, which is exactly the turn a melee step takes it; most remaining retreats came on that turn. A visible 0-HP target with a melee unit committed now counts as a holding forecast.
- **Fix:** the one-city gate stays open while a Roman campaign is live; while its capture forecast holds, committed units skip the Roman heal and retreat, and a committed veteran founds only if the capture still forecasts without it.
- **Result (480 rotated games, seeds 930001 and 940001, master -> gene):** abandoned sieges 185 of 385 -> 127 of 380; retreat orders leaving a siege 145 -> 29; Roman captures per game 1.56 -> 1.95; 0 panics. Rome's growth is flat, not up: cities T100 2.90 -> 2.92, one city at T40/T50/T75 67/42/25% -> 68/41/26%, foundations 2.20 -> 1.91 (veterans finish sieges instead of founding), Rome wins 15 -> 15; best/worst faction wins 8.4 -> 9.3 (Egypt 126 -> 140). Hybrid training cells (defend on and off) and quiet cells identical (no Rome seat).
- **Still open:** keeping the city. Rome's captures rise by 0.4 a game but its cities at T100 do not, so the new captures are lost again or replace foundings.

## 2026-11-21: Hard Rome could not plan a war at one city, and speculative staging cancelled its foundings
- **Correction (2026-11-21, see the entry above):** `roman_early_war` opened the one-city gate only while Rome had no veteran, so the first promotion a siege earned closed it again and dropped the conquest city mid-siege; together with `roman_veteran_retreat`, freshly promoted wounded veterans walked home from nearly fallen cities. Fixed by `roman_finish_siege`.
- **Cause 1, by trace (single-game reruns of the 120 rotated all-Hard games, seed 930001):** barbarian camps are nearly absent on the crowded six-empire 48x32 map (Rome saw one in 1 of 12 traced games), so `roman_hunt_radius` left recruits idle beside the capital for 30-60 turns. The war that should replace the camps was never planned: `conquest_min_cities` is 2 (Rome had no override), so a one-city Rome nominated no conquest city and the campaign planner had nothing to price. Rome needs a war for its second city and a second city to plan a war. Gene `roman_early_war`: without a level-1 melee veteran, one city is enough.
- **Cause 2:** `stage_supported_defence` (the peace-time stand against a neighbour's visible units that could declare) ran after `plan_settlers` and replaced a Roman veteran's FoundCity with a step beside the city; between foundings it pulled the founder back and forth (game 930027: FoundCity on a legal 140-value site at T44 and T63, both replaced). Only military founders can be picked (every staging filter needs `u.military`): 0 overwrites for any other faction in 840 all-Hard games, 26 for Rome in 120. Gene `roman_found_first` exempts this turn's Roman founder.
- **Result (480 rotated games, seeds 930001 and 940001, master -> both genes):** Rome on one city at T40/T50/T75 62/41/27% -> 56/32/21%; second city median T44 -> T41; cities T100 2.95 -> 3.20; foundations 2.15 -> 2.48; never promoted 78 -> 44; never founded 112 -> 67; wins 14 -> 16; 0 panics. Most of it is `roman_early_war`. The other factions lose a little of what Rome gains (Athens wins 145 -> 124, cities T100 5.06 -> 4.88).
- **Rules 48 re-measure (master 50b497f7f, same 480 rotated games):** Rome on one city at T40/T50/T75 71/48/35% -> 67/42/25%; second city median T47 -> T44.5; cities T50/T100 1.92/2.65 -> 2.00/2.90; foundations 1.81 -> 2.20; never promoted 105 -> 76; never founded 141 -> 104; Rome wins 11 -> 15; 0 panics. Other factions' wins: Egypt 139 -> 126, Athens 107 -> 99, Celts 86 -> 95, Byzantium 63 -> 72, Germany 74 -> 73; best/worst 12.6 -> 8.4. Quiet cells (M07, S10 at T60) identical. The rules-47 direction reproduces on fresh games.
- **Missed prediction:** we expected Rome's gain to come mostly out of its war target without changing who wins. On rules 47 it cost the leader instead: Athens' wins fell 145 -> 124 while Rome rose only 14 -> 16, so the genes mainly redistributed wins among the others. On rules 48 the cost again falls on the leader (Egypt 139 -> 126).
- **Still open:** the war starts but converts slowly. On master Rome already plans a campaign by T40 in 83 of 120 games; with the gene 104. Many end within 1-3 turns as `insufficient_army` (the plan forecast passes, `campaign_can_take` does not), and the first promotion still comes at median T38-39.
## 2026-11-21: Re-recording the README media: what the first run got wrong (`app/e2e/media/launch.media.ts`, `stepped.ts`)
- **A CDP screenshot ignores the device scale.** `Page.captureScreenshot` returned 1280x720 from a 1280x720 viewport at 1.5x, and ffmpeg then upscaled every frame to 1920x1080: the first clips were soft. Pass `clip: { x: 0, y: 0, width, height, scale: 1920 / width }`. `page.screenshot()` does honour the scale. The older stepped captures (`combat-fx.media.ts`, `moments-fx.media.ts`) have the same flaw.
- **A pointer parked at (2, 2) opens the Gold tooltip** on every HUD clip, and the media cursor ring sits in the corner. `park()` rests it in the bottom right corner with the ring hidden. With the HUD hidden that corner is map, and the map outlines the hovered tile, so there it rests on the hidden top bar instead.
- **`tick()` holds a CSS animation at time 0 on the first step it sees it.** One long step after opening a dialog photographs it still transparent. Step in 50 ms slices (`run()`).
- **The committed late-game saves predate India.** `p892-race-panel-t203` and `sidebar-mid-t117` show India owning Nagasaki, Sendai, Fukuoka and Matsue. `p892-fighter-t204-1432` and `p895-athens-bomber-1916` are worse: each lists a seat under the wrong people (in the turn-172 save "India" owns Emain Macha, Alesia and Tara, and "Byzantium" owns Kyoto, Edo and Osaka). `stepped.ts::save` renames the Japanese cities and puts the two turn-172 seats back, in memory only. The save files themselves are still wrong and other specs load them.
- **The human seat has one or two units in every committed save,** as the 2026-10-24 entry found. Battles are filmed from the seat with the biggest army in contact (Sparta), set in memory by `save(name, { seat })`.
- **`img2webp` is not in the cloud container and `apt` has no `webp` package.** `webp.sh` falls back to ffmpeg's `libwebp_anim`. `wasm-opt` cannot be downloaded there either: build with `LEGATUM_WASM_NO_OPT=1`.
- **Cost:** about 0.5 s a frame on four shared cores (1 s when the box is busy or the map is zoomed out), so the full set (about 4,700 frames) takes roughly an hour.

## 2026-11-21: Survive-him round 1 landed: three fixes, and what the first night taught

Merged to the playtest line for 8.9.74: `neighbour_alarm` (production reacts to a conqueror nearby; Hard in his Sparta seat takes no city by T100 in 38 of 64 runs against 14), the four Hard-Rome genes (Rome's cities at T100 rise from 48% to 60% of other seats' on held-out seeds), and the production-thrash fix (one source of truth for a threatened city's production; India's Archer finishes T36, not T41; captures unchanged). Not merged: `astra/rally-and-arm` (India loses Kabul 1/32 against 32/32, but Athens founds 1.6 fewer cities by T60 when nobody attacks), and three no-gain branches with genes off. Six streams ended as dead ends with traced causes; three of them agree that the cities he takes are lost before the war starts (no Walls, one defender, no Gold for a unit). Nothing has yet moved on his own recorded orders: tapes older than rules 45 misfire on the current engine, so every gain is against Hard or a scripted rusher in his seat. His Switzerland 8.9.72 tape is the first that replays here. Integration notes: default-on AI genes move `pins.json` and both fixed-tape pins in `hard_vs_human.rs`, and branch code arrived unformatted with one clippy error in an example, so the lead runs fmt, clippy, `repin.sh` and the `hard_vs_human` tests after merging AI branches and before the full verify. **Later the same day (8.9.75):** two Codex fixes merged. `leader_war` lets a conqueror turn on an urgent VP leader when its army can reach a known city in time: on his Switzerland 8.9.72 tape his recorded orders win by T150 in 19 of 48 runs against 48 (neutral flat; 1 to 4 order misfires per run because the tape is rules 45 and the line is rules 46), at a cost of 21 Mongol cities lost on their other front across 19 runs against 5. The siege clock replaces the one-turn hold forecast with an eight-turn survival clock for hold, door and emergency production (Gergovia falls T32 in 1 of 24 runs against 16; late starts 485 to 17; captures 217 to 200 on the Sparta tape, 21 to 24 on Rome 8.9.67, untraced; Hard planning p95 8.9 to 17.1 ms per call) and deletes the unused `siege_matchup` gene. Not merged: `rally-and-arm-2`, which missed its cost marks (quiet-attacker Athens still founds 0.44 fewer cities and makes 3.4 less Science at T60).


## 2026-11-21: Hard Rome's promotion hunt scattered its army; bounding it doubles early veterans, Rome still trails at T100
- **Cause, by trace (`crates/sim/examples/rome_trace.rs` on smoke tapes):** "it does not fight for its first promotion" was half right. Hard Rome did go looking: `drill_veterans` sent up to four recruits a turn, before the campaign pass, to the nearest visible prey within 10 hexes or else to frontier reconnaissance, so recruits walked 15 to 31 hexes out (one shuttled between two tiles for 15 turns). Barbarian camps sat 15 to 26 hexes from Rome's capital in 7 of 8 traced games, while young rival cities sat 4 to 6 hexes away. Strike forecasts (temporary print, 237 of them in three games) had forces of 1 to 3 units and none could take a city; campaigns were dropped as infeasible two turns after they were picked.
- **After promoting**, 78 of 100 healthy, free veterans found no site: at war, the Settler's danger-avoiding route field marked every site unreachable. A soldier cannot be captured on the road, so the Settler's capture-danger detour and trip risk do not apply to it (gene `roman_soldier_route`). Wounded veterans also fought on to death at 4 to 36 HP (gene `roman_veteran_retreat`).
- **Result (64-game eight-seat smoke, rules 45):** first promotion by T100 30/64 at median T47 -> 44/64 at median T37; veteran-founded by T60 8/64 -> 26/64; Rome cities T50 1.50 -> 1.83 and T100 1.67 -> 2.14 against about 2.8 and 3.5 for the other seats (T100 ratio 0.48 -> 0.60, target 0.85). Pricing each expected first promotion as a city in strike and campaign values (gene `roman_promotion_value`) drew Rome into more campaigns but more of them ended infeasible (48 -> 78), so it moved T100 by 0.02 only.
- **Still open:** Rome plateaus after T75. The limit is Hard's early city assaults against weak neighbours (campaigns dropped as infeasible), which every faction shares.



















## 2026-11-20: One sample served every order; frequent sounds are chosen by ear

A target player disliked "the sound that the game makes when i click on things like queuing production, moving units". One file, `orders/commit.m4a` (three footsteps, 0.77 s), played for every unit move and every production, research and goal order. Nobody on the build side can hear a sound, so the fix was an audition page (current sample beside ten candidates under 0.5 s from the CC0 packs in `~/LegatumData/audio-src`, with a loudness setting); the target player chose a page turn for production and research and a cloak swish for unit orders. Changed: new feel event `queue` (`feel/haptics.ts`, `store.ts::OrderFeedback`, `orders/queue.m4a`), used by the production queue, tile placement and the research queue; `orders/commit.m4a` is now the swish. Both are cut at -24 LUFS, quieter than the -20 LUFS set, because they play dozens of times a turn.

## 2026-11-20: Why the 8.9.69 neighbour-fall warning did not arm anyone (gene `neighbour_alarm`)
- Traced on his Sparta 8.9.57 and Rome 8.9.71 tapes with `crates/sim/examples/neighbour_alarm.rs` (tape seat, current Hard rivals, per-city `alarm_diagnosis`). Three faults: in Rome it **never fired** (Egypt, six hexes from the fallen Kabul, never knew the city, and the sighting path needed an elimination); in Sparta it **switched off** for the next victim whenever his army came within 6 hexes (the Celts saw it on T24 and T26 only); where it stayed on (India, the Mongols) it was **outvoted** in unwalled cities with a shooter (Mongol Workshops under an alarm of 122 to 142).
- `neighbour_alarm` (default 1) gives production its own alarm: fronts from a public capture plus a sighting, half again per capture in the last 20 turns, on at the border; an alarm above local defence builds the strongest-per-turn defender ahead of economy builds. Feeding the same changes into `neighbour_fall_power` (positioning, reserves, alliances) made Egypt lose its new cities to Hard-Rome (6.09 to 3.72 cities at T100); production-only fixed that.
- Hard in his seat, 64 reseeded runs: no capture by T100 in 38 of 64 against 14 (Sparta T23) and 39 against 29 (Rome T27). Hard is the proxy; his tape seat holds only to T32 on rules 45, where the Celts fall at T32 in every arm (already walling, units unaffordable at 160 Gold). Ledger: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/survive-him/neighbour-alarm/`.
- A walled city under a multi-turn siege reads `falls: false` until the turn it falls (`city_risk_with` forecasts one turn): Gergovia T29 to T31, 66 to 98 threat against 26 defence. Not fixed here.

## 2026-11-17: Every empire starts with its capital founded (rules 45); the opening did not speed up

Designer decision: moving the starting Settler was an edge he could get by re-rolling the map, and starts should be as alike as possible. `game.rs::try_new_game` founds each capital on the start hex mapgen already balances (`rules/cities.rs::found_city_at`, a `Chronicle::Founded` entry on turn 1, no `CityFounded` resolution event, so no celebration fires). The capital now produces from turn 1 instead of turn 2, yet a 16-game Hard probe to T40 (seeds 450000 to 450015, eight empires each) shows the first trained Settler still at median T14 and the second city at T19 (third city T28 to T29): the population-2 gate on Settlers absorbs the extra turn. `scoring.rs::capital_stands` keeps its never-founded guard because `blank_game` and hand-built scenarios still have empires with no founding record; removing it gives them an instant capitals victory. Pins and tapes moved because of the earlier turn of production, not because any rule about later cities changed. Report: `.reviews/astra-start-with-capital.md`.


## 2026-11-16: Rome's veterans found cities for nothing but the veteran (rules 44); Hard still cannot play Rome
- `astra/rome-free-founding`: no Gold price and no price counter for a veteran founding (designer, from his first Rome game: the price was "a lot of money" on top of an army's upkeep, and it pushed him to build the Pyramids because he could not afford units). Hard's Gold reserve for founding is removed.
- **Hard's Rome does not expand:** matched eight-seat smoke, Rome 1.5 / 1.75 cities at T50 / T100 against 2.75 / 4.05 for the other seats; 2 of 8 Roman seats found with a veteran by T60. The best Roman seat founds its second city at T60 and third at T96; other factions' Settlers average T25, T40 and T64. The designer, playing, had taken two capitals by T55. The gap is earning the first promotion: Hard does not go and fight for it.
- The feared runaway (a free founding letting Rome out-expand everyone) did not appear in AI play; it has not been tested with a human.

## 2026-11-16: Third art pass: one base for all 54 figures, four late wall families
- `astra/art-pass-3` gave every unit figure the same plain cream disc by editing the base alone, so the second pass's repaints and the older figures read as one set at map size; added the four missing late-era wall families with construction stages; improved the Pikeman, Barbarian Pikeman, Phalangite and Panzer.
- **A shared base raises the outline score between unrelated figures:** eleven more pairs tripped the silhouette check and were accepted by the lead (pike carriers against each other most of all).
- Not fixed, blocked by the image service: the Barbarian Archer's bowstring, the Legionary's pilum shank, the Infantry figure. Pike lengths are still compressed to fit the frame. Alpha erosion in the cut-out can erase a thin arrow shaft; the pipeline now keeps it.

## 2026-11-16: A neighbour's fall is a warning, and a second front on a busy leader (genes `neighbour_fall`, `busy_leader_front`)
- From the designer's reading of his Sparta 8.9.57 win ("surely India knew i was coming for them next"). After a nearby conquest Hard raises its defence and alliance valuation toward the conqueror; a reachable Contain war on the runaway leader is allowed without the funded-siege odds while the leader fights elsewhere. On his tape India declares at T38 and offers the Mongols an alliance at T40 (it did nothing until T62), with 6 units and 76 Gold at T45 against 1 and 44.
- **Why India sat still:** it nominated Sparta every turn but knew no Spartan city to aim at; `commit_odds` (`leader_commit` 2, `contain_min_odds` 30) needs a funded siege forecast, which could not reach 30% without a target, so Contain was rejected before its value met Economy's.
- **It did not make the seat lose more:** Hard in his seat, 48 paired seeds, T2 18 to 19 wins, T20 23 to 19, T40 45 to 47; none significant. The winner attacked by two or more empires at once before T100: 15 to 16 of 50. Right behaviour, too weak to matter: six units at T38 do not stop an army that has taken two capitals.
- The cloud batch runner has one upload mechanism, `--input-file NAME=FILE` (the earlier `--tape` option is gone).

## 2026-11-16: Second art pass merged; the "everyone stands" rule was the lead's mistake
- `astra/art-pass-2` reviewed 151 paintings at full size (29 wrong, 32 poor, 7 minor) and changed 46: 15 units, 3 portraits, 12 city paintings, the Stock Exchange (its own painting, no longer a Bank recolour), era scenes, the Aztec crest, construction frames. Real errors fixed: a crossbow passing through its pavise, a "pikeman" holding a hooked axe, stirrups on the Cataphract and Greek Horseman, a foot on the crossbow's prod, a jet with no canopy, wheel hubs floating in their rims.
- **The 15 units were repainted standing at rest because the lead told the painter the whole set stands, from looking at two figures.** Much of the set is in action. The designer left the call to the lead: the 15 stay (each fixed an error), poses are not forced from here, and every figure gets the same base (`legatum-art` skill). The 15 silhouette pairs the standing figures tripped are accepted in `tools/art/distinctness.json`.
- Still open: four late-era wall families, the Barbarian Archer's bowstring (generation failed), the Legionary's pilum shank a little short, Panzer against Tank, and one base for all figures.

## 2026-11-16: Hard buys the best unit it can train; why Switzerland bought Spearmen in era 5 (`astra/hard-buys-best-unit`)
- Traced on the designer's Sparta 8.9.57 tape: Switzerland's eight late buys (six Soldiers, a Trooper, a Ranger) came from the `frontline` pass and one `emergency`. Its researched Assault Trooper, Arquebusier and Cuirassier were all blocked by strategic supply (no spare Iron or Niter), so the legal choices were Soldier, Horseman and Ranger and the selector took the Soldier on strength per Gold. The bought units then walked off singly; four died to barbarians.
- The fix is a floor under the shared purchase scorer (best trainable unit of the class; one generation back only when the resource is missing; never further; otherwise keep the Gold unless a city falls without a body) and a hand-off so bought units join the plan they were bought for. It merges `astra/hard-purchase-scorer`.
- **`named_fund` stays 0: its gate failed again** (rivals with the fund on were still weaker than rivals with it off from the Sparta T40 position).
- **The upstream fault is supply:** an empire with no Iron or Niter in era 5 has no good purchase to make. Securing strategic resources is not yet something Hard plans for.
- The fixed all-AI game's first battle between AI empires moved again; the Training test now searches 400 turns. Hard's caution genes are making the AIs fight each other later.

## 2026-11-16: Hard uses supported defence (`astra/hard-supported-defence`, gene `supported_defence`)
- With enemy melee able to strike one of its cities, Hard keeps a garrison in, ranks reachable defenders by soak against the attackers in view, and orders useful melee units into the city as supporters by the engine's shared-damage and pooled-reply forecast. In the T40 position of the designer's abandoned Athens game, Athens is held through T45 from T36 in 26 of 36 runs (9 of 36 with the gene off); the supported-defence-hold Lab card 0 of 50 to 50 of 50.
- **Mixed when every empire has it** (19-card war suite, 64 seeds): The Ring around Chur loses 1.98 to 0.01 cities a run, Hollow Swiss success 45 to 43 of 64, India Against the Veteran loses 1.96 to 2.17 cities. Seat-only it is neutral or better on every card.
- **One "mixed-team occupancy" is reported by the war suite in both arms:** it predates this change. Not yet looked at.
- The stale-nominee re-scout test needed `supported_defence = 0` in both arms (the gene also moves the idle units it watches), and the plain human-seat pin reads 0 VP at T100 with the day's genes together and the fund off.

## 2026-11-16: Germany's cost is a faction rule: each Production building costs 1 Food (rules 43)
- `astra/germany-industry-food`: the qualifying set is derived from each building's own Production stat (Workshop, Pyramids, Forge/Ruhr Foundry, Factory); the Ruhr Foundry's own -2 Food is gone. The city panel shows "Industry: -2 Food (Workshop, Ruhr Foundry)" and each qualifying build card shows the -1 Food.
- **It passes the designer's test for a drawback:** 16,384 paired Hard games per arm, switching it off raises Germany's win share 13.38% to 14.81% (+1.43 points, 95% interval +0.80 to +2.09). Cities at T60 and T120 are unchanged (3.50, 4.60); population at T120 is 29.28 with the rule, 30.62 without.
- Hard prices the Food: at T47 in a traced game it chose a Settler (2037) over a Workshop (1940) where the old valuation took the Workshop (2071).

## 2026-11-16: Rome merged (rules 42); its Hard is still short of the target
- `astra/rome-integrate` rebuilt Rome on the playtest line after Egypt (a direct merge of `astra/faction-rome` conflicted in 31 code files: both added a faction in the same places). Rome has its own portrait, seal, Legionary and Castrum art; Byzantium's Tagmatic Soldier has its own icon; eleven factions and Random fit the picker list at 1000x600 without scrolling.
- **Hard's Rome expands too slowly:** eight-seat smoke, cities at T50 / T100 1.875 / 2.625 against 2.75 / 4.05 for the other seats; 3 of 8 Roman seats founded with a veteran by T60 (0 of 8 before the task's fix). The weakest seat did not fight until T54 and never made a veteran. A Roman human plays fine; a Roman AI rival is weak until it learns to earn the first promotion early. Colonia Victrix (the goal) is not built.

## 2026-11-15: Egypt merged with its own art; what the merge needed (rules 41)
- `astra/faction-egypt` was written against the old faction sheet (`piece`, `win`, `lose`) while the sheet was redesigned on the playtest line; at merge the sheet entry lost those fields, the Royal Granary row became `onCard` and `SPECIAL_BUILDINGS.Egypt` lists the Granary. A faction with no goal (`factionGoalVp` 0) shows no goal block.
- Placeholders pointed at India in five places (`factionInfo('india', ...)`, `ornaments.tsx::pick`, three tests). The painted set from `claude/art-egypt-rome` replaced them: `people-egypt`, `seal-egypt` (an SVG seal like the Aztecs', no raster crest), `medjay`, `building-granary-egypt`, icons `unit-medjay` and `building-royalgranary`.
- **A new `people-*-360.jpg` is not seen by Vitest until `ornaments.tsx` itself changes** (the glob transform is cached per file); a dated comment above the glob is the existing convention.
- `unit-legionary.svg` was Byzantium's Tagmatic Soldier icon, not an orphan; the art pass redrew it as a scutum and pilum for Rome. Byzantium needs its own icon when Rome merges.

## 2026-11-15: The named Gold fund made the rivals weaker; it is off (`named_fund` default 0)
- Measured by the coach on the designer's Sparta 8.9.57 game with Hard in his seat on paired seeds: against rivals with the day's six genes on the seat won 31% from T2 (17% with them off), 25% from T20 (5%), 57% from his T40 position (42%). One gene at a time from T40 (48 seeds, control 69%): `named_fund` off cut the seat to 29% (p 0.001); `army_door_production` off to 54% (p 0.14); `reachable_wars`, `unit_concentration_turns`, `supply_race_cap` and `peace_army_sites` showed nothing.
- In his game the fund had Switzerland save 1,067 Gold and spend it from T95 to T110 on seven Spearman-class units and a Trooper in era 5, in one city: the fault it was written to remove. The cause is only partly traced.
- **Its own scenarios read well (Chalco 0 of 50 to 50 of 50, the three-city hold 42 to 49) and hid this.** A short defence card measures the purchase it was built around, not what the fund does to an empire that is never attacked. The gate for turning it back on: this game's T40 position, the human-seat benchmark and the war-scenario suite.
- Review: `docs/playtests/reviews/2026-11-15-8.9.57-sparta-win.md`.

## 2026-11-15: A stale best target no longer blocks every campaign, and Hard goes to look (`astra/hard-stale-nominee`, gene `stale_nominee`)
- In his Athens win the Celts sat 38 turns beside him with 8 to 9 units against his 1: their best target, Eleusis, was last seen on T44, and `stale_intel` (15 turns) dropped the campaign every turn from T61 to T88 without anyone going to look. Now a fresh reachable city is nominated first and an idle Scout or fast unit is sent to refresh a stale best target. In the replay the Celts hold a Marathon campaign option at T61 and see Eleusis at T62.
- **`forecast_siege_rounds` panicked on an empty force** (`force[0]`), reachable from the committed-force filter, the combat probe and the whole-army diagnostic; it returns None now. This was the `war_diagnosis` crash at `opportunity.rs` noted earlier.
- Paired 50-game smoke: empire-turns with a stale gate and no surviving campaign candidate 33,124 to 29,353.

## 2026-11-15: A review brief with a false premise produces a confident wrong verdict
- The lead told Astra's faction critic that every faction can already buy tiles, from an old copy of the design skill held in context. Tile buying was removed (`2d4304ed4`; rules reference section 9: "Absent"). The critic rejected the land-purchase faction as "a pricing variant", flagged that it could not find the action, and judged it anyway. **Before stating any current rule in a brief, read it in game-rules.md on the playtest line; a skill copy from earlier in the session is not the source.**
- The Codex review lanes read the main checkout, which is many rules versions behind the playtest line. A design review must be told to read `claude/calendar`.

## 2026-11-15: Hard discounts a Settler site near a foreign army at Peace (`astra/hard-peace-armies`, gene `peace_army_sites`)
- From his Athens win, T87: Hard's best site (98) was beside a Celtic army two turns before the Celts declared; he founded far away. `enemy_army_near` and the site blocks read `threat_units`, which lists empires at War only. The discount uses local force, public army strength, border contact and whether that empire is fighting someone else; allies never count; a site at War keeps the stronger block. At T87 the exposed site falls from 98 to 10 and the farther one (22) is chosen.
- **Not shown to help beyond that position:** over a paired 50-game smoke, foundings within three tiles of any foreign army were 13.6% before and 13.7% after.
- **The Horseman two tiles from the site was outside his view.** The changed choice came from visible patrols and public information; a fix that needed the hidden unit would have been a cheat.
- Still wartime-only reads, left alone: `development::exposed_city`, `production::opening_exposed`.

## 2026-11-15: Hard saves toward one named Gold fund and spends it in a burst (`astra/hard-named-fund`, gene `named_fund`)
- One fund at a time, in his order: emergency defence, then an upgrade wave, then the campaign army; there is no VP-purchase-first rule (designer, 2026-11-15). Troop purchases in the fund's passes take the strongest affordable viable unit of the era. Chalco one-turn defence 0 of 50 to 50 of 50 (two Landships where the old code bought one Ranger); the T111 three-city Aztec hold 42 to 49 of 50; Aztecs take Cologne 0 of 50 both ways.
- **It does not cover the late Spearman buys seen in his Athens win:** those came from the `door`, `ready_defenders`, `emergency` and `frontline` passes (purchase-pass tags over the whole game), not `second_troop_buy`. `astra/hard-purchase-scorer` is the shared scorer for all of them.
- **The human-seat pin is a sum of the day's five genes.** With named fund alone the rival-search benchmark read four cities at T62 and 2 VP at T100; merged after door defence, supply cap, reachable wars and concentrated production it reads three and 0. One fixed map: a pin, not a measure.

## 2026-11-15: Hard starts a slow unit in the cities that finish it soonest (`astra/hard-one-unit-at-a-time`, gene `unit_concentration_turns`)
- A duplicate combat-unit start is deferred when another eligible city finishes that unit sooner. At T146 India starts Early Tanks in Srinagar and Chittor only, not in four cities (one-turn card 0 of 50 to 50 of 50).
- **It does not get the tanks to the front sooner:** "two Early Tanks near Srinagar by T156" is 1 of 50 in both arms. Fewer starts did not speed the builds or move the finished units; the gap is delivery, not where they start. Plain Hard's `vp_t100` in the human-seat pin moved from 0 to 2.

## 2026-11-15: Hard declares only wars its army can reach (`astra/hard-reachable-wars`, gene `reachable_wars`)
- A new Contain or coalition war needs a local force that can reach the target, and the target's public Gold counts as the units it can buy in one turn. A recent Campaign on another front blocks a fresh declaration. On the Cologne card the Aztecs declared on Germany at T111 in 50 of 50 runs before and 0 of 50 after (Cologne taken in 0 of 50 both ways: the war never paid).
- **Two default-on genes can each explain the same replay.** After the merge the supply-cap replay test failed in its control arm: with `supply_race_cap` off, `reachable_wars` alone kept Athens off Alesia. A gene's test turns the sibling genes off, or it stops testing its own gene the day the sibling lands.
- The Cologne card and baseline on the playtest line are the rules-40 ones; the branch's re-encoded copies were dropped at the merge.

## 2026-11-15: The campaign supply term is capped by the race terms (`astra/hard-supply-cap`, gene `supply_race_cap`)
- A campaign's supply value can no longer exceed its largest positive capture, race or denial term. In the T163 replay of the Germany 8.9.43 game the Alesia supply term falls from 14,616 to 23, and Athens no longer switches to a campaign for supply alone.
- **A branch cut before a rules bump can carry a view fixture the new rules refuse.** Its `T163-p3.view.json.gz` held three Demobilise cards (removed in rules 40) and both tests panicked on `unknown variant Spend` after the merge; the cards are now Veterans. `refresh-save-version-fixtures.py` does not look inside `crates/ai/src/fixtures/`, so run the branch's own tests after a renumbering merge, before the build.
- **The ten-turn scenario does not show it** (`scenarios/lab/athens-take-german-city.json`: a German city taken in 0 of 50 runs before and after). The effect is on which plan is chosen, which a ten-turn capture count cannot see; judge it from plan traces and full games.

## 2026-11-15: Hard builds defenders for the army at the door (`astra/hard-door-defence`, gene `army_door_production`)
- A threatened city and its reachable neighbours within four hexes build the newest trainable line defender until Walls plus garrison cover the visible at-war army. In the T115 Chalco and Texcoco position (`scenarios/lab/aztecs-hold-chalco-texcoco.json`) the Aztecs hold both cities in 34 of 50 runs, 28 with the gene off.
- **Counting units still in production as defence made it worse** (a trial with trainable Landships fell back to 28 of 50): a defender is worth nothing until it stands in the city. Arrival-aware credit is open.
- **Codex's cloud verify showed 14 Playwright failures on this branch and on `hard-supply-cap`;** the local verify on the playtest line is the one that counts at merge.

## 2026-11-15: Supported defence: one reply split three ways was why cities could not be defended (`claude/supported-defence`, rules 39)
- **What was wrong.** Attackers pooled damage on a city, but the defending side replied once (the city's or garrison's counterattack, split among the attackers), and relief units went in one at a time, each fighting nothing.
