In the designer's example at average rolls (walled size-5 city, Rifleman garrison, three enemy Riflemen, three friendly Riflemen ordered in every turn) the old rule lost the city on turn 5 having hurt the attackers only 54, 38, 37 HP; the supporters added nothing until each was the garrison.
- **The rule now.** What gets past the city's HP is split evenly per hit among the living garrison and supporters, each share judged against the unit that takes it; every supporter adds its own counterattack to the pool. Same example: the city holds and all three attackers are dead by turn 3. Alone the garrison falls on turn 2 (unchanged); with each supporter pinned by its own extra attacker, the city falls on turn 3 (unchanged from the old rule).
- **Gotcha: "healthiest" is the wrong backfill.** HP alone puts a full Spearman ahead of a wounded Rifleman that takes a third of the damage per hit. `battle::soak` ranks by HP over the damage the worst attacker deals it (the engine's own damage curve), not HP x strength: the damage table is exponential in the strength gap, so a product understates the gap between eras.
- **Gotcha: supporters now take damage on their own tiles.** The old chain moved every reached relief unit into the city before damage landed, so a supporter's death and Sparta/Aztec kill credit were keyed to the city tile; `Carry::relief` now carries the city tile so kill credit still counts hits on the city.
- **Test-only average rolls.** `rules::battle::AVERAGE_ROLLS` (cfg(test)) makes every roll 100 for turn-by-turn worked examples; the city strike honours it too.
- **Not modelled yet:** the attacker's forecast cannot see the defender's orders, so the app still forecasts with no supporters (`forecast_city_assault_supported` exists for when the card names likely ones); Hard does not support or cut support on purpose (increment 2), and its `relief_forecast` gene models the old chain.
## 2026-11-15: World Age cards must be something the player does, not something he buys (Veterans replaces Demobilise)
- **Why Demobilise went (designer, 2026-11-15):** "disbanding 2 units just means i save up gold and purchase the units -- so it could have just been gold". A card paid in units is a price, so it never changes what the player does; it only moves his spending. The test for a World Age card: does it send him somewhere he would not otherwise go (take a city, win fights)? Tribute and Monument failed the same test (rules 31); Demobilise was the last paid card, so the whole claim path went with it (`Action::ClaimObjective`, `Claim`, `claim_check`, `Family::Spend`, `ai::objective_deck`, the app's claim button and This Turn item).
- **Veterans (rules 40):** "your units earn 4 promotions", counted in `FightStats::promotions` where `promotion::grant_xp` raises a level. Evidence for N=4: 8 Hard-vs-Hard games to turn 260 (`objectives=deck`, 64 empires, 424 thirty-turn windows): promotions per empire per window are median 0, p75 2, p90 3, max 13; in the 79 windows with 8+ kills the median is 3, p75 5, p90 6. Across a whole game an empire earns a median of about 8 promotions (0 to 28, the big warriors 22-28). So 4 is more than two skirmishes (p75 of all windows is 2) and less than a whole war (a warring window's p90 is 6), and the Age never expires. Barbarians can give a unit only its first level, so four separate units can still cash four promotions off barbarians: it is a campaign, not a cheap one, but not a closed door.
- **The crown race panel was already gone from the era-up takeover** (deleted with the crowns, 931844eb4). (2026-11-22: its "Victory points open now" panel was replaced in World Ages games by the Age score check.)

## 2026-11-15: A Hard change that passed its agent's "full suite" still failed verify on three pins
- The Settler-guard branch reported a green suite, but verify on the playtest line failed `runner::tests::scripted_hard_city_fire_tape_is_pinned` and both `hard_vs_human` example benchmarks (`plain_hard_rivals_are_the_default_benchmark`, `rival_search_is_the_old_benchmark`). Any change to Hard's play moves these; they are expected to move, not regressions (here the seat went from 2 to 4 cities at T50 on the old benchmark and from 0 to 2 VP at T100 on the default one).
- Agent briefs for AI changes must name the step: run `tools/sim/repin.sh`, update the two `hard_vs_human` pins with a dated clause in their comment, and commit `crates/sim/tests/pins.json` with the fix. Otherwise the build chain loses a 15-minute verify finding it.

## 2026-11-15: Hard's Settler guard waited on open ground for a fog threat, released its escort when the read fell to 0 and forgot a sniper after 20 turns (`claude/settler-guard-fog`)
- Both of his early snipes on Athens (8.9.40 Byzantium, T17 and T96) came from a guard that judged each turn from what it saw that turn. A wait ordered for a fog threat was checked only against visible units, so the Settler sat two tiles from Piraeus while his Horseman stood in fog three tiles off. The escort called to a Settler was released the turn nothing was in sight, the 11-14 escort call stopped at `escort_search_distance` 6 (the nearest troop stood 7 to 8 away), and `SNIPE_RECORD_TURNS` made Athens forget on T96 a rival that had taken its Settler on T17.
- Fix, all in `hard_defense.rs::guard_exposed_settlers_duel` with no new gene: an unseen-threat wait happens only in an own city or on the escort's tile, else the Settler goes to the nearest city (`fog_wait_home`); the escort pair lives in `CampaignMemory::settler_escorts` until founding or home; the call for an escort has no distance cap; `ThreatTracker::settler_snipers` records any rival whose unit is one of our former Settlers, kept for the game and read by `recent_surprise_war`.
- Counterfactual replay of his tape (his orders as taped, Athens's seat on the new brain): from T14 the Settler goes into Piraeus on T16, sits there while his Horseman is adjacent on T17, founds on T29 instead of being taken; from T80 the Horseman walks beside the second Settler T88 to T92 and it founds on T93. Orders at the two positions are not replayable on a tape past the first divergence, so these are one-seat branches, not a game.
- The existing tests that asserted the old wait on open ground changed (`a_settler_with_an_escort_two_turns_away_goes_home_under_an_unseen_threat`, the unseen-flank test in `hard_defense_tests.rs`): that wait was the bug.
- Pairing needs memory because the guard is stateless per turn; the view alone cannot say which troop a Settler was promised (a garrison returning to a city looks the same as an escort arriving).
## 2026-11-15: Hard plays the capitals victory through the existing valuation (`claude/capitals-victory`)
- **What changed.** `crates/ai/src/capitals.rs`: our capital under a known rival unit within 6 hexes is held (garrison, shooter first, Walls 4,000 above every other build); rival capitals are worth more as fewer rivals hold one, the last one is worth a win (10 VP-equivalents, 100 per point in target values); `WinFacts` carries each team's standing flag and a shared 4,000 milli-logit stake, 10,000/0 when one team holds the last. One gene, `capitals` (default 1; 0 turns the guard and the target terms off, used by the fixtures and the recorded-trip tests that predate the rule); `capital_guard` stays 0 and keeps its Settler-escort meaning. Existing fixtures give every first city an original-capital flag, so with the rule on the enemy capitals in them pulled targets (11 tests moved); the fixture and those tests set `capitals` 0.
- **Gotcha.** The VP projection in `plan.rs` multiplies `capital_capture_vp` by 100 as real VP, so the capitals bonus must not go through it (it would start a fake VP hold); it enters target ranking through `capital_race_vp` and the win chance through `project_capital_capture`.
- **Gotcha.** A team's capital is gone only when all its members' capitals are: the test fixtures' team 0 has two players, so a test that drops "our capital" must drop both.
- **Not done:** defending an ally's or another rival's standing capital when a rival is one capital from winning; `city_lost` (which prices our own city loss) does not know it is the capital.

## 2026-11-15: The capitals victory needs "has not founded yet" told apart from "capital razed" (`claude/capitals-victory`, rules 37)
- **The rule.** The only team still holding an original capital of its own wins at the end of the turn (`scoring::holds_own_capital`, after last-team-standing and before the VP hold). A razed or sacked capital leaves no city behind, so "no capital" alone cannot say whether a team fell or has not settled yet. `scoring::capital_stands` counts a living member as standing until a city of that founder exists or `Chronicle::Founded` names it; the chronicle is written at the end of `resolve_turn`, after scoring, so the cities check covers a capital founded this very turn.
- **Replacement capitals do not count.** The test is `City::original_capital`, not `is_capital` or `rules::cities::is_original_capital` (which also accepts any current capital and a chronicle match), so a team that rebuilt after losing its first capital has fallen. An occupation lock needs no case of its own: the captor owns the city.
- **Public on purpose.** `PlayerPublic::capital_stands` is visible to every viewer, like the treasuries: the rule is the whole race, and a hidden raze would make a win a surprise. Capture VP (`capitals_taken`) was already public.
- Ending earlier than before is the point: the designer found hunting every last city and Settler tedious. No AI valued its own capital or counted rival capitals when this landed; see the branch report.

## 2026-11-15: Hard's production alternation: the bonus fix is on, the emergency gene is off (`claude/production-commit`)
- **Fix A, on.** `production.rs::choose_item_inner`'s "finish what was started" bonus read `production_banked`, which the view zeroes for the build under way (`core/view.rs`), so the item just left got up to +50% and near-equal units swapped each turn. It now reads `production_stored` (the active build's own progress, or the bank). The same read sits in the city-focus chooser (`production.rs` near line 245, a player-set focus, not Hard's routine pass); it was left alone.
- **Gene B, off.** `emergency_bombardment_only` (default 0): the emergency pass stops firing on `needs_ranged` alone (an enemy unit at 3 hexes, no shooter) when there is no bombardment, no exposed capture and threat is at most local defence. It is off because it removes the one interrupt for a shooter with an enemy at 3 hexes; the designer rules on turning it on.
- **Measured on rules 36** (4 games of 8 Hard seats to T150, seeds 9311000-3, no CI): switches away from an unfinished item 2,505 of 17,368 city-turns (14.4%) before, 2,007 of 17,562 (11.4%) with A, 1,742 of 17,489 (10.0%) with A and B. A-B-A flips 678, 422, 306. Completions 1,890, 1,844, 1,873 (flat). Near an enemy (within 3 hexes) switches 844, 766, 556. Cities changing hands 30, 25, 34 (noise). Unfinished Production at T150 about 40,000 in all three arms, so the parked Production is not what A or B cut.
- **Moved pins (expected, from A):** `default_ai_tapes_are_pinned`, `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape_is_pinned` and the three mixed-AI fixture tapes, repinned with `tools/sim/repin.sh`. The diagnosis scenario guards (G4 T153, Sparta 8.9.26, Pyramids) were not rerun on rules 36. Cause 3 (the `threatened = threat > 0` value cliff) is untouched.
## 2026-11-15: Hard took Peace one turn after capturing a city, because the captured city counted as in danger (`claude/peace-prize`)
- **Cause.** `endangered_by` and `collapsing` called any city at half HP or less "in danger" as soon as an enemy stood near it. A city Hard has just taken is at 0 HP from Hard's own siege, so the capture that opens the road to the enemy capital added +30 to the Peace score and opened the "own danger" gate (fable-scenarios-1115, Game 4 T126: Chittor held by 132 of our power against two Assault Troopers of 55). Peace was taken in 12 of 12 salts, India declared again at T139.
- **Fix (default).** Low HP is no danger by itself. A city is endangered when the enemy beside it outweighs what holds it (`city_power` already falls with HP), and `collapsing` needs the enemies within 2 to outweigh our units within 2. `endangered()` (the legacy and `peace_pair_scope 0` path) still has the HP clause.
- **Measured (rules-30 engine with the patch ported, 12 salts).** VP at T145 6.17 to 6.83, at T175 7.75 to 8.67; Peace at T126 12 of 12 to 0 of 12, India Peace still comes at T141 to T165 in 9 of 12. Guard T153 stays at 11 of 12 wins. Games lost by T176 went 2, 4, 3 of 12 (before, fix, fix + prize), not understood. `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/peace-prize-1115/`.
- **Prize term (gene `peace_prize`, default 0).** Prices the best enemy city the forces in place take inside the truce, remembered sightings included, with the campaign planner's own forecast. On top of the fix it adds +0.08 VP at T175 here (noise): the bug was the whole gap in this scenario. Left off until the designer approves.
- **Gotcha: a fix that changes Hard also changes the other AIs in a replayed tape.** The tape's other seats are shadow Hard brains, so any AI change breaks the replay hashes before the cut. For measurement, keep the old behaviour before the cut turn (scratch patch only).
- **Test that encoded the bug.** `tests/diplomacy.rs::peace_acceptance_is_deterministic_...` had a 50-HP city held by three Troopers against one and expected "danger"; it now has four enemy Troopers. `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` re-pinned.

## 2026-11-14: The display calendar (`claude/calendar`)
- **What it is.** A year beside the turn ("Turn 81 · 1130 AD"), display only: `crates/core/src/calendar.json` holds the anchors, `calendar.rs` interpolates, `ui_rules` exports 1001 labels into `rules.generated.json` and the app indexes them (TS has no second interpolation; past turn 1000 it clamps). No state, no save shape, no RULES_VERSION change.
- **Why it is a tech-pace gauge.** The sim report now has "Tech pace against the calendar": median turn and year each tech is learned beside `tech_real_year` (22 of 45 set; the rest, Future included, are null). Read it with the anchors: a tech learned long before or after its real year means the era's turn budget is off, not necessarily the tech.
- **Draft anchors differ from the era lore in places.** The designer's numbers were kept (era starts: Ancient 4000 BC, Classical 1000 BC, Medieval 500 AD, Renaissance 1400, Industrial 1750, Machine Age 1880, World War 1914, Modern 1950, Future 2010).
## 2026-11-14: a recolour passes the distinctness score and is still a placeholder
- The Machine Age placeholders (Biplane, Howitzer, Trench Infantry) were tinted copies of Fighter, Artillery and Infantry, accepted in `distinctness.json`; the Biplane was a monoplane. `tools/art/unit-audit.py` finds copies by structure with colour removed (histogram-matched greyscale correlation and edge correlation: copies 0.99+, every different painting 0.32 or less) and by icon/roundel drawing; run it when adding units. The Artillery (a World War unit) had been painted as the Machine Age howitzer, so the old painting moved to the Howitzer and the Artillery got a WWII gun. A pose can fail on silhouette alone: the first Trench Infantry (a bayonet charge) hit gist 0.78 against the Crossbowman, and a crouching run passed. Giving the Biplane its own roundel exposed that `bomber+fighter` alone scores 0.81 against `scout+settler`, so the aircraft needed their own roundels too. Image generation replies often say 'no path' while the image is saved under `~/.codex/generated_images/`.

## 2026-11-14: The Modern era, a ninth era between World War and the Future (`claude/ninth-era`, RULES_VERSION 35, increment 1)
- **What it is.** Era 7 (0-based 6, the old "Modern") is displayed as World War with its contents unchanged; the new Modern (0-based 7, 1950 to 2010) sits after it, and the Future is era 9. Five techs (Mechanised Warfare 654, Composite Armour 670, Guided Missiles 686, Jet Engines 702, Green Revolution 720: the Future's old costs) and the Future five up one step (840 to 930, about +30%, the same step the World War era and the Modern era have over their predecessors). Mechanised Infantry (3 moves), Main Battle Tank, Rocket Artillery (range 3), Jet Fighter (strike 6) and Jet Bomber (range 9), the Supermarket, and Air Defence. Apollo Program is increment 2 and is not promised anywhere.
- **The curve.** `ERA_STEP_PERCENT` gains a 20% step after the World War era and `DESIGN_MELEE_LINE` takes 50 for the Modern era (the World War era's own value), so each Modern unit keeps its World War line's design numbers and stands exactly one 20% step above it (Infantry 89, Mechanised Infantry 107; Tank 94, Main Battle Tank 113; Artillery 77/98, Rocket Artillery 92/118; Fighter 103, Jet Fighter 124; Bomber 114, Jet Bomber 137). The Future keeps its 50% over the era before, so `MELEE_LINE` ends 107, 161 and the Future units rose about 20% (Hunter-Killer 134 to 161, Autonomous Armour 144 to 173, Stealth Bomber 153 to 184). `MELEE_LINE[8]` is 161, not 160: the exact product is 160.54.
- **Air Defence reuses the Fighter duel, it is not a second system.** For a strike on a hostile city's tile, `air::air_defence_at` finds the Air Defence and `resolve_ranged_phase` stands a temporary Jet Fighter unit (fresh id, full HP) in the city for the duel; `plan_fight` runs unchanged, then the fight's `damage_to_defender` is zeroed before `apply_fights` (a building is not shot down) and the temporary unit is removed (it is never a hangar aircraft, so the writeback loop never sees it). One interception per Air Defence per turn, before any Fighter, and a Stealth Bomber is not met. The forecast shows it (`BattleForecast::air_defence`), which needed the building visible: `CityMemory`/`CityView::has_air_defence`, defaulted and skipped when false so rules-34 view JSON still reads.
- **Judgment calls.** Oil now covers three eras (Machine Age, World War, Modern; the designer said two was fine, the third is a placeholder). A Panzer upgrades like a Tank into an ordinary Main Battle Tank, losing its extra move and second Oil (every special unit upgrades into the plain next unit). The Supermarket needs no minimum population (the Mill has none) and +4 Food at 360 Production; Air Defence costs 320 (a Star Fort's) and yields nothing.
- **Gotcha: vitest's fs module cache serves stale `import.meta.glob` results** after asset files are added without changing the globbing module (`icons.ts`, `cityArt.ts`, `unlockArt.ts` all failed on new files). Run with `--fsModuleCache=false` once (it does not touch the shared cache).
- **Hidden era-count assumptions again found by grep:** the research numerals (`eraResearch.ts`), the unit plaque numerals and metals (`unitEra.ts`), the Guide era list and resource table, `resources.ts` `ERA_RESOURCES[7]` for Silicon (now the last), `vpParts.tsx` `e.era < 7` (it was already stale since the Machine Age), the hard-coded "+48 against city HP" in the Guide's Stealth Bomber paragraph (the number moved with the Future), `DEMOBILISE_MIN_COST`, `production.rs` `sims_target_mix` (one more row), the AI composition ladders, and the AI's bomber-range tiers (`air.rs::bombers_likely`, `air/coverage.rs`). The solo VP target stays capped at 10.
- **Placeholder art.** The Main Battle Tank reuses the present-day tank painting that the Tank's WWII repaint replaced (restored from `e80cee075^` as `main-battle-tank-*`); the other four units, the seven icons, the Supermarket's and Air Defence's unlock paintings and the `contemporary-*` city sprites are byte copies of the World War neighbours (icons carry an XML comment so their data URLs differ, as the Machine Age's did). So `src/artDistinct.test.ts` now lists many new identical pairs besides the seven old ones; `tools/art/distinctness.json` was not touched, the new pairs are for the lead to accept or for increment 2 to paint away. The takeover painting falls back to the World War era's. An aliasing shortcut (one slug or icon name for two kinds) is not available: `icons.test.ts` and `unitArt.distinct.test.ts` insist on one asset per kind.

## 2026-11-14: Modern era art painted, and what the distinctness check taught (`claude/ninth-era`)
- The five units (Mechanised Infantry, Main Battle Tank, Rocket Artillery, Jet Fighter, Jet Bomber) and the Air Defence and Supermarket paintings replace the byte-copy placeholders; icons and roundels are drawn by hand. Made with `codex exec --image REF -` (prompt on stdin; the image tool prints the saved path under `~/.codex/generated_images/`), then `unit-art.py` and, for the buildings, `wonder-art.py`'s key plus a fit to 256/128/64. Prompts sit beside the art.
- **Shared-base figures fail on gist, not colour.** Every unit sits on the same oval, so two wide, low pieces (an APC beside a tank) score a silhouette gist of 0.82 to 0.85 whatever their paint. What worked: make the composition TALL (a squad of three standing and kneeling soldiers beside the vehicle), and for aircraft change paint value (pale blue-grey jet, dark camouflage bomber against silver and olive neighbours) plus a mirrored bomber. Regenerating the same prompt gave samples that passed and failed, so shortlist four and let the check choose.
- The roundel class check compared the Jet Bomber with the Hunter-Killer at 16 px; a wider-swept silhouette with separate engine pods fixed it without touching `distinctness.json`.

## 2026-11-14: The Celts retaking a city they founded no longer sack it (`claude/special-units`, RULES_VERSION 34)
- **Ruling (designer, Celts game turn 156):** "i took back my own city, and it razed it?" The sack in `battle.rs::capture_city` had no founder check, so a Celtic recapture burned the Celts' own city. Now `c.founder == captor` takes the normal capture path (full population, no Gold, no camp, no `CityRazed`); a city founded by anyone else, teammates included, is still sacked. Everything that predicts a sack had to agree: `forecast.rs` (`sack_gold` None), `CityView::founder` (new public field), AI `opportunity::sack_gold_value` and `Planner::sacks_city` (kept-city valuation), and the player text (faction sheet, Guide, tile card). Lesson: when a rule gets an exception, grep every forecast and valuation that mirrors it, not only the resolver.

## 2026-11-14: Landship is displayed as "Early Tank" (display rename; `claude/special-units`)
- **Ruling (user):** "nobody knows what a landship is." Every player-facing string now says Early Tank / Early Tanks (`data::unit_name`, tech effect lines, the Aztec sentence, app data, Guide, resources, unlock text). The code identifier stays `Landship` everywhere (`UnitKind::Landship`, `LANDSHIP_MOVES`, art slug `landship`, icon `unit-landship`, saves, tapes), so no rules change and no `RULES_VERSION` bump. When writing player text, say Early Tank; when writing code or tests, say Landship.

## 2026-11-14: The rival-assault rule only covered attackers at war with each other (Tara; `claude/special-units`, RULES_VERSION 34)
- **What happened.** The 2026-11-11 "stronger side walks in" rule (`movement.rs::settle_by_force`) ran only when some pair of claimants was `hostile` to each other, and built sides by "not hostile". At Tara (designer's Celts game, turns 62-64) India and Sparta were both at war with him and at Peace with each other: no pair was hostile, the rule never ran, and the generic "peaceful teams contested the tile" bounce fired every turn, with no message when units had fought that round. Every test used attackers at war with each other, so none saw it.
- **Fix.** The rule runs whenever the claimants are not all one permanent team; sides are permanent teams (`same_team`), so Alliance partners of other teams and attackers at Peace are separate sides; a claimant that already has a decided loser reason no longer counts as a peaceful rival for the winner (the winner used to bounce off the loser). Tests: `rival_assault_tests.rs`, the `tara_*` cases. Lesson: a rule keyed on "hostile pairs" must be tested with the pairs that are not.

## 2026-11-14: Special units: a unit's own rules and its owner's faction-wide rules are two different factions in one fight (`claude/special-units`, RULES_VERSION 34)
- **One `faction` argument cannot carry a hired Company.** A hired unit is its seller's version (stats, name, phalanx, bow), but the seller's faction-wide rules (Mongol +1 move, Spartan ignored wounds, terrain defence) must not travel. Combat now takes both: `faction` (the owner's, for faction-wide rules) and `made_by` (`GameState::made_by`, `PlayerView::made_by`: `Unit::origin` for a Company, else the owner). The pure-version functions (class bonus, phalanx, mounted city penalty) take only `made_by`. Code that read `state.player(u.owner).faction` for a unit's stats or name is the place to look for a missed Company.
- **"Only special units" empties the shop's old problems.** With one unit per seller the grouped, folding shop (`shelves`, "+N more", the density spec) had no job and was deleted; a seller that cannot field its unit yet is still listed (`Offer::unavailable`) so the player sees why (Panzer needs Armoured Warfare) instead of a missing row.
- **"+50% against mounted" is ambiguous.** Built as 50% of the unit's own strength on top of the melee line's +40% (a Pikeman 32 gains +12 and +16). The weaker reading, 50% more of the usual bonus, would be +7. If playtests say the Reisläufer is too strong against mounted, that is the first dial.

## 2026-11-14: The Machine Age, increment 1: an era slots in without moving Modern, if the curve's step is split, not added (`claude/machine-age`, RULES_VERSION 33)
- **The curve.** `MELEE_LINE` is grown from `ERA_STEP_PERCENT`, so inserting an era with a new step would have raised every Modern and Future strength. Splitting the old 45% Industrial-to-Modern step into 21% and 20% (1.21 x 1.20 = 1.452 against 1.45) gives 74 for the Machine Age and keeps Modern 89 and Future 134 on the same rounding. The design line needs a Machine Age value too: 49 is the only integer that lands Trench Infantry 74, Howitzer 62/80, Landship 86 and Biplane 60 together (47 and 53, the obvious picks, miss the Howitzer by one). So the era factor is 74/49 = 1.51, not the proposal's "about 1.39".
- **Era order is the TechId order.** `eras::current_era` walks the known-tech set in `TechId` order and assumes eras are contiguous in it, so new technologies must sit between Electricity and Autonomous Systems in the enum, not at its end. Old techs keep their names (serde by name), so saves would decode; the version bump refuses them anyway.
- **"Pre-Modern" meant two things.** The triangle, the Outrider escape and the 33% city penalty all read "era tier below Modern". With a mounted unit in the Machine Age they split: the triangle stays era-gated (Landship +40% against a Howitzer, Trench Infantry +40% against a Landship), while Outrider and the city penalty are about horses, so they now read `data::is_horse_mounted`. Same answers for every existing unit.
- **The one upgrade choice fits as a chosen first step.** `upgrade_plan_via(from, via)` takes the first step from `data::upgrade_choices` and then follows `upgrades_to`; `Order::UpgradeAircraft { to }` is optional (None = default line), so every other order and the AI are unchanged. `AircraftView::upgrade_options` carries each line's final form and price for the Air wing.
- **Half city damage is a percent, not a strength line.** A strength penalty through the damage table is not "half". `combat::city_damage_percent` multiplies the Walls percent by `data::city_hp_damage_percent(kind)`, and the forecast carries it (`BattleForecast::city_hp_percent`, used by `city_percent()`), so resolution and forecast cannot drift.
- **Biplane sight is native sight added after the LOS cache** (`visibility::add_air_sight`, beside `add_territory_sight`): from the air, so terrain does not block it, and it counts for contact.
- **Hidden era-count assumptions found by grep, not by the compiler:** the app's research numerals and names (`eraResearch.ts` had its own seven names), `EraObjectives.tsx` `focus.era < 7`, `airWing.ts` `owner.era === 7`, the unit plaque metals, `resourceUsersThisEra`, the AI's `sims_target_mix` table and `.min(6)`, `objectives::age_pool` `2..=7`. Exhaustive matches caught the rest. The effect-art lookup matched "Infantry" inside "Trench Infantry"; it now tries the longest unit name first.
- **Gotcha: the shared vitest module cache bakes `import.meta.glob` results.** `app/node_modules` is the main checkout's (symlinked into worktrees), and its `.vitest-cache` keys a transformed module by content. `cityArt.ts`, edited before the Machine Age city sprites existed, kept serving a glob without them, so `missingCityArt()` failed after the files were added. Adding asset files that a module globs needs a change to that module (or a fresh cache entry) before its tests see them.
- **Functionality check (no balance read):** one 4-player Hard game (seed 3141, `vp-target=60` so it runs to turn 400): every seat entered the Machine Age (T142 to T159) and Modern (T170 to T187), no panics, no research idle turns; the AIs built Biplanes (17 to 20 each for three seats), Stock Exchanges, Landships, Howitzers and Trench Infantry, then Modern units and Stealth aircraft. Two 6-player games (seed 2718) entered the Machine Age at T133 to T157 and Modern at T158 to T167. Machine Age to Modern took about 26 to 30 turns; Industrial to Machine Age about 30.
- **The Stock Exchange uses no resource.** First built as an Oil supply tier like the Research Lab, it made the AI's Oil opportunity cost price every Bomber at zero in the Germany T185 regression (a Stock-Exchange-ready city outbid it), and it would put Oil pressure on an era whose units and aircraft already share Modern's Oil. Lead's call, open with the designer.
## 2026-11-13: the Fable seat's five capture lessons, as Hard genes, do not win After the Riflemen; Hard loses it by giving up, not by bad assaults (`claude/capture-tactics`)
- **Where Hard loses.** In 24 of its 29 losses (salts 0-49, rules 30) the seat never makes a melee attack on Tenochtitlan, and in 21 of them it fires 2 shots in all. The campaign runs T135-T141, then `funded forecast unavailable` drops it and the plan returns to Peace. The teacher's lessons are about how to assault, so they can reach at most the few runs that assault.
- **Lesson 3 against sustained storms.** `charge_preview` (hold a charge the preview says trades badly, unless it captures this turn) won exactly the 4 runs where baseline Hard made losing lone charges, but it also holds the charges of a multi-turn storm that wins only as a sum: with `capturer_upgrade` Hard takes the city on T136 in 20 runs by charging two melee units three turns running; adding the preview holds the second unit and drops the arm to 3/50. A per-charge veto cannot see a storm's value; the storm forecast has to.
- **Relief is unknown intent; price it as both.** Modelling the owner's adjacent melee as relief that skips its attacks on our units made the storm forecast optimistic, and Hard charged on T133 with three mounted units (1/50). Counting those units as both attackers and relief (they may do either) gave 18/50. In a multi-round forecast relief only delays a lone attacker's capture a round, so it does not by itself make Hard bring two attackers.
- **A Gold reserve set inside `plan_modernization` protects only later purchases, and the view hides unaffordable refits.** The Soldier's 210 Gold refit was never a candidate: `UnitView::upgrade_cost` is None until the upgrade is affordable, and Siege buys in earlier passes took the Gold. `capturer_upgrade` uses the refit's total and adds it to `modernization_savings`; it then upgraded the Assault Trooper (higher gain per Gold) rather than the Soldier the teacher chose.
- **50-run cards reshuffle.** Every behaviour change flips whole runs both ways (capturer_upgrade: 22 gained, 19 lost, net +3), so pairing by salt does not shrink the noise much; about 7 wins is the smallest difference worth reading. All five genes stay 0. Results: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/capture-tactics-1113/README.md`.

## 2026-11-11: Two empires at war with each other could never take a ruined city they both assaulted (designer's Lucerne; `claude/city-relief-chain`, RULES_VERSION 27)
- **Cause:** `team_captors` picks a captor only when every surviving claimant is on one side. With two sides hostile to each other, an empty 0-HP city made them meet (a meeting engagement), and whoever survived on both sides was "contested by an enemy". Lucerne sat at 0/200 for turns while both kept ordering units in.
- **Rule (designer, 2026-11-11):** force decides. Force is the damage each unit's attack would deal to the city at a 100% roll, unclamped, over every unit that moved onto the tile this round or shot at it this turn; allies add up; only a side with a melee claimant can win; exact ties bounce. The two attacking sides no longer meet over the city.
- **Readings chosen (not in the designer's words):** the roll is fixed at 100% so the number is predictable and forecastable; units that died to the defenders this round still count (their force went into the assault); shots count for the whole turn's ranged phase, since shots resolve before every movement round; a claimant on the owner's side (a teammate) keeps the old contest. The forecast does not show rival force: listing which rival units can reach the city this turn needs a reach pass per rival, more than a small addition.

## 2026-11-11: Hostile claimants of a city tile bounced each other after its garrison died, so nobody held or took Bukhara (designer, T67; `claude/city-relief-chain`, RULES_VERSION 27)
- **Cause:** after a round's fights, every surviving military claimant of a tile is a rival. Bukhara was at 0 HP; three Swiss Phalangites killed its departing 4-HP Keshig (parting hit, G21) while a Mongol Phalangite was ordered in. The owner's relief was a claimant beside the three attackers, so `team_captors` skipped the tile (a claimant hostile to the others) and all four got "contested by an enemy". The 2026-10-04 "reinforcing goes first" fix only covered an empty city; a garrison that died or stepped out in the same round left the old bounce. The same bounce hit a city with HP whose garrison died to overflow, or stepped out, while relief was ordered in.
- **Rule (designer, 2026-11-11):** the owner's units ordered into their own assaulted city are relief, outside the contest for the tile. The assault is absorbed city HP, garrison, then relief in order (highest HP first, then approach tile), overkill carried per hit (`battle.rs::carried_damage`; first written as a pooled defence-ratio carry, replaced the same day); the first relief alive becomes the garrison and enemy claimants get "the city was reinforced". The city falls only when all are dead. Relief reached by overflow deals no counterattack.
- **Tuning question, settled (designer, 2026-11-11):** the pooled defence-ratio carry of overkill was harsher than a direct attack (three hits of 51, 43, 52 on a 4-HP Keshig killed a fresh Phalangite that the same hits deal about 30 each to). His call: "the damage that each unit gets should be judged based on the damage inflicted to that specific unit type". Overflow is now per hit: a hit that kills a layer carries `direct x leftover / hit_damage` onward (`combat::carried_share`), where `direct` is what the same attacker and roll deal to the next layer, and later hits strike the current layer directly. The Phalangite now holds on about 10 HP. This also replaced the city-to-garrison pooled ratio (`city_overflow`, removed) for cities with no relief.
- **Gotcha for event readers:** a relief unit that falls in the chain emits `UnitMoved` and `CityReinforced` before the round's `Battle` events and its `UnitDestroyed` is at the city tile; it has no `Battle` of its own.
## 2026-11-11: aircraft XP rides on the temporary map unit, so no second promotion system (`claude/aircraft-xp`)
- **Mechanism.** An aircraft acting in the ranged phase is already a temporary `Unit` with its own id (`air::ghost`). Giving `Aircraft` an `xp` field, copying it into the ghost and back, and dropping the `is_aircraft` skip in `promotion::grant_xp` is the whole rule: `combat_xp`, `level_for_rules`, `plan_fight` and the Battle event's strength breakdown already read `Unit::xp`. `promotion::base` had to test `unit_stats(kind).military` instead of `rules::is_military`, which is false for aircraft, or every plane would earn 0.
- **Gotcha: a Bomber one-shots a test city.** In the core test arena a Fighter strike took the city to 0 (250 damage), so the XP was the defeating blow's 10, not 3. Tests compute the gain from the first resolution instead of hard-coding it.
- **Gotcha: Silicon for a Stealth upgrade in a test needs the tile owned and improved** (`resource`, `improved`, `owner`, `city`), plus Autonomous Systems; a bare deposit is not supply.
- **Strongest Army** (`army::aircraft_value`) now counts the plane's level like a ground unit's, the only AI-visible read of the new field.
## 2026-11-11: World Ages built behind `objectives=deck`; the Monument could not be a project, and a Celtic sack hides what it took (`claude/world-ages`)
- **Cards that never expire have to be flows.** With no countdown, any Hold card ("N Libraries") or resource card is met by ordinary growth sooner or later (the measured failure of master and deck v1), so the pool is only paid acts (Tribute, Monument, Demobilise) and hard targets (Humble the mighty, Breach, Gain N cities from the Renaissance). Every counter is read against the snapshot taken at the reveal (`EraRecord.base/levels`), so nothing banked earlier counts.
- **The projects system does not fit a Monument.** `ProductionItem::Project` is Germany-only and never completes (it converts Production to yields every turn, never banks). The Monument is a one-time `BuildingKind::TriumphalArch` instead: offered only while a revealed, unscored Monument card is open, priced at that card, unbuyable, never installed in the city (finishing it adds one to `FightStats::monuments`). Not offering it before the reveal is what keeps pre-reveal Production from counting.
- **A Celtic sack deletes the city before objective scoring runs**, so capital, wonder and Walls status must ride on the event: `CityRazed` now carries `prize` and `walled` (and `original_capital`). Captured (kept) cities are read from the state at turn end; Walls survive a capture (only Markets are plundered).
- **`EraView.entered_by` changed meaning in a World Age game:** it is "reached the era by technology", while every living empire holds a record (and can score) from the reveal. Anything reading `player.eras.contains_key` as "entered" is wrong in this mode; use `objectives::era_of`.
- **Demobilise prices a unit by cost, not age.** A "created after the reveal" test would make the player build units only to disband them (paying twice). A minimum cost of about 70% of the era's melee unit keeps pre-built Spearmen from standing in without that.
- **The AI prices the paid acts in one currency.** Gold-equivalent cost (Production at the 2 Gold buy rate) against the win-chance gain from +1 VP (`winchance::WinFacts`), converted at three turns of income per percentage point. The constant is a first guess, not fitted; no balance sims were run.
## 2026-11-21: the Mac map check can pass with a run that drew nothing (8.9.73.1)
- **What happened.** `tools/ui/webkit-mac/check.sh` on 8.9.73.1 printed one `TypeError: undefined is not an object (evaluating 'm().toScreen')` and still said "0 differing images": run 1 of each invocation saved no image, and the compare only looks at runs that have images.
- **Cause, as far as traced.** In run 1 (a fresh WKWebView origin) the page was still on the title screen 30 s after `body.js` clicked Continue: `views` null, no canvas, no error text. A second click loaded the game, and run 2 loads on the first click. So the first Continue click on a cold start is ignored in the Mac engine. Not traced into the app code, and not known whether it predates 8.9.73 (5 of 6 invocations on 8.9.73.1; the one clean 8.9.73 invocation followed another by seconds).
- **How to apply.** Read the check's `error` lines and count the images per run; "0 differing" alone is not a pass. Owed: `body.js` should wait for the game and fail the run when it never loads, and the title screen should not drop a click made before the engine is ready (tracker).
- **The tour's city fixtures were all idle,** which is why the first Buy a tile button looked findable: an idle city auto-scrolls its panel to the picker. A control that must be findable is shot in a building city at 1000x600 and scroll 0 (`legatum-ui`).

## 2026-11-21: landing on the playtest line while master's code is far behind (8.9.73)
- **A tag-based branch does not merge into master, but it merges cleanly into `claude/calendar`.** `tools/cloud/verify-remote.sh` merges the branch onto master first and stops on conflicts in `plans/ai-war*` and the coach skill. `claude/calendar` already carries master's docs and skills, so cut feature branches from it, not from the tag, and verify can run on the branch itself.
- **`tools/scenarios/publish.sh` never loads the state in the engine on the playtest line.** It reads `RULES_VERSION` from master's `crates/core/src/lib.rs` (26) and compares it with the installed app (46), so it checks only shape, version and copy and says so. A scenario cut at the previous rules version needs `tools/sim/refresh-save-version-fixtures.py OLD NEW` first; "Turn on the Banker" went from 45 to 46 that way, with no engine load at 46.
- **`tools/ui/webkit-mac/check.sh` needs Pillow.** Homebrew's `python3` has none and the compare step dies after the runs finish; `PATH="/usr/bin:$PATH"` picks the system Python, which has it.
- **31 Playwright tests in 17 spec files fail on the playtest line** (build agent's report, listed in the playtest line's learnings entry for rules 46; not re-run at the tag by the lead). Compare a branch against that set until they are fixed or marked.

## 2026-11-20: the `reachable_wars` filter keeps a serial conqueror off the leader, and counts a Swiss Reserve as an army (Swiss 8.9.72 review)
- **What happened.** In his Swiss 8.9.72 win, a war on him (Contain) was the Mongols' top-valued plan on 23 turns (T85-T148; up to 1,224 during his hold) and Byzantium's on 19. `plan.rs::choose_plan`'s `reachable_wars` retain removed it every turn: `recent_other_campaign` (a campaign on anyone else within `runaway.rs::FRONT_TURNS` = 15) and `campaign_against`'s "no second front" are true on every turn of a serial conqueror's game, and `reachable_war_force` failed because the army was across the map and because it adds `prospective` defenders bought from the target's public Gold (558-819 from his 4,000-6,700), although spending below 5,000 would cost a Swiss leader his Reserve's 2 VP.
- **Evidence.** Env-gated traces of the candidates and each filter clause under `p881_trace` at `playtest-8.9.72` (0 strategic mismatches). The Mongols with the gene off from T135 cut his recorded orders' win by T150 from 48 to 25 of 48; neutral arms (explicit builtin genes, the gene off on Byzantium, the Aztecs forced to War) stayed 48/48.
- **Lesson.** The gene was shipped against dead wars (Germany 8.9.43); its one-front clauses also remove the only war that can stop a leader whose strongest rival is always fighting someone. Not changed (review only); idea #58.
- **Tool gotchas.** `remote.sh --with-uncommitted` ships every untracked file in the worktree, so `takeover_gap.py`'s 82 MB state dir rides along on every later job from that worktree. A hold win's one-turn decision diff to the win turn reads 0 pp on every row; rerun the key turns with `--turns ... --end-turn 240`. His Coach-question answers are in `<app data>/training/answers/<game id>.jsonl`.

## 2026-11-11: Hard's defence passes ignore an army staged at Peace; "break the hold" fired on a capture that could not break it (Swiss 8.9.30 review)
- **Staged army at Peace.** `analysis.rs::Ctx::build` marks only empires at War as hostile, so `threat_units`, `door_defense` and the production defence passes never count a neighbour's army parked beside an unwalled city until it declares. Only the threat read sees it: the Mongols logged "army 40–80 near us (trend +20 to +40)" for seven turns at Peace, kept a Settler, the Pyramids and a Cataphract in production with 0 Walls in 5 cities, and lost Shangdu two turns after his declaration (T51 → T53). Hard in his seat goes from 1/24 at T50 to 24/24 at T65 over that war.
- **"Break the hold" on a non-breaking city.** On T131 the Celts (+1,785) and Aztecs (+525) priced a capture of pop-1 Lucerne as breaking his hold, though its bounty (61 Gold) left the Reserve at 5,090 and it held no VP; Bern and Khanbaliq, whose capture would have dropped him under 5,000 on T130–T132, were never targeted. The term needs `win.hold_margin < 0` after the capture, so something in the capture world removes 2 VP that the rules would not; suspect `sack_vp_denied`'s crowns counted again by `winchance.rs::projected_vp`. Untraced.
- **Hard drops a walkover war between captures.** In his seat Hard plans his Mongol war three turns earlier (T48) but returns to "Peace with the Mongols" at value 0 on T54–T56, T60 and T64–T68: `military.rs::campaign_against` refuses while "our army is holding a captured city", and Contain carries a −133 to −274 "retaliation" term.
- **Tool gotcha:** `takeover_gap.py --engine playtest-8.9.30` fails on the box ("not a valid object name"): the tag is shipped as branch `takeover-recording`, but `git archive` is called with the tag name. Leave `--engine` out (the save's commit sha works) or pass the sha. To cap box threads, add `--env RAYON_NUM_THREADS=4` to the `remote.sh` calls in `run.sh` and `takeover_gap.py` and `--threads 4` in `cut.sh` (local edits, not committed).

## 2026-11-11: the map jumped under the cursor 420 ms after a unit was clicked (`claude/no-nudge-while-aiming`)
- **Cause.** Selecting a unit schedules a layout nudge in `MapCanvas.tsx` (the effect that reveals a unit the left panel covers): 420 ms later the camera snaps so the unit has clear space around it. Only a pointer press, wheel or key cancelled it. Click a unit, move the mouse to the destination, right-click about half a second later: the snap lands just before the click and the order goes to the wrong tile. It was not the focus animation (a map click never requests focus).
- **Fix.** Moving the mouse more than 8 px after the selection cancels the nudge. A still mouse still gets the reveal. Test: `e2e/keys.spec.ts`, both cases.

## 2026-11-10: short_objective round 3: price the short step in Production, not win chance; Biel 50/50 untold, war suite +2.5, designer seat flat (`claude/ai-objective`)
- **The Biel gap was the currency, not the forecast.** The attacker-side forecast says Biel falls in 3 turns on T132 in all 50 runs (round 2's "8 of 50" counted pinned Holds, not forecasts). The plan layer's win chance saturates for a leader: the Swiss sit at 99.3% to 99.99%, so Biel's expected loss was 13 points and captures 4 to 35, and from T133 every home loss priced at 0. Priced in Production (city 400 + 80/pop at capture or fall odds, less forecast losses), T132 reads home loss 1,099 against the best Campaign's -825, and the step holds: **Biel 50/50 untold** (told 49), Dyrrachium 50, Two Cities 0.
- **Athens two pikemen T112 was a Hold pinned over a war at home.** Athens' restored plan is Contain against Sparta at its door. The step pinned Holds every turn a city was endangered: fewer cities lost (19 runs to 10) but the Pikemen lived, and killing them is half the objective. Now a Hold is pinned only over a plan that takes the army away (a Campaign, or Contain on a team not at our door): Athens 12% to 12%, Biel war cut +32.8, Mongol bombers +15.6, war suite mean **+2.5 pp**, no case significantly worse.
- **Nominations need the plan's coming war, not only wars already on.** With at-war targets only, Dyrrachium fell to 14/50: on T37-T39 (before the declaration) the army marched on Nicomedia, and a T40 pin on Dyrrachium came too late. Nominating the routed plan's war target too (the step starts no war) gives 50/50.
- **The supply term is collected about 12% of the time at most** (war suite 64 runs plus Lab 32 runs, default genes, `WAR_SUPPLY_LOG`, `supply_report.py`). Of 2,678 Campaign switches priced with supply, 1,437 had a full 10-turn horizon. At most 23% of those took the city in the horizon, and at most 12% saw supply in use rise after it. The term is typically as large as everything else in the campaign's value together (median ratio 0.99), and only 7.8% of the priced term belongs to switches whose supply arrived. A correction is proposed in the report, not built.
- **Designer seat, paired (`designer_seat.py --seat-params`, 48 salts, 30.7 min at 12 threads):** training 36.1% to 36.2%, held out 58.8% to 59.7%; every tape moves at most 2.9 points. Flat within the tool's ~3 pp noise. Whole-game play from his positions is neither hurt nor helped.

## 2026-11-10: a short-horizon objective step closes most of the told gap on Biel and Dyrrachium; the supply term had to come out of it (`claude/ai-objective`)
- **Numbers (50 runs, seat only, same seeds):** with gene `short_objective` alone (untold), Hold Biel 34 to 45, Dyrrachium 2 to 50 and Two Cities 0 to 0, against the told ceiling of 49, 50 and 0. On the 26 Lab cards, wins move only on Biel, Dyrrachium, wide India (+4) and walled Nicomedia (-1). In the war suite (128 runs), the mean is +0.5 pp: Hold Biel cut +15.6 and Mongol bombers +15.6, but Athens two pikemen T112 -7.0 [-11.7, -2.3], untraced. The step costs about 18% more war-suite wall time.
- **The supply term breaks short pricing.** Pricing nominations with `price_campaign` as is, the step pinned Indian Bikaner on Two Cities in every run: plan value 20,626, of which `supply` was 20,592, twice a certain win (`supply_term` 400 x `supply_shortage_percent` 300 x Germany's double supply). Chalco went from 32 to 0. Inside a 5-turn window nothing a captured deposit feeds is built, so the step leaves the supply term out. The plan layer still carries it at full size; that magnitude is worth its own look.
- **(Corrected in round 3, entry above: the forecast was right; the win-chance currency was the problem.) The attacker-side siege forecast underrates a besieged home.** `forecast_siege` played from India's side (its units sent beside Biel) says Biel falls within 5 turns in only 8 of 50 runs on T132; it falls in 16 baseline runs. The step therefore mostly pins Campaigns on India's nearby cities rather than a Hold. That still beats the baseline's offensive plan, but it is the main gap left to the told 49.
- **`plan.rs::execute_hold` bug, fixed:** the Hold held the unit already in the city first, which spent its move and blocked `door.rs`'s swap of a stronger defender. It now makes the same swap (`door.rs::garrison_swap`, shared). Told as a pinned Hold, Biel went from 6/50 to 50/50. It affects Hard+Search holds too.
- **`strike_upgrade_wait` works alone, not with the step:** keeping strike units from upgrading wins Dyrrachium 50/50 on its own (Horseman #42, the card loop's finding). Combined with the step, Biel drops from 45 to 37: Rifleman #3 doesn't upgrade on T136 in 8 runs, even with a home-door exception. Measure them separately.
- **`breach` doesn't open Two Cities:** gene `breach` 100 gives 0/50 untold and told; the walkers are never beside the cities on the strike turn. The air-ground design is in `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-objective-1110/README.md`.

## 2026-11-10: told the card's goal, Hard wins Hold Biel 49/50 and Dyrrachium 50/50; Two Cities stays 0/50 (`claude/ai-objective`)
- **The split (rules 26, 50 runs, same seeds; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/ai-objective-1110/`):** baseline Hold Biel 34, Ride to Dyrrachium 2, Two Cities 0. With `legatum-sim scenario --tell-objective` (the seat's Hard brain gets the win condition as a pinned plan, every executor its own): 49, 50, 0. On two of the three cards the whole gap is not knowing the goal; on Two Cities it is execution.
- **Hold Biel, traced:** `plan.rs::choose_plan` starts a Campaign on T132 in all 50 runs (and keeps choosing Campaigns or Peace) while ten Indian units stand around Biel; the siege-stance pass (`breach.rs::siege_stance_cities`) then sends the Cavalry, Phalangite and Soldier bought for Biel at an enemy city. The purchases themselves are fine (`buy_ready_defenders` buys a Rifleman on T132). Told "hold" as the quiet plan, it wins 49. Telling it with `PlanKind::Hold` on Biel instead lost 44 of 50: `plan.rs::execute_hold` runs before `plan_military` and holds the Field Cannon already in Biel, so `door.rs::rotate_exposed_garrisons` never swaps the Rifleman in and Biel falls on T135. The Hard+Search Hold plan has the same flaw.
- **Dyrrachium, traced (not the walls alone):** T37 to T39 the plan runs a Campaign on Ravenna; Dyrrachium is not a candidate (`in-reach campaign force gate` on T37, then off the nomination list in `plan.rs::campaign_candidates`). Ravenna is walled by T40, and the plan switches to walled Nicomedia in all 50 runs: 1260 against Dyrrachium's 323. Capture odds barely separate them (45% walled without Siege, 52% open: `forecast_siege` already halves every attack, and `capture_odds` reads only turns), and Nicomedia's deposit adds a `supply` term of +1080 (`supply_term` 400 x `supply_shortage_percent` 300 on 2 Production a turn). Realized: Hard takes Nicomedia within the forecast's 4 turns in 7 of 50 runs.
- **Fix, gene `capture_horizon` (off by default):** a walled siege with no Siege counts its siege turns at the gene's percent, and the supply term counts only the share of the 10-turn plan horizon left after the capture. Seat-only at 200: Dyrrachium 2 to 48 of 50, Biel and Two Cities unchanged, told arms unchanged. Each half alone leaves Dyrrachium at 2/50: the slower walled siege still loses to the supply term (16% odds still give +384), and the horizon share alone still leaves Nicomedia ahead.
- **Two Cities, traced (told):** the Bombers strike a Ranger and rebase on T143 instead of hitting Chalco, the Cavalry upgrades to a Tank on T144 (the strike force upgrading mid-strike, as on Dyrrachium in the card loop), the Tank walks home on T146 (`door_military`), and Xochimilco is never a candidate (`campaign nomination shortlist`, then `in-reach campaign force gate`). Aircraft orders are added after the ground plan (`Brain::add_air_orders`), so no ground pass can count on a same-turn Bomber strike: a strike-and-walk-in needs the air plan inside the move planner. Not built here.
- **Measurement gotchas:** the seat must be wrapped by the same scripted brain the session builds (`ScriptedBrain::with_difficulty`); wrapping it is result-neutral (34/2/0 again). Use `--params-seat-only` for any gene on a Lab card: the opponents are Hard too.

## 2026-11-10: The mounted bonus applies only in melee (rules version 26); a Field Cannon's shot on Cavalry went from 3-4 damage to 10-16
- **Cause of the playtest's 3 damage:** the +40% class bonus applied to a mounted unit whether it charged or was shot (`combat::fight_bonus_of`), and Cavalry's 79 base makes the bonus +31, the largest in the game. Field Cannon (65) against Cavalry: defender 110 on open ground, 121 fortified (6) on Hills (the designer's 118 had fortify 3).
- **Before and after, forecast average damage to the Cavalry:** open ground 4 before, 16 after (defender 79); fortified on Hills 3 before, 10 after (defender 90). The cannon's 65 against 79 on open ground is still below even, so Cavalry stays tough under fire, just not immune.
- **Rule (designer, 2026-11-10, an exception to the rules freeze; first built as an experiment, then made the default and the switch deleted):** a mounted unit that is the target of a shot gets no "Mounted against ranged", a Keshig's shot included (`fight_class`); charges, meetings and melee keep it. The melee line's bonus against mounted is unchanged. `RULES_VERSION` 26.
- **Where it reaches:** the engine, forecast, AI fight estimates (`tactics::fight_bonuses`), the app's aftermath estimate and strength chips, and the Guide, unit-card and Keshig text. The kind-only `combat::class_bonus` now means the bonus in melee.
- **Modernization valuation keeps the kind-only bonus on purpose:** `modernization.rs` still uses `combat::class_bonus` (the bonus in melee). The army lab still has mounted beating a ranged army 100% in every era (exchange 2.65x to 3.61x), so mounted remains the counter to ranged and zeroing its edge there would undervalue it; the rules freeze allows no AI change beyond the fight estimates.

## 2026-11-10: Load-failure copy: the 11-09 save messages leaked engine detail and an "Error:" prefix; every failure now reads as two facts
- **Found by the UI review of `claude/app-hygiene`:** Load Game with a non-save file showed "Error: This file is not a Legatum save: it is not valid JSON." (`pickSaveFile` used `String(e)`), and a damaged save showed "the turn number is out of range", which a player cannot act on.
- **Now:** `pickSaveFile` runs through `loadErrorMessage`; `parseSaveFile` throws a `SaveFileError` whose message is player text and whose `detail` (and the engine's own damaged-save text) goes to `console.warn`. Not a save: "Nothing was changed; choose another file or start a new game." Damaged: "Your save file is kept; ..." The engine string in `crates/wasm/src/json.rs` is unchanged. `copyRules.ts` bans "valid JSON", a leading "Error: ", "unknown owner" and "off the map". The tour (`old-save` spec, shots 06 to 08) covers not-a-save, damaged at the title and damaged in game.

## 2026-11-09: Save validation on load (CL1) was already in the engine; what was missing was saying what is wrong. Phone code is deleted down to its shared hooks
- **CL1 was stale in the review:** the WASM load path (`Session::from_save_json` to `json::parse_state`) has run `core::validation::check_state` since `cdc290c22`; the server's `check_state` only adds the same call plus a `player_view` pass. A corrupt save did not reach the engine unchecked. The gap was the message: structure failures all read "Invalid saved game structure", and the app turned every engine parse error into one generic line.
- **Now:** `check_state` names each failure ("the map lists 5 tiles but its size needs 7", "a unit has a wrong id, an unknown owner or a position off the map"), prefixed "The save is damaged: ". `loadErrorMessage` logs that text to the console and shows the player one fixed sentence ("This save file is damaged and can't be loaded."); raw serde errors stay hidden. A chosen file's envelope is read by `parseSaveFile` (not JSON, wrong format, unknown format version, no game data); the player sees one sentence for not-a-save and one for an unsupported version (2026-11-10 entry).
- **Phone code:** deleted `app/src/mobile/`, `PhoneSheet`, `playwright.phone.config.ts`, the `tablet-touch` Playwright project, `reframeCamera`, the `.phone-game` styles, and the phone-width e2e variants (a 390 px width now renders the desktop layout, which is not a target). Still there, because other branches are editing those files: `usePhoneLayout` and its `phone` branches in `SeatBar`, `TopBar`, `VpTrack` (`phone-vp-strip`), `Minimap`, `LensSwitcher`, `AlarmScreen`, `FirstGameHints`, and the touch-card and haptics code (touch input, not layout).

## 2026-11-09: End Turn paint cost: the selection pulse was real but small; the race panel's layout is not in the cost; the End Turn block is the engine timer and the terrain rebuild
- **Capture:** `app/e2e/perf-app.spec.ts` (`tools/perf/app-measure.sh`, now with `LEGATUM_PERF_LATE` for a game that ends before turn 200: seed 7000001 ends at turn 178 on current master) plus a new "selected city" test, run on this Mac (Chromium, 1000x600, release preview) rather than the cloud box. Do not read `perf.frames` under `?perf=1`: the FPS overlay resets it every second. Count `legatum:mapDraw` measures instead.
- **Pulse (CL17):** with a city selected the map repainted every frame for the ring alone. Idle for 3 s on a late save: 90 map draws and 593 ms of main-thread task time selected, against 186 ms unselected. Now a pulse-only frame clips to the ring's box (`ringClipRect`) and holds the idle-life clock at the last full frame: 339-361 ms selected (pulse overhead 407 to 160 ms, about 60 percent less). The remainder is the scene's draw calls, still recorded each frame; a separate pulse layer would remove it but put the ring over the unit tokens, so it was not done. Pixel check: a pulse-only frame differs from a forced full redraw only by the ring's phase and ambient smoke or water phase.
- **Race panel (CL18 as named in the review):** not reproduced. A CDP trace of six late End Turns shows layout at about 5 ms and style recalculation at about 16 ms in total, so there is nothing to defer. What blocks the main thread at End Turn is the engine's timer task (125-220 ms, the WASM step on the main thread, fixed by the Worker work) and the first animation frame after the new view (80-90 ms, the terrain rebuild, `perf.buildMs` about 70; this is CL18's terrain half, untouched), then smaller autosave (35 ms) and `remember` (20 ms) phases.
- **End Turn numbers (turns 151-165, selected city, this Mac):** 292 ms click to paint and 231 ms blocked before, 299 and 230 after (335 and 262 on a second run at load average near 200): the pulse fix does not move End Turn, because the draws during End Turn are terrain-rebuild draws, not pulse draws.
## 2026-11-09: Short scenarios paired by dice seed resolve gene changes the full-game gate can't; an imitation of the designer built from Hard plus his habits predicts him only slightly better (`claude/scenario-gate`, `docs/sim-results/scenario-gate/`)
- **Gate:** `tools/sim/scenario-gate.sh` plays all 44 Lab and war cards per arm on shared seeds (0.26 CPU-s a card run). Turning the 11-08 siege-stance genes back off reads −0.042 card SDs [−0.060, −0.025] and −2.50 pp of objectives met [−3.01, −1.99] (z ≈ 4.7 and 9.6) at 128 runs; the human-seat benchmark read the same genes +1.46 pp [−0.05, +2.98]. A/A on other dice covers zero (−0.011 [−0.045, +0.023]).
- **Pairing by seed halves the noise:** paired SE 0.009 SD against 0.017 unpaired, because a card plays the same game until the candidate's orders first differ. Gene A/B in short positions should always share seeds.
- **The held-out half is small:** on its 15 cards alone a null change's interval at 128 runs (−0.076 lower bound) is wider than the −0.05 margin, so the wrapper defaults to 480 runs; and siege-off reads +0.022 SD there (its big losses, Hollow Swiss 92% → 0%, are train cards) while the objective column still fails (−1.04 pp [−1.84, −0.24]). Read both columns and the per-card table.
- **8 of 44 cards are deterministic in Hard's hands** (1 to 3 distinct scores over 128 seeds; `celts-steal-a-settler` is 150 every run): the dice never reach the decision, so they only move when the play changes.
- **His tapes are mostly standing orders:** in 108 turns of Aztecs 8.9.29 he gave 36 production orders (most of them Walls), 5 research queues, 1 purchase and 2 declarations; cities run on automation. "Predict his orders" is therefore a small sample per category, and Hard already agrees on 50–70% of foundings and 33–80% of research.
- **Genes don't change shadow predictions:** the designer gene set (strike eagerness, early prize, focus fire, commitment...) predicted exactly what Hard did on both tapes; only order-level habits moved anything (Walls first: production 28% → 38% of his choices on held-out Celts 8.9.26; no routine purchases: purchases he didn't make 82 → 56).
- **Dropping all purchases made him easier, not harder:** imitation v1 (no purchases but aircraft) in his empire let Hard in the seat do +0.047 SD better than against Hard (war-swiss-tepid T180 +1.34 SD, war-india-sparta 59% → 88% met), because his documented habit is to hold Gold *and spend it when attacked*. v2 keeps purchases for a city with an enemy army within three hexes: Hard in the seat then does as well against it as against Hard (−0.012 SD [−0.040, +0.016], objectives −0.75 pp [−1.32, −0.18]; held-out rival cards 0). It imitates two habits, not his strength (10 straight wins); the missing part is his strike and capture tactics, which needs per-situation imitation from his recorded orders.

## 2026-11-09: A parked obsolete army can't buy its way out: the Celts at St. Gallen had 30 Gold for a 1,000-Gold refit (gene `march_refit`, off; code not merged, only on branch `claude/modernize-before-march`)
- **The arithmetic that decides it:** an upgrade costs 200% of the cost difference for every step (Spearman to Pikeman 144 Gold, to Musketman 260), and units upgrade only in their own territory. The Celts at T100 of 8.9.29 had 30 Gold and +1 a turn for six Spearmen and a Phalangite; no refit option ever priced positive on the cut card (`celts-parked-at-st-gallen`). Modernising has to start before the war (treasury and production), not once the army is parked.
- **A fogged city is not a target:** the first retarget picked an unseen Aztec colony (the forecast assumes one fortified Spearman in a fogged city) and walked the group past walled Solothurn's strike. Retarget now takes only cities in sight, like the siege stance.
- **The card is not his game:** cut from his T100 state, the Aztecs are a Hard AI with 363 Gold that buys Pikemen and kills the parked army (11 combat units to 3 or 4 by T107); he left it alone. Hard takes an Aztec city 3/100 from the card with the gene off and 0/100 with bit 1 (score −5.5 [−20.2, +9.2]).
- **200 Hard games per arm, paired seeds** (`parked_army_census`): bit 2 (upgrades ahead of buildings while the plan fights a war) buys +1.7 upgrades a game [+0.4, +3.0] with captures unchanged (+0.4 [−0.4, +1.1]); bit 1 (parked army) raises idle-army turns +5.4 [+1.2, +9.6] because its fallback holds the group three tiles out, which the census counts as idle, and captures stay flat (+0.6 [−0.9, +2.0]); all bits cut city attacks −4.0 [−7.5, −0.5]. War suite −0.2 points (the gene rarely fires there). Designer seat with the gene on every Hard seat: held out −2.2 pp [−5.4, +1.0], training −1.8 [−4.4, +0.7]. Left off, and the gene code was not merged (it lives only on the branch); report `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/march-refit-1109/README.md`.
- **Unrelated, found on the way (`celts-strike-the-leader` is still broken):** revision 2 is unwinnable for a Celtic seat: the Celts burn what they capture, so its `OwnCity` objective can never be met (0/50). The new parked-army card uses "the Aztecs hold at most 8 cities" instead, but `RivalMaxCities` is no fix for the strike card: in 36 of 50 Hard runs Sparta's own AI razes Tlatelolco on T88 (a city it holds but did not found, population 3, 30 Gold) and the Celtic seat captures nothing in any of the 50 runs, so the card was a free win 72% of the time (a revision 3 with that objective was tried and dropped). Every win-condition kind in `crates/core/src/scenario.rs` checks the end state, not who caused it, so the card needs a "the seat captured N cities" win condition before it can test a strike. Until then it scores 0% for every arm. Separate observation: Sparta's AI razing a captured city it could keep is worth a look.

## 2026-11-09: An early alarm on a border army cannot help when the army is out of sight until the declaration turn (gene `early_alarm`, off, code not merged and only on branch `claude/early-alarm`; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/early-alarm-1109/`)
- **Idea #43 re-measured on master:** on the Lab cards the flat pair (`settler_safety` 1 + `peace_settler_capture` 100) still hurts the defender (`swiss-strongbox` Mongol capture 20% to 54%, `hold-athens` 98% to 90%) and `capital_guard` changes nothing. The 48/48 "saved Germany" result was one tape of his orders; no card reproduces it.
- **The Lab cards cannot measure an opening alarm:** every early card is cut once the attacker's army is already at the gates (T12-T24) and the Hard attacker never snipes a Settler on the Settler cards, so gene-on runs replay the control run for run. A rush census (`crates/sim/examples/early_alarm_census.rs rush`: one seat becomes the scenario rusher, marches on its nearest neighbour and declares once three troops stand within three hexes) is the instrument that does.
- **Finding:** the read is mostly right when it fires (70% of alarms are followed by that rival's declaration within 15 turns) and expansion is untouched (cities at T75 +0.000 per seat [-0.015, +0.015] over 200 paired games), but the victim sees the army too late: in 71% of 181 rush wars it saw fewer than two rusher troops within six hexes before the declaration turn, and only 10% gave a turn or more of warning. Victim cities lost before T60 -0.030 [-0.064, +0.004]; designer seat held out 58.1% to 58.5% (no loss). The next lever is sight (sentries or scouts on the approach, `sentry_posts` / `intel_value`), not valuation.
- **Gotcha:** a `cb.sh bin` that waited behind other agents' builds left an old `legatum-sim` in the scratch target, so the first gene-on card batch failed with "unknown field `early_alarm`" and the first identity check compared master with master. Check the binary's timestamp before trusting a gene arm.

## 2026-11-09: Pricing a war as a sequence moves the plan, not the captures (gene `war_sequence`, off; code not merged, only on branch `claude/war-sequence-value`)
- **Change (Fable review F2/F3):** a Campaign's price adds the owner's next cities the surviving force is forecast to take (chained odds, discounted over a 20- or 30-turn horizon) and the elimination; three vetoes became prices; a capture rolls the plan on to the next city.
- **Finding 1, vetoes are load-bearing for new wars:** with the second-front veto and the nominated shortlist both turned into prices for every rival, Hard Germany in the Lab's two-cities card declared on India mid-war (64 of 64 runs) and left the Aztec cities (38 of 64 runs took Chalco, then 0). The second-front price (`retaliation_permille`) only counts home cities exposed, not the war the army abandons. Fix: prices only for wars already on; new wars still come from the nominators and keep the veto.
- **Finding 2, the roll-on is undone by the plan:** in 200 Hard games to T100 the roll fired 185 times in 118 games, but 106 of those campaigns ended `plan_changed`, 71 on the same turn (`choose_plan` runs right after `reconcile_campaign`), and only 18 captured. In the fixture the plan keeps the rolled target, so the switch comes from something the fixture lacks (another candidate's sequence price, Peace, or a faction override); untraced.
- **Numbers (200 paired games, T100):** campaigns started +1.43 [+0.92, +1.94] a game, captures +0.04 [-0.16, +0.24], eliminations +0.01 [-0.01, +0.02]. War suite -0.7 points (none significant). Strike-the-leader Lab cards 0/64 both arms. Designer seat (48 salts) held out 58.1 -> 60.3 (+2.2 [-0.9, +5.3]), training -2.2 [-4.8, +0.3], all -0.6 [-2.5, +1.4]. Horizon 20 and 30 gave identical war-suite results: the chain is cut by the 4-city cap and the force test, not the horizon. Report: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/war-sequence-1109/`.
- **Lesson:** a better price on the plan layer does not reach the board while execution below it (assignment, F1) and the plan's own re-choice each turn drop the commitment; F1 first, as the review ordered.

## 2026-11-09: Assigning every free war unit to one objective lost half the war-suite goals (gene `objective_assignment`, off; code not merged, only on branch `astra/objective-assignment`)
- **What was tried (Fable review F1):** on War turns a Hard seat's free land units, after the emergency passes, were assigned greedily by marginal value to the routed Campaign's city, a visible enemy field stack, or a threatened own city, replacing the middle block of military passes with scoped assault, siege, field-fire and city-defence executors. Gene 0 kept the old pass block.
- **Numbers (paired, same seeds, only the seat on the gene):** war suite (19 cases x 50 runs) goals met 23% to 12%, -10.8 points [-12.8, -8.9]; designer seat held out 58.1% to 48.9%, -9.2 [-12.4, -5.9] (3 tapes x 5 starts x 48 salts); Lab Hold Biel 35/50 to 4/50 and walled Nicomedia 3/50 to 1/50 (seat only); Training round 2 capture 74/168 in both arms. The bar (twice the war-suite goals, no held-out loss) failed on both counts.
- **Where it lost:** Hollow Swiss T76 and Tepid Swiss T220 went 94% to 0% (captures 0.94 to 0): the city objective exists only for a routed Campaign, and the scoped executors skip `seize_open_city` and the late attack passes, so short capture windows are missed. Hold Biel 42% to 12% and India vs Sparta T142 58% to 32%: the hold executor does not reproduce the old counterattack, ranged fire, wound recovery and late defence sequence. Mongols at Chur T137 went 6% to 38% (a concentrated group beats the old cascade). These causes are inferences from aggregate telemetry; no case was turn-traced, and one gene-on run reported an illegal shared tile (untraced).
- **A confound to remember:** a Lab run with the gene on every seat showed Hold Biel 35/50 to 50/50 because it also changed the attacking Hard AI. Compare card experiments with `--params-seat-only`.
- **Lesson:** a unit allocator cannot make a Contain plan name a city, and replacing the whole middle pass block drops protective work those passes do. A next try needs a target for every active war plan and objective-scoped executors that keep defence and short-window capture; do not rewrite the pass block wholesale. Report: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/objective-assignment-1109/README.md`.

## 2026-11-09: What a capture lock would have cost the designer: his capturing units leave in 1-3 turns and go on to take more cities
- **Question:** his proposed rule (a military unit inside a captured city cannot leave while at war with the civ it was taken from; design skill, open proposal). Counted on 10 of his 13 wins since 10-27, replayed on their own recording engines with 0 hash mismatches (`tools/decision-audit/designer_seat.py` helpers; three older tapes don't build and one panicked).
- **Finding:** 59 captures; in 40 the capturing unit left before Peace or elimination, usually 1-3 turns after the capture, and 29 of those 40 units later captured another city (one Tank took five cities in 24 turns as India; a Sparta Trooper took four more). The lead's claim that he garrisons captures anyway was wrong.
- **Where it bites:** the long conquest games (Sparta 14 of 18 captures bound, India 6 of 8, Swiss 8.9.24 6 of 9, Mongols 4 of 7). Five of ten games are barely touched (0-4 captures). His army is 2-8 military units at T60 and T100, so each locked unit is a large share.
- **Weak spot confirmed:** taking a civ's last city lifts the lock, so early eliminations of small civs (Swiss by T43, Mongols by T27, India by T41) pass almost free.
- **Caveats:** the capturing unit is inferred (his unit on the tile after capture, adjacent the turn before, melee preferred); Peace is the first non-War state. Not decided: whether Alliance counts as Peace, and re-locking if war resumes.

## 2026-11-08: Releasing 8.9.28: a locked screen stalls the Mac WebKit map check, and a scratch HOME isolates the built app from his saves
- **Map check:** `tools/ui/webkit-mac/check.sh --runs 4` printed nothing for 45 minutes and hit the cap (exit 124) with Legatum closed. `ioreg -n Root -d1` showed `IOConsoleLocked` true: a locked session gives the hidden WKWebView no animation frames, like an occluded one (8.9.27 entry). Check `ioreg -n Root -d1 -a | grep -A1 IOConsoleLocked` before starting it; if locked, the check cannot run unattended. 8.9.28 shipped without it (map changes were banners and sprites, not terrain chunks).
- **Isolating an in-app test:** Tauri takes `app_data_dir` from the identifier compiled into the binary, so changing `CFBundleIdentifier` in a copied bundle does not move the data folder. Launching the binary as `HOME=<scratch> LEGATUM_ARCHIVE=<scratch>/archive …/Contents/MacOS/legatum-desktop` does: AppData (autosave, `training/`), `~/LegatumData` (`app-info.json`, `scenarios/lab/`) and WebKit storage all land under the scratch home, and the live `autosave.legatum` stayed untouched. Clicking through Training still needs desktop control; without it, only the launch-time writes can be checked.
- **Landing-time breakage from the scenario sweep:** branches cut before the sweep still named deleted scenarios (`athens-empty-capital` in the drop-in e2e spec and in an `include_str!` inside `app/src-tauri`, which the verify never compiles). After deleting scenarios, `git grep` every deleted id across `app/`, `crates/` and `tools/` on each branch that merges afterwards.

## 2026-11-07: Training hang at T44 (playtest 8.9.27): the native search never started in the desktop app, and the failure path waited forever
- **Symptom:** End Turn on a turn with battles left the progress card at "15 of 15 s" and "Resolving…" for good. `legatum-desktop` sat at 0% CPU, a `sample` showed no `training` or `rollout-*` threads, and there was no `training/` folder.
- **Cause 1:** `training_request_json` sent the state in the save encoding (`json::state_to_json` writes `setup.seed` as a string for JavaScript), and the desktop app's `training_start` read it with plain serde (`expected u64`). Every native Training search failed to start, from the first desktop build of Training mode. The browser build runs the WASM path and never hit it; the tests mocked the Tauri side. Now both ends use `legatum_ai::training::TrainingRequest` in plain serde (the string passes through the webview untouched), with a test at a seed past 2^53.
- **Cause 2:** the start failed while he planned, and `complete(null)` left `applied` null. `finish()` treated "no result" as "not done" and waited for a wake that had already happened. The cap+5 s guard was in a poll loop that had already exited. Now `TrainingDriver` has a `settled` flag. Every failure falls back to Hard's pending orders, with a console warning and a `fallback` line in the training log. A watchdog settles on Hard at the cap + 2 s whatever the search is doing.
- **Lesson:** a value that crosses a language boundary twice (Rust → JS string → Rust) needs a single type on both Rust ends and a round-trip test. Any promise the UI waits on needs a timeout that doesn't depend on the code that failed.
- **Workaround for a stuck game:** relaunching hangs again on the same turn. A copy of the save with `search` removed from `setup.players` and the archive dropped (its setup hash no longer matches) loads as plain Hard.
## 2026-11-07: A committed operation executor makes strikes run (took 97%, from 6-23%), and executed strikes lose; the kill check fails a third time (branch `claude/operation-executor`, `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/operation-search-1107/round4-executor/`)

- Forcing a plan "while it stays on offer" is not commitment: the plan layer drops a campaign the funded-forecast and reachability gates reject, Sparta's campaign rule overrides Economy and Peace, the reconciler ends the campaign and withdraws its units, and every later military pass re-assigns the units.
