An operation has to own its units (ordered first, before every other pass), its plan (injected when the plan layer drops it, ahead of faction rules) and its campaign (the reconciler skips it) at once; any one of these alone leaves it at the old rates.
- With all three, strike operations took in 96.6% of samples, struck in 86.9% and captured in 17.9%, and they lost: mean selection gain -15 pp for a plain strike, -11 for a bought strike. Round 2's winners (the Swiss bought war, +16 to +19 pp) were Hard with war genes, not the war. Only 10-turn strikes at Sparta T28 and T30 confirmed (+18.8 pp). Gene-only operations (snipe Settlers, Walls, contest the hold) lead the means at +2 to +3 pp.
- Kill check 3 of 38 windows (8%), mark 30%. The executors are no longer the cap; the operations are. Widening the generator with whole-army strikes is the wrong direction: the next generator should vary when to strike and with what (strike timing, roster size, short strikes), as the training addendum's per-unit coordinate descent suggested.
- A home city "under attack" must mean at War: `threat_units` holds no foreign unit at Peace, so the release override needs a war (the unit test sets one).


## 2026-11-07: Releasing 8.9.27: the Mac WebKit map check stalls behind a running game, and a box tour without a fresh server shows archive 400 toasts
- **WebKit check:** `tools/ui/webkit-mac/check.sh` sat 23 minutes on run 1 with no output while the designer's Legatum window was in front: an occluded WKWebView gets no animation frames, and `body.js`'s `frames()`/`settle()` waits are long by design (up to about an hour per zoom). With the game closed the same check finished 4 runs, 0 differing images, in minutes. Run it only when Legatum isn't running, or kill the `wk` PID and rerun later.
- **Box tour archive toasts:** the release reviewer's box tour (`remote.sh <sha> --npm -- ... npx playwright test e2e/tour`) showed "archive upload failed (HTTP 400)" toasts in about 38 shots. The extended verify of the same code, which builds `legatum-server` fresh, passed every spec with the console-error check on, and the desktop app archives through Tauri (`archive_stage`/`archive_commit`), never `/api/archive`. A stale server in the box slot gives this; build `legatum-server` in the tour command (as for local tours, 2026-11-06) before reading archive errors as a release blocker.
- **Landing on a moving master:** the training-mode verify went green against `bd9d47733` while `wonder-lost-clear` landed underneath, and the merged result failed to compile (a new `City.wonder_lost` field missing from a test literal). A green verify only covers the master it merged; check `git log` for code landings since its start before merging.

## 2026-11-07: Operation search (strategy reset increment 1): the kill check fails twice, because named operations rarely become the plan (branch `claude/operation-search`, `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/operation-search-1107/`)
- **Result:** at Hard's review points in the designer's windows (Sparta 8.9.26 T18–T30, Swiss 8.9.26 T30–T40, India 8.9.25 T13–T22 and T148–T152; 16 determinized states per operation, played to the end), the best operation beat Hard's plan by 10 pp or more with the confirmed interval clear of zero in 2 of 22 windows (9%) with generator 1 and 5 of 22 (23%) with generator 2. The mark is 30%. Recurring winners: spending the hoard on a war (`buy_strike`, Swiss T34–T38), lookouts (Sparta T20, +21.9 pp confirmed), Sparta's early veteran strike, founding now. Re-picking among Hard's own plan candidates gained +0.0 pp on average, as in the value-net regret study.
- **Why it fails:** a forced plan stays only while the plan layer still offers it, and the strike operations' target took in 20–28% of samples: the funded-forecast and reachability gates drop the city even with the widened genes, so `strike:X` mostly plays as Hard with war genes. Sparta's campaign override also beats a forced Economy or Peace plan. Next: inject the forced Campaign offline and give it a roster, then re-run before any fit.
- **The selection maximum lies.** The best of 10–23 operations on 16 samples showed +10 pp in 64% of windows in both rounds; on fresh samples it held in 27–41%. Always confirm the winner on fresh determinizations, and read the confirmed number.
- **Determinized rollouts are optimistic for the seat:** Hard wins 7 of 16 from Sparta T20 on the seat's determinized views, 20 of 48 from the true state (takeover curve). The memo's "2 s a window" assumed 30-turn rollouts; playing to the end costs about 2 CPU-seconds a rollout from T20, so 70–130 s a window on a loaded Mac.
- **Celts 8.9.26 (extra training tape, generator 3 with the lead's leader operations, 21 windows on his and the Germany and Sparta seats): 0 of 21 pass.** `strike_empty` took its target in few samples and `celts_bundle`'s bought war in none; the selection maximum showed +10 pp in 15 of 21 windows and held in 1.
- **On a rival's seat, the determinized world plays the designer as Hard** (`determinize` gives every seat a Hard brain), so rival-seat windows measure beating Hard in his seat, not him: root cause 4 again.
## 2026-11-07: Designer-seat score control: held out 57.4% (29.0% before Celts 8.9.26 replaced 8.9.25), training 44.4%; positions from rules 10 and 11 carry to 21 unchanged (branch `claude/designer-seat`, `docs/status/designer-seat.md`)
- **Control at master `516cca016`, rules 21:** held out 29.0% [27.1, 31.0] (Mongols 8.9.25 43.8%, Celts 8.9.25 2.9%, Swiss 8.9.24 40.4%), training 44.4% [41.9, 47.0] (Sparta 8.9.26 70.8%, Swiss 8.9.26 55.8%, India 8.9.25 6.7%). 1,680 games with the retired tape, 14.4 minutes on the Mac with nice (another agent's search running alongside). The plan guessed "about 20%" held out; the gap is Mongols and Swiss 8.9.24, which Hard converts from T60 on.
- **The two weakest tapes are wide, late wins:** India (T177) and Celts (T159) stay under 20% even from T80; Hard can't finish what he built there. Sparta and Mongols are 100% from T60 to T80: those games were decided by mid-game, so their late cells carry no signal. The earlier cells (T2 to T40) are where a better AI must show.
- **No state changed on load:** `seat_run` re-hashes each recorded state after deserialising it in the current engine, and all six tapes (recorded on rules 10, 11 and 19) matched. The intervening rules add fields with defaults, so the takeover method needs no `save_version` restamp here (that is only for `cut.sh` and app loads). The JSON keeps `states_changed_on_load` per tape: a nonzero value means a rule changed an existing field and the tape needs a look.
- **The run is deterministic** (same commit, same numbers: a local Celts rerun and the Swiss 8.9.26 tape on the Linux build box matched the Mac cell for cell), so a frozen control is one run, not a distribution, and the nightly box run is comparable with local runs.
- **Swapping Celts 8.9.25 (2.9%) for his easy Celts 8.9.26 win (87.9%, 34/48 from T2) moved held out from 29.0% to 57.4% with no AI change.** One tape decides the set's level, and an easy win is a near-ceiling tape with little signal. Read held-out changes per tape, and restate the plan's 40% checkpoint target against this control.
- **A shared cargo target dir across `git archive` trees served a stale binary:** archived files carry the commit's time, so cargo saw an older tree as fresh and kept the last tree's uplifted example; the Celts 8.9.26 export failed its T1 hash check (the hash check is what caught it). Now one target dir per tree. A stale measured `seat_run` would not fail loudly, so this matters most there.
- **Mongols 8.9.25 has no finished archive:** the tape is the in-progress archive (T85), not the 11-06 save.
## 2026-11-07: Avoiding an aggressive neighbour's land costs Hard more than the cities it saves (placement genes)
- **What:** pricing a site by the specific neighbour's aggression (`site_aggressor_risk`) makes Hard lose fewer young cities, but it wins less. The land it gives up is worth more than the cities it saves. Crediting land a rival would otherwise take (`site_denial`) is neutral to slightly positive and is now on. Backfill deferral (`site_backfill`) rarely fires: its "nothing unexplored within 4" test fails while Settlers are still choosing.
- **Evidence** (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/settle-strategy-1107/`):
  - Risk at 100: young-city losses on his two Swiss seats fell 0.92 to 0.75 a run, and seat wins fell 83 to 73 of 288.
  - Risk at 100, human-seat gate: seat-only -4.39 pp [-6.23, -2.55], while rivals-only were harder (-2.59 [-4.73, -0.45]).
  - Risk at 25 did nothing measurable.
  - Denial at 100: seat-only +1.03 [-0.77, +2.82]; census best/worst 2.50 to 2.48 and 2.57 to 2.16 held out; held-out young-city losses -0.24 a game [-0.35, -0.12].
- **Also:** Walls can never be bought (`economy::is_unbuyable`), so "Gold for Walls" in a plan means Gold for a defender.
- **Also:** agreement with his founding sites is a weak target on the Swiss maps. His sites are dense, and he settled toward India on 8.9.26, so a risk penalty moves Hard away from his choices even where it is "right".
- **Change:** `site_denial` 100 on for Hard. `site_aggressor_risk` and `site_backfill` were removed from the code (off by default and no gain, so dead code; no plan builds on them, and a deferral version, founding the risky site later behind an escort or Walls, is a different mechanism that would be written fresh). The numbers above stay as the record. Git history keeps the code (`0db34c07b`).

## 2026-11-07: Training mode skateboard: the battle search changes Hard's orders in half the lab's battles and four in five of a real game's, and most real-game deviations lean on units the seat can't see (branch `claude/training-mode`, `docs/sim-results/training/skateboard-1107/`)
- **Lab (21 fixed cells x 8 dice, 2,048 rollouts a battle):** Hard captures 29.8%, Training 34.5% (+4.8 pp; discordant pairs 13 for Training, 5 for Hard, so not yet significant), Production lost equal (283 vs 281). Nearly all of the gain is one cell: the Mongol Horsemen against a ranged garrison went 0/8 to 8/8. The descent's start was Hard's own orders in 87% of battles; "wait" won 10%, storm and focus 3%.
- **Self-play (6 Training empires, 150 turns, 16 threads, after the window fix below):** 254 battles on 66 turns, 80% changed; the search took 6.0 s a battle turn on average, p50 4.4 s, p95 14.0 s, and 3 turns hit the 15 s cap. **182 of the 202 deviations had enemy units in the arena that the seat could not see:** the arena's window (7 tiles) is far wider than sight, so most of an omniscient teacher's lessons can't be used as they are by a fogged student. Log field `hidden_dependent`. Check the student on fogged positions before distilling any of this. Played live, these orders are a pillar 6 question (the UI reviewer flagged it): Training plays moves chosen with hidden information.
- **War suite (35 cases, 16 runs each, 1,024 rollouts a battle, same commit both arms):** Training -2.0 points on the suite mean (45% to 43%, 16 to 15 of 34 at human parity), with big swings both ways: German air early +100, Mongol bombers +56, Athens pikemen +31; German air T214 -62, Japan v Sparta -56, Swiss tepid T220 fresh -56, Celts wild hold -31. A search that is right inside a 7-tile arena can be wrong for the war (a hold that needs the army elsewhere), as the memo warned. The search doesn't beat Hard yet; it is a data source, not a level to tune toward.
- **Hard's orders that leave the window looked like "hold" in the arena** (an out-of-window `MoveUnit` was dropped), which made "retreat -> hold" a top pattern for a reason that was the cut, not the battle. Fixed: such a move heads for the arena tile nearest its real destination (`Arena::toward`). The lab numbers are unchanged by the fix: the lab's 24x15 map fits inside one window, so no move leaves it.
- **The WASM engine runs on the main thread, so the in-browser search must yield:** it runs in 12 ms slices with 24 ms gaps while he plans and 60 ms slices once he ends the turn; the desktop app runs it on a native thread over every core but one (`training_start`), and the WASM build is only the fallback.
- **A report line about the AI is filtered out under the report's Team filter** unless its `players` includes the viewer; the Training line lists the viewer first.
- **Log volume:** a decision line with its gzipped arena is about 4 KB (893 lab battles: 3.8 MB), so a whole game's log stays in single megabytes.
## 2026-11-07: Static merge gates: what eighty landings would have tripped, and a hook that split commands at `2>&1`
- **What:** the docs gate became `.claude/hooks/merge-gates.sh` over `tools/gates/check.py`: hygiene (secrets, personal data in exported files, saves, big binaries, dead code and legacy markers, migrations), engine invariants (floats, hash-order containers, unseeded RNG, clocks, render crates, saved-state shape vs `RULES_VERSION`, stale `.current.` fixtures), the art lookalike check, and reviewer markers by path (docs, `copy: clean` for player text, UI). The copy reviewer is the docs reviewer's copy pass rather than a second agent: both read the same diff, and one marker is cheaper than two runs.
- **Evidence:** replaying the 80 most recent master merges through the static checks (about 1.8 s each, 142 s in all) found 4 real problems and, after tuning, no false ones: two saved-state changes without a bump (`GameState.air_contacts` in `claude/land-air-intercept-proxy`, a `GameState` field in `astra/diff-reseed-objectives`, both at a time the rule required a bump) and two `/Users/<name>` paths in `docs/playtests/diffs/README.md` that were later fixed by hand. On master itself: 4 `HashMap`/`HashSet` uses in core, all lookup-only (now `gate-allow` with the reason), zero floats, RNG or clock use outside tests, `.current.` fixtures all at `RULES_VERSION` 20, and one personal reference in an exported playtest review (removed). Of the 80 merges, about half touched UI paths, so the UI-review marker is the expensive part of the gate, not the static checks.
- **Tuning that mattered:** "legacy"/"old" as words fire on 15 honest comments about old saves; only bare markers (`// legacy`, `// old:`, `// legacy code`) are flagged. Shell `# VAR=x cmd` usage lines look like code, so commented-out code is checked by statement shape in Rust/TS and by `ast.parse` in Python, never in shell. Binaries over 2 MB are routine under `docs/design/` (art masters), so the art folders are exempt. The personal-data patterns are read from `tools/release/export-public.sh` so the gate and the exporter cannot drift, and only exported paths are checked (`docs/sim-results/` holds 840 home paths but is never published).
- **The `2>&1` bug:** the old hook tokenised with `shlex` and `&` as punctuation, so `git merge --no-ff B -m "..." 2>&1` became the words `... 2>` and `1`; `2>` was taken as a second branch and the merge was refused with "cannot resolve `2>`". The new tokenizer drops redirections with their targets (`2>&1`, `>/dev/null`, `&>log`, `2> err`) and honours quotes, so `>` or `&` inside `-m` messages are safe.
- **Change:** `tools/gates/` (check, hygiene, engine-invariants, art, markers, common), the hook and its test (`.claude/hooks/test-merge-gates.sh`, 66 cases), the docs reviewer's copy pass and `copy: clean` marker line, the UI reviewer's marker, and `LEGATUM_GATE_BYPASS` (logged to `.progress/merge-gates/bypass.log`) in place of `LEGATUM_DOCS_BYPASS`.

## 2026-11-07: A lost wonder race left Thun idle behind an unblocked Mill that the app called "can't start yet" (Swiss 8.9.26 T92, branch `claude/wonder-lost-clear`, RULES_VERSION 21)
- **Root cause: the race resolves inside the winner's production phase, after any lower-id loser has already produced.** Replaying his tape at `playtest-8.9.26` (92 turns, 0 hash mismatches): Thun (city 17) built the Mill T90-T91 (79), and on T92 he set its queue to Hagia Sophia then Mill. Thun put 30 Production into the wonder; India's Allahabad (city 18, resolved after Thun) completed it the same phase, so `resolve_wonder_race` cleared Thun's wonder and paid 15 Gold. Thun's phase had already run, so nothing started the Mill: T93 opened with Thun idle, queue `[Mill]`, Mill unblocked (`production_blocker` None; it would have started by itself that turn with its 79). The app's `waitingQueueText` assumes an idle city with a queue is blocked and fell through to "Mill can't start yet", the only message on the Choose production decision. The `WonderRaceLost` report line was sent but sat among the T92 war declarations. A loser with a higher id than the winner never even idled: its own phase started the queue at once. Six cities lost that race on T92.
- **Fix:** the loss is recorded on the city (`City.wonder_lost`), its queue is held for that turn's phase in either resolution order, and the city view carries it; any production order or its next phase clears it. The app turns it into the named decision, report line and moment card in one wording (`app/src/wonderRace.ts`), and says a genuinely blocked next item's own reason. Rival wonder pace was already public (`GreatWorkView.production_per_turn`, unused by the app), so the build card now shows "also building: India (about N turns)" and a city on the wonder warns with the fastest rival's turns left.
- **How to apply:** an idle city with a non-empty queue is not proof the head is blocked. Anything that clears a city's production outside its own phase (a race, a capture, a gift) must either restart the queue or record why it stopped, or the waiting-queue copy will invent a reason. Core tests: `projects_tests::a_lost_wonder_race_stops_the_city_for_a_choice_in_either_resolution_order`.
## 2026-11-07: The AI's modernization tally makes a resource-starved empire build obsolete units without end (8.9.26 Swiss coach review)
- **What:** with `modernization_value` 100 (default), `production.rs` counts each existing combat unit as its squared strength against the enemy units it sees (`modernization.rs::modernization_percent`), so a Spearman next to Riflemen counts as about 0.1 of a unit. When the next era's unit is blocked by supply (`resource_blocker`), the only startable answer is another era-I unit, which also counts as 0.1, so `desired_army` never fills. `composition::obsolete` never marks the melee line, and its shooter check is skipped when `modernization_value` > 0.
- **Evidence:** AI India in his 8.9.26 Swiss win was the most advanced AI (first into the Renaissance, T85) but had 0 Niter T81–T135: 31 era-I units after T85, 23 of 24 at T130, unit upkeep 22 Gold and net income 1 Gold a turn. Tape replay at the tag, 720/720 AI orders reproduced.
- **Rule side:** a starved empire has no fallback (no resource trade, no resource-free unit line), so it is stuck with era-I land units until it settles or captures a deposit; put to the designer as a design question.
- **Change:** none yet; idea #41 extended with the fix and its gate.

## 2026-11-07: Doc audit: faction docs drift because decisions land in skills first and every rule restates itself in five places
- **What was stale:** the `legatum-factions` current table still listed Swiss Great Work buying, Celtic Ambushers and a 30 Gold sack, Mongol Scout and civilian Peace passage, Sparta's slow heal and level-4 retirement, and described Byzantium only as Mercenary Companies; the `legatum-design` "Decided" block still gave Germany a −1 move, Byzantium bought peace and Mongol slip-past; `docs/status/next-tasks.md` still queued "Germany: −1 move only outside its own land"; `agreed-vs-built.md` still said the Aztecs were not built; `game-rules.md` had no Mongol long-road commerce in the faction table, still described the Yam Relay Station as a city-held Courier Hall, and said Germany plays the common AI defaults when `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` carries a full tuned Germany table. The design skill's calls-to-arms notes were wrong the other way: the 10-18 "removed" entry was itself superseded on 10-23.
- **Why:** a decision is written once into a dated skill entry, but the same rule lives in the current table, the rules reference, the design skill's summary block, the status ledgers and the app copy. Cuts (6 on 11-06/07, the Germany rework) updated the newest places only. History entries were never marked superseded, so a reader cannot tell a current rule from a dated one.
- **Gap between decision and code:** Germany's first-building discount (decided cut 11-07) and the Byzantium Theodosian Walls and Constantinople kits (decided 11-06/07) are not on master (branches unmerged), so the tables now say "on master" and "decided, not on master" separately. Code on master is the truth for current-state rows.
- **Change:** current tables match master (RULES_VERSION 20); old entries carry "(superseded YYYY-MM-DD: ...)" marks. Rule for the next cut: grep the faction's name and the rule's old number across `.claude/skills`, `docs/status` and `app/src/factionSheets.ts` in the same change.

## 2026-11-06: A replaced building's name leaks through prose tables, so Germany's text is now linted for "Forge" (UI review of `claude/land-germany-rework`)
- **The Forge-slot Foundry showed the standard name in six prose tables** that were not routed through `itemName(…, faction)`: the Factory card's "upgrades your Forge" (`BUILDING_STATS` summary), the supply tooltip's and missing-resource alert's user list (`RESOURCE_USERS`), the Lens legend, the faction sheet's resource line, the Machinery rule line (`FAMILY_NAMES`) and the Industrialization card. A corpus test (`textArtifacts.test.tsx`) now fails if any Germany block says "Forge" outside a comparison phrase; `itemSummary` joined the corpus because the card summary was not in it, which let the leak pass first.
- **A tile card read the supply by the tile owner's faction** (`t.owner ?? view.me`), so a rival hovering a German deposit saw Germany's doubled 4 and 6. "Inside your borders" is always the viewer's faction.
- **A bare "+3" meant the difference on one surface and the Forge's own number on another** (unlock card "Against the Forge: +3 Production", sheet "a Forge gives +3"). The unlock card now says what the standard item itself gives ("A Forge gives +3 Production", "Walls cost 60 Production and give +10 city strength", `pieceCompare.standardText`) for every faction piece; the build card's "vs" line and the Guide's "compared with" sentence still print deltas, each labelled as one.
- **The new hyphen-before-digit copy rule (`(^|\s)-\d` on rendered text) found a real one on its first run:** a negative combat strength in the battle report ("Strength -1 (Scout 5, Wounded −4)", `format.ts::formatBreakdown` printed the total with a hyphen).
- **`industrialized` was a dead parameter** left from the old Factory-slot Foundry's movement rule (`void industrialized;` in `unitStatsFor`, threaded through `itemDescription`); removed.

## 2026-11-06: The Germany rework lifts Germany from 6.2% to 15.9% on current master; the Foundry's Food cost (-2 or -3) is not measurable (branch `claude/land-germany-rework`, `docs/sim-results/germany-landing-1106/`)
- **Paired census, 4,096 Hard games, seed 9311003:** master 6.24%, branch 15.87% (+9.63 pp [+8.32, +10.95]), Foundry at -3 Food 15.90%. Best/worst faction falls from 3.14 (Athens 19.6 / Germany 6.2) to 2.60 (Athens 17.9 / Switzerland 6.9). Athens, Mongols and Celts each lose 1.5 to 2.2 pp to the stronger Germany; the weakest factions are now Switzerland, Sparta and India at 6 to 9%, so the 1.5x pillar is a shared-AI problem now, not Germany's.
- **A negative yield on a faction piece printed "+-2 Food" on the faction sheet** (`premium.tsx::pieceStats` formatted every yield as `+${n}`); the first piece with a drawback in a building yield exposed it. Fixed with a test that no sheet contains "+-".
- **`census.py` from the Swiss playbook crashes on any census where a Swiss seat has no `cities_at_turns`** (its Swiss block runs unconditionally); the win-rate and paired parts are independent of it.
- **Playwright specs on the box's `fixtures.ts` serve `app/dist`, not the Vite source:** after editing UI code, `npm run build` before a spec, or it asserts against the old bundle (a "fix" looked unfixed for one run).
- Three arms of 4,096 Hard games each took about 4 minutes and $0.04 apiece on one c8g.16xlarge spot server; three `run-batch.sh` launched at once with `nohup` died with exit 254 right after launch, and ran fine under `bash -x` in the background.
## 2026-11-06: Modern era alone does not prove a Fighter; Hard now reads hangars it has seen (gene `air_stage_read` 1, branch `claude/land-air-intercept-proxy`)
- **`decisive_buy.rs::could_intercept` treated every Modern-era rival as Fighter-defended,** so the Bomber buy that decided the designer's Swiss and India games could never fire once rivals reached the Modern era. At gene 1 interception is priced from what Hard has seen: 25% for an unseen Modern rival, 50% after a reported Fighter interception, 60% for a remembered Fighter base in range, 75% for a Fighter seen in range. An air-strike report must identify the attack without revealing the kind of an aircraft in a hidden hangar.
- **Measured (4,096 eight-empire Hard games a arm, seed 9311003, zero panics):** no faction moved more than 0.5 pp (India -0.19, Switzerland +0.42); the human-seat gate's rivals-only arm is noninferior (-0.05 pp strength, 473 vs 472 seat wins of 2,048), the seat-only arm inconclusive. A hygiene fix that does not change balance, shipped default on; gene 0 keeps the old certainty for ablation. Landed on top of the shared `walls_garrison` and `army_war_extra` defaults with the pins unchanged.
## 2026-11-06: After the shared Walls rule, India's extra-guard removal still pays +2.4 to +2.6 points (gene `india_playbook` 2, branch `claude/land-india-playbook`)
- **The first India playbook was mostly the Walls rule.** On the old master its bits 1+2 gained +6.03 pp, of which Walls-as-garrison was +4.39 and the guard removal +1.06 [-0.01, +2.13]. Once master made Walls the garrison for every Hard faction (`walls_garrison` 1), bit 1 was a duplicate and its peacetime-quota half is master's `walls_peace_quota` (null), so bit 1 and its code were deleted and Hard India plays 2.
- **What is left is real and bigger than the old screen said.** Merged branch against master, 4,096 Hard games, India win rate per seat: +2.43 pp [+1.20, +3.57] on seed 9311003 (9.82% to 12.25%) and +2.58 [+1.37, +3.82] on held-out 8241001; no other faction outside its interval. Removing the extra network guard and the 250/150 defend margin and door defense is worth more once the troop-per-city upkeep is gone: the guards were funded by the quota that Walls now replace.
- **Tooling:** `census.py` crashed on the master copy (a null Swiss T100 cities value); the fixed copy on the branch reads it. `tools/sim/repin.sh` with `LEGATUM_OFFLOAD=1` ran locally for a minute when the box marker `~/LegatumRuns/box/offload-unavailable-until` was set (a failed local cargo write in a worktree sandbox): delete the marker and rerun if the box is up.
## 2026-11-06: The value-based Walls rule does not beat the blunt shared one (gene `walls_garrison_value`, branch `claude/land-walls-value-and-price`, `docs/sim-results/walls-garrison-value-1106/`)
- **Question:** does pricing Walls against a troop by expected city damage saved (and crediting troops for attacks) do better than "Walls are the garrison" for every Hard faction? 4,096 eight-empire Hard games, seed 9311003, against master.
- **On top of the blunt rule (both on):** nothing moves (no faction outside its interval; cities lost 1.615 to 1.602, eliminations 9.72% to 9.71%). **Replacing it (`walls_garrison` 0, gene 100):** worse than master, cities lost 1.746 and 11.04% eliminated, Athens -2.01 [-3.52, -0.52], Sparta +2.71 and Switzerland +2.47. The best/worst ratio reads 2.88 and 3.02 against 3.14, but it is the quotient of two noisy extreme seats and the intervals behind it all include 0.
- **Likely why (from the code, not isolated by an arm):** a rule that counts a Walls-less city as needing a troop whenever the local ratio is under 100 puts back the troop per city the blunt rule removed in quiet cities, and the attack credit it gives troops raises captures (12.92 to 13.97 a game). The gain of the shared rule was cities kept, not a better troop-versus-Walls trade.
- **Kept:** the gene (default 0, nothing changes), the `walls_cost` experiment (default 60; the Walls price stays the designer's call) and the early-capture counter. Params files that set a shared gene to its non-default value switch off that gene's built-ins only; `walls_garrison` has none, so `{"walls_garrison": 0, "walls_garrison_value": 100}` is a clean value-only arm.

## 2026-11-06: Walls as the garrison is worth 2 to 5 points to every faction, not just the Swiss and India; most other shared quotas are null (quota ablation census, branch `claude/strategy-gap-lab`, `docs/sim-results/strategy-gap-1105/`)
- **The rule we found one playbook at a time is general.** Gene `walls_garrison` (the `swiss_playbook` 1 rule without its faction test), on one faction at a time, 4,096 eight-empire Hard games, seed 9311003: Athens +5.0 pp, Mongols +4.5, India +4.4 (the India screen's env arm to the hundredth), Aztecs +4.2, Byzantium +4.0, Sparta +3.1, Germany +3.0, Celts +1.7. All intervals exclude 0. Cities lost and eliminations fall for every faction. Shared across all factions it holds on held-out seed 8241001, and in the human-seat gate it makes the rivals harder for a Hard seat to beat (-3.03 pp [-5.37, -0.78]). Upkeep barely moves: outside the Swiss `army_banker` path the peacetime quota still counts every city, so the gain is cities kept.
- **The lean war army (`army_war_extra` 1) helps the factions that fight most:** own effect Byzantium +3.2, Celts +2.5, Mongols +1.6, Sparta and India null. Together with walls (A7) the gate's rivals arm reads -4.15 pp [-6.35, -1.90].
- **Null:** `army_hard_extra` 0, halving `defend_margin_percent` and `door_defense`, wider and poorer Settler sites. `conquest_runaway_percent` reaches nobody: it is read only under `conquest_bank_goal` (Switzerland only), so all 4,096 games matched control.
- **Exploiter seat `peace_cycler` (prediction 1) found nothing:** Hard does not demobilise in a truce (the target's paid units went 6.8 to 7.3 from Peace to the redeclaration, 24,254 truces), and the cycling seat lost 4.6 pp.
- **Read a shared AI change by its own effect, not by the shared arm.** Win share in an AI-only census is zero-sum. When every faction defends better, the conquerors (Sparta -1.9) and the faction that already had the rule (Switzerland -2.9) must lose share, though each gains from the gene alone. One own-effect arm per faction (`faction_overrides: {F: {gene: v}}`) separates the two; the gate's rivals arm measures "is the AI harder" against a fixed opponent.
- **Setting a shared gene in a params file switches off `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` for that gene** (built-ins apply only while the gene equals the shared default), so Athens's `army_war_extra` 0 would silently become the new shared 1. A shared-default arm must restore each built-in in `faction_overrides`.
- **Tooling:** the fresh control reproduced the published census game for game (and a control through an empty params file matched too), so arms on one binary are exact pairs. Three parallel `run-batch.sh` launches died polling (exit 254), each terminating its server. Two at a time, with a retry on exit 254 and `MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded`, ran 28 censuses for about $1.30.
- **Landed on the branch the same day (e7e862a6e):** with both genes as shared defaults, the census against master reproduced A7 game for game. The follow-up `walls_peace_quota` (the peacetime quota counting only unwalled cities) was null, and upkeep did not move: the peacetime quota rarely binds. Flipping the defaults broke four plain-path fixture tests (Swiss and India settler fixtures; pinned to `walls_garrison` 0) and one runner tape pin. It also flagged that Germany's and Switzerland's built-in `army_war_extra` 1 now repeated the shared default, so they were dropped.
- How to apply: before porting the next faction playbook, try its lever as a shared gene with one own-effect arm per faction; a rule that pays one faction usually pays all of them. Recommended (lead's call): `walls_garrison` 1 as the shared Hard default, then `army_war_extra` 1.

## 2026-11-05: AI India loses on the garrison quota, like the AI Swiss; its drawbacks cost the AI 6 to 9 points (8.9.25 India coach review)
- **The kit already pays the AI; conversion fails.** In the 4,096-game Hard census (seed 9311003) AI India has the most cities at T100 (5.28) but wins 8.7%. With 8+ cities at T100 it wins 30% against 58% for the other factions. It loses 3.59 cities a game (others 1.35 to 1.98), is eliminated in 17.2% of seats, trains 29 military units with 6.2 upgrades (fewest) and spends the least Gold (1,118).
- **Screening his plan, India seats only (paired, 4,096 games each).** Walls as a city's garrison (the Swiss playbook's rule applied to India: a walled city needs no troop of its own) **+4.39 pp [+3.18, +5.61]**, 8.70% to 13.10%, eliminations 17.2% to 12.0%. India's lean linked garrison (network guard off, shared defend margin and door) +1.06 [-0.01, +2.13]. Both together +6.03 [+4.79, +7.27] (14.73%); held-out seed 8241001: walls-as-garrison +3.29 [+2.12, +4.47], both +5.27 [+4.03, +6.52] (9.53% to 14.80%). In his seat the same switches help from T2 (5% to 16%) but hurt when handed over at T120 (52% to 31%) with two neighbours at War: trust the census for a faction gene. Null or worse: Walls first alone (+0.5), a lean war army (+0.3), more Settler weight (−1.1), poorer and farther sites (−0.2), sites that ignore corruption (−0.6, his territory towns), upgrade priority (−0.1), the Diwan committed (−0.35 with completion 44.6% to 54.5%), no Office chase (−0.1), free campaigns (−0.2).
- **Is it the faction?** Partly, by design. Remittance off (non-capital cities keep all their Gold) +8.89 pp; corruption off +6.49. Rivals' India-specific targeting off (Sparta's India strike paths, the India-link campaign bonus) −0.76, null: India is not losing because rivals single it out. The remittance drawback hits an AI that pays upkeep for a troop per city hardest (upgrades double without it). He paid 445 Gold of upkeep all game with five units and lived with it.
- **Two code findings from tracing Hard in his seat at T149.** India signs Peace with the 8-VP Mongols (`diplomacy.rs::wants_peace_why`); refusing Peace with the strict leader at 60%+ of target is +7.3 pp [+1.8, +12.8] in his seat. `decisive_buy.rs::could_intercept` treats every Modern-era rival as Fighter-defended, so the Bomber buy that decided his Swiss and India games can never fire once rivals reach the Modern era.
- **Tooling.** The box filler ran 9 of 20 arms in two and a half hours while verify jobs held the cores; arms in their own `remote.sh` jobs (one build, three arms in sequence, 20 threads) took 15 minutes an arm. Filler specs with an `env` field gave an arm identical to control game for game; pass env switches on the command line inside a remote job instead. `remote.sh` returned a `seat-out/tr` trace directory as a copy of app/src twice: tar traces inside the job.
- How to apply: before tuning a faction, run its kit-off rule arms beside the AI arms. Here the drawbacks are worth 6 to 9 points to the AI, the AI-side lever 4.4. Fix the quota first. Then ask the designer whether the drawbacks are too big for any player who garrisons normally.
## 2026-11-05: Germany's four strengths are dead weight and its movement drawback carries the faction (branch claude/faction-ablation; `legatum-sim --rule-off`, `tools/sim/ablate.sh`)
- **Each faction rule now has a switch, so "does it matter" is a measurement.** `RuleExperiments::faction_rules_off` (51 `FactionRule`s, all nine factions) turns one strength, drawback or goal off in the engine and in what the AI reads; `ablate.sh <faction>` runs control against each switch on the same 4,096 seeds (8-empire Hard, seed 9311003) and writes `docs/sim-results/faction-ablation/`. Control and arms cost about $0.15 each and 3 to 5 minutes on one spot server.
- **Germany (4,096 games per arm, win share per Germany seat, paired by seed):** Ruhr Foundry off +0.05 pp [-0.63, +0.74], 10% discount -0.38 [-1.34, +0.57], Industry projects -0.05 [-0.52, +0.41], Five Ruhr Foundries goal +0.14 [-0.79, +1.07], movement drawback off +3.42 [+2.22, +4.57] (6.35% to 9.77%). Four dead strengths; the one rule that moves the share is the drawback, and it moves it the wrong way for the design (Germany pays for 140 turns for nothing that arrives before then).
- **Why:** the median game ends at T183, Hard's Germany learns Industrialization at T141 (15% of seats never do), and the Foundry chain (Workshop, Forge, Foundry) comes after that; the goal scored in 5.5% of seats, first at a median T192.
- **A switch must live where the data function is, not at its callers.** `building_stats_for(faction, kind)` and the other faction-keyed data, combat and promotion functions take no state; 39 of them got a `*_rules(off, ...)` twin (the old name calls it with the empty set), and every call site that has a state or a view now calls the twin; the old names stay for tests, the UI projection and the frozen proxy AIs. With the set empty every pin, tape and census stays byte-identical (verify: no pin moved).
- **The set is a bit mask, not a `BTreeSet`:** `RuleExperiments` is `Copy` and passed by value in hundreds of places; a `BTreeSet` would have made every one a clone. `FactionRuleSet(u128)` iterates in declaration order and saves as a sorted name list (absent when empty).
- **Splits:** where the faction table lists one rule but the code has two mechanisms they are two switches (`swiss_cheap_buys`/`swiss_wonder_buys`, `celts_sack`/`celts_no_keep`). `celts_no_keep` off means the Celts keep cities and no sack happens at all.
- **Known confounds (AI, not rules):** faction-tuned genes (`industry_tech_bonus` for Germany, `swiss_playbook`, `aztecs_playbook`, Sparta/Mongols flavour in `production.rs`) are not switched; an arm measures the rule as the current AI plays it. Intervals are about +-1 to 2 pp at 4,096 games, so a rule worth under about 1 pp reads dead.
- How to apply: before redesigning or tuning a faction rule, run `ablate.sh` on it; redesign what reads dead (a strength that cannot be told from nothing) instead of nudging its number, and read a drawback that moves the share far more than any strength as a balance problem.
## 2026-11-05: The aircraft card is the unit card (branch `worktree-agent-a886dbddd197c0567`)
- **A look-alike card drifts; sharing the parts keeps it whole.** `AirWing` now renders `up-head`, `up-status`, `up-stats`, `Action`, `improve-row` and `up-hints` from the ground-unit card (`Action` and `UnitBadge` moved to `ui/UnitAction.tsx`, `ImproveRow` exported), so a later unit-card change reaches aircraft with no second edit. About 25 lines of `air-*` card CSS were deleted; only the picker rows (`air-target*`) remain.
- **Review fixes (same day):** a refused order's status is built from the refused order itself (the accepted-state headline said "Ready" over a refused rebase); an aircraft that still needs an answer says "Awaiting orders" in its status even when its job (on guard) is already clear; Hold (F) and Skip (Space) call the same `holdAir` when a target is in reach, so that card shows only one of them; every kind's hint handles the same four target states (hostile, at Peace, spared barbarians, none) through `noTargetReason`; Strength is `unitStatsFor(...).ranged`, the number the strike forecast uses.
- **Gotchas:** `Action` puts no space between label and key badge, so aircraft button text reads "Strike…S" (tests updated). The shared `.hint` rule is italic on every card, so "not italic" would need a change to the unit card's hints too. The `evidence save` e2e tests (8.9.2, 8.9.5) fail on current master with "saved by an earlier Playtest with older rules", unrelated to this card. Playwright wipes `test-results/` on every run, so copy shots out before starting the next spec.

## 2026-11-05: The Aztec playbook for Hard: his lean army is what the AI can use, his rush is not (gene `aztecs_playbook`, branch `worktree-agent-a7a2ea813003c346c`)
- **Hard can't follow his Aztec game from the opening: 0 to 3 of 64 from T2 in both 8.9.22 games, with or without the gene.** `seat_run` (new: Hard, with any params, in his seat from his recorded states; 8.9.22 states from `audit_states` at the tag decode at RULES_VERSION 12 with no migration) holds 2 to 3 cities to T150 against his 7 to 9 at T100. Its traces show why: no good free site within reach from T40-T60 (`sites_ok`), fresh unwalled colonies taken by neighbours at war, 9 to 12 units for 6 cities and the Pyramids in a two-city empire. His first cities came from neighbours (two sniped Settlers and Sarai by T36; India by T40).
- **His opening is the wrong lesson for the AI.** Sparta's early-prize bundle on the Aztec seats (melee first, assault wing, early campaign) took early captures from 9% to 36% of seats and cost cities and wins (-1.3 pp; -1.1 with Settler waves after T45). Target choice by softness, Settler weight, site floor and distance, the colony pipeline and a lower aggression knob were all null (one-arm screening, 4,096 AI-only games each).
- **The lean war army is the lever.** A conquest or containment war wants `cities + army_war_extra` (5) units. On the Aztec seats plus 2 gave +1.6 pp, plus 1 +2.9, plus 0 +2.7. With frontier Walls and the New Fire as the closer (gene 7), held-out seed 8241001: Aztec win rate 13.9% to 16.5% (+2.66 pp [+1.32, +4.00]); early military share 42% to 34%, cities lost 1.89 to 1.72, final VP 4.50 to 5.02. The Swiss playbook found the same direction from lean garrisons the same day: the shared war quota may be too big for everyone (follow-up).
- **The stock anticipatory defense builds an Archer before Walls in any city that sees a rival city within 12 hexes.** `walls_beat_arrival` takes "rival city distance / 2 + 3" as the arrival deadline when no army is in sight (no pop-1 colony's Walls beat it) and only runs after the opening's hand-off. A frontier "Walls first" placed after that branch never fired (the first census arm was identical to control): find which branch of `choose_item_inner` owns a city before adding one below it.
- **In-seat effects swing with the rivals.** The same gene in his seat: 60 to 57 of 384 runs before master's Swiss and Celtic playbooks landed, 55 to 76 (p 0.014) after. Trust the AI-only census for a faction gene; read seat runs as direction only.
- **Two `seat-run.sh` runs from one worktree deleted each other's inputs** (a shared `audit-in/` plus an EXIT trap): one run died reading its params. Inputs now go to a per-invocation folder.
- **A human-seat gate can be blind by construction:** no benchmark map seats the Aztecs, so an Aztec-only gene reads 470/470/470. Say so instead of calling it a pass on evidence.

## 2026-11-05: The AI Celts couldn't hold their own goal; fixing the Wild's execution is worth +2.8 points, garrisons and targets nothing (gene `celts_playbook`, branch `claude/celts-playbook`)
- **The AI Celts cleared their own warband camps.** Barbarians are at War with everyone, so every attack pass took a camp guard (10 HP from Celtic attrition) as a free kill and walked into the camp; `clear_barbarian_camps`' keep-one-per-city rule was never the only pass that decided. A tape trace showed the Celts clearing 1 to 5 of their own camps every turn after a call. With gene 0 the AI Celts hold Raise the Warbands 0.9 turns a game (1,024 games). Fix: drop the guards of camps in our own land from `Ctx::enemy_units` (`spare_own_camp_guards`). When a faction must keep a hostile object alive, filter it out of the target list, not just out of the pass meant to attack it.
- **The AI never re-called the Wild.** `wild_now` asked for a camp site in every city, but `rite_plan` lists sites only for cities without a camp, so after the first camp stood (or a natural camp sat in one city's land) the call never fired. The unit test's fixture listed a site for every city, which hid it. A view-driven decision needs fixtures built the way the core builds the view (`celts_real` in the tests).
- **Measured (4,096 paired 8-empire Hard games per arm and seed, two seeds):** part 2 (the two fixes, settling after a call, a window one VP short of the target) takes the AI Celts from 8.6% to 11.4% of games won (+2.8 pp [+1.9, +3.6]; +2.9 [+2.0, +3.8] on seed 9500001). Light garrisons (+0.2) and soft war targets add nothing: the AI Celts already left half their cities empty, the freed units stay in the field rather than becoming Settlers, and soft targets add wars and sacks, not wins. Light garrisoning is not punished either (cities lost 1.57 to 1.52).
- **His "don't trigger it too early" holds for the AI, his turn number does not.** Calling whenever the 2 VP drew no lead (median T12) cost 4.3 points (cities at T50 3.26 to 2.52); a fixed window at 45% of the game (T135, near his T127) measured worse than 70% (+2.1 against +3.2). The rite also places its own camps (farthest free tile), so "camps as a border shield" is not an AI choice: 54% face the nearest rival in every arm.
- **The human-seat benchmark can't see an AI-Celts change:** rivals-only was identical game for game (no benchmark rival is the Celts) and only the Celts map moved in seat-only (4 to 9 of 256). Gate faction-AI work on an AI-only league with the faction in the games.
- **Parallel `run-batch.sh` launches die polling** (exit 254 while waiting; one `MaxSpotInstanceCountExceeded`); sequential launches completed. Chain spot batches.
- How to apply: before tuning a faction's strategy, replay a few tapes and check the AI executes its kit at all (does the goal hold, does the action fire twice); here the mechanics bugs were the whole gain.

## 2026-11-04: Land train 1104 (fix-joint-assault, fix-settler-safety, ai-coherence-1103, ai-cheapest-objective on, diff-reseed-objectives)
- **Merge order and conflicts:** the five branches merged in that order onto master at RULES_VERSION 12. Conflicts were only the usual hotspots: `pins.json`, `game-00002-seed-18446744073709551615.json`, `chart.html` (taken from one side, regenerated from the merged ledger), `plans/ai-war-progress.txt` and `params.rs` (every gene kept), `ideas.md` rows 42 and 43 (joint-assault note and settler-safety status both kept) and `game-rules.md`. No fixture or scenario from any branch was at version 11 (`refresh-save-version-fixtures.py 11 12` refreshed 0 files).

Aztec Lab median 770 -> 840; AI-only era objectives scored 3,772 -> 4,002.
- **Pins moved by the train, regenerated once on the box (`tools/sim/repin.sh`):** `default_ai_tapes` 12389593954834913744 / 1635103556144716332 / 14527640447115903168 became 18257902493994209169 / 8744141792963456894 / 1638788786218131252, and `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` 2394086338275385916 became 5397539954008965849. Cause: the AI decisions changed (`finish_fallen` 50, `escort_buy` 90 and `snipe_read` 90, `sparta_valued_expedition`, `cheapest_objective` 1) plus the barbarian raider fix in `crates/core/src/rules/barbarians.rs`; `game-00002-seed-18446744073709551615.json` was regenerated with them. `rehash_scenarios` moved no hash (it only re-encodes `war-celts-wild-hold-t150`, reverted as noise); `rules.generated.json` and `garrison-current-hashes.json` did not change.

## 2026-11-03: The Celtic sack is 30 Gold per population and takes no technology; "Gain 2 cities" counted from the end of the entry turn (branch `claude/celts-sack-simplify`, RULES_VERSION 12)
- **The sack's take was two hidden numbers; now it is one visible one.** The old Gold, `population x 25 + floor(treasury / 4)`, could not be forecast (treasuries are hidden), and the technology steal fired in 0 of his 2 sacks and 12% of AI sacks. Now `economy::celts_sack_gold(population) = 30 x population`, the forecast shows it exactly, `sack_gold` is public in events, and the steal (`eras::sack_tech`, the AI's `sack_tech_value`) is deleted.
- **Measured before changing:** 512 balanced 8-empire Hard AI-only games (`legatum-sim --players 8 --difficulty hard --games 512 --seed 9410001 --tape-out`, tapes replayed with a throwaway splitter on the box): 1,297 Celtic sacks, mean 178.9 Gold = 155.6 population part + 23.3 treasury part, mean population 6.22, 28.75 Gold per population (29.7 before T100 falling to 27.8 after T200). 30 per population gives 186.6 (+4%); 25 would give 155.5 (-13%). Population is read from the turn before the sack and matched the event total exactly in 75% of rows, so the ratio is good to a few percent.
- **"Gain 2 cities" was not the net count.** Replay of his 8.9.25 save at `playtest-8.9.25` (tape, 159 turns, hashes match): he founded three cities in Modern, not four (T139 city 38, T140 city 40, T147 city 42), lost none, and the era scored at T147 on the third. The Modern era record was created at the end of T139, the turn research entered it, and its city base was read then (8 cities, city 38 already among them), so the T139 city was founded before the era existed for the count and did not count; the card showed 0/2 after he had founded one, 1/2 after the second founding, and scored on the third. No rule changed for this: the count is net (`cities_now - base`) but nothing was lost in his game. Same entry-turn effect applies to every `gain` objective, and scoring any era resets the base of every other open era (`objectives::restart_counting`); both are listed for the lead in the branch report.
- How to apply: when a player says an objective "took more than it said", replay the tape and print the era record's base next to the live count each turn before guessing at the rule.
## 2026-11-03: The Aztec trickle was the siege pass; stopping it saves units but takes no cities (genes `finish_fallen` on at 50, `joint_assault` off; branch `claude/fix-joint-assault`)
- **Find the pass before fixing the behaviour.** `decision_audit --diff-only --army-detail` on his tape names the planning pass of every AI order (the AI seats reproduce the recording). 35 of the 45 Aztec moves into his cities' strike range came from `siege.rs::siege_war`, which sends every free unit within eight hexes to a firing or screen tile at once, so the far ones arrive alone. It wasn't a per-unit attack pass pricing only its own shot.
- **The 0-HP city was abandoned, not out of reach.** On T93 the campaign was withdrawn by the second military pass while two full-HP Rangers stood two hexes from Colony 7. Running the city-assault coordinator on any city at half HP or less before withdrawals (`finish_fallen` 50) takes it in 32 of 32 runs with Hard in his seat (13 of 32 before). The later T153 case had nothing in reach.
- **"Together or not at all" mostly produced "not at all".** Gating the siege on a forecast of the group that can step in this turn (`joint_assault`) cut lone city-strike hits by 24% (Hard seat) and 41% (his orders), and units lost near his cities by a third. But against his recorded orders the Aztecs took 1.2 of his cities instead of 2.9: the trickle had been wearing down a defender who doesn't adapt. It also cost the Tepid Swiss T220 war scenario 23 points, and it leaned toward weaker rivals on the benchmark (+1.27 pp, not significant). Three things stop a massed assault from forming. The siege target can have too few reachable tiles in range: Bibracte's ring has three, yet the pass keeps choosing it. The army stalls in staging. And in the Lab scenario the war plan never brings the army at all: Byzantium plays Contain, then makes Peace at T137.
- **Branching his game with only the AIs' memory restored diverges at once.** The tape seat at salt 0 already loses two cities that he kept, because `OpponentMemory` doesn't carry the brain's strategy or caches. Compare arms against each other, never against the recorded game.
- **The attack regression check reads 0% at this branch's base** (`war-golden-horde-thin`, 0 of 64, and `war-golden-horde` 0% in the suite), below its 60% floor. That predates this branch.
## 2026-11-03: Settler capture priced by what the AI can see: right on his game, soft on the benchmark (branch claude/fix-settler-safety; `snipe_read` and `escort_buy` on, `capital_guard` off)
- **Germany's T13-T14 trip was defensible from its own view.** Its fixtures (his tape's AI seat views plus warmed memory, `crates/ai/src/fixtures/india-1103/`) show one Spartan, then one Indian Soldier in sight, and an escort beside the Settler. The decision diff's "go home" alternative was right only in hindsight (a human with a second Soldier in fog). The real execution bug came at T15: the duel escort pass skips a Settler whose own tile is unsafe, so the escort stood still while the Settler turned home beside an Indian Soldier. `snipe_read` now walks the escort home with it.
- **The Celts could not have seen India coming.** At T17 they had not met India and saw none of its units; the only public evidence was India's surprise war on Germany one turn earlier. The fix has to be a record read (a recent sole-declarer war makes a sniper, and unseen route flanks become a risk), not a better read of visible units.
- **Holding the capital's last troop for any rival troop in reach froze expansion.** In the Lab position `india-take-the-settler` a Spartan Soldier parked three hexes from Leipzig for twenty turns. The Settler stepped out and back, and the second Settler walked out alone and was taken: Germany lost a Settler in 54 of 200 runs (from 2) and had 1.87 cities at T36 (from 2.90). Now it takes two troops, a war or a sniper's troop. Check a defensive rule against a parked neighbour before trusting a fixture.
- **The flat capture prior is now clearly harmful.** Re-gated after `settler_stalk` made the benchmark seat snipe: with `settler_safety` 1 and `peace_settler_capture` 100, rivals-only was +3.12 pp [+0.83, +5.42] (softer) and lost more Settlers (0.48 to 0.55 a game); seat-only was -4.39 [-6.35, -2.54]. The 10-24 "softer" verdict holds more strongly.
- **On his game the new genes stop the gift: his own orders from T12 end T37 on 1 city instead of 4, and Germany lives.** On the benchmark (2,048 games) all three read rivals +1.17 pp [-0.98, +3.27] and rival T100 cities fell from 5.35 to 5.27. Run singly on his T12 orders, `snipe_read` alone keeps both Settlers (his T37 4 cities to 1) and `capital_guard` alone changes nothing, so `snipe_read` ships on (its benchmark cost, +0.63 pp and 5.35 to 5.28 cities, is within noise, and the benchmark seat rarely snipes) and `capital_guard` stays off. `escort_buy` (0.00 pp) is on but rarely fires: a 160-Gold troop is rarely affordable once an army is in sight, and Germany's windfall went on improvements at T2-T4, before it had seen any rival. A tape branch with reseed off and only AI salts gives one deterministic outcome; report it as one game, not 48/48.
- How to apply: the benchmark's Hard seat is not the sniper he is, so it cannot see a snipe defence. Split a bundle gene by gene on his game before deciding: one gene can carry the whole effect while another only costs.

## 2026-11-03: Coach review of the 8.9.25 Celts win: a hold on public camps that nobody clears, and a tape seat that needs his reflexes
- **A late tape seat has to repeat his reactive orders.** His recorded orders from T130 won only 20 of 32 runs when the AI seeds were salted. In the other 12, an AI cleared one warband camp during the hold, and the tape has no Call of the Wild on that turn. Adding the call he always made (`recall_wild` in the coach runner) gives 32/32. Any faction goal a player re-triggers (the Wild, a lapsed held goal) needs the same treatment before a tape branch is read as a loss.
- **The decision diff at its default end turn measured nothing in this game.** Hard in his seat wins 0 of 48 by T160 from all 30 positions, his T147 one included. Hard's Celts don't run his closing sequence, and a reseeded salt redraws the era objectives as well as the dice (`decision_audit.rs` overwrites `setup.seed`). Use `--turns ... --end-turn 240` and `--window 8` runs as well. The objective redraw is still open in both the diff tool and the what-if runner.
- **Celtic cities killed their own warband camp guards** (57 city shots and 28 unit attacks at barbarians; 18 kills in the two turns after the first call), so every goal camp stood empty from about T128. This is the known auto-fire bug, with a second cost: the Celts' own goal becomes trivial to clear. The AIs didn't clear the camps anyway. With their camp-clearing pass run first, his orders go 32/32 → 0/32 from T130 to T148. That is an upper bound; four neutral perturbations at T140 stay at 91–100%.
- **The Aztec AI was right not to kindle the New Fire at 7 VP.** Forcing it from T110 to T140 helped him (Hard seat 12 → 59% from T120), because 30 population cost them their crowns. "Bank permanent VP early" is not a universal rule; price what the rite costs in held VP.
- How to apply: in a review, count rival units within 3 tiles of each removable VP source on every hold turn, and branch with the rival's denial pass first, plus neutral perturbations as a fragility control.

## 2026-11-03: Barbarians spared the Celts one way only, so Celtic cities shot them and sleepers woke for them (branch claude/celts-barb-peace)
- **A one-directional rule leaks through every shared predicate.** The Celts rework (RULES_VERSION 11) made barbarians skip Celtic targets inside `barbarians.rs` and the movement round, but left `rules::hostile` / `at_war` alone. Everything automatic that asks "is that an enemy?" kept answering yes: the walled city's own shot (there is no city order, so it fired at any barbarian within 2 tiles), the garrison's `AutoFire` target, the Sleep/Heal wake-up, city heal blocking, the Threat lens and Settle advice (`known_threats`), the city-under-attack alarm and map ring, route danger, "wounded next to enemies", the "rival in sight" hint and the air-wing prompt (a rite camp stands in every Celtic city's land, so planes asked for a decision every turn). The designer saw the first two in playtest 8.9.25.
- **Fix: split "hostile" from "threat".** `rules::is_threat(state, observer, other)` and `PlayerView::is_threat` (hostile and not a barbarian toward the Celts) now guard everything automatic or advisory; attack orders, targets and manual shots still use `hostile`/`at_war`, so the Celts clear camps as before. The AI gets `Ctx::threat_units` beside `enemy_units` (targets): a missed site only leaves a Celtic AI over-cautious, never makes it walk into a barbarian. The app has `diplomacy.ts::isThreat`/`barbariansSpareMe`. When a rule exempts one side from another, add the matching predicate in the same change and grep every `at_war` / `hostile(` caller for the automatic ones.
- **Found by the audit, not the playtest:** `Ctx` Settler danger and remembered sightings, the scout's "Threatened" stop, `purchase_defense::city_attacks` (so a Celtic city "fell" to a barbarian and bought defenders), `modernization_foes`, `hollow_march` guard checks, and `heal_blocked_by_adjacent_enemy`. No `RULES_VERSION` bump: the city shot and wake-up change resolution but no save field, and 8.9.25 saves keep loading (a mid-game save replays the new rule from its next turn).
- Open rule questions left for the lead (not changed): a Celtic unit ordered onto a barbarian tile still attacks; route planning still treats a barbarian tile as a wall; barbarian tile yield denial stays; a Celtic walled city can no longer shoot a barbarian at all (no manual city strike exists).
## 2026-11-03: Hard spends the hoard on the decisive Bomber; where it is based decides its worth (branch claude/fix-spend-hoard, gene `decisive_buy`)
- **The reserve was keyed on a label.** At the 8.9.24 Swiss T140 Hard was at War with three empires with 4,358 Gold, Flight known and two Riflemen; its plan read Peace ("no second front"), so `hoard_spent_at_war` kept the 5,000 hoard as the reserve, and routine `plan_buy` never considers a military unit anyway. `decisive_buy` 1 prices a purchase by the capture odds it adds to VP-bearing enemy cities at war (Bombers strike in the siege forecast) against the VP the Gold puts at risk, and fires there.
- **A Bomber priced by VP captures alone picked the wrong base.** The first version bought it in Biel, whose range adds Khanbaliq's 4 VP at 10% odds: +0.12 VP over Hard by T161 (48 paired runs). His base, Shangdu, reaches Sarai, Karakorum and the units threatening Almaliq: +1.71 [+1.14, +2.27], better than his own T140 orders (+1.08). The plane's value is every strike it can make, not only the capture that justified buying it; the base is now picked by strike reach.
- **A benchmark can't see a late-war gene.** Level 1 was identical to the control in all 2,048 rivals-only and seat-only games and all 2,000 AI-only games: those games rarely reach a decisive Bomber. Land units too (level 2) read neutral (rivals +0.05 pp, seat -0.49). Ship a gene like this on its recorded decisive turn plus a benchmark that shows no harm, and say so.
- **A recorded seat view plus a warmed shadow memory is a cheap regression fixture** (`crates/ai/src/fixtures/swiss-1102/`): one throwaway example on the tag replays the tape with a Hard shadow brain in his seat and dumps the view and pre-plan memory at the turn; the view loads on master unchanged and reproduces the tag's decision.
- How to apply: when a purchase or unit decision differs by where it happens, value the placement with a T-turn branch (`decision-audit/run.sh --alt orders:FILE`), not just the decision.
## 2026-11-03: Hard walked Settlers off good land for threats the rules say cannot take them (gene `found_now`, on at 10; branch `claude/fix-found-now`)
- **The decisive "he founds, Hard routes" turns were mispriced threats, not site values.** Traced on the games' own tags: at 8.3 Tollkeepers T36 the only unit beside the Settler was a barbarian camp's Scout (it only wanders and reports), at 8.9.5 Signalwrights T58 a barbarian Soldier standing on its camp (the guard never leaves), at 8.3 T44 a Ranger of a rival at Peace. Hard's exposure and settler-danger maps count any reach as certain capture, so it retreated or went home. `found_now` prices capture per unit from the public rules (raider or empire at war still a veto, Scout 5, guard 0, Peace rival its surprise-war prior), keeps the search's border verdicts (Swiss 8.9.24 T145 still waits) and discounts the search's own choice by the chance a rival claims the land first. T36, T44 and T58 now found; T118 (two raiders in reach) and T137 (border risk, quality 31) do not.
- **Gate:** Lab `germany-found-by-the-scout` (8.9.23 T64, a barbarian Scout three tiles off) 111 to 200 of 200; benchmark rivals-only -0.39 pp [-2.00, +1.27], seat-only +0.34 [-0.98, +1.66]: right direction, within noise, so shipped on the "not weaker" bar. Known miss: Germany 8.9.23 T31 (at war, an army six tiles off, his found -0.54 VP) also founds now; the fresh-city loss looks one turn ahead.
- **Old games can be audited on master's engine when their states still decode:** `audit_states` on the recording tag, then `decision_audit --states` here. 8.9.23 and 8.9.24 decode; 8.3 to 8.9.15 do not (retired faction, title and building names), so gate those on the tag with the change ported. A state cut for the Lab from `audit_states` output needs `save_version` and a string seed stamped in (`parse_state` refuses it otherwise).
- **The first extended verify failed the archived Sparta 10-25 Kyoto test** (founding beside an army the search's `founding_survival_weight` forecast dooms): founding in place now charges that forecast too. Run the crate tests before the benchmark, as the strike-escort entry says.
- How to apply: before re-pricing a Settler decision, print what put the own tile out (exposure, danger, border, quality); several "risks" were rule facts the AI could read.

## 2026-11-02: Rebase at any distance (`claude/rebase-anywhere`, RULES_VERSION 11)

- **`data::REBASE_RANGE` is gone.** An aircraft rebases to any other own city with hangar room; the turn cost, the after-ranged-phase timing and hangar caps are unchanged. `rebaseOptions` and the AI's `air::coverage::rebase` now consider every own city (the AI still picks by value, and still skips endangered bases). The version is 11, the same number `claude/celts-rework` bumps to: the next train must reconcile the two (compare against the master it lands on, see the train-1102 entry).

## 2026-11-02: Integration train `claude/train-1102` for 8.9.24: two branches can each bump to the same RULES_VERSION

- **The bump collided.** `astra/walled-garrison-los` raised `RULES_VERSION` 8 to 9 on its own base while master's train-1101d also reached 9, so the merge kept 9 and the garrison rule change would have loaded and replayed the designer's 8.9.23 saves under new rules. The train bumps to 10 (`tools/sim/refresh-save-version-fixtures.py 9 10`, then `rehash_scenarios`, which re-encodes the embedded states without moving a hash, and `UPDATE_UI_RULES=1` for `rules.generated.json`). After merging a branch that bumps the version, compare its number with the master it lands on (`git show <branch>^:crates/core/src/lib.rs`), not only whether it changed. A decode of every tracked state afterwards: 70 at 10, the old refusal fixtures at 5 and 8, none at 9.
- **`git checkout --ours` on a conflicted file drops the other side's non-conflicting hunks too.** Resolving the `hard_vs_human.rs` snapshot conflict that way removed `claude/ai-expansion`'s `other_params` arm support; it was restored with `git diff <base> <branch> -- FILE | git apply -3` and the snapshot hunks resolved by hand. Use `--ours` only on generated files (pins, snapshots) whose other changes you have checked.
- **Every `Order` variant now round-trips through the tape** (`crates/wasm/src/tape.rs::every_order_kind_survives_a_tape_round_trip`). `variant_index` has no wildcard arm, so a new variant fails to compile until it has a sample. Removing one name from the loader's known list makes the test fail (checked).
- **Coalition package:** only `coalition_push` merged (see the progress log). On the merged train the four genes (`coalition_push`, `colony_compound`, `settler_escort`, `settler_stalk`) split as rivals-only -3.22 pp [-5.42, -1.12] (rivals clearly stronger) and seat-only +1.66 [-0.39, +3.66]; the all-seats train benchmark, 513 to 449, -3.13 [-5.27, -1.03], is that rival effect, not a weaker seat. Read an all-seats drop below the floor as role-mixed until a seat/rivals split says which. Receipts `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/train-1102-8.9.24/`.
- **UI review fixes.** The painted-aircraft plan views copied the Fighter miniature's US star insignia onto every faction's planes; repainted unmarked with a Codex image edit (the first fighter prompt was refused as "violence"; describing it as a scale model's decals passed). The Germany More doubled the unit stats once Industrialization was known (`itemCardText` ignored the flag); More now drops a first sentence that restates the card line (`moreText`); air-range labels treat the base city tile as occupied.
- **Verify:** the first extended run failed fmt and clippy (the cherry-picked `promo_scan` example, dropped from the train as media-pipeline code, and `.clone()` on a `Copy` item) and 24 Playwright timeouts while the UI reviewer's tour shared the box; the final run (`8701f6740`) passed with 2,125 Rust tests, 3,108 Vitest and 632 Playwright, 0 unexpected.

## 2026-11-01: Integration train `claude/train-1101d` (ten branches onto master `80ae6ad97`; rules version 9): the version bump reaches states the branches never touched

- **Order and conflicts.** Merged one at a time in the order given (military-focus, city-yields-visible, no-ink-construction, heal-order, path-reserve-wait, germany-five-foundries, capital-vp, tech-tree-rules, air-oil-ai, late-war-closer). The two UI branches merged clean in `CityPanel.tsx` (yields row, stop-build button and the summary-line amounts coexist). Conflicts were small: `legatum-ui/SKILL.md` (no-ink had an older copy of two military-focus bullets: kept its own "Removed" text and military-focus's newer two), `plans/ai-war-progress.txt` and `pins.json` (both kept or taken, repinned later), `plan.rs` (capital-vp and germany both rewrote the capture swing: the sum is `conquest + goal_taken + capital_capture_vp` and `t.vp -= own_goal_delay`, because Germany's branch had replaced `own_goal_lost`), `data.rs` (tech-tree's new `RAILWAYS_ROADWORKS_RANGE` plus Germany's `GERMANY_GOAL_CITIES = 5`), `supply_value.rs` (air-oil's idle-aircraft Oil demand plus tech-tree's Fusion Power building filter), `game-rules.md` (Stealth Silicon and the three-plane hangar text kept with the new Oil entry) and the `rival_search_is_the_old_benchmark` comment in `hard_vs_human.rs` four times (the snapshot itself passed unchanged on the merged code). The first merge commit lacks the Co-Authored-By trailer (the harness would not let me redo it).
- **Scenario states: take the branch that changed the blob, then fix the version inside it.** tech-tree renamed `QuantumComputing` to `StealthTechnology` inside nine embedded scenario states while germany re-versioned every scenario, so all nine conflicted in the base64 blob. Taking tech-tree's blob and setting `save_version` to 9 in the decoded JSON, then `rehash_scenarios`, was right: the state hash does not cover `save_version`, so no hash moved except one. A decode of every tracked JSON/gz state showed two Scenario Lab cuts the coach had added on master (`athens-break-the-hold`, `swiss-hold-the-south`) still at 8: `scenarios/lab/` is not read by the `wasm` scenario test (only `scenarios/` and `war/`), so nothing failed, but the Lab lists them. After a bump, scan every embedded state (`grep -l save_version` misses the gz and base64 ones), not only the files the branches touched. Left alone as refusal evidence or pre-existing: the version 5 postgame and archived AI states, `sparta-colony11-t186.json`.
- **Capital VP broke one late-capture test, not an AI.** In the `late_view` fixture the target city 2 is the rival's original capital, so at nine VP the capture scores the tenth point and starts a hold; the late-war-closer's "elimination path" term (only while a capture leaves us short of a hold) no longer fired. The test now prices the rival's second city. Nothing else in 2,117 Rust tests moved except pins.

Vitest snapshots needed no update (3,065 passed, 2 skipped).

Celts 8.4 8 to 42 wins (+13.28 [+8.20, +18.36]) and Switzerland 8.5 29 to 10 (-7.42 [-12.11, -3.12]); not attributed to a branch. 64-game Hard smoke (seed 8320000): 0 panics, 0 stalls, mean length 227.3 to 203.1 turns, capital VP 113 in 91 seats (Sparta 45), Germany goal awarded in 4 seats against 1, Aztec goal 21 to 10 and Sparta goal 12 to 3 (not investigated).
## 2026-11-01: Coach review of the second 8.9.22 Aztecs win: "easy" was the AI, and sight alone doesn't make a war

- **A faction signature can look decisive and be nearly neutral.** He won T153, 11 VP to 4, with 97 population against 43, yet Flower Wars gave him 13 population all game. Switched off from T90 (a core flag gated on `state.turn >= T`), his recorded orders still win on the same turn; in 192 AI-only games the AI Aztecs win 17.0% with it and 15.8% without (even share 12.5%). Measure a signature by switching it off from the deciding turn and in AI-only games before proposing balance; his lead was cities (14 against 7) with 3–6 military units.
- **Seeing the leader is necessary, not sufficient.** Unlike the morning game, Athens, Sparta and the Mongols knew 4–7 of his cities 4–6 tiles away, and giving every rival the whole map changed nothing (Hard in his seat from T90 23 → 27 of 32). Athens's Contain sat at +650 to +1,270 for 70 turns with 4–6 military units; both campaigns ended in 5–8 turns (`insufficient_army`, `infeasible`). The binding failure is that a Contain plan never grows the army its campaign needs.
- **Of the three Codex branches, only `astra/coalition-push` moved this game:** Hard in his seat from T90 23 → 12 of 32 (`see-the-leader` 16, `war-era-resources` 21, master 23); nothing moved T112 (30–31). Core is unchanged from the tag to master, so the tag tape warms hash-exact on any AI branch; his tape seat breaks on every branch (237–442 failed orders), so only the Hard seat measures them.
- **A tape seat after a VP source is removed reads 0/32 for a reason that isn't a loss:** without the New Fire his orders reach 8 VP by T153, where the tape ends and the seat goes passive. Hard in his seat without the New Fire wins as often (31/32 from T112), two turns later.

## 2026-11-01: Coach review of the 8.9.22 Aztecs win: a war the AI can't see is worth zero, and buildings eat the war resource

- **An AI that knows none of the leader's cities values war on him at 0, whatever its army.** `plan.rs::contain_candidates` scales both "VP denied" and "leader's race" by reach (`15 − distance`, where `distance` is the nearest leader city or unit in the planner's view, else 99). A debug print of the view showed Athens held 0 Aztec cities at every traced turn T100–T180 (army 2,152, him top threat 145 → 622). Its war on him was worth 0 or −1 every turn, even during his hold. The Celts burned the only two Aztec towns they had seen (T138, T148), their view then held none, and Contain fell to −260. Their plan was "Peace with the Aztecs" for 17 turns while his army was in Switzerland. It came back (+1,494) the turn his units walked into view. Fix direction: scouting the leader, not a new war term (review rec 1).
- **`contain_after_campaign` is not the fix for this fallback.** The war was already among the candidates, valued −260. The gene (Celts only, or every AI) changed nothing from T100, T115 or T125 (Hard seat 18/24/32 → 18/24/31).
- **The race term dies with our own chance.** `leader_race` multiplies by our own win chance, so a 0%-chance AI (Athens from T150, the Celts from T176) gives 0 for stopping a 99% leader. Whether a hopeless AI should play kingmaker is a design question for the designer.
- **Obsolete armies come from era resources.** The Swiss put their Niter into three Banks during their war with him and then bought 9 Spearmen and 2 Horsemen; Germany held no strategic resource all game; Byzantium spent all of its on buildings. He kept the current era's Coal for units and put the old era's Niter in Banks.
- **Decadence off moved the balance.** With bands at 0% this game, the removed rule (slope 45) put back from T115 cuts Hard in his seat 24 → 13 of 32. That is the largest lever in the review; the best AI-only change (all five rivals at War from T100, no Peace) is 18 → 12.
- **Method:** a core switch gated on `state.turn >= T` (here `COACH_CELTS_KEEP_FROM`) gives a mid-game rule counterfactual whose warm replay stays exact before T. Without it, the Celts keeping their captures from T125 lets them win 7/32 against a Hard seat. Unit names in reviews must be the display names (`data.rs`): `Soldier` is a Spearman and `Ranger` an Archer.

## 2026-11-01: Integration train `claude/train-1102` (nine Codex branches onto the train-1101 master): the duel stall is the world-wonder rule, not the outrider branch

- **Order and conflicts.** `celts-tour-flake` merged clean. `ui-trims-1031` conflicted in `store.ts` and `CityPanel.tsx` because master had added the save rules-version fields: kept master's, dropped the unseen-unlock state and two imports the branch no longer uses. `economy-trims-1031` conflicted in the diplomacy files: master had removed `war_calls`, the branch removed the signing fee, so the no-fee side won everywhere, the four coalition free-alliance tests went with the fee, and `game-rules.md` took the branch's alliance text with master's India row (one 10%-per-hex corruption rule). `simplify-rules-1031` (17 commits) was the big one: master's no-grandfathering removed `Unit::grandfathered`, `Aircraft::grandfathered` and the old `unit_upgrade_need` while the branch added upgrade steps and hangar-aircraft army value, so `army.rs` (auto-merged, still reading `grandfathered`) and `resources.rs::unit_upgrade_need_to` were rewritten without it; the Mongol slip-past (deleted by the trims) came back in `reach.ts` and the Guide and was dropped again, while the branch's allied-unit transit rule was kept on top of master's reach code. `capture-and-peace-clarity` conflicted only in an import (master dropped `overreachWarning`). `outrider-escape` conflicted only in generated files. `world-wonders-1031` had 23 conflicted files because master had deleted the milestone machinery the branch still renamed ("Great Work" to "world wonder"): master's structure won with the branch's names and rules (the seven wonders, `isGreatWork`, the race refund, known builders), the branch's raze block on wonder cities and `milestone_name` were dropped (S6 allows razing them), and Fusion Power now gives both its +5 Production in every city (S5) and the ITER wonder. `retake-hold` conflicted only in `game-rules.md` (kept both entries). `objective-deck-1031` conflicted in four UI files and one test file.
- **The captured-city victory gate is out.** Removed from `scoring.rs` (the 10-turn hold and the turn-limit ranking), `PlayerPublic.captured_city`, the AI's `needs_capture` and `urgent` handling, the race panel and the Guide, and `game-rules.md`; the deck test now asserts a hold starts with zero captures. The zero-capture-winner row stays in `tools/sim/objective-deck-report.py`; the branch's report (0% zero-capture winners) was measured with the gate and says so at the top.
- **What silently broke after clean or half-clean merges.** `GreatWorkView` gained `production_per_turn` (two AI test literals), the endgame test cleared a milestone that no longer exists (it must call `set_work_completed(false)` instead), `TreatyActivated` lost `signing_cost` (two TS test literals), `VpModel.overreach` became `raceRules` (`vpParts.tsx`), and `cargo check --workspace --all-targets` was green at every step from the second merge on. The first `--app` verify caught all of these plus 20 `purchases.wasm` full-tree snapshots: city food 20 to 22 (Code of Laws now reaches existing cities) and a +1 Production Pyramids row.
- **Generated files, regenerated once on the box after the last merge** (same commands as the train-1031 entry): `rules.generated.json` (only the removed `outriderReason` line), `rehash_scenarios` (no scenario hash moved), `garrison-current-hashes.json`, `tools/sim/repin.sh` (the three mixed fixtures, `city-strike-awareness.json`, `diplomacy-alliance.json`, `pins.json`). Moved pins against master: `default_ai_tapes` 3949770044240811493 / 14515071654567630563 / 443221552828584113 became 1098388342243405451 / 2147920703664837657 / 15463178379203522454, `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` 1102315824648781614 became 7544826870747949932. Other stale expectations: the `rival_search_is_the_old_benchmark` first-map snapshot (now a T294 VP-hold win with 2 VP at T100), and `session::only_a_hard_seat_with_the_flag_plays_search...` (the search seat no longer searches inside 150 turns of its duel; it passes at 500, taking about 100 s unoptimised).
- **One gate is not green: `hold_metrics::standard_hard_duel_holds_are_consistent_and_never_stall`.** Duel game index 5 (seed 6, Celts against Switzerland) ends at the 1,200-turn cap with Celts 9 VP, Switzerland 7.
