Bisected by commit on the box: the test passes at the `outrider-escape` merge (`74422f466`, so the branch's own gate failure was an interaction that its base did not have) and fails from the `world-wonders-1031` merge on (`ada8991b1`, `c0e253e67`, `dc67b8331`, `e9e91e24`). The trace: Switzerland completes the Pyramids on T73, the Celts burn its city on T182 (`GreatWorkLost`), and the world-wonder rule ("completion permanently uses the builder's one, even after capture or destruction") means Switzerland can never hold a wonder again; the Celts hold Hagia Sophia (2 VP) and their own Wild goal flaps (completed T193, lost T195). The Celts sit at 9 VP for 900 turns missing only the era 4 objective (a Swiss city count and a capture from the VP leader are not things a sacking faction does) while Switzerland resettles and the Celts keep burning its new cities (20 razings after T800). It is the same family as the stall-1101 9-VP lock (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/stall-1101.md` on `astra/stall-1101`): fewer repeatable VP sources, and a duel is the worst case for the seven-wonder, one-per-empire cap. The 8-empire benchmark did not get worse (turn-cap games 2 to 1). Not fixed: the options are a design call (restore the one-per-empire allowance when its wonder is destroyed, the repeatable Future-era project stall-1101 proposes, or accept a rare duel stall and loosen this test).
- **Combined human-seat benchmark** (seed 310001, 256 salts x 8 maps = 2,048 games per binary, `LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`, plain Hard rivals, one all-seats `ctl` arm, paired bootstrap over salts; receipts and per-map table in `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/train-1102/`): master 496 (24.2%), train **463 (22.6%): -1.61 pp [-3.66, +0.44]**. Per map: Switzerland 8.3 -9.8 [-17.2, -2.0], Celts 8.4 -5.5 [-10.2, -0.8], India map -3.5 [-9.0, +2.0], Switzerland 8.5 +6.6 [+2.0, +11.3], the other four within noise. Turn-cap games 2 (master) and 1 (train). The branches were not benchmarked one by one, so the per-map swings are not attributed. (The spot quota is 256 vCPUs and the build box holds 64, so the arms ran on one server each.)
- **64-game all-faction Hard smoke** (seed 8320000): 0 panics, 0 stalls, 64 VP-hold wins, games of 150 to 281 turns (mean 220), mean cities / VP per faction: Athens 5.25 / 5.25, Aztecs 5.55 / 4.70, Byzantium 4.24 / 4.78, Celts 4.93 / 3.52 (0 wins in 60 seats), Germany 4.67 / 5.09, India 7.34 / 5.74, Mongols 5.64 / 5.00, Sparta 8.29 / 4.71, Switzerland 3.91 / 4.67. Wonder completions: Pyramids 64, Hagia Sophia 64, Great Library 64, Eiffel Tower 36, Grand Bazaar 34, Royal Arsenal 26, ITER 13; no game completed one wonder twice and no empire completed two.
- **The Mongol Shangdu turn-44 case from `capture-and-peace-clarity` holds with the Mongol trims:** the branch's city test only used plain Soldiers, so it now also runs with a Mongol garrison at 28 and 100 HP (`zero_hp_city_is_captured_when_garrison_leaves_three_claimants`); one of three claimants takes the city in all four cases.
- **Verify (extended, `--app`) on the final train:** fmt, clippy, repin (no pins diff), WASM, app build, 3,026 Vitest (2 skipped), and Playwright 603 expected, 26 skipped, 0 unexpected, 0 flaky (none of the known load flakes appeared, so there were no solo reruns) all passed; Rust 2,085 of 2,086 (the duel above).

## 2026-11-01: with seven eras, an empire that captures nothing tops out at exactly the 10 VP target, so a stalemate has no exit
The VP ceiling for an empire that captures nothing equals the 10 VP target: six era points, a 2 VP faction goal and one 2 VP world wonder (one completion per empire). Miss one era point, or lose the wonder to capture or razing, and the only way to 10 is capturing someone else's wonder or goal city. When the wars stalemate the game never ends: train-1102 duel seed 6 (Celts 9, Switzerland 7, about 900 turns at 9 VP) and the stall-1101 eight-empire games (seeds 310146 and 310190). This is how "no win without conquest" is achieved, and also why a stalemate has no exit. A backstop is a design call for the designer (asked 2026-11-01).

## 2026-11-01: train-1103 (faction-trims, ui-fixes-1101, hide-never-available on train-1102)
- **Verify merges the branch onto the current master snapshot, so a progress-log line added to master while the train was held broke the merge in 2 seconds** (`plans/ai-war-progress.txt`). Merge master into the held train before verifying it, not after.
- **Only faction-trims moves pins.** On master's base it moves default tapes 0 and 1 and the city-fire tape; tape 2 stays at train-1102's value. ui-fixes-1101 and hide-never-available are app-only.
- **Germany's new goal is nearly unreachable for Hard.** In the 64-game smoke Germany built Ruhr Foundries in 33 of 55 seats (mean 1.6 against 4.7 cities) but held the goal in one (the 240 Production Engine: 34). The Foundry needs Industrialization, Iron and Coal, and costs 440. Mean Germany VP 5.09 to 4.07. A design question, not tuned here.
- **The duel stall moved, it was not fixed.** The 8-duel hold test passes on train-1103 (the Celts v Switzerland game index 5 ends on turn 417 instead of stalling at 1,200), but the same game is nearly as long, so the missing exit for a stalemate remains.
- **Benchmark:** seed 310001, 2,048 games per arm: train-1102 463, train-1103 487 (+1.17 pp [-0.24, +2.54]); Germany 8.6 +7.8 [+1.6, +13.7], Celts 8.4 -3.9 [-7.0, -0.8]. Receipts in `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/train-1103/`.

## 2026-11-01: ui-fix-1103 (the train-1103 UI review findings)
- **Sparta's desertion clock is a 5-turn cycle, not 10.** `restless_desertion_in` is 1 to 5 (`rules::SPARTA_DESERTION_INTERVAL`); the 10 is the battle window that pauses it (`SPARTA_BATTLE_WINDOW`). The race panel's clock is five pips ("3 of 5 turns left") with the 10-turn rule in its tooltip.
- **Era objective text is public and shared** (`era_views` is built once for every viewer), so a faction's own building name for it ("Ruhr Foundry", "Lyceum") is applied in the app by `factionObjectiveText`, in the two places that read it (`eraObjectives.ts`, `vpModel.ts`). Public report lines keep the standard name.
- **"To keep the old file, use Load Game first" is false.** Starting a new game writes the autosave at once (`startGame` calls `writeAutosave`), and Load Game refuses an older-rules file too, so nothing keeps it. A file chosen with Load Game is only read, so that refusal says the file is not changed.
- **The build-list name overflow was the tooltip's info button.** A card with a rich tooltip gets a 26 px "ⓘ" beside it (`.tip-trigger:has(> .tip-info)`), leaving "Roadworks" and "Scriptorium" 58 px. The button is now 24 px with a 2 px gap, the art column gap is 6 px and "Build →" has no minimum width; the world wonders drawer lost its extra 20 px side margins, so its cards are as wide as every other card. `e2e/tour/ui-fix-1103.spec.ts` asserts no card name has `scrollWidth > clientWidth`.
- **The T203 save and both post-game fixtures were recorded before "one wonder per world".** The save showed the Great Library held by four empires and the post-game timelines had seven empires completing it. They are re-staged (the first completion kept); other fixtures still holding it twice, such as `p893-unseen-attacker`, are not shot.
## 2026-11-01: wonder art (claude/wonder-art-1103)
- **Codex image generation, not Gemini, makes the map paintings.** No `GEMINI_API_KEY` is set on the Mac; `codex exec -i REF.png ... -` (prompt on stdin, `-i` swallows a trailing positional prompt) with the built-in image tool returned 1254 px images in about three minutes for 16 jobs at once. A flat magenta field keys cleanly to alpha (`tools/art/wonder-art.py`); the soft glow the generator paints round the patch has to be eroded or it leaves a purple rim.
- **Stages must reuse the master's atlas-edit geometry.** `atlas_edit` fades the ground from the image's own bounding box, so a flat footings stage faded differently from its master and the patch edge jumped; passing the master's box fixed it (`atlas_edit(img, box)`).
- **A fixture with seven wonders in one city is unreadable:** each painting is about three hexes wide. Space sites three hexes apart (outer ring, every third tile) and judge the seven in two groups.
- **Vitest's transform cache hides new art files.** `import.meta.glob` in `unlockArt.ts` was cached by that file's content in the box slot's `node_modules/.vitest-cache`, so the four new wonders were "missing" until the file itself changed. When art files are added, edit the module that globs them (a comment is enough).
- **The 64 px distinctness bar caught three look-alike wonders** (Hagia Sophia, ITER, Royal Arsenal at 0.67 to 0.71 against 0.66). A colour grade per piece fixed it without touching the bar.


## 2026-11-01: art that changes a card's tile count needs the screen tour, not just verify
- `verify-remote.sh --profile extended --app` runs every Playwright spec except the screen tour. The wonder-art branch painted Hagia Sophia, so the Education unlock card had two `.unlock-tile.building .unlock-art` images. The tour's single-element locator hit a strict-mode violation, and only the release check (which runs the tour) caught it, at both sizes. Both feeding branches had passed verify, and the art agent ran only its own tour spec.
- How to apply: a branch that adds or replaces art on cards, map pieces or the Guide runs the full screen tour (`npx playwright test e2e/tour`) before merging. Tour locators that pick an art tile name the item (`filter({ hasText })`), never "the only one".

## 2026-11-01: renaming an enum breaks every reader of old records, including the archive index (branch claude/archive-index)
After winning a game the designer got "invalid index line: unknown variant `Tollkeepers`". `~/LegatumData/archive/index.jsonl` holds every finished game's metadata (594 lines on his Mac, most from September under the old faction names Tollkeepers, Choir, Remnant, Veyr, Crucible, Glassroad, Oathbound, Signalwrights). `Index::refresh` deserialized each whole line as `Metadata` only to collect `game_id`, so the first old line failed the new game's commit and the game was not archived. The index now reads just `game_id` from a JSON value. Lesson: a reader that needs one field must not parse the whole record, and any enum rename (factions were renamed to real peoples 2026-10-10) needs a grep for every place old serialized data is read back: archives, index files and tapes, not only saves (saves are refused by `RULES_VERSION` on purpose).

## 2026-11-01: a rivals-only benchmark arm only splits by seat what a gene switches
- `quality_gate.py --arms` switches parameter sets, not compiled code. If an AI change isn't behind a gene, a "rivals-only" arm still runs the candidate's logic in the human seat, so it can't isolate rival strength (Codex refused `rivals-arm-1101` for this reason). Even with a gene, the arm needs an explicit seat override, or the builtin fallback makes it identical to all-seats (`defend-goal-objects`).
- Results: `contain-builds-army` rivals-only 484 → 439 seat wins (rivals +2.2 pp, interval includes zero), yet on the designer's Aztec save Athens attacked him less (T68 262 → 224). `defend-goal-objects` made rivals weaker (489 → 525), and the Heir was still captured 13/32, so it stays off.
- How to apply: every AI change ships behind a gene (default on) so its rival strength can be measured; a rivals-only run always passes the seat override.
## 2026-11-01: a city build focus must build its own kind first, not just weight it (branch claude/military-focus)
The designer set a new capital to Military automation and it built a Granary, then a Farm. `automated_choice` multiplied military unit value by four but kept every other option in the race, and also dropped any option Hard valued at 0 or less. Hard sees almost no value in one more unit in peacetime at population 1-2, so the economy pick won (and the panel said "worth more than Military builds"). Science and Gold already excluded units, so Military was the odd one out. Now every focus picks among its own kind whenever one can start (favored items get a value floor of 1), and other items fill in only when none can (for Military: no unit's upkeep can be funded, `troop_unfunded`). Hard's own production does not use this path, so no sim or pin changes. A player-set focus is an instruction, not a weight: the AI chooser's judgement belongs inside the category the player picked.
## 2026-11-01: no ink construction (claude/no-ink-construction)
- **A canvas-drawn object beside painted art is a blocker, even when it is an "effect".** The designer saw the wonder-construction crane (`constructionFx.ts`: an ink A-frame, jib, cable and load box, 1.04 sizes to the right of the footprint so it floated over the neighbouring hex) and asked "i thought we fixed all the simple drawings?". Removed with it: the ink workers, the scaffold ticks, the dashed string line and corner stakes of the plot, the block and timber piles, the diagonal ramp, the dust arcs, the Walls' dashed ring and ink post, the founding moment's six surveyor stakes and the pen-and-quill nib. A site is now only the painted stage (1 to 3), then the finished landmark; Walls are only the painted overlay clipped by angle. Under 10% progress a wonder site shows nothing on the tile (the tile still carries its chosen-tile ring and the city banner its production meter); a painted "cleared plot" stage would be new art.
- **How it slipped through:** the 2026-10-24 construction proposal specified "drawn in ink over the stage art" furniture, and the art pass that replaced the simple drawings had already finished by then. A feature added after an art pass needs the same art bar as the pass. Borders, rings, arrows, meters and text are UI; a building, person, machine or tool drawn from strokes is not.
- **An unreachable fallback is still a line drawing:** `terrain.ts` kept an ink rampart (`drawWall`) for a city with no wall art at all; every era has painted walls, so it could never run. Deleted. Grep for `else if (!stem)`-style fallbacks when an art set completes.
- **The tests could not see any of this.** The old test asserted the crane's x position. The new ones record every canvas call for each stage and fail on any stroke or line call (they are weak where the sprites have not loaded, so the screenshot spec `e2e/no-ink-construction.spec.ts` is the real check).

## 2026-11-01: Heal is back as a flag on Sleep, so old tapes stay byte-identical
- The designer: "heals the unit until it's healed then brings them back into the action queue. i find that I 'f' them and then i forget they exist." Fortify heals too (any resting unit does), but a sleeper stays asleep at full HP and never returns, so a healed unit vanished from the queue. The fix is the old Heal's one real feature, waking at full HP, not a second way to rest.
- Shape: `Order::SleepUnit { unit, until_healed }` (flag skipped when false) plus `StandingOrder::Heal`, not a restored `HealUnit`. Old tapes wrote `HealUnit`; `orders.rs` and `wasm/src/tape.rs` read it as plain `SleepUnit`, which stays correct only because no new order owns that name. A new `HealUnit` order would have changed what every old tape replays as; the flag changes nothing for them (`heal_orders_leave_tapes_and_saves_unchanged`, `a_heal_order_survives_a_tape_round_trip...`). Adding the flag meant `..` in about a dozen `Order::SleepUnit { unit }` patterns across `ai`, `sim` and `wasm`.
- `wake_healed` is its own function after `heal_and_fortify` (called in `resolve_turn`, `resolve_tactical_turn` and the test harness) so `heal_and_fortify`'s signature, used by many tests, did not change again.
- The queue reason ("healed to full, needs orders") comes from the last resolution's `UnitHealed` event in the store's `report`, passed through `pendingDecisions`'s `local` argument; surfaces that build their own `local` (SeatBar, the End Turn review) pass `seat` and `report`, the others fall back to "needs orders". A save loaded mid-turn has no report, so the reason is lost but the unit is still in the queue with full HP.
- Heal is hidden at full HP (a disabled button on every healthy unit costs a row of space at 1000x600 for nothing); where a damaged unit heals nothing (closed foreign land) it is off and the reason sits under the row, as the 7b review demanded.
- Known gap: a Heal unit that a border change strands in closed land keeps its order and heals 0; `check_heal` only guards the moment the order is given.
- No `RULES_VERSION` bump: the change is additive, a v8 save loads and plays identically, and a bump means rewriting about 50 fixtures (saves, AI archives, scenario states, tapes) and re-pinning, which earlier trains batched into one bump. A first attempt at 9 failed `legatum-wasm` fixture loads and the app's `rules.generated.json` fixtures at once. Fold this into the next train's bump.

## 2026-11-01: UI review fixes on train-1101d (ledger, net Food, active order, capital card)
- The score ledger overflowed at 1000x600 because one row broke the row contract: a hand-written `<tr>` with a two-line label, no chevron and a heading ("Kept for good · +2 each · earned faction goals") that was false for it. Rows now come from `rowsOf` with `kind: 'capital'`, so every row shares the contract and the `score-ledger` tour's closed-ledger fit check covers it. A tour spec that fails loses all its later shots; read the missing-shot list first.
- A hover or tooltip that must list sources uses `Tip`'s `summary` prop (`CityYieldHover`), not its content: content opens on click, the summary is what a hover shows. `title` is not read on touch and was why the stop button had no tip on the box.
- `.game .refined-unit-panel .act-pair .act` (specificity 0,4,0, or 0,5,0 with `:last-child`) overrode `.act.active` (0,2,0), so Heal looked identical pressed and idle. The fix selector has to out-rank the pair rule (six classes); a computed-style assertion in the tour spec is what catches this, since `aria-pressed` was correct all along.
- The core's "cannot produce" refusal is left as is (about 40 wasm snapshots and tests pin it). The player's wording is built at the toast (`plainOrderError` in `format.ts`), where the unit's kind and the levy numbers are already in the message. Core refusals that named raw ids ("unit 7 already has an order") now name the unit kind or say "That unit is not yours" (a rival's unit kind is not given away).
- Tech effect lines come from core as data (`data.rs`) and are reworded once in `factionEffectLabel` (`data.ts`): "per Granary family" to "for each Granary and Mill", the viewer's own factory name, the true minus. Art lookup (`unlockArt.ts`) now keys on the rewritten text, so a Railways card's three rules get three paintings.
- Every city can now be razed, original capitals included (designer, 2026-11-01). The captor scores its first-capture point before it can raze (a captured city cannot be razed on the turn of capture), so razing never takes a point away; it denies rivals theirs. Hard AI prices that denial in `raze::rival_capital_vp` (1 VP while some empire off our team and not the founder has not scored it) on both the `raze_value` and the old pressure path.
- Razing an original capital moved one pin: `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` in `crates/sim/tests/pins.json` (the scripted Hard tape razes a captured capital now that the order is legal and priced). Repinned with `tools/sim/repin.sh` on the box; the three mixed tapes and the other runner pins did not move.
- Merging master into the train after 80ae6ad97 brings `scenarios/lab/athens-empty-capital.json` at rules version 8: `scenarios.wasm.test.ts` fails with "incompatible rules version: saved Some(8), current 9" until it is re-encoded with `rehash_scenarios` (run it on any scenario file added to master before the bump lands).
## 2026-11-01: placeholder art slugs shipped because nothing compared art across unit kinds (claude/future-unit-art)
- **What happened:** `unitArt.ts` mapped the new Future kinds to existing art (`HunterKiller: 'infantry'`, `StealthFighter: 'fighter'`, `StealthBomber: 'bomber'`) and `data.ts` `unitIconName` did the same for icons, with a comment "until their art pass". `icons.test.ts` even excluded the three kinds from its distinct-icon check, so every test passed while the Robotics card showed a doughboy. Fix: the art, and `unitArt.distinct.test.ts` (two kinds sharing a slug fail unless whitelisted with a reason, plus a whole-file-set check per slug). The icon test no longer has an exclusion list.
- **A new `.svg` in `app/src/assets/icons` does not appear in the box's vitest run until `icons.ts` changes** (the glob is read at transform time and the box shares a transform cache, so the old file list is served; `icons.test.ts` then fails with "expected null not to be null"). `icons.ts`'s header comment already carried this note; add the new unit names to it, as the wonder branch did.
- **`key_magenta` erodes thin parts.** It pulls the alpha edge in about 4 px at 1254 px, which turns a 14 px antenna into a stray speck near the top of the 256 master. Prompt for no antennas, whips or other thin protrusions; check the 256 master's top rows for isolated opaque pixels.
- **The distinctness metric compares the display stand.** Aircraft on the same stand score about 0.6 (colour bar 0.60) and 0.76 to 0.78 on silhouette (bar 0.77) whatever the aeroplane looks like; the Stealth Bomber pairs with the olive Bomber and Fighter are accepted with that reason rather than repainted on a different stand (aircraft never stand on the map).
- **Codex image generation works headless for art:** `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check -m gpt-6-luna -i reference.png - < prompt.txt` writes the image to `~/.codex/generated_images/<session id>/`; the session id is on the log's `session id:` line, so runs can go in parallel. About a minute each, and the first Hunter-Killer render was usable.

## 2026-11-01: painted placeholders (claude/painted-placeholders)
- **Barbarians field six kinds, not two.** `rules::barbarians::unit_kinds` steps melee Spearman → Pikeman (Feudalism) → Musketman (Gunpowder) and ranged Archer → Crossbowman (Machinery) once half the living empires know the technology, and every camp spawns a Scout. Each now has a painted raider (`assets/units/barbarian-*`); any other kind (a Company that turned barbarian) keeps its ordinary figure on the barbarian base (`barbarianArtSlug`).
- **The distinctness check catches a new figure that copies a neighbour's pose, even across owners.** The first barbarian Musketman (upright, gun held diagonally) scored gist 0.85 against the Rifleman and 0.79 against the Arquebusier and was repainted kneeling to fire; the kneeling figure then met the Cataphract on silhouette alone (gist 0.79, colour 0.28) and that pair was accepted. Two more pairs were accepted with reasons in `tools/art/distinctness.json`: the barbarian Archer against the Archer (the same unit) and the barbarian Crossbowman against Infantry (only at the gist bar, 0.77). Run `python3 tools/art/distinctness.py --render --check` on the box (it needs the app's Playwright Chromium) for any new figure.
- **`stageImage` upscaled the Walls stages.** Its 129–256 px order tried `-256` then `-128`, and the Walls stages have no `-256`, so a Walls site at normal zoom drew the 128 px file stretched to about 220 px. A missing size now falls back to the next larger file first.
- **Codex image generation without numpy:** the Mac's Python has Pillow but no numpy; magenta keying in pure Pillow (`getdata` lists) takes about 5 s per 1254 px image, fine for a handful. One of six jobs came back already transparent: check `min(alpha) < 255` before keying, or the key erases nothing but the erode still eats the edge.
- **A blood-red base ring read as the crimson seat.** The UI review shot barbarians beside Switzerland's units: `#9a3322` is nearly the crimson seat's map ring, so ownership rested on the dark base and the missing roundel. The ring is now ink with a blood-red notch at its left end, like the far-zoom roundel.


## 2026-11-01: 8.9.23 UI-review fixes (claude/train-1101d)
- **The outlined "Kindle the New Fire" primary was `unitCardLeads`, not a style bug.** With a unit selected, the dock's call to action steps back to an outlined `.cta.stepped` unless the pending decision is urgent (siege, offer, treasury, broken order). A ready faction goal was treated as routine, so it drew weaker than the unit card's button. `goal` now leads, and the unit card steps back; the This Turn card that repeated it is gone, so the Return hint shows once.
- **A disabled button inside a `Tip` is never the hit target.** `.tip-trigger button:disabled { pointer-events: none }` hands the pointer to the Tip wrapper, so `elementFromPoint` on a disabled goal button returns the wrapper and `sidebarAssertions.whole` failed ("is whole and unobscured") although the button was fully in view. `whole` now accepts the control's own `.tip-trigger` wrapper.
- **`.cta-sub` is a flex row with a 6 px gap, so every `Amounts` piece is its own item.** "+2 VP, kept for good" drew as "+2 [seal] , kept for good" with a gap before the comma, and the `amt-tight` fix could not help. `SeatBar` wraps the sub's `Amounts` in one span.
- **Core's effect lines are copied into `app/src/rules.generated.json`.** Changing "Fighters can upgrade to Stealth Fighters: range 7, guard radius 5" in `crates/core/src/data.rs` fails `textArtifacts.test.tsx` (which reads the generated file) until the JSON is regenerated (`UPDATE_UI_RULES=1 cargo test -p legatum-ai --test ui_rules`, or the same string replacement and the test to confirm). The Sakoku label ("Not closed yet · 0 of 30 turns") is a core string too, and is in a snapshot (`sakoku.wasm.test.ts.snap`).
- **At 1000x600 the goal row is one line, with the details in the button's hover.** The slim rail's button is about 110 px wide, so its label wraps (balanced) rather than touching the border; the 22 px dock label drops to 19 px in the rail so "Raise the Warbands" stays inside its button.

## 2026-11-02: start-position lean recalibration (claude/start-lean)
- **The faction ratio is about 3 on master, and the start layer is not the cause.** 6,000 AI-only Hard games (8 players, Standard) on the engine of the day: best/worst 2.82 on the first engine (Athens 18.9% and Sparta 18.6% against Switzerland 6.7% and the Celts 6.9%) and 3.25 on current master (Athens 20.4% against the Celts 6.3%); noise alone gives about 1.1 (95th percentile 1.2). Pillar 8 (at most 1.5) is missed by 2x with or without the new starts. 192 games (Codex's smoke) cannot say anything about a ratio. Compare the SD of the nine rates as well as best/worst, which is an extreme and moves with one faction. Details in `docs/sim-results/start-lean-2026-11-02.md`.
- **Floor-only shipped; the faction lean was parked.** A lean that ranks sites by raw Production hands Sparta, Germany and the Aztecs the better start (+23% Production). Held to 8% quality and 2 Production of the site a seat was picked for, it moves the kind of terrain by 12 to 52% and leaves Production within 1.4% of the mean, but it did not lift Switzerland, the Celts or Germany, and on the current engine it read 3.32 against floor-only's 3.17 and master's 3.25 (SD of the rates 4.91 against 4.75 and 4.55 pp, P 0.05). A band of 10% / 4 Production already moves Sparta's win rate by 2.7 pp. The banded lean is commit `a412c2534` and `LEAN_BAND` in its `mapgen.rs`.
- **The Production floor and lopsided-site scoring remove every dead start (5.9% of starts under the floor on master) at a cost.** It adds about one Production to every seat and widens the Food-plus-Production spread between seats on a map from 19.8 to 31.6 (same ruler for both), because two sites with the same yield total now differ in quality. Athens (no lean) won 1.8 pp more in one floor-only run (2.3 sigma; not reproduced on the current engine).
- **`start_metrics` must use the runner's faction draw.** `legatum-sim` shuffles the nine factions into eight seats (`runner::setup_for_game`); `GameSetup::all_ai` rotates them. Metrics on the rotation gave the same factions a fixed position.
- **Map-dependent tests are brittle to any start change.** After a mapgen change expect: `tools/sim/repin.sh` (pins and fixtures; run it through `tools/cloud/offload.sh` so the changed files come back), `npx vitest run -u` for wasm snapshots, `terrain-units.spec.ts` (`SEED` pins a deep-wood scene; loop seeds with `sed` inside one `remote.sh --npm` job and run `scripts/build-wasm.sh` first in that job, or the loop reads a stale `wasm-pkg` and picks a seed that fails in the gate) and the `MapCanvas.test.ts` touch preview (fill the fixture terrain with Grassland, as the neighbouring tests do). Merging master meanwhile can collide on `RULES_VERSION`: master was already at 10 for train-1102, so the next train still has to bump this branch's map change to 11 once, with `tools/sim/refresh-save-version-fixtures.py 10 11` and `rehash_scenarios`.
- **Running a sweep from an agent shell.** `nohup cmd &` inside one Bash call dies with the call; use `run_in_background`. zsh does not word-split `$var`, so `set -- $spec` quietly yields one argument. A Bash `until` loop blocks (a bare `sleep` is refused); Monitor timers return at once.

## 2026-11-02: the WASM tape loader silently dropped new order kinds (branch claude/promo-showcase, 27b61619d)
- `Tape::from_json` in `crates/wasm/src/tape.rs` skipped order kinds it didn't know as "unknown": `UpgradeAircraft`, `SetScienceConversion` and `SetCityAutomation`. Late-game replays of AI tapes came out as a different game, with 12–36 hash mismatches each. The fix keeps them; 24 of 24 games then replay with 0 mismatches.
- It also reaches saved-game replays (`Recording` in `crates/wasm/src/history.rs`), so any coach replay of a designer game that used city automation or the post-tree Science choice could have diverged on master before the fix.
- How to apply: every new `Order` variant needs a tape round-trip test. A replay that reports hash mismatches is a bug to fix, not noise to filter.

## 2026-11-02: the air-strike aircraft was a canvas drawing, because the art audit only looked at the standing map (branch claude/painted-aircraft)
- `drawAircraft` in `app/src/map/combatFxDraw.ts` drew the Fighter and Bomber as ink strokes, and the Drone shot drew a stroked quadcopter. Both only exist during a turn's playback, so the barbarian and construction audits never saw them; the designer found it in a promo trailer. They now fly painted plan-view sprites (`app/src/assets/units/air/<name>-plan.png`, made by `tools/art/air-plan.py` from Codex image generation using each miniature as the reference, asked for a straight top-down view, nose right, no stand). The Drone shot uses one drone cropped from `drone-swarm-source.png`. `FxShot.craft` now carries all four aircraft kinds (a Stealth strike used to draw as a Bomber).
- Image generation returned some of the plan views already keyed to alpha and some on magenta; `air-plan.py` accepts both. Run `codex exec --skip-git-repo-check -i ref.png - < prompt.txt` from a scratch directory (it refuses outside a trusted directory), and the saved file is in `~/.codex/generated_images/`, not in the working directory.
- A sprite loaded lazily by the draw call is missing on the first frames of the first strike of a session: the pass looked empty until the image arrived. `store.ts` now warms the plan sprites when the turn's plan is built (`warmAirArt`).
- The aircraft's pass used to start mid-run: the strike's `launch` was about 0, so the plane appeared directly over the target and dropped bombs at once. The plan now starts a bomb run 1.3 flights late, so the aircraft crosses the map in from its base first.
- `e2e/tour/painted-aircraft.spec.ts` shoots each kind (and the Drone) at normal and close zoom, entering, over the target and leaving. When a spec needs a hangar aircraft, the fixture's save is edited as text (`"aircraft":[...]`, `next_unit_id`) as in `e2e/air-wing.spec.ts`.
## 2026-11-02: Hard's slow expansion was production quotas, and its Settlers die to Scouts from Peace (branch claude/ai-expansion)
- **Diagnose with the Settler gates, not the founding turns.** `expansion_probe SAVE SALTS TURNS [PARAMS]` plays a designer save's setup with Hard in every seat and logs, per idle city, the scored options and `SettlerGates`. On his 8.9.23 Germany start the Settler passed every gate in 918 capital choices to T60 and lost 641 of them to a troop: the capital-garrison quota (an original capital with no troop on its tile scores 2,200 plus the unit bonus) fires whenever the starting Soldier is out exploring, and it outbid the flat Settler value. Then the two-troop `army_ok` gate held the second Settler 5 to 8 turns. Site scoring and population were never the limit.
- **91% of captured Settlers were taken by a Scout, 83% by a surprise war from Peace, 99% with no escort beside them** (106 captures in 48 games). Hard's own `settler_raid` snipes with Scouts, so AI-vs-AI games bleed first Settlers at T15-T19. The escort search skipped every unit in a city, the only troop a young empire has.
- **Fix by value, measured per gene:** `colony_compound` (a Settler scores a city's compounding return per Production; one troop, not two), `settler_escort` (a quiet city's garrison may escort) and `settler_stalk` (close on an exposed rival Settler at Peace up to 3 turns away; the Snipe plan prices the war). Rivals with all three: the designer's seat 513 to 444 of 2,048 (-3.37 pp [-5.62, -1.12]); Hard on his map founds cities 3 and 4 at T30/T47 instead of T42/T56, still far from his T22/T31 and 9 cities at T81 (Hard 5). The stalk alone is neutral; the escort alone halves Settler losses (0.58 to 0.50 a rival) without moving wins.
- **A rivals-only arm of a default-on gene needs the seat's genes switched off explicitly:** `hard_vs_human` arms now take `other_params` for the seats the arm's mode leaves out (mode `opponents` + `other_params` off = rivals only; mode `seat` + `other_params` off = seat only).
- `tools/ai-quality/chart.py` fails on master with `KeyError: 'ci95'` (a ledger row without the field); not fixed here.
## 2026-11-02: the rules reference drifts about 18% in a busy week
- An audit of `game-rules.md` against master checked about 250 claims and found about 45 wrong or stale, despite the "update it in the same change" rule. Examples: Keshig cost and strength (82 → 164 Production), Byzantine melee surcharge, Celtic melee −2, barbarian timings, Castle and Mill prerequisites, line-number citations that no longer pointed anywhere.
- Most drift came from numbers changed in data tables and from line-number citations, not from new systems: the named rule changes of the week were all correct.
- How to apply: cite `file::function`, never line numbers; after a train with many data changes, run a sampled claim audit (about 40 claims) before agents design on top of it.

## 2026-11-02: run logs leak account details into git
- The open-source audit found 8 tracked `batch.log` files from cloud sim runs; one carried the AWS account ID. 71 files under `docs/sim-results/` hold home-folder paths and 10 hold instance IDs. Nobody wrote these by hand: tools copied raw logs and absolute paths into committed results.
- How to apply: tools that write into `docs/` print repo-relative paths and never copy raw cloud logs; keep raw logs under `~/LegatumRuns/`. The audit report is `docs/status/oss-readiness-2026-11-02.md`.

## 2026-11-02: stacking AI fixes that each help can make the AI weaker
- Rivals-only, with seat override: coalition-push alone made the rivals +1.03 pp stronger; coalition-push, see-the-leader and contain-builds-army together only −0.59 pp. contain-builds-army grew Athens's army (6 → 9 median) but cut its war declarations on the leader (40 → 29) and its attacks on his cities (271 → 257): a bigger army it then felt it had to protect.
- Only coalition_push shipped (train-1102). The ai-expansion genes stacked well with it: the train's four genes were −3.22 pp rivals-only.
- How to apply: gate each AI gene alone and in the planned combination, and replay the coach's decisive turn for any gene meant to change behaviour toward the leader. The benchmark alone can hide behaviour moving the wrong way.

## 2026-11-02: after the priced colony, Hard's expansion gap is land, and more Settlers made it weaker (branch claude/ai-expansion-2)
- **Decision gates no longer bind on his map.** Lifting every Settler gate at once (priority 4000, pipeline 4, open cities 8, site floor 20, reach 14, `colony_compound` 300) moves Hard from 5.20 to 5.72 cities at T81, against his 9. Its own Settler pace already matches his to T31; his early lead is the Byzantine Settler he took on T16.
- **What it lacks is known land.** About 7 good legal sites lie within 8 hexes of Hard's cities through T80 in the true state; its view knows 5.6, then 4.2, then 3.4. 60 to 78 land tiles there stay unexplored while its Scouts die out. Weighting home frontier tiles and rebuilding Scouts (`home_scout`) left the fog where it was and cost 0.6 cities, so the hidden land is not a Scout-targeting problem; a third of it lies near barbarian camps. In his game Byzantium never left one city; in Hard's games it founds 4 more on the land 4 of his 9 cities stand on.
- **More Settlers is not more strength.** `settler_slow_turns` 12 (with or without `settler_pipeline` 3) gave rivals more cities by T100 (5.32 to 5.53) and made them weaker against the designer's seat (+1.56 to +1.71 pp rivals-only; Hard in his seat -0.98). Expansion helps when the cities can be held and used; the next gain is the land race (taking or denying a neighbour's early sites), not a looser pipeline.
- **A diagnostic that compares a gate with the true state needs both:** `expansion_probe` records true legal sites, sites in the seat's view, unexplored land and its reachability at T40/60/80, so "no room" can be split into fog, danger and real scarcity.
- `tools/ai-quality/chart.py` now plots a ledger row without `ci95` as a point (row 130 broke every run).

## 2026-11-02: Hard loses the land race to a neighbour by T20, and pricing land does not win it back (branch claude/ai-land-race)
- **On the designer's 8.9.23 Germany map the land toward Byzantium is settled by T17.** Byzantium founds its second city at T11 to T17; 0.02 of Hard Germany's foundings a game lie on Byzantium's side (the designer: 4 of 9). Valuing a site by what it denies the nearest rival (`land_contest`) moves Hard's foundings toward its neighbours (mean distance to the nearest rival city 7.25 to 7.01, 6.90 at 200) without one more city: the contested land is already gone when Hard's second and third Settlers walk.
- **The designer's lever was vision, not pricing.** To T40 a Byzantine Settler is outside a city about 9 turns a game; Hard's seat sees it on 1.1, and on 0.9 turns it is unescorted, unseen and within 6 of the seat's capital. Sentries on every neighbour (`sentry` 2) and early campaign value (`early_campaign_value` 200 and 400) did not raise sightings or snipes. A war alone does not freeze an AI neighbour: Byzantium at war with the seat by T30 founded more cities (6.2 against 4.8).
- **Camps in front of land are rare where it matters:** 0.27 known camps within 10 hexes on an average sampled turn; `camp_land` did not change a game on his map and was neutral on the benchmark.
- **Gate (2,048 games per arm):** no arm's interval excludes zero; `land_contest` rivals-only -0.68 pp but seat-only -0.44 (opposite signs, so noise), `sentry` 2 rivals-only +0.68 (weaker). Both new genes stay at 0.
- How to apply: before pricing a land grab, check when the land is taken (`expansion_probe` records foundings with positions, camps every 10th turn and every rival Settler to T40 with its distance, sight and escort). The next lever is a unit watching a neighbour's settling lane in the first 20 turns, priced by the expected snipe.

## 2026-11-02: a new standing order silently inherited the Sleep badge
- `render.ts` drew `u.standing_order === 'Heal' ? 'mark-heal' : 'mark-sleep'`, so `AutoFire` (added 2026-10-31) showed the Sleep moon on the map: an archer firing every turn looked asleep. The designer caught it in play. Fixed with `mark-autofire` and, at first, the A key (`claude/autofire-icon`), which is also WASD panning (`gameplayKeys.ts::PAN_KEYS`), so it moved to V the same day. Check PAN_KEYS and every key handler before picking a hotkey.
- How to apply: map a `StandingOrder` to its badge with an exhaustive `switch` or `Record<StandingOrder, string>`, so TypeScript fails when a new order has no badge. Never default to another order's icon.

## 2026-11-02: 8.9.24 Swiss review: sweep the first swing, the capital saved by Gold, defence floors help him
- **A 4% to 46% jump between two turns names the decisive moment.** Hard in his seat from T40, T42, T44, T46 (48 runs each) wins 4%, 46%, 38%, 62%: between T40 and T42 he declared on the Mongols and took an unescorted Settler (Biel T44, Mongols stuck on 3 cities to T80). Hard from T40 declares at T41 in 48 of 48 runs and captures nothing (3 cities to the Mongols' 4 at T50), so the AI's Settler snipe misfires. Branch every 2 to 5 turns around the first war or capture before reading anything from T30/T50/T90 branches.
- **"Walls and Gold" was half right.** Lugano had no Walls until T63; the T58-T61 Mongol raid left it at 30 of 200 HP and two bought Composite Archers killed the melee units. Replaying the tape seat with `COACH_SKIP_BUY` for T58, T59 or both loses the capital by T75 in 32 of 32 runs (control 0 of 32). Check which of the player's stated reasons the event log supports before writing them down.
- **Turtling rivals help him.** A scripted defence floor (Walls, 3 shooters, 2 melee) on all four AIs from T50 raises his Hard-seat win rate from 66% to 100% (their T100 cities fall from 5 to 6 down to 3 to 4); on the Mongols alone, to 78 to 84%. A "defend more" AI change needs the expansion measured next to the defence.
- **The Swiss kit is a multiplier, not the cause.** AI-only Swiss in his five-faction lineup wins 11.9% of 320 games; tolls off 4.1%, interest off 9.1%, the Alpine Redoubt off 12.5% (neutral for the AI), all off 4.1%. His T90 position wins 94% with the kit and 16% without it, yet Hard with the kit wins 0 of 32 from T1 (eliminated in 56%).
- **The new genes left no mark on rival expansion:** with `colony_compound`, `settler_escort`, `settler_stalk` and `coalition_push` off for every AI the rivals' T100 city counts are unchanged (Germany 4/4, Byzantium 5/5, Mongols 8/7.5, Sparta 6/6). `coalition_push` fires (Germany and Byzantium declared together at T107) but the coalition made 19 hits on his cities in 160 turns, 10 of them T147-T155.
- How to apply: runner, scripts and the throwaway kit switches are in `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-8924-swiss/`; the review's whole compute was about 12 box-minutes.

## 2026-11-02: Celts rework, barbarians spare the Celts; a raid-value AI call made them much worse (branch claude/celts-rework)

- **What changed:** barbarians never attack the Celtic empire (`rules::barbarians::spares`; camps, scouts, raiders, no raids on a Celtic city), the melee −2 and the ranged +20 cost are gone, the sack stays; `RULES_VERSION` 11. The Celtic AI no longer treats barbarians as a threat or danger.
- **Result (3,000 balanced-draw Hard games, 8 empires, two seeds, control = master):** Celts 6.1% to 8.5% a seat appearance (paired +2.4 pp [+1.6, +3.3]); Athens 20.2 to 19.9, Mongols 16.6 to 16.5, Sparta 15.9 to 16.0, Aztecs 14.6 to 14.2, Byzantium 13.1 to 12.6, India 10.6 to 10.3, Germany 8.0 to 8.1, Switzerland 7.5 to 6.5. Best/worst 3.3 to 3.1: the Celts stay below the even share, and Athens still leads.
- **The AI idea that failed:** the designer asked the AI Celts to value raising warbands near rivals. A raid value (call the Wild when its camps would stand within 6 hexes of at least 2 rival cities, and keep those camps) cut the Celts to 3.7% (the rule alone: 9.0%); gating the same call to after expansion gave 7.5%, with or without keeping the camps. The camps cost the Celts their own yields (a camp and the barbarian units around it deny tiles) and bring rival armies to clear them. A raid value needs the denial cost priced first; until then the VP window alone decides the call.
- **Pins that moved:** only runs with a Celtic seat. Of the three 40-turn mixed tapes, the one without Celts is unchanged; the other two, the scripted city-fire tape (a Celtic seat) and the garrison replay (a Celtic seat) moved.
- **How to apply:** measure an AI change in a ladder (rule alone, plus each AI piece) before shipping it; here the rule alone gave 9.0%, and the one AI term that looked obviously right took 5 pp off. Four arms of 3,000 games cost under $0.20 and 15 minutes on one spot server.
## 2026-11-02: the Stealth tech card said every unit twice
- Core's effect lines "Fighters can upgrade to Stealth Fighters: strike up to 7 tiles away; ..." did not match the strict upgrade parser (the colon), so they stayed as rule rows, and `BenefitLedger.line` shortened them by hand into stats the unit rows already showed. Rules 3, 5 and 6 of six described two units. The ledger and the unlock moment both read `techCardUnlocks`, so the fix lives there: clauses become the unit's `notes`, the resource comes from `unitNeed`, and the upgrades merge into one rule.
- How to apply: a new tech-card rule that names a unit the same card unlocks belongs on that unit's row; `techCardUnlocks.test.ts` checks every faction and tech for a rule row repeating a unit name. Change core's line, not a TS copy, when the wording is wrong.

## 2026-11-03: train-1103b (start-lean, celts-rework, rebase-anywhere, techcard-fold)
- **One bump, three branches.** start-lean was cut at version 10 and never bumped; celts-rework and rebase-anywhere each bumped 10 to 11. The merge kept 11. `refresh-save-version-fixtures.py` raised on scenarios already at the new version; it now skips them, so it runs cleanly after merging a branch that already bumped.
- **Per-map benchmark swings after a start-position change are not AI strength.** Fixed maps regenerate their starts: Switzerland 105 to 9, Mongols 2 to 60, pooled +0.15 pp. Read only the pooled number.
- **A tour assertion that fails drops the rest of its test's shots.** The Stealth unlock card wording ("upgrades from Fighter" against the spec's "From Fighter") blanked five baselines at both sizes. Wording that gates shots belongs in `expect.soft`.
- **A subagent waiting on the box can run for hours:** the UI review returned at 09:19 after a 23:57 start. Check `date` before every long wait.
- **A card that shows an off-screen destination needs a map marker at the screen edge, and panels hide edge markers (UI review 8.9.25).** Hovering a far rebase city rang nothing on the map because the city was off-screen. `drawAirOffscreen` draws a named arrow plate on the line toward it, slid out from under any panel (the map's `lensAvoid` rects); a marker clamped to the raw screen edge sat under the left dock and the right race panel.
- **A picker that opens inside a scrolling card must scroll itself into view and shed its hint.** At 1000x600 the Rebase list sat below the fold with no scroll cue; the picker now scrolls into view (`revealPicker`), the long how-it-works hint hides while a picker is open, and the rebase spec asserts every city row is in the viewport. Each row shows its own "N of M hangar slots free".
- **One wording for an upgrade path: "From Fighter".** The unlock-moment card said "upgrades from X" while tech cards and the ledger said "From X"; both now say "From X" and the one-line turn report says "(from X)". Three older specs asserted the old phrase.
- **Merging the two AI branches onto the train moved only the scripted city-fire pin** (both branches had moved it on their own; one repin on the merged code). Final benchmark with them: 453 against master's 449 (+0.20 pp [-2.10, +2.54]); mean smoke game 193.8 to 189.3 turns, 0 panics and stalls. Master's code was unchanged since the base receipts, so those receipts served as the control.

## 2026-11-02: the 8.9.24 Settler snipe missed for two reasons, and the escort turned back in fog (branch claude/ai-strike-escort)
- **Plain Hard in a 5-player game runs the "duel" Settler guard** (`settler_safety` 0), whose threat is visible units only. The Mongol escort walked out with the Settler at T37 and went back to garrison at T38, when the Swiss Soldier stood in fog; the Settler walked on alone two hexes past Thun and was taken at T41. `settler_escort` 2 counts a route within 2 of a rival city that can declare as a threat and makes a Settler wait a turn for an escort two turns away; from T36 the escort stays beside it and the Mongols found their 4th city at T43 in 8 of 8 branches.
- **Hard's snipe declared with a unit that could not catch the Settler.** The Snipe reach used the view's `moves_left`, which is last turn's leftover (the Scout showed 0 at planning, `docs/learnings.md` already warned for Settlers), and broke ties by unit id, so a Scout three hexes off beat the Thun Soldier two hexes off. Even a unit that reaches the tile rarely captures: a moving Settler outruns an equally fast pursuer, and only a pursuer that starts beside it pins it. The designer shadowed it for three turns and declared from the next hex. `stalk_contact` 1 does the same: Snipe on contact only, the stalk moves ahead of the Settler with this turn's moves and may use a quiet garrison. Hard in his seat from T40 (48 runs): 2 wins with the 8.9.24 genes, 20 with the new seat genes (the coach's T42 branch, Settler already taken, was 46%).
- **Each half alone was noise on the benchmark; together they made rivals stronger** (rivals-only -1.46 pp [-3.22, +0.34]; escort alone -0.49, contact alone +0.49). Rival Settlers lost per game barely moved (0.534 to 0.522): on the benchmark maps the contest is rare, on his map it was the game.
- **The T58 raid could not be finished with what was there.** Two bought archers killed both Mongol melee units on T60 and T61; the next capturers stood 7 and 9 hexes away in garrisons, and Lugano heals 20 a turn. `finish_kill` (hold a nearly-down siege while a capturer marches) keeps the campaign and walks the garrisons out, and Lugano still falls in 0 of 32 replays of his orders; on the benchmark it helps whoever has it (rivals-only -0.59 pp, seat-only +0.44), so it ships at 50. The Scenario Lab cut (`swiss-strongbox`) measures something else: Hard declares in only 29 of 200 runs (median T65), so its 5 to 6% is a declaration problem, not a finishing one. The lever left is the coach's first half: arrive together before the first shot.
- **A behaviour gene can break a rare case a test already pins:** the escort wait replaced a Settler's retreat into its walled home (the Sparta 10-25 Kyoto test) and only the full test run caught it, after two gates. Run the crate's tests before the first gate.
- How to apply: "what would the new AI do here" needs a branch, not a shadow replay (a shadow brain's orders never reach the tape). The probes (`snipe_probe`, `coach_branch`, `raid_probe`) are in `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-8924-swiss/strike-escort/`, not landed.

## 2026-11-03: Hard never declared on unwalled Lugano because shooters ahead of the infantry hid the march (branch claude/ai-declare)
- **Look for missing candidates before re-pricing a decision.** In `swiss-strongbox` (Hard Mongols, 29 of 200 runs declared) the war value never got a say: the plan layer had no Campaign candidate on any turn. `campaign_forecast` judged the city by the units in the two-turn approach whenever any stood there, and three Rangers with no melee unit among them ended the forecast, while the march force a few hexes behind (a Soldier with them) forecast the capture. `war_trace` with `WAR_TRACE_DIAG` now prints prospective targets at Peace (reach and march forces, both siege forecasts, the outlook), which showed it in one run.
- **The forecast fix alone recreated his game's raid.** With only the fall-through, Hard declared in 200 of 200 runs at 7% capture odds from four turns out and took Lugano in 1%. Staging at Peace and declaring when the force in reach takes the city (the Sparta genes `planned_stage` and `early_campaign_muster`) without the fix also did nothing (1%: no candidate to stage for). Together, `march_past_reach`: 85% (both values).
- **Gate (value 2):** rivals-only -2.54 pp [-4.35, -0.68], the first AI gene this week whose interval excludes zero; seat-only +1.46 [-0.05, +3.08]. His 8.3 Switzerland map took most of it (104 to 56 seat wins). From his T58 position, his orders and purchases replayed, Lugano falls in 31 of 32 runs (0 of 32 before); from T50 the Mongols declare at T58 with four units at the walls instead of T54 with one, but his bought archers turn the campaign onto Thun and back (3 of 32).
- **The finish came from the marching, not the forecast.** Splitting the gene: the forecast for new wars only (1) and for wars under way too (2) both take Lugano in 31 of 32 from his T58; staging only at Peace takes it in 0. A Campaign plan at war marches every free unit toward the city, even alone, and that brings the capturers the T58 raid lacked. The same marching costs the late Mongol war scenarios (seat-only: Solothurn 14% to 0%, Chur 32% to 23%): units arriving put a melee unit in reach of a bigger walled city and the plan re-aims there (St. Gallen, T161) and takes nothing. Values 1 and 2 read identically on the suite; 2 is better on both gate arms (rivals -2.54 against -1.95, seat +1.46 against +0.49), so 2 ships.
- **Run the war-scenario suite seat-only as well as all-seats:** all-seats showed `war-japan-sparta-t142` 28% to 4%, which seat-only shows is the Sparta opponent getting stronger, not Japan's play.
- **`german-second-front` (0%) is vision, not declaration:** Germany starts at T110 knowing no Swiss city, then sees only Winterthur 11 hexes off; Lugano is never in view. The coach's recommendation 3 (keep the leader's capital in view) is the next lever there.
- How to apply: a behaviour gene that changes what a recorded position decides needs `tools/sim/repin.sh` and, for archived decision tests, the gene pinned to 0 next to the others (`air/japan_regressions.rs`). The replay probe (`coach_branch`, with the Mongol declaration turn and units near Lugano) is in `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-8924-swiss/strike-escort/` (not landed).

## 2026-11-03: what the designer does better than Hard, measured across 23 games (decision diff)
- Method: replay each of his games on its own tag (0 hash mismatches in all 23), swap one category of his orders for Hard's on one turn, continue both with Hard, 48 paired runs each (`docs/playtests/diffs/`). 610 decisions valued.
- His edge: army routes and targets (+6.8 VP over 118 turns), founding now instead of walking on (+5.8), garrison and escort placement (+4.7), and spending the hoard on the decisive unit (+3.9 over only 6 turns: the largest per decision).
- Not his edge: Hard's war declarations beat his waiting (−4.2), Hard's Settler routes beat his (−3.8), and more Settler production alone is negative. So "be more aggressive" and "build more Settlers" are wrong lessons; "found sooner" and "spend the hoard when it is decisive" are right.
- Limit: one-turn swaps can't value multi-turn tactics such as his Settler snipes (several turns of setup); those come from the coach reviews instead.

## 2026-11-03: rivals let the 8.9.25 Celtic hold run because the plan term never became a move (branch claude/fix-contest-hold)
- Cause: the plan priced "contest the hold" for the war, but the only pass that removes a camp ran fourth from last with leftover units and a 12-hex leash. The new early pass (`contest_hold`, `crates/ai/src/contest.rs`) prices each removal with the win estimate and takes his orders from 32/32 wins to 0/32 from T130, T140 and T148.
- First version found camps and assigned forces every turn but cleared almost none. Three late-pipeline traps undid it, each invisible from the pass itself:
  - The last military pass (`focus.rs::hold_stragglers`, gene `gather_hold`) cut every walk that ends in Celtic city fire, so the units stopped one or two hexes short.
  - Movement reconciliation drops a risky melee attack unless the unit is in `assault_attacks`.
  - Re-choosing the "best" camp every turn sent the same unit to a different camp each turn, so it circled between them. Keeping the camp a unit's `goto` already points at fixed it.
  - Lesson: an early pass that commits units must also exempt them from the late order-rewriting passes, and must keep a multi-turn target. A per-turn debug line (sources, force, gain, issued order) found all three in one 1-game run.
- The win estimate gives an AI whose own chance reads 0 no reason to stop a leader. The gain therefore multiplies the holder's lost chance by our share of the race that reopens, floored at an even share, as `plan.rs::leader_race` does.
- The human-seat benchmark can't see this gene: no benchmark game has a Celtic team on its target with the Wild, so all 2,048 games were identical in every arm. Judge it on his positions and the war scenario. The AI-only league (88 of 2,048 games differ, AI Celts 8.4% both ways) shows it rarely fires between AIs.
- Harness note (coach runner `coach8925_whatif`): branches at T130 and T140 give identical distributions, and `reset_plan` changes nothing. Restoring guards at T130/T140 together with `COACH_SPARE_FIRE_FROM` breaks his tape (he never reaches 10 VP, so both arms read 0%). Only T148 is a valid guarded test.
## 2026-11-03: Keeping a siege through a Peace offer (astra/fix-keep-siege, gene `peace_siege_value`, merged off)
- **A siege forecast misses a siege with no capturer on hand.** At the 8.9.24 Swiss T147 the Mongol attack on the Swiss capital had no melee unit in place yet, so `forecast_siege` gave the capture no value and Peace looked free. The gene prices the visible capture opportunity against the offered Peace instead.
- **Danger from other wars can't justify this Peace.** The old relief test counted danger from every war, but Peace with the Swiss relieves only Swiss danger. The gene allows relief only when its own capital is threatened by that enemy.
- **Shipped off.** It refuses the Swiss T147 Peace (0 hash mismatches on the tag), but the benchmark can't tell it apart (453 vs 455 seat wins), and the 8.9.25 Celts diff found Hard's acceptance of Byzantium's Peace mid-siege at T82 was right (−0.4 to −0.6 VP for refusing). Turn it on only with a paired T147 value and a case where keeping the siege wins.

## 2026-11-03: A strong start is not the same as a decided game (8.9.25 India at T37, coach)
- **Measure the start by handing the seat to Hard at several turns, not by the start score.** His India start was 58th percentile on `start_quality` and 94th on room (India's widest-land rule), and Hard from T1 on his map won 25% (AI India in 256 AI-only games: 12%). Handing the seat over at T16 gave 8%, at T22 56%: the swing was two stolen AI Settlers (T17, T18) and an empty German capital (T22), not the map. Hard's T37 Production on his map (54.5 against a typical 37) shows what the map is worth.
- **The decision diff on an unfinished game:** run with `--end-turn` past the stop (T100 here); no branch finishes, so `diff_report.py` prints 0 for every win column, while `value.jsonl`'s `wc` (end-position estimate, basis points) separates the arms. The window and rival reports already use `wc`. Rival-diff drift is high on a short tape (76-95 of 96 runs), so confirm the top rival item with a direct what-if (here `settler_safety` + `peace_settler_capture` in every AI, his tape seat, a neutral gene as control).
- **`settler_escort` 2 does not cover either case seen here:** Germany's Settler had an escort (its only Soldier, which emptied the capital), and the Celtic route came nowhere near an Indian city. The "within 2 of a rival city" trigger misses a Scout or troops standing in the open.


## 2026-11-03: A changed default and a faction rule both leave stale copy behind (UI review of claude/merge-ui-1103)
- **Changing a default changes every sentence that states it.** The 6-empire lobby passed its tests, but the title screen still said "You against seven AI empires" and the Guide "a free-for-all with seven rivals". Grep for the old number as a word and as a digit across `app/src` and e2e literals when a default moves.
- **A rule that changes who threatens whom rewrites what that faction reads.** After barbarians stopped counting as a threat to the Celts, the Threat lens's empty state ("No known enemy military can reach any tile") still showed beside three visible barbarian Spearmen. A neighbouring parked stack also now hides a Celtic city's banner for many turns, because banners rise above tokens only during an alarm. Both are queued in `ui-celts-banners`.
## 2026-11-03: a new solo lobby has 6 empires, so e2e specs that start from defaults get a different map
- A new Single Player lobby now starts at Standard's default of 6 empires (`MAP_SIZES.default`), not 8. The map is generated for the player count, so a spec that clicks Start game with seed 424242 no longer gets the old 8-empire map.
- How to apply: a spec that needs the full table of 8 (race panel rows, the 8-column score ledger, all eight lobby seats) clicks "+ Add an AI empire" twice before Start, which gives back the same setup the old default made. A spec that only needs "a game" can stay on the defaults.

## 2026-11-03: Why "Gain 2 cities" took three (designer's 8.9.25 Celts game)
- **A gain made on the turn you enter an era never counts.** `objectives::update` snapshots the era's base at the end of the entry turn, after that turn's orders, so his T139 city (founded the turn research entered Modern) was in the base. He needed T140 and T147 too. The lead first guessed the net count (losses subtracting); the replay showed no city lost, so check the base turn before blaming the formula.
- **Related, by design (R1):** scoring any era calls `restart_counting`, which re-snapshots every other open era, so progress on another era's gain objective resets ("what scored one era never counts for another"). Population, Territory and Buildings gains are net (`now - base`, floored at 0); Techs shares the entry-turn effect.

## 2026-11-03: Codex findings from the 8.9.25 Celts review (branches astra/ai-coherence-1103, astra/ai-cheapest-objective, astra/diff-reseed-objectives)
- **A value-0 plan can run for 50 turns.** Sparta's "forced expedition" (T37–T86) was a synthetic value-zero Campaign kept alive by an unconditional Sparta override, which also licensed a value-0 declaration later. Gene `sparta_valued_expedition` requires a positively valued campaign or a priced battle; seat wins with it in the rivals 475 → 451 of 2,048. Its home defence while a positive campaign runs (+2,252 on Bukhara at T70, capital endangered) is still unexamined.
- **What looked incoherent often isn't.** The Aztecs' T133 Peace against their own Contain plan was an emergency Peace for an endangered city; the T147 declaration followed when the danger passed. Byzantium did try Peace three times. The idle Spartan Settler had no legal site on 23 turns. Trace before fixing.
- **Barbarian raiders blocked on their own guards because the destination was occupied,** not a wrong coordinate ((16,18) is the correct offset). A core fix cut friendly blocks 152 → 11.
- **The old objective bonus couldn't reach early production picks:** choices made before the general option comparison never saw it. A bid that competes with every build reaches them (`cheapest_objective`: Aztec Lab median 770 → 840, AI-only objectives scored +6%).
- **Decision-diff salts now leave the map seed alone,** so objectives no longer redraw per run. The Swiss T140 Bomber moved +2.38 → +2.21 VP. The coach's what-if runner still redraws (documented in the coach skill).

## 2026-11-03: A scenario cut on a branch breaks when master bumps RULES_VERSION first
- `war-celts-wild-hold-t150` was cut on `claude/fix-contest-hold` at RULES_VERSION 11. The sack change landed on master first with 12, so after the merge `crates/wasm/tests/scenarios.rs` refused it ("incompatible rules version: saved Some(11), current 12"), though both branches verified green alone. Fix on the merged code: `tools/sim/refresh-save-version-fixtures.py 11 12`, then `rehash_scenarios` on the box (the hash was unchanged). When landing a branch that adds scenarios after a version bump, run both before the verify.

## 2026-11-04: An archived war fixture went stale under new supply rules (astra/war-golden-horde-regression)
- **The attack check read 0/64 against a 60% floor, and nothing was tracking it.** Bisect: Swiss `hoard_at_war` first cut the thin attack 15/16 → 4/16, then a rules train cut it to 0/16 even with that gene off. The archived Mongol army starts 18 Iron and 2 Niter short; after T136 all 16 combat units are unsupplied (−25% strength), Hard's capture estimate falls 12% → 3%, and it takes Peace. That is correct play in that position, so the fixture, not the AI, broke.
- **How to apply:** a rules change that touches supply, costs or upkeep can make an archived war fixture measure something else. When a war scenario drops to 0%, check the position's supply and the AI's own estimate before hunting a regression. Retire the floor, keep the number as an observation, and cut a replacement on current rules (queued: `war-attack-fixture`).

## 2026-11-04: The siege card was hidden in every normal game, and the tests passed
- **It lived inside a collapsed disclosure.** `SeatBar.tsx` put `<SiegeAlerts/>` inside the `sidebar-turn-more` `<details>`, which is closed whenever the VP race panel shows (most of a game). Players saw only "Turn details · 1 city alert". It now leads the This Turn body.
- **Two tests hid it.** `city-under-attack.spec.ts` checked the card was first *inside* the details (true, and invisible); the `combat` tour's 21j shot called `revealSidebar(page, 'turn-more')` before checking the dock fit, which opened and scrolled it first. A fit check must shoot at scroll 0 before any reveal helper (queued with the 1000x600 fit fix, `siege-card-fit`).
- **Lifting a layer for one case broke the common one.** Raising own city banners above all tokens (for barbarians parked by Celtic cities) covered your own Scout beside your capital on turn 2. Banners now rise only when a unit not yours stands beside the city.

## 2026-11-05: AI Swiss lost on garrisons, not on the hoard; his research queue decides his map (branch claude/swiss-playbook)
- **The gap was a troop per city.** AI Swiss garrisoned every city with a troop (the garrison floor wants cities + 1, at 3 Gold a turn each beyond two) and walled a founded city first only 18% of the time, 29 turns after founding. The designer walled every new city first and kept 2 to 5 troops across 4 to 22 cities. Pricing the Redoubt as the city's garrison (`swiss_playbook` 1) took the AI Swiss from 6.37% to 10.22% win rate per seat in 4,096 eight-empire games (+3.85 pp [+2.74, +4.96]); with the climb's lowered Swiss expansion genes back at the shared defaults (2), 11.58% (+5.21). Half the eliminations, twice the Reserve. The earlier Swiss genes (`hoard_at_war`, `decisive_buy`) were about spending; the bigger lever was what the Gold never had to pay for.
- **A troop-count rule must count walls that are coming.** Counting only walled cities left every young empire's minimum at "all cities" (nothing is walled yet), so the cheaper troop still won the time-discounted ranking and Redoubts stayed at 20% first. Counting cities that can raise a Redoubt (buildable or under way) took them to 47%.
- **On his 8.9.24 map his turn-1 research queue is the whole T1 difference.** A standing 5-tech queue set at T1 decides the first five techs. His (Masonry, Currency, Writing, Irrigation, Bronze Working) against Hard's (Irrigation first, Masonry fifth): 12.4% → 16.6% win chance for Hard at T161 and 13.0% → 36.4% with level 2, with total losses 17-23 of 64 down to 3; the unit-move swap changes nothing. Yet Masonry first AI-only cost 2.5 points, and his whole opening 3.7: the order that wins next to an aggressive neighbour loses on an average map. Measure a research opening on the map that motivated it and on the census before shipping.
- **Tooling:** the legatum-sim census markdown reports share of all wins; the 6.9% Swiss figure is win rate per seat (`census.py` here computes it). `decision-audit/run.sh --engine` needs a commit; to try an uncommitted gene on the recording engine, check out the tag in a scratch worktree, apply the diff, and run the script's steps through `tools/cloud/remote.sh --with-uncommitted`. Today's start placement puts the Swiss on another tile of his 8.9.24 map, so `--setup` runs on the current engine are his map, not his start.
- How to apply: when porting a faction playbook, list his whole-game plan against the AI's code paths first; the lever is usually a quota or price the AI applies to every faction that the faction's kit makes wrong (here Swiss upkeep plus the Redoubt), not a new behaviour.

## 2026-11-05: Turning a faction gene on by default breaks tests that use that faction as a generic fixture
- **Six tests failed only on the combined landing,** though both playbook branches verified green with their genes at 0. `tests.rs` uses Switzerland as the fixture for plain Hard reinforcement (now a Horseman, not a Soldier, under the Redoubt garrison), `goal_sparta_wild_tests.rs` tested the plain Celts camp-clearing and settling path the playbook changes on purpose, and the human-seat benchmark's fixed-game milestones moved.
- How to apply: when flipping a gene on through `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS`, grep the tests for that faction, pin the tests that describe the plain path to the gene off (as they already pin `colony_compound`), and update fixed milestones that describe "the current AI". Verify the landing branch with the flip, never only the gene-off branch.
- **A params file can't switch a built-in faction gene off through `decisions`** (Aztec landing, same day). `AiParams::decisions(faction)` applies `BUILTIN_FACTION_DECISIONS` to every gene still equal to the shared default, so `"decisions": {"aztecs_playbook": 0}` is silently re-set to 7 for the Aztecs. A gene-off control arm or test must use `faction_overrides` (`{"Aztecs": {"aztecs_playbook": 0}}`), or mutate `brain.decisions` after construction. Check every gate's control arm for a gene that is now on by default.

## 2026-11-05: `filler.sh fetch ID` without a DIR syncs the batch results into the current directory
- **The DIR argument defaults to `.`,** so running it from the repo root starts copying a census (1.2 GB for 4,096 games) into the working tree. The ops check caught it before any file landed.
- How to apply: always pass a scratchpad directory (`filler.sh fetch ID "$SCRATCH/id"`), read the summary at the end of `output.log`, then delete the copy.

## 2026-11-05: Copying his faction playbooks paid through bugs and shared quotas, not through his moves
- **Combined census on master (4,096 games, seed 9311003):** Swiss 6.4% to 10.8%, Celts 9.7% to 12.1%, Aztecs 15.2% to 17.0%; the gains held together (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/playbooks-combined-1105/`).
- **What paid:** the Celts' two Wild bugs (clearing their own camps, no re-call), and shared quotas their kit makes wrong: a troop per city for the Redoubt Swiss, a cities+5 war army for the Aztecs.
- **What didn't transfer:** his visible moves copied literally. Aztec Spearman rush −1.3 pp, Swiss Masonry first −2.5, Celtic light garrisons and soft targets 0, his wild turn as a fixed percentage worse than 70%. Those moves were answers to his particular map and rivals, not rules.
- How to apply: when porting a playbook, first audit whether the AI executes the faction's signature at all (tape trace), then look for a quota or price the kit makes wrong. Copy a move only behind a condition that reads his reason, such as an aggressive neighbour within reach.

- **2026-11-05: Athens has almost no compensating weakness, which is why it tops every AI census (20.0% a seat on master, 4,096 games, seed 9311003).** Code (`data.rs::building_stats_for`): the Lyceum is a cheaper Library (70 vs 80) with double Science (+6 vs +3), ruins add 20 Science, and the only standing cost is Markets at +1 Gold instead of +3. The rules reference had the 70-cost Lyceum filed as a drawback; corrected. The goal's price (3 free techs to every rival) is paid late: the AI broadcasts at a median T164, when the techs barely matter. Athens's VP lead comes from Great Works (1.16 a seat, highest), era objectives (2.73) and the Most Advanced title (0.22, double most factions), not from its goal (31% completion).

- **2026-11-05: Germany's signature strength arrives in the last quarter of the game.** In the 4,096-game census on 562226396, Hard's Germany learns Industrialization (which brings the Ruhr Foundry and ends its −1 move) at a median T141 (p25 T137), in 85% of seats; games end at a median T183, so 44 turns remain (p25 28). The drawback runs ~77% of the game and the payoff ~23%, before the Foundry is even built. This prompted the faction-rule ablation test (`legatum-factions`, "Every rule must earn its place").

- **2026-11-05: Why the AI keeps missing his "obvious" strategies (Fable's strategy-gap review, `docs/design/strategy-gap/fable.md`).** Every playbook gain this week (Swiss, Celts, Aztecs, India) came from deleting a frozen quota or fixing a bug, never from copying his moves: army size is a formula (`production.rs::uncapped_army`), a troop per city is bought by `GARRISON_FLOOR_VALUE` before any value comparison, and the evaluator (`winchance::WEIGHTS`) has no term for time bought, defensive depth or tempo while it rewards army share. Gene hill-climbing can't move structure, every census is Hard vs Hard (he is the only exploiter in the league), and porting per-faction playbook flags leaves the shared quota that caused the gap in place. Measured overnight by the quota-ablation census on `claude/strategy-gap-lab`.

- **2026-11-06: The faction-rule ablation across all nine factions (`docs/sim-results/faction-ablation/README.md`).** Of 51 switches, 13 are dead (no effect within about ±1 pp) and two are backwards: Byzantium wins 20.2% instead of 11.3% with Mercenary Companies switched off, so the AI's hiring loses it games; and the Swiss win more without their hoard goal (+1.27), so chasing 1,000 Gold costs the AI more than the 2 VP pays. Sparta's +50% civilian cost is by far the heaviest rule in the game (+17.5 pp when removed). A rule with an exactly-zero Δ is worth checking for an inert switch: Sparta's goal changed 4 of 4,096 games, so it is genuinely dead, not broken. Germany's arms ran against their own control; the other eight shared one (`--control-dir`), valid because an empty switch set is byte-identical.
## 2026-11-05: The capital star follows `original_capital`, not `is_capital`
- **Two flags, two meanings.** `is_capital` is the city that gets the capital bonus now and moves after a capture; `original_capital` (public on every explored city, fog included, with `original_capital_of` the founder) stays for the whole game and is the one the +1 VP scores on. The banner's star seal, the far seal, the minimap frame and every hover line read `capitalMark` (`app/src/capitalMark.ts`), so a replacement capital never wears a star.
- **The point is per captor, not per team** (`battle.rs::capture_city` keys `capitals_taken` by captor). The star is hollow only when you scored it yourself; a teammate's capture leaves it filled and the line says you can still score it. The rule also excludes the founder's teammates (before 2026-11-05 only the founder itself), so the UI, `capital_capture_vp` and `rival_capital_vp` in the AI all compare teams. No pinned sim moved.
- **A thin four-point star is not a clear marker at banner size.** Inline in the text run it read as a "+"; the star now sits in a round seal that ends the banner (disc about 1.4 banners tall, gilt ring), and the outlined form needs a fatter waist to survive. A seal that is taller than its banner needs sprite padding above and below; the meters under the banner move down by that padding.
- **A star is not decoration once it means something.** The city panel kicker used it on every city; other kickers now use a plain dot, so a replacement capital cannot look like the scoring city.
- **A VP icon swallows the full stop after it** (`.amt-tight` pulled the icon's margin back, but the VP seal has no side space); `.amt-vp.amt-tight` keeps it.
- Tour fixtures stage the case through the rules (`capitalsFixture`: founder, owner, `originalCapital` and `capitalsTaken` in the core JSON), not by patching the view. Give the seats `is_human: true` there, or the AI moves the Settlers instead of founding.

- **2026-11-06: With six Codex lanes on the build box, `verify-remote.sh` Playwright fails one random, unrelated spec per run.** Four of six verifies on 11-05/06 failed on a single test each, a different one every time (title screen never rendering "Single Player", `net::ERR_NETWORK_CHANGED` on audio, 90–240 s timeouts in map specs), and each passed in the other runs and locally. Assertion-class failures, timeouts included, are never rerun by `verify-branch.sh`, so every flake costs a full 20–40 minute rerun. Dropping `.progress/astra-queue/max-lanes` from 6 to 4 gave a clean 635/635 on the next run. Keep the box at four lanes while branches are being verified, and check a failing spec locally on the branch (`npx playwright test <spec> --project=game` with `LEGATUM_SERVER_BIN`) before suspecting the change. Locally, an old `legatum-server` binary returns 400 on `/api/archive`, which fails the console-error check in specs like `vp-holdings`; that is the binary, not the branch.
- **2026-11-06: `remote.sh . -- bash -lc '...'` mangles `$` in nested awk:** a totals line came back "passed 0 failed 0" with cargo exiting 0. Trust `cargo test`'s exit code and grep for FAILED/panicked; put awk in a script file if totals matter.

- **2026-11-06: A "gene off" arm can silently stay on, and a "Hard" test can run Normal (Astra's strategy-gap review).** `tools/sim/quality_gate.py:gene_arms` writes shared values, and `DecisionParams::with_builtin_faction` replaces any value equal to the shared default with the faction's builtin, so a shared `swiss_playbook: 0` (or any gene with a faction builtin) does not reliably mean off; only `faction_overrides` is applied after builtins. The strategy-gap lab used explicit overrides and its controls reproduced the published census game for game, but older gate rows may be affected (being checked by Codex `gate-off-switch-fix`). Separately, `crowns.rs::two_step_choice_prefers_a_city_that_advances_the_next_era` builds a Normal planner while the behaviour it asserts is Hard's, whose cheapest-objective branch returns first: a passing behaviour test proves nothing about a difficulty it does not construct.

- **2026-11-06: Overnight Codex lessons (gate-off-switch-fix, core-rule-bugs, swiss-hoard-misplay).** (1) To judge whether a historical gene arm was really off, compare it with the shared default *at the commit it was measured on*, not today's: the October economy arm of 100 was effective because the default was then 200. The audit of 48 ledger rows found none misconfigured. (2) Two branches each bumped `RULES_VERSION` to 13 independently; merging them needs a fresh bump (14) plus regenerated current-rule fixtures and the UI rules snapshot, even when no scenario hash moves.
