Check master's version before bumping in a long-lived branch. (3) The strategy-gap report and briefs still called the Swiss hoard "1,000 Gold"; the rule has been 5,000 since 10-22. Read the number from `game-rules.md` or code before quoting a faction goal in a brief.

- **2026-11-06: A shared AI default can silently kill a faction rule.** Hard's lean war army (`army_war_extra` 1, shared since 11-06) wants cities + 1 units, which is exactly Byzantium's levy cap (one per city plus one), so the AI never meets the cap and the ablation reads thin levies as dead (+0.8 pp). When a shared gene changes army size or spending, re-check every faction rule that caps or prices the same thing. Also from the Walls-at-100 landing (`docs/sim-results/walls-100-1106/README.md`, measured together with the Scriptorium cut): first Walls T29 → T33, early captures up about 15%, Sparta +1.5 to +1.8 pp, Switzerland and India down 1–2 pp, best/worst 2.20 → 2.66 (2.52 held out); the price favours rush factions and hurts the two weakest, so their fixes must come from their own rules.

- **2026-11-06: A faction's unique unit can be dead for the AI because the AI never fields it, and a production bonus won't fix that (Mongol review).** The Keshig ablation read "dead" (+0.6 pp); the designer's Mongol game made 111 Keshig attacks by T83 (94 shots, 55 at walls) while Hard Mongols field 0.7 Keshigs a game and attack with them 0.9 times. A throwaway switch that tripled the mounted share in `composition_bonus`, credited the bow against Spearmen and freed Horseman upgrades from the Gold reserve moved nothing (0.71 → 0.80 Keshigs, 15.9% → 15.8% wins, 900 paired seats): units come from the defence and early-counter paths (score 2,600+) and Gold purchases, not from the bonus. `unique_unit_bonus` has terms for the Byzantine, Celtic and Aztec uniques and none for the Keshig. Before reading an ablation as "the rule is weak", count how often the AI builds and uses what the rule gives (the `mongol_keshig.rs` probe in `~/LegatumRuns/coach-tools/coach-8925-mongols/`).

- **2026-11-07: Unlanded Codex branches piled up to 195, and nothing recorded which were dead.** Triage (`docs/status/branch-triage-2026-11-07.md`): 57 already on master by another route, 102 superseded or abandoned, 16 with unique unlanded work; 56 tips were a Merge or WIP commit and 31 had no task report. Almost no branch commit reverse-applies to master (RULES_VERSION 5 → 14 and the faction renames moved everything), so "already landed" had to be judged by feature, not patch. 160 were archived as `archive/astra/<name>` tags and deleted. Prevention: the WIP limit in the legatum-workflow skill (no new Codex write tasks while more than 5 finished branches await review), and archive or land each branch when its report is read. Also found: the human-seat benchmark has no Aztec or India seat, so their playbooks are invisible to the gate.

## 2026-11-07: Landing a backlog of Codex branches (landing agent)
- **Equal `RULES_VERSION` numbers merge silently.** `astra/objectives-capture-accounting` and master had each bumped to 14 for different rules, so `git merge master` reported no conflict in `lib.rs` and the branch kept 14 with master's fixtures. Before landing, compare the branch's version with master's *and* check whether the branch bumped at all (`git diff $(git merge-base master B) B -- crates/core/src/lib.rs`); give each rules branch master+1 at landing time. Chaining the rules branches (forecast 15 → rule-bugs 16 → Athens 17 → objectives 18, each land branch merging the previous one) let them verify in parallel; the fixture refresh per bump is `refresh-save-version-fixtures.py OLD NEW`, then `UPDATE_UI_RULES=1 cargo test -p legatum-ai --test ui_rules` and `rehash_scenarios` on the box through `offload.sh`.
- **Codex branches that passed their own checks still failed the landing verify**: two new clippy lints (`cloned_ref_to_slice_refs`, `redundant_redefinition`) and a test assertion message caught by `copySource.test.ts` ("rounds down" is banned in any core string, test messages included). Codex lanes run clippy before their last master merge; expect lint fixes at landing.
- **Each landing that moves an AI pin re-pins every later branch.** After the Swiss gene landed, forecast-truth (which alone left the pin unchanged) moved `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` again on the merged code. Merge master and repin right before the final verify, not at prep time.
- **`release-docs-hygiene` broke the evolve scripts:** `from tools.evolve import cmaes` fails when `python3 tools/evolve/evolve.py` runs as a file (the repo root is not on `sys.path`; the unit tests pass because they run as modules). Kept `import cmaes` in the evolve scripts; check `--help` of every script whose imports a branch touches.
- **Don't chain `git worktree remove` and `git branch -D` after `git merge` with `&&` across a pipe into grep:** the merge's conflict status is lost and the worktree went away mid-conflict. Append progress lines only after merging master into the land branch, or the `plans/ai-war-progress.txt` append conflicts on every merge.
- **A zustand selector that returns a fresh `[]` loops forever** (`useGame((st) => st.orders[st.seat] ?? [])` in `TileCard`, automation-cannot-choose): every TileCard test failed with "Maximum update depth exceeded". Select the raw value and default outside the selector to a module-level constant.
- **`offload.sh --prep wasm` vitest runs can fail `icons.test.ts` ("Celts Ranger … null")** while the verify's own vitest passes: the box slot's shared transform cache keeps an old `import.meta.glob` file list (see the note in `app/src/icons.ts`). Trust the verify step, not a slot rerun, for that one test.
- **Run at most two or three verifies on the box at once.** With five landing verifies plus Codex lanes, Playwright dropped to 2 workers and four branches failed on timeouts and `ERR_NETWORK_CHANGED` that all passed when re-run alone (38/38 and 36/36).

## 2026-11-07: An AI-only ablation can call a drawback "backwards" when the AI overspends
Byzantium's Theodosian Walls kit (branch `astra/byzantium-theodosian-walls`): switching off the one-armed-unit-per-city levy *lowered* Byzantium's share, 15.61% → 13.88%. Without the cap, Hard bought more troops and paid 578 Gold of upkeep per seat instead of 382, but captured only 1.70 cities instead of 1.62. So the cap protected the AI from its own overbuilding. The verdict reads as a measure of the AI's troop valuation, not of the rule. The signature, buying Walls with Gold, measured dead: only 21% of seats bought any. This is the Keshig pattern again (dead in AI play, decisive in the designer's hands). Before cutting a rule on an AI-only verdict, check whether the AI used the rule at all.

## 2026-11-07: Landing freshness-nightly and rules-batch-1107 (landing agent)
- **A rule cut can make shipped scenarios illegal, and the merge hides it.** Closing Peace borders to Scouts and civilians (Mongol passage cut) left units standing in foreign territory in seven scenarios (`runaway-conqueror`, `war-golden-horde*`, `war-sparta-nicaea`, `war-walls-rise`, `war-german-stand`). The branch had moved them one tile and re-encoded, but taking master's blobs for every scenario conflict dropped that. The wasm scenario test panics on the first illegal one only, so it took two verifies to find all seven. After taking master's scenario blobs, diff the branch's decoded states against its base and reapply every non-version change, then `rehash_scenarios`.
- **A leftover import fails `tsc -b` (app-build) while vitest and Playwright pass** (`ProductionItem` in `CityPanel.test.tsx` after the Ambusher cut). The UI reviewer found it by building; the first verify also failed there.
- **Master moved by one merge between verify and landing** (my own freshness landing). Merge master into the land branch again and run the workspace test on master after; the verify stays valid when the intervening change is tools-only.
- **Playwright: five unrelated specs failed once (console errors, 90-120 s timeouts) and passed on the identical re-run**, while two other lanes held the box. Flake, not the branch.
- **UI follow-ups not done from the rules-batch review:** (1) world-wonder cards still show a hatched "BUY" cell although wonders are never buyable; drop the cell, keep the note. (2) The Celts sheet states "barbarians never attack the Celts" three times and the lower half is empty at 1440x900. (3) The Mongols Goal line is a 4-sentence block; name the Keshig where its strength line says "A Cataphract that can shoot". (4) Sparta unit panel clips "Skip this turn" at 1000x600. (5) The loading tip could carry the 60 Gold figure. Sparta now has a kill-heal strength with no paired price; check it against pillar 8.


## 2026-11-07: A release agent's "what's new" can describe cut rules as new features
The 8.9.26 release agent summarised the merge log as "Sparta: units heal slowly; Celts get the Ambusher; Mongols can pass borders at Peace; Switzerland can buy wonders". All four had been *removed* in that build (`ba5e57662`). It read the rule names in the merge subjects as additions. Check every player-facing "what's new" line against the merge's direction, i.e. the diff or the decisions log, before relaying it to the designer.

## 2026-11-07: Landing peace-two-party, mongol-horde, takeover-gap-trace (landing agent)
- **`remote.sh . -- 'cargo test ...'` fails with exit 127** (the quoted string is taken as one command name); pass the words unquoted: `remote.sh . -- cargo test --workspace --profile release-checked`.
- **`repin.sh` with `LEGATUM_OFFLOAD=1` silently runs locally when the box was marked unavailable** (`~/LegatumRuns/box/offload-unavailable-until`, set after a capacity refusal) and then dies in the sandbox with "Operation not permitted" on `target/`. Wait out the marker; do not run it locally.
- **Two landings that each shift Hard's play both move the fixed-map benchmark milestones** in `crates/sim/examples/hard_vs_human.rs` (`plain_hard_rivals_are_the_default_benchmark` vp_t100 1 to 0 under two-party Peace; `rival_search_is_the_old_benchmark` cities_t62 3 to 4 under Peace plus the Mongol horde). The pin step passes while these example tests fail, so they only show up in the `test` step; refresh them in the same landing and note which change moved them.
- **The "Peace is two-party" Guide paragraph restated the rule already in the paragraph above it** and used "binds" and "permanent teammates"; the UI reviewer caught it. A rule moment is stated once, in plain words.

## 2026-11-07: "Fix the expectation and merge" skips the green verify
The Peace and Mongol landings each ended on a red verify: a stale benchmark expectation in `hard_vs_human`'s pinned-outcome tests, plus a Playwright timeout. The landing agent fixed the expectations, passed the Rust workspace tests and merged without another full verify. Two-party Peace changed `vp_t100` in `plain_hard_rivals_are_the_default_benchmark`, and the horde changed `cities_t62` in `rival_search_is_the_old_benchmark`. Any AI or diplomacy change moves these pinned outcomes, and repinning doesn't touch them (noted 10-31 too). The rule stays: after any fix to a red verify, the branch needs one fully green verify before the merge. The lead ran an extended verify on master afterwards to close the gap.

## 2026-11-07: The designer's "training mode" idea had been built a month earlier, and nobody remembered
His idea: search the exact combat at a city attack, play the winning move, and log where Hard differed. That is `crates/ai/src/search/tactics.rs` option `x` (2026-10-06). It deviated from Hard in 36% of 4,666 decisions and gained +0.4 pp [−1.6, +2.3] over 512 games, because four candidate moves capped it. The state-of-the-AI page didn't list it, which is the drift he complained about. Before scoping any AI idea, grep `crates/ai/src` and the learnings for an earlier build. The lesson for the revival: the move generator is the ceiling, not the rollout count.

## 2026-11-07: `verify-remote.sh master` checks nothing on the app side
Verifying `master` itself diffs master against master. The changed-path planner then turns off the app lane (WASM, vitest, the app build, Playwright) and runs only fmt, clippy, the sim builds, Rust tests, smoke and repin. It prints VERIFY PASSED in about 4 minutes. To check master's health after merges, pass `--app` to `verify-remote.sh` (it has no `--full`; that flag is only on `tools/astra/verify-branch.sh`).

## 2026-11-07: A rule invented in a memo got presented to the designer as his own
Fable's first training-mode memo listed "Do not train on his games. They are the test set." I copied it into the next brief as "his rule", and then asked him about relaxing it. He never said it, and he wants the opposite: his games always train the AI, with an overfitting guard. Before calling anything "his rule", find his words in a skill, the decisions log or human-reasoning.md. A design memo's "do not" list is the memo writer's opinion.

## 2026-11-07: The bug-save copy of his lost Swiss game was his next game; the archive kept the real one
The copy in `~/LegatumRuns/bug-saves/swiss-8926-sparta-20261107/` was taken at 10:19, the second he abandoned the 30-turn Swiss game and started a new one, so it held turn 1 of the new game. The app writes every game, finished or abandoned, to `~/LegatumData/archive/` (`index.jsonl` lists outcome, turns and roster; the tape is `2026/MM/<game_id>.tape.json.zst`). Check the save's `turn` and roster before reviewing, and fall back to the archive. The archive has the tape and telemetry but no AI memory; replaying at the tag recovers the plans (0 mismatches here).

## 2026-11-07: Signing an Alliance with an empire under attack puts you at War with all its attackers at once, and the wars outlive it
Under the call-to-arms rules a new ally joins its partner's *defensive* wars immediately (`diplomacy.rs::alliance_wars`, `WarJoined`), with no call to decline; only the partner's own offensive wars raise a call. In the 8.9.26 Swiss game the designer accepted the one-city Aztecs' offer with their capital at 0 HP: he was at War with Sparta and the Celts the next turn, the Aztecs were eliminated that turn, the Alliance ended ("Team eliminated") and both wars stayed. Hard in his seat declines the same offer (the AI prices the immediate wars: −218) and keeps Zürich in 48 of 48 runs; with the Alliance signed it loses Zürich in 48 of 48. The offer card names the wars but not the ally's state.


## 2026-11-07: The nightly's faction shares are wins per game, the censuses' are wins per seat
The Swiss "regression" from 11.58% to 6.71% was partly a denominator change. The 11-05 playbook census reported Swiss wins per Swiss seat (3,610 seats in 4,096 eight-player games). The nightly's `win_share_by_faction` divides by all 4,096 games. On the same denominator the nightly v16 Swiss figure is 7.62%, not 6.71%. Most of the real drop, 3.22 pp, came from the shared `walls_garrison` and `army_war_extra` defaults (11-06), which made rivals stronger (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/swiss-gap-1107/README.md`). Before comparing a nightly share with a census share, convert to per seat (× games ÷ seats). Best/worst ratios are unaffected when every faction sits in about the same number of games. Also: `swiss_playbook` 3 gained in the census but lost at the T50 takeover on his map (15/32 → 12/32). A census gain doesn't guarantee a stronger seat in his positions.

## 2026-11-07: India's "resource starvation" was half supply misallocation
India's era-I army in the 8.9.26 Swiss win wasn't only a Niter shortage. It had 1 Iron deposit (3 supply), which Medieval units (Pikeman, Crossbowman, Knight) need, and Coal from T97. It spent the Iron on two Forges (T94, T121) and built just 4 Cavalry with the Coal, from T126. Hard funds buildings before units and never weighs a Forge's +3 Production against the unit that Iron would field. Its "best startable unit" also ignores older-era units that use a resource it does hold. Shortage is the normal case: the median empire has 2–3 of its era's resource against 4–9 wanted (game-rules §3). Any resource AI must allocate scarce supply between buildings and units. It is in the queued `obsolete-army-and-deposits` task.

## 2026-11-07: Search on top of Hard lost scenarios because its evaluation wasn't the game
The Training skateboard always includes Hard's own orders as a candidate, yet it lost 2 points on the war suite, with big swings both ways. A search can only underperform its baseline when its evaluation is wrong, and this one was wrong in three ways:
- **The arena:** a 15×15 cut with two empires and no aircraft (`training.rs:333`). The air scenarios swung −62 to +100.
- **The score:** arena Production lost, not the scenario's hold, deadline or VP goal. The hold and tepid scenarios lost 25–56 pp.
- **The plan:** each turn re-plans, and rollouts continue with plain Hard, so plans that need a follow-up never get one.

Fix: screen candidates in the arena, then confirm the top few against Hard's orders in full-game rollouts. Play a candidate only when it beats Hard there. Keep the plan across turns. Spec: `.progress/astra-queue/hold/training-round2-fidelity.md`.

## 2026-11-07: his Sparta win (8.9.26, 10 VP to 0) was not the lean quotas, and a Lab cut from an older rules version needs the version raised
- **Restoring the pre-11-06 army defaults on rivals changes nothing against his recorded orders.** Rivals with `army_war_extra` 5 and `walls_garrison` 0 kept the same cities as the control in every window (Mongols T15/T19, Aztecs T32/T37, Byzantium T56/T62; 32 paired runs). Both genes act only under a war plan or a Walls rule, and his victims had neither when his army arrived: 1 unit, with a Settler or wonder in production. A war quota of 10 with door 300 helped the Aztecs a little and hurt Byzantium. The lever is what a city builds with an army at the gate, not the quota.
- **Salting only AI seeds on a tape seat gives 32 copies of one game** (all arms identical). Reseed the dice too (`RESEED` in the throwaway `joint_assault_gate` patch). His orders then fail 57–86 times a run in later windows, so victim survival is an upper bound and only the paired arm differences mean anything.
- **Hard Sparta's gap is sieges and veterans, not the kit.** On his map Hard Sparta makes 17 city attacks a game to his 91 and takes 2.1 cities by T122 to his 18, while training more units (7.9 against 5 by T85) and keeping 0.5 level-3 units at T85 to his 5.
- **`cut.sh --state` refuses a state from an older `RULES_VERSION`** ("incompatible rules version: saved Some(19), current 21"). When the format changes in between are additive (here `required_teams` and `wonder_lost`, both serde defaults), raising the extracted state's `save_version` to the current one cuts and measures normally.
- **A scenario cut against Hard from his position can't test a defence:** Hard Sparta from his T62 position doesn't attack within 15 turns, so Hard Byzantium "holds" 50/50, and the scripted `--rusher` (which waits for Spearmen) didn't storm either.

- **2026-11-07: `codex exec -s read-only` can read the whole repo and every state file; the read-only sandbox only blocks writes.** In an empty scratch dir with `--ephemeral`, Luna ran `head` on `CLAUDE.md` and listed the lead's tapes folder. The LLM-teacher harness (`tools/llm-teacher/teacher.py`) therefore also passes `--disable shell_tool` (plus apps, plugins, computer_use and the browser features), after which the same probe returns `NO_TOOLS`; `teacher.py smoke` repeats that probe. Any future Codex call that must not see the repo needs the same flags.
- **2026-11-07: `claude -p` fails with "OAuth session expired" from inside an agent session**, even with a cleaned environment: the nested CLI reports `loggedIn: false`. Run the Haiku calls of the LLM-teacher harness from a normal terminal (the user's own shell), not from a sub-agent's Bash.

- **2026-11-07: Luna as a chooser over the operation-search candidates is not usable: with option order shuffled it names the same top pick on only 4 of 18 windows (22%) across 3 repeats, mean rank correlation 0.26, mean gain vs Hard's plan -1.3 pp, worst pick -43.8 pp.** It does speak the generator's grammar as a proposer (143 of 146 proposals in grammar, 24 distinct), but whether any proposal is worth +10 pp is unmeasured: `operation_search` has no extra-operations input yet. Round-4 referee: only 3 of 18 windows have a confirmed winner with the interval above zero, so agreement with the confirmed best is not scoreable below 5 clear windows. Results: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/llm-teacher-1110/README.md`.

## 2026-11-07: A finished landing agent kept waking up and sending stale advice
The morning's "lander" agent finished its 14 landings, then resumed several times when other agents messaged it by name. Each time it re-sent its old report and told live agents that master was at RULES_VERSION 18; master was at 20, then 21. A finished agent's view of master is frozen at its last turn. Give landers task-specific names, never a reusable one like "lander". Tell agents not to message finished agents. When a stale one resurfaces, correct the agents it messaged at once.
## 2026-11-07: his Celts win (8.9.26, 13 VP to 4): barbarian peace pays in a human's hands, the sack doesn't, and the tape is easy for Hard

- **Barbarian peace is a weapon once the Wild is called.** The Celts' camps raid every empire but the Celts, so an early Wild (his T88) points the barbarians at the neighbours: Sparta took 13 barbarian attacks after T88 against 2 before and spent 93 attacks on barbarians, Germany 13 against 1 and 67. With the rule switched off from the branch turn (`rule_experiments.faction_rules_off`, no core patch), barbarian attacks on Sparta and Germany fall 4–8x. Hard Celts calls the Wild at median T136, when it is only a closing 2 VP.
- **The 60-Gold sack bought nothing in a builder win:** 2,520 Gold from six sacks T124–T135, all after his only purchase, and 6,075 Gold unspent at the end. Sack off from T120: 41 → 37/48 on his orders (noise).
- **A takeover curve can have a hole.** Hard in his seat wins 34/48 from T2 and 39/48 from T20 but 0/48 from T10 (Germany 41/48): at T10 the world matches his game up to the T16 Byzantine Settler that Germany sniped, and Hard in his seat doesn't take it back at T17 as he did. Sweep around the first swing, as on the Swiss 8.9.24 tape.
- **This tape is easy for Hard: 87.9% at the designer-seat control** (Celts 8.9.25 was 2.9%). Swapping it in as the held-out Celts tape moves the held-out score from 29.0% to 57.4% at the same commit; rerun the control on one commit after the swap and treat the jump as a re-base.
- **Fresh brains with restored memory don't reproduce the tape at salt 0 early:** the tape seat read 0/48 from T2 to T100 (66–198 failed orders a run) and 41/48 from T120, so rule arms on his orders only measure the finish here.

## 2026-11-07: Sparta's desertion from first contact starts around T5 and collapses AI Sparta
Moving the desertion clock from T100 to first contact (designer's decision; branch `claude/sparta-desertion-contact`, not landed) changed AI Sparta's share from 12.6% to **2.4%** (4,096 games). Hard Sparta loses 14.2 units a seat, and 99% of seats lose at least one. First contact happens at about T5, when an early Scout meets a neighbour. The least-fought unit deserts first, so the Scout goes at T10 and then the first Soldiers. Only 56% of Sparta seats ever fight, with a median first war at T53. On the 8.6 Sparta benchmark map, Hard went from 46/64 to 0/64. Three AI tries (a barbarian hunt gene, an expedition gate change, forced early campaigns) all failed: no war gate fires before T10. Lesson: before switching a timing rule to an in-game event, check when that event actually happens in the AI census. The turn-100 gate had hidden how early contact comes.

## 2026-11-07: balanced founding sites (designer): every start totals 8 Food and 8 Production, and the census barely moves
Designer: "i find myself restarting if im not next to a forest or something because there isn't enough production otherwise". Before the change, the Food and Production of a start tile plus ring 1 (visible bonuses included) ranged 3 to 16 and 0 to 14 across 5,003 starts on 1,000 maps (Production p25 4, median 6, p75 7; within-map spread median 4 and p90 7 in both yields; 7% of starts (347 of 5,003) had Production 2 or less). After: every start is 8/8 on all 1,000 maps and the within-map spread is 0 (`crates/core/examples/start_yields.rs`).
- **The target had to be 8/8, not the old p75 (11 Food, 7 Production).** Terrain yields sum to 2 per tile (Grassland 2/0, Plains 1/1, Hills 0/2) or 3 (Forest 1/2), and a Coast tile is 1/0, so a start with `w` water tiles in ring 1 can reach at most 14 - w + (forests) Food plus Production, with Forests only on the 6 - w land ring tiles. 11/7 (18 in total) works for at most one water tile; 8/8 (16) works for two, and three water tiles reach 14 at most (a 7/7 target with three forests). Starts with three or more water tiles in ring 1 are no longer candidates (`START_MAX_RING_WATER` 2). Production 8 is above the old p75 of 7; Food 8 is below the old median of 10, which counted two to three Wheat/Cattle bonuses in the ring.
- **Resources stay out of ring 1** (the simpler of the two options): the guaranteed Wheat/Cattle/Stone moved from "within 2" to "exactly 2 hexes out". Everything else was already further out.
- **`site_quality` must count the founding territory as its fixed totals.** Without that, the search picked starts on pre-sculpt terrain and `validate_rivals`' 24-point spread check threw 301 attempts away over 100 two-player seeds (the old limit was 200). With ring 1 counted as 3 x (8+8), retries are back to the old level (Standard 16-22 per 100 seeds at every player count; Small 8p is the crowded case at 130, 247 with teams).
- **Maps for a given seed change, and tapes regenerate their map from the seed**, so `RULES_VERSION` went to 24 (precedent: the 2026-11 start-site change went 10 to 11); saves keep their own map but are refused by the version rule like every other bump. Human-seat benchmark maps are regenerated from their setups: per-map results flip (Mongols 0% to 100%, Germany 100% to 0%), so only the overall score is comparable, and it is not paired.
- **Measured (4,096 eight-empire Hard games per arm and seed, against master `3212f61aa`):** per-seat win rates moved by at most 1.4 pp except Switzerland on the held-out seed (8.32% to 6.94%, -1.38 pp [-2.60, -0.17]; main seed -0.42 [-1.61, +0.78]). Best/worst 2.48 to 2.60 (seed 9311003) and 2.16 to 2.52 (held-out 8241001); the worst seat is Switzerland in all four. Human-seat benchmark, plain Hard, 8 maps x 64 salts: 36.5% [34.0, 38.9] control against 33.8% [31.8, 35.7] on the branch (unpaired, different maps).
- A resources test that demands the bonus fill reach 75% of its target failed on one seed (48: 63%); the bound is now 60%. The fill is a count of deposits that cannot touch each other or the starts, so it varies with the map.
## 2026-11-07: Sparta's desertion is removed outright (RULES_VERSION 23, `claude/sparta-upkeep`)

The designer cut the rule (first the first-contact version, which collapsed AI Sparta to 2.4%, then the double-upkeep replacement) and Sparta's only drawback is now the +50% cost on Settlers and non-military buildings. Core: `restless_turns`, `Player::last_battle_turn`, `Unit::last_fought_turn`, the `RestlessRegiments` and `UnitDeserted` events, the view's `restless_*` fields and the `sparta_desertion` switch are gone (rule bit 43 stays vacant). AI: the desertion term in the quiet plan, `sparta_restless_target` and the determinizer's clock are gone; `FIRST_THIRD_TURNS` keeps its AI users. UI: the clock, banner, report lines and Guide paragraph are removed. Measured on the new engine, 4,096 Hard AI-only games each: Sparta wins 11.2% (seed 9311003) and 11.3% (held-out 8241001); best/worst faction 15.8%/6.3% (Mongols/Switzerland) and 16.0%/7.0%. Human-seat gate overall 0.363 [0.338, 0.387] (64 salts per map); designer-seat score held out 56.0% [53.6, 58.4], training 48.9% [46.3, 51.5] against the 57.4% / 44.4% control. Non-obvious: nothing else read `last_battle_turn` or `last_fought_turn`, so they could be deleted rather than kept for saves; the version bump refuses old saves anyway.
## 2026-11-07: his Germany win (8.9.27, 14 VP to 3): Hard can't win his seat, and a dice-bound tape can't price faction rules by outcome
- **Hard in his seat wins 0–5 of 48 from T2 to T120** (designer-seat 5.4%, master `25c1566fc`); the Aztecs win most runs. The other three games of the day read 56–88%. "The AI is bad" here means the rivals lost to his late plan (Science project → Flight T134 → two Bombers → five cities in 8 turns), not that the AI economies were weak.
- **His recorded war is dice-bound:** with no rule change and the dice reseeded, his own orders from T120 win 0 of 15. Every rule-off branch that breaks one late order collapses to about 2 VP, the same as a reseed. Price rules on the exact tape (salt 0) by the first failed order and direct accounting instead: projects off from T120 → Flight T136 → the T134 Bomber buy fails; −2 Food off from T120 → the identical game.
- **Hard treats Industry projects as a fallback only** (`crates/ai/src/production.rs`, "routine production skips Projects"): in 24 of 32 Hard-seat games, switching projects off changed nothing.
- **The Ruhr Foundry's −2 Food never bound:** 328 Food over 164 Foundry city-turns cost him 2 population by T120, and no Foundry city fell below 12 Food a turn. Review `docs/playtests/reviews/2026-11-07-8.9.27-germany.md`.

## 2026-11-08: same-era resource reveals (rules version 25); the AI's research order, not its valuation, decided when it learned them

- **Rule (designer, 2026-11-07):** each strategic resource is revealed by a technology of the era it is used in (Niter by Gunpowder, Coal by Industrialization, Oil by Combustion, Silicon by Autonomous Systems; Iron stays). Core change is four lines in `resources::stats().revealed_by`. The supply checks, the AI's supply valuation (`supply_value.rs` reads `SupplyView.revealed`) and the UI (`RESOURCE_INFO.revealedBy`, regenerated `rules.generated.json`) follow with no further code. Every unlock card, tech blurb, Guide paragraph and tour fixture that named an old reveal tech was edited by hand; `Education` and `Electricity` no longer reveal anything.
- **Census, 4,096 Hard games a run, seeds 9311003 and 8241001:** best over worst 2.48 to 2.11 (rule only) or 2.26 (with Hard's research genes) on 9311003, 2.15 to 2.17 or 2.31 on 8241001; nobody under 7.9%. Aztecs lose about 2.5 pp and Sparta 1.7 (their units want Niter and Coal early); Athens and Celts gain. `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/resource-reveal-1107/`.
- **With the rule alone Hard learns the reveal at the end of the era:** median turn into the era it is used in: Niter +7, Coal +25 of 28, Oil +16, Silicon +37. The old reveals came 4 to 12 turns before the era began. Cause: `research_order` puts the unit and defence techs first whenever the empire is threatened, and Industrialization and Autonomous Systems aren't in that list. A research value bonus alone barely moved it (`reveal_tech_bonus` 150: Oil +16 to +7, Coal still +25; even 400 left Coal at +25). Adding the reveal techs to the first tier (`reveal_tech_first` 1) is what works: Coal +8, Oil +7, Silicon +15 with a 150 bonus; first only (no bonus) gave Coal +13. Both genes ship on (150, 1).
- **Iron Working also joins that tier** (it reveals Iron): an empire with an enemy at the gates researches it before Mathematics, because the Classical units need the deposits; `a_neighbour_an_era_ahead_is_a_threat` and `research_responds_to_food_production_gold_and_army_bottlenecks` now say so.
- **Reveal to first use is short once the tech is learned:** Hard's median first Coal user is 11 turns after the reveal, Oil 5, Niter 7; Silicon 1 (the Future era has few turns left, so only 29% of seats reveal it by game end under the shipped genes, 53% under the old rule).
- **The telemetry that measured it:** `EmpireOpening.first_reveal_turn` and `first_use_turn`, each indexed by `Resource::STRATEGIC` (Iron, Niter, Coal, Oil, Silicon), absent when empty; `analyze.py` in the results folder gives the medians and the turns into the era.
- **Fixtures keyed to old reveal techs:** the modernization tests use an archived T142 state whose empire learned Education, Metallurgy and Industrialization for their reveals; `modernization::tests::saved` now adds the new reveal tech whenever the old one is known so the deposits stay visible. Unlock-density e2e: Education no longer has two rows, so Gunpowder, Combustion and Autonomous Systems take its place (the last Future card has no "Later" button).
- **Landing:** two other branches also took rules version 22 (this one was cut at 21); merging master kept 22 silently, so the version was raised to 23 by hand and the fixtures refreshed 22 to 23; at landing, Sparta (23) and balanced starts (24) had taken those numbers, so it landed as 25.
## 2026-11-07: Parallel rules branches keep colliding on RULES_VERSION, silently
At least three times today, a branch that bumped `RULES_VERSION` merged master after master had taken the same number. The merge kept the shared value with no conflict: objectives at 14, then resource-reveal at 22 against Germany's 22. Only a hand check caught it. With four rules branches in flight (Germany, Sparta, balanced starts, resource reveal), the lead now chains them and each takes master+1 at merge time. A static check would make this mechanical: when a branch changes rules files, `tools/gates/check.py` should require its `RULES_VERSION` to be strictly greater than master's.

## 2026-11-07: Scenarios are data, so they ship without a release
The Scenario Lab bundled its scenarios at build time (`import.meta.glob`) and the merge gate treated `scenarios/*.json` as code, so a new learning scenario waited for a playtest build and the full flow. Now the desktop app also reads `~/LegatumData/scenarios/lab/` each time the Lab opens (`scenario_dropins`, validated with the same `check_file` the engine runs on start), `tools/scenarios/publish.sh` drops a file there in seconds, and a Lab-only branch gets `tools/gates/scenario.py` instead of the verify and docs review. Catch: the installed app is behind master whenever a rules bump landed since its build (8.9.27 is rules 21, master 23 on 2026-11-07), so a scenario cut on master is refused by the installed app until the next build; the Lab shows it greyed out with "Made for rules version 23; this app is 21." instead of failing at Begin.
## 2026-11-07: a coalition that acts in the designer's scenario doesn't move AI-only games (gene `runaway_coalition`, on by the lead's call)
- **Measure "the runaway's army is away" from the target city, not from all its cities.** In "The Horde Looks East" the Mongol army attacking Byzantium stood next to Dyrrachium, a city it had captured there, so "8+ tiles from every Mongol city" never held and Sparta's second front fired 0 times in 64 runs. Measured per target, it fired 41 times.
- **At Peace, `ctx.reachable` is false into the rival's closed land.** A front that declares on assembly must test reach to the staging ring (3 tiles out), or every ally-at-Peace front reads unreachable.
- **No Peace with the runaway is the biggest single lever in the scenario:** Byzantium's starting cities held 0.5 → 2.6 with bit 1 alone (Peace with the Mongols 1.25 → 0.29 a run), because the horde no longer frees its eastern army by signing Peace. Bits 1+2: 3.1.
- **Full games don't move:** human-seat rivals arm +0.54 pp [−0.88, +1.90], only 154 of 2,048 games differ by T100, a T100 runaway (VP lead 4+) wins 60%/56% either way. The gate's Hard seat is rarely the runaway; a human-runaway scenario is the missing test. Report `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/coalition-front-1107/`.
- **Sim output now records `team_vp_turn_100`**, so a census can read pillar 11 ("does the T100 leader win?") by VP lead, not only by the tied-leader set in `vp_race.leaders`.
- **The runaway Peace gate must not inherit the ordinary "endangered home may sue" escape.** With it, India and the Celts signed Peace with the Mongols and freed the horde: Byzantine cities held 3.12 → 2.39, Peace with the runaway 0.59 → 1.96 a run (above the 1.25 control). Only a city about to fall (`collapsing`) escapes. Four older Peace tests now pin the gene to 0 because they test the ordinary escape.
- **In the scenario, second fronts start more often than they finish:** 41 started in 64 runs, 13 struck and ended (4 captured, 2 roster lost), 28 still running when the window closed. The cap is battle results, as in the operation search.
- **Against a rich, empty runaway (his Swiss hold at T134, 4 units for 11 cities), the second front never fires:** its army is never "away" and nobody nearby is at War with it, so bit 2 has no trigger. Bit 1 still acts (Alliances with the Celts 0 → 0.56 a run). A hollow leader needs a declare-and-strike path without the army-away test.
## 2026-11-07: battle-tactics Lab cards, and Lab runs that survived only a clean exit
- **Four short tactics cards, Hard 0/50 on each** (master `ff18a71cd`, states from the tags stamped `save_version` 22): `sparta-walled-nicomedia` (Sparta T64, six Spartan units, walled Nicomedia in 12 turns; Hard takes Syracuse and another town but never hits Nicomedia), `germany-two-cities-one-turn` (Germany T142, two Bombers, an Infantry and a Cavalry, Chalco and walled Xochimilco in 6 turns; Hard walks into Chalco in 48/50 and never strikes Xochimilco: 0 damage), `swiss-hold-biel` (Switzerland T132, ten Indian units around Biel, one Field Cannon inside, 6,104 Gold, 8 turns; Hard buys a Cavalry and a Phalangite and loses Biel on T136 in all 50 runs), `sparta-before-the-walls` (Sparta T28, empty unwalled Dyrrachium 9 tiles off in 12 turns; Hard never declares). Hard's 0/50 on the last one measures its plan, not its tactics; the other three put Hard's army next to the target.
- **A field battle can't be a Lab card yet:** objectives are hold, take, eliminate, cities and score (`crates/sim/src/scenario_cut.rs::objective`); there's no "destroy that army" condition.
- **Lab runs were saved only at the end or on Leave.** Closing the app mid-run lost every order, and his runs are training data for the battle search. The app now writes the run (with its archive tape) after every turn as `abandoned: true`, under one name per run (`<id>-<start time>.json`) that the finished run and its feedback replace; writes are queued so a late checkpoint never overwrites the finished run. Lab runs stay out of `~/LegatumData/archive` on purpose (mid-game tapes); training reads them from `~/LegatumData/scenarios/` and replays `archive.initial_state` with `resolve_turn` at the run's `engine_commit`.
- **A card briefing sentence over 30 words fails `app/src/ui/textArtifacts.test.tsx`;** run it (it needs a current `app/src/wasm-pkg`) after editing card text, before a verify.
## 2026-11-07: A "home endangered may sue for Peace" escape hollows out any coalition against a runaway
In `claude/coalition-front`, giving the runaway-leader Peace gate the usual escape (an endangered home may sue) undid half the effect in the Horde scenario. India and the Celts signed Peace with the Mongols, Peace per run rose from 0.59 to 1.96, and Byzantine cities held fell from 3.12 to 2.39. Coalition members always feel endangered, because the runaway is the one threatening them. Only the "a city is about to fall" escape keeps the coalition intact. Also: full-game human-seat gates barely see coalition behaviour. The gate's Hard seat is rarely the runaway, so only 154 of 2,048 games differed. Measure coalition AI in scenarios where the human is the runaway.

## 2026-11-08: Training search, round 2 — confirm in the full game, then score the goal
Branch `claude/training-round2`; results `docs/sim-results/training/round2-1108/`. Everything is off by default (`TrainingConfig::round2`), except that a melee charge on an enemy city is no longer pruned (that one affects the app's Training mode).
- **The designer was right that Hard plus search can't lose when its evaluation is right.** Re-scoring the arena's top plans in the whole game (aircraft, third parties, production; 8 rollouts of 4 turns, with common random numbers) and playing one only when it beats Hard's orders with the interval clear of zero took the war suite from −2.0 to **+3.7** (45% to 49%, 17 of 34 at parity). The skateboard's −62 and −56 swings (German air T214, Japan v Sparta, Swiss tepid T220) all disappeared. The arena alone dropped exactly what decided them.
- **The one remaining loss is the score, not the simulation:** Celts wild hold −21.9. Training loses fewer units and kills more, but breaks the VP hold less often. A material score can't see a hold. Add the scenario's goal to the score before anything else.
- **Searched tactics raise strike captures by half:** committed strikes on the round-4 windows captured 28.1% against 19.2% (Sparta 8.9.26 49% to 81%), and strike wins rose +2.6 pp [+1.0, +4.2]. A strike still loses to Hard's own plan on average (−9.4 pp), and the kill check stays at 3 of 39. Tactics were half the strike problem; target and timing are the other half.
- **The breach (8.9.27 Germany) is cheap to search:** shots and aircraft expected to zero the city, then the cheapest melee walks in the same turn. Never prune a melee charge on a city as a suicide, because the shots may zero it first. That change alone moved the lab's skateboard Training from 34.5% to 39.9%.
- **The full-game check is expensive in big games.** With every empire acting, the war suite never finished on the shared box; letting only the empires at war with either side give orders made it about 2.4 spot server-hours. On the desktop path (16 threads) 36% of battles still hit the 15 s cap before the check finished. Single-threaded (WASM) it almost never finishes, so that path needs arena-only plus the breach.
- **Spot box gotcha:** a spot interruption killed 4 box jobs at once 15 minutes in. Split long box runs into per-game jobs so partial results survive. A box job can't lower its own nice value once it's running.


## 2026-11-08: Training round 2 distilled into Hard (breach, siege stance), `claude/hard-breach`
- **The stance is the win, not the breach.** In the war suite (34 × 32, seat only), `siege_stance` 4 alone gave +5.0 (Hollow Swiss T76 0 → 100%, Tepid Swiss T220 62 → 100%). `breach` 100 alone gave +0.0 and fired once in 272 scenario runs. Two things stop the designer's breach in Hard's games: a plane is rarely in range of a city a walker can reach this turn, and the city has to be visible (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/hard-breach-1108/`).
- **A breach has to price the owner's reinforcement and the shots already ordered.** The first version sent the Bomber at a city the Artillery was already zeroing, while the Spearman next to it (the air planner's usual target) walked in first, because owners reinforce attacked cities before movement. Tepid Swiss T220 fell from 62% to 50%.
- **Own units claiming one empty city tile take turns; they don't bounce** (`rules/movement.rs` step 5). The "several melee arrivals would bounce" comment in `city_assault.rs` doesn't match the rule. A single-walker filter built on that comment cut Tepid T220 to 15%.
- **The lab gap past 34.5% is the strike forecast.** On the hills cell (Training 7/8) and the walled 100-HP Sparta cell, `forecast_siege` returns no capture where the search's rollouts take the city. A stance that trusts the forecast can't get there.
- **Zsh doesn't word-split `$VAR`:** `extra="--ai-params f"; cmd $extra` passes one argument. Use an array (`extra=(--ai-params f); cmd "${extra[@]}"`). It silently broke a batch of scenario runs.
- **The tactics cards were saved at rules 22 on their branch.** Rehash them with `rehash_scenarios` before running them on 23, or the loader refuses them.
## 2026-11-07: A better siege estimate made Hard braver and worse
`siege_oracle` (training increment 1; archived as `archive/astra/siege-oracle`) fitted Hard's assault estimate to combat-lab outcomes. War-suite captures rose 6.1 pp, but units lost per captured city rose from 7.67 to 10.29, and Hard got weaker in the human seat (seat −7.3 pp, rivals −5.1 pp). Knowing more fights are winnable made Hard commit to fights that its tactics then lost expensively. Calibration without better tactics just spends more units. It fits today's main finding: execution (battle tactics) is the bottleneck, so estimates and plans that make Hard fight more backfire until it fights better.
- **`siege_stance` 4 on the designer seat at 96 salts (follow-up):** all tapes +0.4 [−2.0, +2.8], held out +1.5 [−2.1, +5.1], India 8.9.25 −2.5 [−6.6, +1.6]. Doubling the salts didn't move it off flat. It misses the turn-on bar (held-out interval excluding a drop of more than 2 points) by 0.1, so it stays off. A designer-seat effect worth turning a gene on for needs about 200 salts, or a gene with a bigger effect.

## 2026-11-08: the siege forecast priced garrison sorties that never happen (`claude/siege-forecast-calibration`)
- `forecast_siege_rounds` let the besieged garrison charge the weakest attacker every round. Hard garrisons in the combat lab made 0 melee sorties in 403 siege turns, so storms the search wins came out as "no capture". Gene `forecast_garrison_holds` 1 plus `siege_stance` 6 takes the lab from 29.8% to 41.7% (41.1% on held-out dice) and the war suite +5.1. The designer seat at 96 salts stays flat (−0.1 [−2.5, +2.3]), so the defaults stayed 0 at first (`docs/sim-results/ai-quality/siege-forecast-1108/`; superseded below: the guarded arm shipped).
- The calibration does nothing at `siege_stance` 4: four rounds past arrival is too short for a melee storm on a garrisoned city even with the sortie removed. Six or eight rounds are needed, which matches the 2026-10-17 lab finding that storms often take five or six.
- **200-salt designer seat, `forecast_garrison_holds` 1 + `siege_stance` 6:** all tapes −0.1 [−1.8, +1.5], held out −1.5 [−4.0, +1.0]. Mongols 8.9.25 −3.7 and India 8.9.25 −2.4 were negative at 96 salts too, so this looks like a real cost on those tapes, not noise. Human seat: seat only +1.37 [−0.34, +3.08], rivals only −1.86. The held-out bar (≥ −3.0) failed, so the defaults stayed 0 until the guards below. Before re-proposing, look at what the storm does on Mongols 8.9.25: Hard plays the Mongols there, and the Mongol horde AI with longer storms may overcommit its riders.
- **Tracing the designer-seat cost found two mechanisms, not one** (seat only via `seat_run --continue hard,params:FILE`, paired dice; seat-only runs separate the seat's own play from the rivals getting stronger). Mongols 8.9.25 T60 (−22 pp): the horde left a threatened front to storm a city and lost a young city behind it. India 8.9.25 T80 (−12.5): the calibrated forecast in the plan layer, plus a stance on another war's city, turned a dead war "pressing", so Hard refused the Peace it otherwise signs and lost its army. Fixes: `stance_home_guard` 6 (target-aware: a home's guards may still hit a city near that home; without the exception Hollow Swiss T76 went 100% → 3%), `stance_plan_war` 1, and `forecast_garrison_holds` 1 limited to the stance. The guarded arm passed the non-inferiority rule (200 salts: all +1.2 [−0.5, +2.9], held out +0.5 [−2.0, +3.0]; human seat-only +1.46) and is on by default.
- **Gotcha:** a forecast "calibration" leaks into every caller of `forecast_siege`: the plan layer's campaign values and Peace terms, not just tactics. Scope a calibration to the decision it was measured on.
## 2026-11-07: A better siege estimate made Hard braver and worse
`siege_oracle` (training increment 1; archived as `archive/astra/siege-oracle`) fitted Hard's assault estimate to combat-lab outcomes. War-suite captures rose 6.1 pp, but units lost per captured city rose from 7.67 to 10.29, and Hard got weaker in the human seat (seat −7.3 pp, rivals −5.1 pp). Knowing more fights are winnable made Hard commit to fights that its tactics then lost expensively. Calibration without better tactics just spends more units. It fits today's main finding: execution (battle tactics) is the bottleneck, so estimates and plans that make Hard fight more backfire until it fights better.

- 2026-11-08 (lead): **The designer-seat gate can be noninferiority when lab and war suite clearly gain.** The siege-forecast fix (`forecast_garrison_holds` + `siege_stance` 6) raised the combat lab 29.8% → 41.7% and the war suite by +5.1. The designer seat stayed flat at 96 salts (−0.1 [−2.5, +2.3]): its tapes rarely hinge on one city assault. The three-gate "all improve" rule would block tactical fixes forever. New rule for tactics-only changes: designer seat all-tapes lower bound ≥ −2.0 and held-out lower bound ≥ −3.0 at 200 salts, plus a human-seat gate not significantly worse. Strategy and valuation changes still need the designer seat to improve. Root cause found: `forecast_siege_rounds` had the besieged garrison sortie every round, and Hard garrisons made 0 sorties in 403 lab siege turns.

- 2026-11-08 (lead): **A hung box job stalled the whole landing queue overnight.** balanced-starts' fixture job sat at "box: shipping a patch" from 03:41 to 09:20. Its waiter loop (`while ! grep ...; do sleep 10; done`) had no deadline, and the coordinator was waiting on balanced-starts, so nothing landed in 6 hours. Fixes: every waiter on a box job gets a deadline (e.g. 2x the job's usual wall time) and reports when it hits it; commit before submitting so jobs don't ship patches; the lead checks queue progress against the clock, not only on notifications.

## 2026-11-08: the same-era reveal's fixed-dice gate drop (36.5% to 26.2%) is dice, not an AI bug
- **Gate arms:** control `d91710fb`, branch `bdc021888` (rules only: the `reveal_tech_bonus` gene defaulted to 0 there, so the arm is the pure rules change). Nearly all of the drop was `playtest-8.6-sparta-win` (57 to 6 of 64) and 8.4-sparta (26 to 17).
- **The 64 salts of one map are a handful of games.** All salts share one set of dice, so on 8.6-sparta the control's 64 games ended in 16 distinct (win, end turn) outcomes and 37 of them were the same game (win at T175); the branch's 64 had 9 outcomes and 50 were the same loss at T181. A map result is close to one coin flip, and the printed salt interval doesn't see that.
- **Traced salt 1 on both builds (throwaway per-turn tech/supply/unit/production trace in `hard_vs_human`):** the two games are identical until T92, when the control's Sparta learns Education and sees Niter and the branch's doesn't (Gunpowder came at T104 in both, so Niter was visible 12 turns earlier, Coal 43 turns earlier: Metallurgy T109 against Industrialization T152). Sparta's production diverges at T97-T102 (Markets against Rangers and a Workshop), Niter supply starts at T109 against T118, Riflemen need the Coal and appear around T146 in the control only. Nothing is stuck: queues advance, Niter and Coal supply start the turn after the tech, no idle supply, no orders failing. By T150 the branch's Sparta has 1240 army to 1891 and 7 cities to 8, the final VP is 9 to 10 for Sparta and 11 to 6 for India. A delayed reveal is a real but small cost on a near-tied map, not a planning or valuing bug.
- **With each salt on its own dice (`LEGATUM_HVH_RESEED_DICE=1`, 8 maps x 64, Hard in every seat), the drop disappears:** control 21.9%, rules-only branch 23.8%, branch with `reveal_tech_bonus` 150 in every seat 22.1%. 8.6-sparta reads 42, 38 and 33; 8.4-sparta 19, 27 and 23. Read the reveal change on the reseeded dice (or a league), never on the fixed-dice gate alone (see 2026-10-18).
- **Genes did not help on the fixed dice either** (210114: 8.6-sparta 10 of 64, 8.4-sparta 1 of 64): the swing on those two maps is the dice flipping, in both directions.

## 2026-11-08: a hidden wariness builds the bloc, but all-in coalition wars cost the AI until battles improve (genes `runaway_coalition` bits 4, 8, 16, 32, `claude/coalition-bloc`; default 55 = bits 1, 2, 4, 16, 32 since the same day, bit 8 off)
- **Wariness replaced the runaway score as the bloc's trigger.** Each AI reads every other empire from public signs (recent captures 0/3/8/30/45/55, eliminations, visible army, VP lead, scaled by nearness), and every response is a seeded draw whose percent is the wariness (or the score): no cutoff, so a leader cannot sit one point under a line and one or two captures give at most 8. The old 60-point runaway cutoff went the same way (`runaway()` is now a draw at the score). Bit 4 values an Alliance at Peace with a partner wary of the same empire and waives `bloc_size_penalty` for such a bloc.
- **The bloc forms and AI-only wars do not rise:** 512 eight-Hard games, wars per game 16.49 to 16.31, alliances 5.06 to 6.77, largest bloc at T100 2.07 to 2.45 (the old free-bloc failure was 36 wars a game). The Horde card's seat Alliances go 0.14 to 1.7 a run.
- **All-in coalition wars (bit 16) are tactics-limited.** Strikes fire four times as often (Horde 0.17 to 1.60 struck a run, `runaway-conqueror` objective 39 to 47 of 64), but captures per war and city battles per war do not move (about 3). The first version, every war a wary enemy fought with a third team all-in, cost the Hard seat 3.6 pp [-4.9, -2.3] on the reseeded gate: the AI commits armies to wars its battles then lose, the same finding as `siege_oracle`. Making the all-in a draw on wariness squared repaired it (-0.6 [-2.3, +1.0] for all bits).
- **A2 declares but takes nothing:** a hollow or counting-down runaway is struck without the army-away test (wars on the Athens hold runaway 1.00 to 2.00), yet Geneva stays 0/64 in `celts-break-the-reserve`: the Celts' army fights Sparta and Athens, no unit is within 12 tiles of Geneva, and even at radius 20 only 0.06 strikes a run strike. Check where the seat's army is before blaming the trigger for a scenario that never fires.
- **Hidden gates:** the human-seat gate cannot see coalition work (rivals arm +1.5 to +2 pp for the seat, seat arm neutral). The Mongol conqueror card (`runaway-conqueror`) is the one that moves. Scenarios must be re-versioned after every rules bump (`byzantium-empty-horde` was deleted by the sweep, restored from the old branch and refreshed with `tools/sim/refresh-save-version-fixtures.py`).
- Receipts: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/coalition-bloc-1108/`.
- 2026-11-08 (lead): **No agent here can click through the built desktop app.** The 8.9.28 cut stalled on the in-app Training and Lab drop-in checks: subagents get only the browser tools, not computer use, and Playwright can't drive the Tauri webview (WebKit is banned on the Mac). Launching the built binary with `HOME` pointed at a scratch home does check the launch-time effects safely (app-info.json, training/ created, saves isolated). Until desktop paths have a headless test, the user's first minutes with a build are the in-app check: say so in the release notes and ask him to try those paths first.

- 2026-11-08 (lead): **The Mac WebKit map check can't run while the screen is locked.** During the 8.9.28 cut, the hidden WKWebView got no frames and sat 45 minutes with no output. Run it while the user is at the Mac, or skip it with a note when the map renderer hasn't changed, and give it a deadline.

## 2026-11-08: Hold Biel reads 50/50 for Hard on master only because rules 25 blinded India to its Coal; in the cut position Hard still loses Biel every run (`claude/hold-biel-defense`)
- **Current master (0b0c1924f), 50 runs:** 50/50 met, median 1200, against the recorded baseline's 0/50 (every run 1080, Biel lost at T135). Bisected on master's first-parent commits with the same scenario state: 0/50 at 32b95728c (rules 24), 50/50 at 8d9f7c871 (rules 25, same-era reveals) and at every later commit. Not the new AI: with `siege_stance`, `forecast_garrison_holds`, `stance_home_guard`, `stance_plan_war` and `runaway_coalition` 3 set back, or `reveal_tech_bonus` and `reveal_tech_first` at 0, it stays 50/50. Putting Coal's reveal back on Metallurgy (one line in `resources.rs`, not committed) brings master back to 0/50.
- **Why:** the state was cut on rules 22 and only rehashed for 23 to 25. India knows Metallurgy but not Industrialization, so from rules 25 it no longer knows Coal: its Coal supply drops, the two Coal Cavalry that broke Biel (units 155 and 166) go idle, and India pulls its army back on T133 (Biel stays at 140 HP). The designer's run (8.9.28, rules 25, rated "too easy", Fun 1) was in that weaker position too: in his events too, nothing attacks Biel after T132.
- **Every rehashed scenario has the same hole.** In 13 of 14 Lab cards and all 13 war cases, some empire knows an old reveal tech but not the new one. In 10 Lab and 3 war cases, units or buildings are still drawing on the lost resource (Hold Biel, Hold the South, Germany two cities, Celts reserve, Mongols eight points and wide India, Sparta next door, India Chur ring, Mongol bombers T150, Swiss tepid T180). A rules change that moves what a state means needs a state migration for the scenarios (or a re-cut), not only a rehash. Check with a scan of `techs` against `supply` records.
- **Hard in the cut position (India given Industrialization, rehashed; 0/50 on master, also with `siege_stance` 0):** traced turn by turn. Biel goes from 200 HP to 140, 51 and then 0 under India's two Cavalry (built-in Siege, full damage), and is taken on T135. Hard's mistakes:
  - **Purchases:** `buy_ready_defenders` ranks by strength per Gold, so Biel got a Cavalry (595), then a Horseman (173), then a Soldier. It never bought the Rifleman (456), the anti-mounted unit the designer bought. A prototype gene that buys the strongest unit countering a visible attacker's class only reached 1/50: purchases alone don't decide it.
  - **Fire:** both Field Cannons shoot the Spearmen and Rangers, not the two Cavalry doing all the city damage.
  - **Bought units:** on T133 they leave Biel (140 HP, two Cavalry adjacent) to chase an Aztec Assault Trooper four tiles away.
  - **On T135, with Biel at 0 HP:**
    - With `siege_stance` on, the siege-stance pass orders Biel's only garrison (the Field Cannon) out toward Allahabad. Allahabad is 4 hexes away, so `stance_home_guard` lets it go.
    - With `siege_stance` off, the units next to Biel `defend_cities`-skip beside it and nobody attacks the 36 HP Cavalry next to the city. That Cavalry walks in. Only one military unit fits on a tile (the classes are military and civilian), so the garrisoned city cannot take a second defender.
  - A fix needs city-damage-focused fire, melee finishing wounded attackers next to a threatened city, and the stance pass never pulling a city's last garrison. That is a tactics change, not a small one.

- **`agent-worktree.sh acquire` reset existing branches (2026-11-08):** it ran `checkout -B <branch> <base>`, which moved an existing branch back to the base and orphaned its commits (a lane's WIP commit on astra work was lost from the branch this way). Acquire now checks an existing branch out where it is and refuses if another worktree holds it; only a new branch is cut from the base. Test: `tools/ops/tests/test-agent-worktree-acquire.sh`.

- **Stored Lab baselines went stale after rules 25 (2026-11-08):** rules 25 moved what a resource-reveal tech means, and the rehash only re-signed the old states. Stored baselines described a position the engine no longer plays: Hold Biel stored 0/50 but replayed 50/50 until the state was migrated, then 0/50 again (India regained Coal visibility). `scenario-reveal-migrate` (one-off) repaired 14 Lab cards and 12 war cases; `scenario-cut` applies the same repair to saves recorded before rules 25. After any rule that changes what a state means, re-record the baselines of every kept scenario on the current engine, not only rehash.

## 2026-11-08: census win share is per game, not per seat
- **Gotcha:** the census `win_share_by_faction` (and the nightly row built from it) divides a faction's wins by all games, but each faction sits in only about 8/9 of them (nine factions, eight seats), so per-game shares understate every faction and reorder close ones. The 8.9.29 census (4,096 Hard games, seed 9311003, master 1fb9adae4) per game had Celts 13.1%, Sparta 12.9%, Athens 12.7%; per seat they are 14.7%, 14.4%, 14.3%. Per seat, best/worst is Mongols 16.5% / Switzerland 9.0% = 1.83 (rules 25), against 2.33 on the rules-16 nightly. Quote per-seat rates (wins / games the faction played, from `merged.json.gz` `players[].faction` and `winner_factions`) whenever comparing factions or against the 1.5x pillar.

- **City defence landed; three existing pins moved with it (2026-11-08):** `hard-city-defense-tactics` took fair Hold Biel from 0/50 to 21/50 (Hard still loses 29/50). A verify on the merged branch failed three tests the Codex report called green: the default AI tape pins (repin), `war-swiss-hold-biel`'s id (must equal its file name), a garrison test written for the old "rest instead of a late shot" rule, and the `hard_vs_human` first-map fixture (4 cities at T50/T62 instead of 3). A breached city's wounded garrison now shoots an adjacent attacker instead of resting. Open: one war-suite Mongol bomber run reports an illegal shared-tile state, cause unknown.
- **`walled-chain-push` fails the strategy bar (2026-11-08):** Nicomedia 0/50 to 20/50, war suite +0.4, but the designer seat is 38.1% training / 58.9% held out against master's 39.6% / 59.2%, so it does not improve the designer seat. Measured before city-defense landed; rebase and re-measure before reviving. Cloud war binaries went stale across checkouts: compare matched fresh reports only.
- **Check escorts before a card says "unguarded" (2026-11-08):** the Celts Settler card's brief said the German Settler was exposed, but its stored state has a German Soldier on the same tile. The docs copy pass found it by decoding the state. Generated briefs must be checked against the stored state, not the task report.
## 2026-11-08: the wariness coalition reads the runaway and still applies no pressure (8.9.29 Aztecs coach review)
- **Wariness stays high without captures.** Captures count for 40 turns (`threat.rs::RECENT_TURNS`), so his T27–T42 captures faded by T82, but army size and VP lead kept every AI at 48 to 82 toward him from T50 (nearness 100%). "3 to 5 captures" isn't what drives it in a human game; the read was right all game.
- **The bloc fails on Gold, not on wariness.** Bloc values were positive for India-Celts (56 to 66) and Sparta-India (59 to 61), yet no Alliance formed at Peace: the Alliance score's upkeep term ("would empty our treasury within 20 turns", India at 4 to 20 Gold) and "wars we would join" (India's own war with Sparta) outweighed the shared coalition target (+25). Both Alliances that did form followed his declarations.
- **Strikes wait on the staging ring.** All three bit-2 strikes expired with 1 attack between them. The Celtic one held 7 units three tiles from an unwalled one-Spearman city for 15 turns because its forecast said 6% (era-1 Spearmen in era 4). A strike whose forecast never reaches a capture neither upgrades nor retargets; it spends its whole budget parked. Walled empty cities drew units to exactly three tiles (just outside Walls range) and no further.
- **Hard storms fine when the forecast agrees:** from his T94 position Hard takes walled Therapne 49/50. The failure is in which strikes the AI sets up (obsolete armies, 0-VP targets), not the storm itself.

## 2026-11-08: a fresh build worktree doesn't reuse a cloned target
- **Gotcha (8.9.30 cut):** building the playtest in a new detached worktree with `target/` and `app/src-tauri/target/` cloned by `cp -Rc` from the 8.9.29 worktree still recompiled every workspace crate (cargo's fingerprints include the absolute source path), so the Tauri build ran past 45 minutes against about 8 for 8.9.29, which reused its own worktree. Reuse the previous build worktree (`git checkout --detach <sha>` after committing or clearing its leftovers) instead of cloning into a new path.

## 2026-11-08: fast-path playtest builds showed "Dev · after 8.9.29"
- **Gotcha:** the title-screen label is baked in by Vite (`app/vite.config.ts`) during `npm run tauri:build`, from `LEGATUM_BUILD_LABEL` or else `git describe` against the last `playtest-*` tag. The 8.9.29 and 8.9.30 fast-path cuts set `LEGATUM_BUILD_LABEL` only on `scripts/build-wasm.sh` and tagged after building, so 8.9.30 (6f594cfb2) reads "Dev · after 8.9.29 (6f594cf)". The code is right; the label is not. Fast path: tag `playtest-X` first, or pass `LEGATUM_BUILD_LABEL="Playtest X"` to the `tauri:build` command itself.

## 2026-11-09: card loop: no Lab card has a valid designer seed, and a closed-loop line search beats Hard on cards in seconds
- **No valid designer seed.** He has finished runs on only two Lab cards (Hold Biel, Six Spartans and a Wall), both on revision 1; both cards became revision 2 when `scenario-reveal-migrate` granted the moved reveal techs (2026-11-08), so the state hashes differ. `legatum-sim card-loop --run` now refuses such a run by name. Any rules bump that re-cuts cards orphans his runs: ask him to replay a card after it is re-cut, or the loop has no designer reference (parity count 0 of 0 today).
- **A fixed order list is not a line.** The first card loop replayed his orders open-loop and every fresh seed hit an invalid order by T134. A line must be overrides on top of Hard keyed by unit and city ids, each falling back to Hard's order when the engine refuses it in that position (`validate_orders`); then one line plays on any dice.
- **Cheap candidates already win cards.** With no designer seed, a greedy turn-by-turn search over "no new order", the battle search's orders, the breach, "march every nearby unit on the target city" and "garrison the threatened city" found lines that win 50 of 50 held-out seeds on Nicomedia (Hard 2/50) and Ride to Dyrrachium (Hard 1/50), and 35/50 on Defend Essen (Hard 0/50), in 7 to 90 seconds a card on this Mac.
- **Dyrrachium is one order:** Hard upgrades Horseman #42 on T37 (`plan_modernization` runs in `plan_cities`, before `plan_military`, and `upgrade_waits_for_attack` only checks for an attack this turn); giving it no order takes the card from 1/50 to 50/50. Nicomedia's divergence is target choice (`press_strike` goes for pop-1 Syracuse), which Hard can't be blamed for, since it doesn't see the card's objective. Essen's is a Rifleman bought in Berlin, not the empty threatened city.
- **One split.** The loop first kept its own split; it now reads the scenario gate's `docs/sim-results/scenario-gate/split.json` (9 of the 25 Lab cards held out), and `legatum-sim card-loop` refuses to search a held-out or unlisted card. The first nightly had already searched eight cards the gate holds out (Athens Break the Hold, both Aztec cards, Byzantium, Celts Steal a Settler, Defend Essen, India No Niter, Mongols Eight Points) and wrote five tasks for them; they are kept but marked contaminated in the queue's README, so the Essen finding above must not be used to tune.
- **Gotcha:** this agent sandbox refuses sccache ("error writing dependencies ... Operation not permitted"); build with `RUSTC_WRAPPER=` (as in the 10-xx entries).

## 2026-11-11: occupation lock built as an off-by-default experiment

- **Where the lock lives.** `City::occupied_from` (set in `battle::capture_city`, skipped from saves when absent) plus `rules::occupation::lock`, which computes the lock live from the city, the war state and whether the civ is alive. Nothing is stored on units, so Peace and elimination release without a cleanup pass, and a replacement unit is locked by standing in the city.
- **Four places must agree.** Validation (`units::validate_unit_order`), resolution (`apply_unit_orders`, the per-round filter in `movement_round`, which also drops a standing route), the player views (`CityView`/`UnitView::occupation_lock`) and the AI's order filter (`drop_leaving_orders_of_locked_units`, called from `Brain::orders` and the scripted Hard pipeline). Missing the AI filter makes every locked unit's order an `OrderFailed`.
- **Disband must be refused,** or it dodges the lock and lets a replacement walk in on the same terms. Upgrades are in place and stay allowed.
- **Gotcha:** `RULES_VERSION` is 27 for the whole batch; the save header refuses other versions, tapes and hashes are unchanged with the switch off because both new fields are skipped when absent.
- **The city freeze reuses the lock's condition** (`occupation::city_freeze`), applied in three places: `process_cities` skips `produce`, `economy::yields_in` zeroes the Production yield with a labelled line (so the panel shows a reason, not a silent zero), and `buy_check`/`company::blocker` refuse. Reusing `BuyBlock::Resistance` for the purchase block kept every `match` on the enum untouched. Capture resistance zeroes all yields and growth; the freeze is Production and purchases only.
- **Peace needs both sides.** The occupier cannot end the lock or freeze itself; a loser that refuses Peace keeps them until it is eliminated. `wants_peace` has no input from the lock.

## 2026-11-11: integrating the four rules-27 branches (relief chain, aircraft XP, World Ages, occupation lock)

- **Four branches that each set `RULES_VERSION` 27 only collide on generated files.** Every merge conflicted on scenario JSON (version line and state hash) and, once, on `pins.json` and the one-line save-compatibility paragraph in `game-rules.md`; no Rust conflicted. Take either side of generated files, hand-merge the prose line (one clause per branch), and regenerate once at the end (`refresh-save-version-fixtures.py` found nothing to do, `rehash_scenarios` rewrote the scenario encodings with identical hashes, `repin.sh` moved one pin).
- **Only `scripted_hard_city_fire_tape` moved** (the scripted Hard city-fire tape): it is a siege tape, so the relief chain and per-hit carried damage change it. The mixed tapes, runner digests and scenario hashes did not move.
- **The occupation freeze and the World Ages Arch compose by construction:** the Arch is an ordinary Production item, `process_cities` skips `produce` for a frozen city (even with the full price already stored), and `buy_check` refuses with the "Occupied: ... builds and buys nothing until Peace" text because `city_refusal` supplies the reason inside `BuyBlock::Resistance`. Tribute and Demobilise claims are empire-level (`claim_check` never reads a city), so occupied cities do not stop them.
- **The lock is positional, so it composes with the relief chain for free:** a relief unit that becomes the garrison of an occupied city is locked from then on, and a locked garrison never becomes relief for another city (its route is dropped and its move refused before the movement round plans reserves).
- **Aircraft XP on a carried kill is the city-attack XP, not kill XP:** a strike's overflow that kills the garrison is no fight of its own (`combat_xp` awards per fight), so the plane earns `city_attack_xp` exactly as a ground attacker would. A test needs a friendly Scout for vision or the strike silently fails.
- **Known gap, not fixed:** AI `determinize` copies `occupied_from` only for the searching player's own cities, so the search assumes enemy cities are not frozen.
- **Tribute in the final Age cost 300 (a no-brainer); it is 800 now,** continuing the 70/170/270/400/580 curve.

## 2026-11-11: UI review fixes for rules 27 (per-hit carry, World Ages, occupation lock)

- **A rules-version bump left the old rule wording in four app texts.** The per-hit carry replaced the pooled overflow in core, but the city strength tooltip, the Guide, `garrisonHeadline` and the Siege hint still said "excess reaches its garrison at the defence ratio" or "added up". Grep the old wording across `app/src` and `game-rules.md` in the same change as the rule; `copyRules.ts` now bans "at the defence ratio" and "scaled by the ratio" so a stale string fails the copy lint.
- **A child's `useEffect` runs before its parent's.** The score ledger scrolled to the open Age in its mount effect, but `ObjectiveSheet` opens the `<dialog>` in its own effect afterwards, so the body had no layout and the scroll silently did nothing. Wait a frame (`requestAnimationFrame`) before measuring inside a dialog that opens on mount.
- **An experiment's second rule needs its own UI pass.** The occupation freeze lived in core and the city panel's note, but every other surface (pending list, map banner, tile card, Buy reason, locked unit card) still treated the city as an idle one with a build to choose. When a rule makes an object inert, list the places that render it as active (grep `unchosen_cities`, `productionProgress`, `cityProductionWarning`) and fix them together. The engine side was one line in the wasm `city_automation_orders` (skip a city with `occupation_lock`).
- **When two refusals apply, give the one the player can end.** A city both resisting and frozen now says "Occupied ... until Peace" (the freeze outlasts resistance), through the same order in `economy::buy_check` and `company::blocker`.
- **The assault forecast needed one more number for the carried-hit breakdown:** `AssaultGarrison::carried`, the expected HP the garrison loses to the carry of the hit that empties the city. The TS words it as a note on the city's own row ("carries −M to the garrison"); it never recomputes `carried_share`. A separate sub-line pushed the walled-city card past the window at both 1440x900 and 1000x600 (`e2e/city-assault-forecast`); the forecast card is `pointer-events: none`, so a hover breakdown is not possible there either.
- **A card that must fit at 1000x600 gets the rank as words on the owner line, not a second row:** the 34 px rank badge in the aircraft card's header pushed its body past the panel's height (`PromotionLine inline`).

## 2026-11-11: A held garrison hides under its own city banner

- **A lock mark on the token alone is invisible in the case that matters.** A locked unit always stands in a city, and an own city's banner is drawn above the units, so a padlock drawn with the token sat under "Vestige" (8.9.31 playtest: "it just feels like a bug"). `render.ts` now collects the padlock positions in the unit loop and draws them after the banners.
- **The turn holds a locked unit by itself** (`occupation::hold_locked_units`, end of `resolve_turn`): melee takes `Sleep`, ranged takes `AutoFire` (the session fires it each turn, so the lock never costs a shot), and its route and `needs_orders` are cleared, so it is never in the Return cycle. Locked units are skipped by `sleeping_threats`, so they never wake. A freshly captured city's capturing unit can arrive with a `goto` past the city on the last movement round; the hold drops it, which is the likely source of the dashed line to a ringed marker in the designer's screenshot.
- **An empire eliminated by the capture releases its own lock.** The test for the hold needs the civ it was taken from to keep a city, or `check_elimination` frees the unit before the hold runs.
- **World Ages now draw ONE card per Age** (`objectives::drawn_from_pool`, user 2026-11-11). `EraRecord::scored.slot` is always 0 in a deck game. No `RULES_VERSION` bump: 27 is the unreleased 2026-11-11 batch (master is 26) and the workflow rule is one bump per batch.

- **Cutting the crowns (2026-11-11, `claude/cut-crowns`): the public army, economy and advancement numbers were load-bearing outside VP.** The three crowns' `TitleView.values` were the only place the AI saw a rival's army, treasury scale and technology progress (win-chance features, determinized rival gold, hollow and runaway reads, intel). They now ride on `PlayerPublic.army / economy / advancement`; deleting the titles outright would have zeroed those reads silently. Recorded `PlayerView` fixtures (`crates/ai/src/fixtures`, `tests/fixtures`) carried `VpSource::Title`, `Chronicle::Crown` and `titles`, which no longer deserialize (an unknown enum variant is an error, an unknown struct field is not), so they were migrated once with the values moved onto the players; the learned value net lost its "crowns held" input by dropping that weight column (version 2, golden scores recomputed), the macro model its two title features.

## 2026-11-11: Peace clears the occupation record, or the old owner re-freezes the city at will

- **`City::occupied_from` used to live until the city changed hands** (Almaliq, 8.9.32.1 playtest): capture, Peace, then the Mongols declare war again and the city is "Held" again (no Production, no purchases, garrison locked). The record is now cleared by `occupation::clear_released`, run in `resolve_turn` after `diplomacy::resolve_boundary` and before `hold_locked_units`, on every city whose owner is not at war with the recorded civ (same `at_war` test as the lock, so teams count) or whose recorded civ is dead. It runs every turn boundary, so a Peace is observed before any later declaration (declarations also resolve in the boundary) and a fresh capture, made at war, is never cleared. `RULES_VERSION` 30.
- **Test trap:** a test that captures a civ's only city eliminates it in the same turn (`check_elimination`), which clears the record for the wrong reason. Give the civ a second city when testing Peace or "kept at war".

## 2026-11-13: Rules 31 (public treasuries, lock as rule, half-population captures, paid cards cut, Modern costs +30%)

- **Always-on exposed a stale-cache bug.** `yields_cache::CityKey` did not include the occupation freeze (`occupation::city_freeze`, which depends on the war state with the civ the city was taken from), so a Peace or war change left a frozen city's cached Production at 0 and rollouts panicked in the cache self-check. The lock was off in every AI game before, so nothing hit it. The key now carries the freeze civ.
- **Public Gold broke three "hidden state" tests** (`ai/tests/diplomacy.rs`, `search::tests::a_determinized_state_never_depends_on_hidden_state`, `core/tests/diplomacy.rs` asserting no `gold` field): hidden-state tests must now vary other private facts (science stored, city HP, production stored), not rival Gold.
- **Halving moved the capture tests.** A size-3 city now falls to 1, not 2; tests that need the old post-capture size must raise the size before capture (the Shizuoka colony-11 save test sets 4).
- **Cutting two of six Age cards** changes every seed's draw (the pool length is the modulus); `age_pool` is now 3 cards (4 from the Renaissance). Tests that counted the pool or asked a seed for a Tribute or Monument card were rewritten on Demobilise.
- **Modern +30% (designer, 2026-11-13):** Replaceable Parts 388 to 504, Combustion 394 to 512, Steel 402 to 523, Flight 414 to 538, Electricity 422 to 549 (era 2,020 to 2,626). In his tapes Modern ran 20.5 turns against 24 to 31 for the others, as costs rose 12% while Science rose 55%; the target is about 26 turns. Not re-measured here (no balance sims); check on his next tape.
## 2026-11-13: the Modern era is the shortest era, and he alone flies in it (his 15 November tapes)
Measured after his note that the plane era "jumps really fast" (per-turn telemetry of 15 human games with 100+ turns; scripts in the session scratchpad `era-pace/`, not kept). Replaying old tapes on the current engine fails (14 of 15 diverge), so this is telemetry and tape orders only.
