- **Turns per era, his seat (median):** Ancient 24, Classical 31, Medieval 26, Renaissance 25, Industrial 24, Modern 20.5. Modern is the first era that shrinks. Its technologies cost only 12% more than Industrial's (2,020 against 1,796 for the era) while his Science at entry grows 55% (56 to 87 a turn). Future jumps to 3,432 but the game is over by then.
- **Flight is the first thing he researches:** about 5 turns after entering Modern (T134), first air strike about 9.5 turns after. After his first aircraft, 80% of his ranged-type attack orders are air strikes and his captures go from a median of 0 to 2.5 per 10 turns (small base, 9 games).
- **The AI does not answer in kind:** AI seats reach Modern 13 turns after him, take Flight about 16 turns into it, and no AI air strike appears in any of the 15 tapes. He has about 20 turns as the only air power, and the game ends a median of 22.5 turns after his Modern entry.
- **Not separable from this sample:** whether the speed-up is the cost curve or his snowball (he holds a median 6.5 VP at Modern entry against 3 for an AI seat). Both fit.
- **Reading:** the complaint is three stacked things, none of which is the era count: a short era, Flight available as its first technology, and a Hard that never flies. Flight turn is estimated from the research queue; captures are estimated from city counts.
## 2026-11-14: release check 8.9.35, eight Playwright specs that the smaller verify gate does not run
The full release check failed 15 of 1,037 Playwright tests (all reproduce deterministically, none is load flake). Seven were stale tests left behind by earlier rules/UI commits; one was a real UI bug.
- **Real bug, the capture forecast overflowed (city-assault-forecast, walled city).** Once the occupation lock became the rule (`0d6251b64`, rules 31; before that an off-by-default experiment) every capture card carries the Occupation paragraph on top of the VP line and the city-fire row, and the card (`overflow: hidden`, `max-height: 100% - 72px`, no scrolling because `pointer-events: none`) clipped its strength table at 1000x600 (73 px over) and its footnotes at 1440x900 (70 px over). Fix is CSS-only, scoped to a card that shows the lock warning (notes, city-fire fold and plain Walls line hidden; tighter padding at short windows). The next card line you add to the capture case will overflow again: measure `scrollHeight - clientHeight` of `.forecast` in `city-assault-forecast.spec.ts` (walled city) before adding it.
- **Stale after the lock became default:** `capture-clears-orders` counted every attacker as needing orders, but the captor is now the city's garrison and needs none.
- **Stale after Nov 11:** `diplomacy` (empire colours follow the faction, `39b7f2f37`: Switzerland is teal, not jade), `premium2` and `reward-emblems` (the three crowns and the `title` reward were cut, `931844eb4`), `victory-hold` and `tour/victory-race` (a rival's alarm primary is Declare war while it is legal, `42ba7f387`; only the near-victory tour was updated then), `race-panel-late` (the turn-203 save's leader now has 7 VP without crowns, so the rival alert row it asserted is gone).
- **Why the gate missed them:** the smaller verify gate runs 54 Playwright tests; these eight specs only run in the extended or release profile, and rules or UI commits on `claude/*` branches were merged on that smaller gate.
## 2026-11-11: A model in one seat beats Hard at a tactical card, not at a whole game

- **`crates/sim/examples/fable_seat.rs`** lets a model play one seat from a text `PlayerView`, with Hard's orders from the same state recorded each turn. Results and lessons: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/fable-seat-1111/README.md`. Card 2 (After the Riflemen): 3 wins of 3 at or above Hard's p90. Card 1: no difference. One whole game as Athens: ahead on VP to T116, down to one city by T137; Hard in the same seat won 3 of 16.
- **The capture lessons Hard lacks:** when a garrison dies an adjacent enemy unit swaps in before the next melee lands, so a capture needs two melee attackers on the same turn or the adjacent units cleared first; a fortified unit next to a 0 HP city should wait for ranged units rather than take a losing trade.
- **Combat resolves before movement.** A unit ordered to retreat is still hit on its origin tile, and a combat preview ("kill 100%, die 0%") ignores other attacks landing in the same resolution. Both cost units before I understood them; neither is visible in the view.
- **A build list drawn from `PlayerView` offers items that cannot start** (no Niter, Coal or spare Iron); the blocker appears only when the order is rejected. Idle Production is banked, not lost.
- **Hard as Athens had 0 VP at T50 in 16 of 16 runs** on seed 20261111, while Share the Learning at T25 gave 2. Worth a sim before changing anything.

## 2026-11-13: After the Riflemen is lost on Gold, not on the plan gate

- **`funded forecast unavailable` in the plan trace names only the first forecast source.** `campaign_candidates` tries `funded_forecast`, then the committed-force grind, then the `campaign_force` grind, then `wall_staging_forecast`. The gated-out row reports the first one only. On this card `funded_forecast` is None on every turn of wins and losses alike (a walled city held by Riflemen is out of reach of any siege window). The Campaign lived on the `campaign_force` grind and died when `siege_local_margin` fell under `campaign_persist` (98 for Athens) on T142. Check all four sources before blaming the gate.
- **A routed Campaign with no `campaigns.active` still triggers Siege purchases.** `siege_buy.rs::walled_war_target` reads the routed plan's war target. On T135, with Cannonry finishing that turn, Hard bought Siege for a Horseman (80 Gold) and later a Soldier. That left too little Gold to refit two Arquebusiers to Field Cannons (74 each, Siege built in) on T136 and T137. 26 of 50 runs went that way and won 2; the other 24 refit and won 19. Gene `refit_before_siege` (off) keeps the refits' Gold first: 21/50 → 35/50.
- **A whole-card order trace is a one-line local change.** Set `SeatOptions::trace` for every run in `scenario.rs` and write each run's trace. The 50 traces separated wins from losses in one table; three hand-picked runs had only pointed at the plan gate.

## 2026-11-14: `refit_before_siege` is on by default

- **The gene's default is now 1 (designer's approval, 2026-11-14), merged onto the rules-32 line as `claude/refit-on`.** Gated value: 1 (the full-game gate ran the candidate arm at 1: 1,024 paired human-seat games, win 33.1% to 34.9%, +1.8 pp [0.0, +3.7], no measure worse; `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/refit-gate-1114/`). The gate ran seat-only on eight fixed maps, with no rivals-only or held-out arm, so the evidence is noninferiority. The other genes on `claude/refit-gate` (`relief_forecast`, `charge_preview`, `relief_kill`, `capturer_upgrade`, `regen_strike`) stay 0. No `RULES_VERSION` bump: an AI default changes what Hard decides, not the rules or the save shape (precedent: the 2026-11-08 siege-stance defaults), so saves load at 32; Hard's games from the same seed differ from before.

## 2026-11-14 — Siege stall at 0 HP: the safety filter bars healthy units from the ring (diagnosis, 8.9.36, his Celts game T143–145)

Replayed from his save (0 hash mismatches). India's committed Strike on Celts Colony 7 had the city at 0 HP from T138; he re-bought a Rifleman garrison three times and walked a fresh one in on T142. From T143 all five roster melee units issued `SkipUnit` until the operation expired on T146:
- `city_assault_turn` returned `None` against the fresh 78-HP garrison, so no joint assault; the fallback `military.rs::strike` prices each unit alone against an unsplit counterattack, so the three wounded units (10–58 HP) were correctly `too_risky`.
- The bug: the two 100-HP units (a Cuirassier that never attacked once in seven turns, and an Assault Trooper) got `campaign_staging_spot = None` although three adjacent tiles were free, because `safe_campaign_tile` is false for every tile next to a garrisoned city. Healthy units can never join the ring; a fourth attacker on T139–142 would probably have killed the garrison before the reinforcement.
- Second fault: `too_risky` roster units fall to `hold_or_upgrade` and sit adjacent under cannon fire instead of withdrawing to heal, pinned by the operation.
Evidence in the session scratchpad (`idle144/`). Fixed on `claude/siege-ring` (fault A only): `military.rs::storm_ring_spot` gives a unit whose own charge passes `too_risky` a free tile beside a fallen (0 HP) city, routed through friends, both in the strike's staging fallback and in `city_assault.rs` arrivals when no tile beside the city is reachable this turn. Replay (`crates/ai/tests/siege_ring.rs`): from T139 the Cuirassier walks round to the far side and India takes the city on T141; from T143 it reaches (37,6) the same turn and attacks on T144 and T145 with the wounded joining the assault again. Fault B was not changed (next entry).

## 2026-11-14 — Pulling wounded units out of a fallen city's ring lifts the siege

- **Holding beside a 0-HP city is what keeps it at 0 HP.** A city heals +20 at the end of any turn with no at-war unit adjacent (`cities.rs::heal_blocked_by_adjacent_enemy`). In the T143 replay, sending the `too_risky` wounded units to `recover_wounded` emptied the ring: the city was back to 20 HP on T144 and the storm ended. The wounded also healed +10 a turn where they stood.
- **Withdrawing them also stranded the healthy unit.** With the ring empty, `focus.rs::hold_stragglers` (incoming fire split among friends ending beside a tile) judged the Cuirassier's lone move too exposed and held it; the retreats also took the tiles on its route. Narrower versions (only under a lethal `incoming_at`, or only when a healthier unit stays beside the city) still lost the storm: `incoming_at` adds every visible threat, including fortified barbarians that never move, so it called all three wounded units doomed.
- **The planner's reach treats friends as walls.** `tactics.rs::known_path_this_turn` refuses any route across a friendly military unit, but the engine lets a unit pass through its own (rules, 2026-10-21). So the joint assault thought the Cuirassier could not reach the far side of the ring in one turn when it could. `storm_ring_spot` routes through friends; every other user of `reaches_this_turn` still underestimates reach next to its own army. Not changed here (it moves every tape).
- **Smoke noise is large:** two 50-game Hard sets (seeds 9100001, 9200001) gave captures 689 to 631 and 650 to 737, abandoned sieges 83 to 82 and 75 to 93. Pooled 1339 to 1368 and 158 to 175: no signal either way at 100 games.
- **Fix (2026-11-14, `claude/settler-freeze`), and a correction to the trace.** Traced by replay: `settler_safety` is 0 by default, so neither `settler_capturable_this_turn` nor `settler_danger` vetoed the step, and the Athenian Scout was not the cause (a peaceful Scout marks no danger while `peace_settler_capture` is 0, and a Scout can capture a Settler in the rules, so ignoring it was not an option). The Settler's one-tile `MoveUnitPath` was valid; `guard_exposed_settlers_duel` turned it into `SkipUnit` because the arrival (11,19) was in next-turn reach (`snipe_exposed`) of Athens' peaceful Cataphract 68 at (9,24), which can declare war, and no escort reached the Settler this turn. The `settler_escort` 2 rendezvous only brings a troop within 2 turns' moves; Soldier 81 was 5 move-points away (3 turns), so the Settler waited and nobody came. Fix: a Settler made to wait this way now sends the nearest eligible troop within `escort_search_distance` to its tile (`hard_defense.rs`, test `a_waiting_settler_calls_an_escort_more_than_two_turns_away`). Replay of his tape from T69 shows Hard now orders Soldier 81 to (12,18) every turn; his own orders keep the replay on the old path, so the Settler's later steps are not observed. A Settler with no eligible troop within 6 hexes still waits (the rival's reach is rechecked each turn, so it ends when the rival moves).

## 2026-11-15 — Hard's production alternation: three causes, and the routine pass is the main one (diagnosis, rules 30 traces, rules 35 census)

Full trace and proposal: `docs/sim-results/ai-quality/production-alternation-1115/README.md`. No behaviour changed.

- **The "finish what was started" bonus goes to the item the city just left.** `choose_item_inner` reads `opt.production_banked`, and the view sets that to 0 for the active build. With `reconsider` re-scoring every combat unit under way each turn, two near-equal units swap every turn (G3 Essen: Soldier and Ranger T39 to T47). Reading `opt.production_stored` instead halved G3's switches in a scratch prototype (11.0 → 5.5 of 12 salts' mean).
- **The emergency pass and the routine pass undo each other.** `plan_emergency_production` fires on `needs_ranged` alone with threat at or below local defence; the routine pass puts the old item back next turn. Not firing on that trigger alone cut G4's switches 13.67 → 4.75 and added 1.6 completions in 20 turns.
- **`threatened = threat > 0` is a value cliff.** A Settler is 726 or 4,228 and a great work 0 or about 1,200 depending on one enemy unit crossing a line. Not fixed by the two changes above; in a real war (G4 T153) they moved switches only 31.7 → 28.0.
- **Switching costs delay, not Production,** because banking is per item: G4 had 566 Production parked in unfinished items after 20 turns (about 5 turns of the empire's output); nothing was destroyed. The routine pass made 64 to 75% of the switches, the emergency pass the rest; Gold buys and score jitter made none.
- **The Pyramids were not lost to alternation.** At 181 of 240 they score below a Settler and every unit even when unthreatened; no prototype arm finished them in more than 3 of 12.
- **On rules 35:** 14% of Hard city-turns switch away from an unfinished item (28% with an enemy within 3 hexes, 12% elsewhere), 4 games to T150.
- **Method gotcha:** an env-switched prototype must be gated to the seat and the cut turn (`view.me`, `view.turn`). Ungated, it changes the other empires during the tape replay and the hash check fails ("replay diverged at 14").
## 2026-11-15 — Aircraft sight and Recon: why a Bomber could not strike, and two places that must agree

- **Cause of "Bombers couldn't see targets" (designer's Bomber game):** `air::validate_strike` needs the target tile in the team's current sight (`battle::check_visible`), but only a city (3) or a ground unit (2) or the Biplane's hangar sight (4) lit the map, while a Bomber reaches 8. Past about 3 tiles from a city nothing was visible unless a land unit stood there. Fixed in two steps on `claude/aircraft-recon` (RULES_VERSION 36): `add_air_sight` now covers every `data::is_aircraft` kind (`data::AIRCRAFT_SIGHT` 4, renamed from `BIPLANE_SIGHT`), and `Order::AirRecon` shows `RECON_RADIUS` 2 tiles around a tile within range for one turn.
- **Native sight is assembled in two places.** `visibility::native_visible_tiles` (single team) and `native_sight_by_team` (the batch used by `update_explored`, contact and memory) each add territory, air and recon sight by hand. A new sight source added to only one of them shows in the view but never reaches explored tiles, tile memory or contact. Both now call `add_recon_sight`; add any future source to both.
- **Recon is recorded as the turn it was ordered and applies only when `state.turn == turn + 1`.** Recording at the end of the ranged phase with a one-turn offset means this turn's fire and movement see nothing new (no same-turn advantage, no order-dependence between players), the planning turn and its strike validation see the disc, and the end-of-turn `update_explored` of that turn still records it. Entries are dropped at the next ranged phase (`air::settle_recon`), so an expired one lingers inactive in the state for one extra turn; visibility ignores it. The field is `skip_serializing_if` empty, so no scenario hash moved.
- **A Fighter that recons still guards**, like one that rebases: `used_fighters` is filled only by valid strikes. Recon costs the Fighter nothing it had.
- **AI effect of aircraft sight:** the AI reads aircraft sight through its own `PlayerView` like a player, so Bombers now find targets 4 tiles from their cities. `japan_regressions::athens_197_uses_its_actual_bomber_against_germany` pinned one target (6, 22) from before the change; it now asserts the Bomber strikes and lands more than 100 damage, not which tile. No benchmark was run: how much stronger Hard's air game gets is unmeasured.

## 2026-11-15: verify ignored the thread setting, and a fix skipped the full suite
- `tools/astra/verify-branch.sh` sets `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS` itself from `LEGATUM_VERIFY_JOBS` (default cores/4), so a `CARGO_BUILD_JOBS=6` passed to it never applied. Size it with `LEGATUM_VERIFY_JOBS` and `LEGATUM_VERIFY_TEST_THREADS`, taken from `tools/ops/free-threads.sh`.
- With verify and the app build each given half the free cores (5 threads), the 8.9.41 app build took about 13 minutes against about 25 under the old cap of 6 beside a verify.
- The production finish-bonus fix was reported without the full test suite. Merged, it flipped the `hard_vs_human` default benchmark pin (the human-playbook seat's VP at T100 went from 2 to 0 against plain Hard rivals: one fixed map, the direction wanted). A fix is reported only after `cargo nextest` on the workspace, even when measurement is deferred.

## 2026-11-15: a released garrison is detected from the view before the turn, so a save loaded on that turn shows no prompt
- The occupation lock fortifies its garrison (`Sleep`, or `AutoFire` for ranged) and `clear_released` later drops the record, but nothing wakes the units, so they stayed fortified and out of the Return queue. The fix is app-only (rules version unchanged): `releasedGarrison(before, after)` compares `UnitView::occupation_lock` in the view before the resolved turn with the view now and stores the unit ids in `TurnReport.released`; `pendingDecisions` turns them into `unit` decisions (`released: <city>`) until a pending order answers them.
- The current view alone cannot say it: a unit that is `Sleep` in its own city with no lock looks the same as one he fortified himself. So the prompt needs the previous view and a game loaded on the release turn (no report) does not show it.
- No Wake order exists. `SkipUnit` and any other unit order clear a standing order in core (`units.rs`), so Fortify and Skip answer it without anything new. An unanswered prompt is not repeated: the next turn's report has no entry (the healed prompt persists because core keeps `needs_orders`).
Evidence in the session scratchpad (`idle144/`); fix in progress on `claude/siege-ring`.

## 2026-11-14 — A Hard Settler froze one step from its site for 11 turns (diagnosis, 8.9.37, his Byzantium game T69–T80)

Replayed from his autosave tape with `decision_audit --seat 4 --diff-only --settle-detail` (0 hash mismatches, 0 AI order mismatches). Swiss Settler 78 left Lausanne on T61, changed target three times (T61–T64), reached (12,18) on T69 and then issued `SkipUnit` every turn to T79 while `choose_site_scored` kept choosing (11,19), the adjacent tile. He declared on T80 and took it on T81.
- **The only foreign unit in reach was an Athenian Scout** parked at (8,19) for the whole stretch: 3 tiles from the site, 4 from the Settler. `move_settler` refuses a step whose arrival tile a unit that could declare can reach this turn (`settler_capturable_this_turn`), and its fallback only sends the Settler home when its own tile is `snipe_exposed`. The site failed the first test and the Settler's tile passed the second, so every branch fell through to `SkipUnit`. Which of the two checks (`settler_capturable_this_turn` or `settler_danger`) vetoed the step was not traced; the Scout is the only candidate on the board.
- **The escort left.** Soldier 81 walked beside it to T68, then went into Fribourg (12,22), 4 tiles away, and stayed. A guard on the Settler's tile would have let it walk on (`home_guard`).
- **Nothing ends the wait:** `settlement_timed_out` never fired ("timeout None" on all 11 turns), no escort was asked for, and the site's value fell from 198 to 72 while it stood there.
- How to apply: a veto with no fallback is a freeze. A Settler that cannot take its last step needs a third choice (call the nearest idle guard, or price a peaceful Scout's reach instead of ruling the tile out), and a wait must be bounded. Earlier case with a different cause: the idle Spartan Settler had no legal site on 23 turns.
- **Fix (2026-11-14, `claude/settler-freeze`), and a correction to the trace.** Traced by replay: `settler_safety` is 0 by default, so neither `settler_capturable_this_turn` nor `settler_danger` vetoed the step, and the Athenian Scout was not the cause (a peaceful Scout marks no danger while `peace_settler_capture` is 0, and a Scout can capture a Settler in the rules, so ignoring it was not an option). The Settler's one-tile `MoveUnitPath` was valid; `guard_exposed_settlers_duel` turned it into `SkipUnit` because the arrival (11,19) was in next-turn reach (`snipe_exposed`) of Athens' peaceful Cataphract 68 at (9,24), which can declare war, and no escort reached the Settler this turn. The `settler_escort` 2 rendezvous only brings a troop within 2 turns' moves; Soldier 81 was 5 move-points away (3 turns), so the Settler waited and nobody came. Fix: a Settler made to wait this way now sends the nearest eligible troop within `escort_search_distance` to its tile (`hard_defense.rs`, test `a_waiting_settler_calls_an_escort_more_than_two_turns_away`). Replay of his tape from T69 shows Hard now orders Soldier 81 to (12,18) every turn; his own orders keep the replay on the old path, so the Settler's later steps are not observed. A Settler with no eligible troop within 6 hexes still waits (the rival's reach is rechecked each turn, so it ends when the rival moves).

## 2026-11-15: the art distinctness check reuses its render cache

- `python3 tools/art/distinctness.py` compares the PNGs in `tools/art/.cache` and re-renders only with `--render` (or when the cache has no manifest). After a repaint, a run without `--render` judges the old art: on `claude/calendar` it reported the pre-sweep Artillery/Howitzer and Biplane/Fighter as identical (score 1.00) a day after they were repainted.
- How to apply: pass `--render` whenever art changed since the last run, and before trusting either a pass or a failure. With fresh renders, removing the ten accepted pairs the sweep made unnecessary leaves 0 failing.

## 2026-11-15 — A verify that never started read as green
- A chained background command called `tools/ops/free-threads.sh` from the master checkout, where it does not exist (it lives only on `claude/calendar`), so verify exited 127 before starting. The follow-up grep then matched the previous run's PASS lines in `.progress/verify/claude/calendar/status.tsv`, and the 8.9.42 build ran without a fresh verify. Caught before install.
- Rule: before trusting `status.tsv`, check its mtime is newer than the run's start, and check the verify command's own exit code. Call the script as `.claude/worktrees/calendar/tools/ops/free-threads.sh` until it lands on master.

## 2026-11-15: his Byzantium win ran on the 8.9.40 AI, and the Settler guard trusts this turn's sight (coach review)
- **The briefed build was wrong.** The lead reviewed it as 8.9.41; the save's `playtest` field says 8.9.40, the archive has one session, and the 8.9.41 app was installed under the running app mid-game. `p881_trace` at both tags decides it: 0 strategic-order mismatches at 8.9.40, 46 at 8.9.41. How to apply: before attributing a playtest result to a release's fixes, replay at the candidate tags; the newer tag's mismatches show where its changes would have acted (here the production fix on 42 seat-turns, the Peace fix once against him, T169).
- **Plain Hard's Settler guard (`hard_defense.rs::guard_exposed_settlers_duel`) decides each turn from what it sees that turn.** On T16 a `snipe_read` fog risk made Athens's Settler wait for its escort two tiles from Piraeus, on a tile checked only against visible units (`settler_capturable_this_turn`); his Horseman stood 3 tiles off in fog and took it on T17. On T90 the escort was released when the read fell to 0, the 11-14 escort call found no troop within 6 hexes on T92–T94, and `snipe_read`'s 20-turn memory had forgotten his T17 snipe of the same empire, so the Settler walked onto its site beside his unseen Knight on T95 and was taken on T96. How to apply: a fog-driven wait needs a fog-proof wait tile (a city, the escort's tile, or back), an escort should stay assigned until its Settler founds, and a rival that sniped us should stay a sniper for the game. Traced with a throwaway probe (`snipe_probe.rs` plus env-gated `eprintln!`s, in the review's scratch worktree), every AI order reproduced.
## 2026-11-15: his early defence, read from 45 tapes (`claude/early-defence-chapter`)
- **His openings changed on 2026-11-07, not with the map.** Builders since 8.9.27 wall the capital first or second whenever Sparta's or the Mongols' capital is within 20 hexes (5 of 5); before that 7 of 23 did. Distance leans (early Walls in 7 of 12 games at 9 hexes or less, 1 of 7 at 15 or more) but the date decides more. A playbook built from his older reviews teaches the older opening. Chapter: `docs/playtests/early-defence.md`.
- **His record avoids the hard case.** No capital of his fell before T61; walled cities held 8 of 8; unwalled cities with 3+ attackers fell 10 of 12 even with a purchase. He abandoned 5 of the 9 games in which he lost a city before T61, so "what he does under an early siege" has almost no winning examples. The archive's short abandoned games are evidence, not noise.
- **Gotcha: replaying many engine versions through one shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` gives stale binaries.** `git archive | tar -x` keeps commit-time mtimes, so cargo treats the new sources as older than the last build's artifacts and reuses them: two versions replayed with a hash mismatch from turn 1 and one failed to compile against another version's core. Touch the extracted files (`find SRC -type f -exec touch {} +`) before each build, and only trust a replay with "hash mismatches 0".
- **Gotcha: `game_dump` at 8.9.11 and older has no `original_capital` on cities;** take the capital as `is_capital` with `owner == founder`. Old saves also use the internal faction names (Tollkeepers = Switzerland, Oathbound = Sparta, Signalwrights = Athens, Glassroad = Mongols, Crucible = Germany, Choir = Japan, Remnant = Byzantium, Veyr = Celts at 8.9.6).

## 2026-11-15: Training questions are a shadow Hard plan compared with his orders (`claude/training-mode`)
- **Third pass, from his Sparta 8.9.57 game (he skipped five of twelve; `astra/coach-questions-3`).** The garrison question counted any unit at War in reach and fired on a barbarian Scout, one Horseman and an Indian Scout; it now needs a force the city forecast says can hurt it. Barbarians printed as "team 255". A Peace he had only proposed was described as made, with Hard's unrelated plan appended. Purchase questions came while he was seven turns from winning: no routine question at or within one scoring action of the target. Two skips in a category double its threshold for the game. A war he declared now says what Hard saw that held it back.
- **Veterans card wording (designer, 2026-11-15: "could be more specific with language. something about an earned promotion"):** "Veterans: earn 4 promotions in battle (a promotion is a level a unit gains from combat experience after this Age began)". Text only; the rule is unchanged.
- **The first two games showed the questions asked about the wrong things (designer, 2026-11-15; `astra/coach-window-question`).** Four of ten were about cities on automation (`production()` never looked at `build_automation`); the war-window Gold figure summed one treasury eight times (now one running shadow treasury, and it names what Hard would buy); importance was a fixed scale per category (now the city's share of Production, threat and how close the race is, with caps of 2 production and 3 purchase questions a game). New categories: a garrison leaving a threatened city, an army at the border at Peace, a refused Peace, a Great Work started at war, a war declared on a large treasury. Every question names its city and tile; a test bans engine Debug text.
- **Renamed "Coach questions" (designer, 2026-11-15, after 8.9.47):** he read "Training questions" as the "Training" AI level. Player text, comments and the rules reference say Coach questions; internal names (`why`, `lobby.training`, `training/answers/`) are unchanged.
- **A peace he accepted was asked about although Hard planned the same peace (his Athens game, 8.9.47, T64).** The shadow brain's executed plan was `Peace { with: TeamId(4) }` but its orders held no accept, so the order comparison saw a difference. `why::plans_peace` now reads the plan as well as the orders. The question also printed the plan's Debug text and "AI 5 (Sparta)"; `plan_clause` gives the plan kind and faction names, and `team_name` is the faction alone. The Tauri answer file is confirmed written in the installed app.
- **"Training" already existed as an AI level** (the battle search for AI seats, `training.rs`, `training/<game>.jsonl`). The designer's Training mode is a different thing: a toggle in the lobby's game settings, "Training questions", with its answers under `training/answers/`. Do not merge the two without asking him.
- **The question check is read-only and deterministic**: the shadow Hard brain has its own seed and memory, so the game's RNG and hash are untouched; a session test plays 25 turns with and without the check and compares every AI order and the state JSON. It starts cold after a load (the shadow's memory is not saved), so a loaded game's first Hard plans are less informed than the rivals'.
- **Settler site scores come from parsing `settler_diagnosis` notes** ("chosen Some((V, ..", "probe tile candidate: score S"). That is brittle by nature: `why::site_scores` has a test, and a changed note format silently stops settle questions (it returns None and nothing fires).
- **Thresholds come from the paper test, not tuned**: R >= 4 for production (the paper's primary), 500 Gold for purchases (the brief; the paper used 500-750), 2.0 for Settler sites (one paper data point at 2.3; 7 candidates in a game), war/peace any difference. They are constants at the top of `crates/ai/src/why.rs`.

## 2026-11-15: the installed app's process is `legatum-desktop`, not `Legatum`
`pgrep -x Legatum` never matches: the bundle's executable is `Contents/MacOS/legatum-desktop`, so that check always reads "not running". Before replacing the app, use `pgrep -f "Legatum.app/Contents/MacOS"`. Found while installing Playtest 8.9.44 (the first thin-LTO playtest build, which launches normally).


## 2026-11-15: the installed app's process is `legatum-desktop`, not `Legatum`
`pgrep -x Legatum` never matches: the bundle's executable is `Contents/MacOS/legatum-desktop`, so that check always reads "not running". Before replacing the app, use `pgrep -f "Legatum.app/Contents/MacOS"`. Found while installing Playtest 8.9.44 (the first thin-LTO playtest build, which launches normally).
## 2026-11-15: Hard's flat treasury is a cap and a cheapest-unit rule, and more Gold alone fixes nothing (coach review, Germany 8.9.43)
- **Measured across 25 of his games** (archive telemetry and tapes, 124 Hard seats): his median treasury 461 Gold against Hard's 72, median peak 2,842 against 241; Hard spends 99% of its net income as it comes, his bursts were followed by a capture within 10 turns 30 of 51 times against 1 of 33, and 243 of the 265 units Hard bought were Ancient-era Spearmen, Archers and Horsemen (164 of them after T120).
- **Why:** `gold_hold.rs::held_gold` caps the reserve at 25 turns of net income (Hard's measured 90th-percentile treasury is 24 turns of income); the upgrade, rescue, door and threat passes spend through the hold one unit at a time; the emergency quote, `hard_defense.rs::second_troop_buy` and a `production.rs` troop pick all take the cheapest military unit, which is always the Ancient one because every era's units stay buildable. `buy_hold` 3 moved Hard's 90th-percentile treasury only from 123 to 141.
- **Replayed, not assumed:** giving the Aztecs 1,300 or 2,600 Gold at T111, or India 500 or 1,500 at T146, changed no capture against Hard in his seat (a bystander given the same Gold moved the numbers as much); Hard spent the money on Siege for another front, single upgrades, Mines and Spearmen. A spending rule, not a bigger reserve, is the fix (idea #56).
- **Gotcha:** his own orders (the tape seat) break on any Gold perturbation, even Gold given to a bystander (Aztec captures 3.0 → 0), so treasury branches need the Hard seat plus a bystander control arm. Check `buy_block` before forcing a buy (the Aztecs had no Niter for Musketmen). Master is still rules 26 while the playtest line is rules 37: a scenario cut from a 8.9.43 state fails on master (`missing field titles`); cut it on `claude/calendar`.

## 2026-11-15: The faction sheet's cards own the special unit and building; rows that say the same are marked, not deleted
- **`FactionPair.onCard`** marks a trait row whose rule the sheet now shows as a painted card (the special unit's edge, the Ruhr Foundry, Keshig, Lyceum and Revenue Office rows). The sheet filters them out; the Guide still prints them, so the flag keeps the Guide's text and its tests intact instead of forking the copy.
- **Cards come from `sheetCards(faction)`**: the special unit (`SPECIAL_UNIT`) then the live special buildings (Agoge, Yam Relay Station and Imperial Diwan are goal or war buildings, not replacements, so their card says "only X can build it" and leaves the goal's rule to the goal block). Specs that used `locator('.pc')` as one card now see up to three.
- **A flex sheet with centred sections spreads spare height evenly:** `space-between` left 100 px gaps on a one-card faction at 1440x900; `flex: 1` sections with `justify-content: center`, a capped single card and a stage of 750 px keeps every faction within about 40 px of its neighbours' gaps.
- **2026-11-15, Egypt and Rome art (`claude/art-egypt-rome`):** Codex image generation again gave first-attempt keepers for both pair portraits, both buildings and the Legionary; only the Medjay needed regenerating. Two traps. (1) When an image comes back already transparent its edge carries coloured fringe pixels, so composite it onto flat magenta first and run the normal key (`unit-art.py`), which pulls the edge in and despills. (2) The stock key's edge pull-in erodes a thin iron shank (the pilum) into a floating tip: use `unit-art.py --edge-key` for any figure with a long thin weapon. The first Medjay (spear 45 degrees up, shield on the right) read as the Legionary and the Horseman at 45 px (gist 0.79 and 0.78); a low wide guard crouch with the spear levelled cleared the gist check where mirroring the same picture only cleared one of the two pairs. A new low flat castrum scored 0.70 against the ITER Reactor at 64 px in greyscale; darkening it (`wonder-art.py master ... -0.4 0.88`) fixed it, as the earlier per-piece grades did. A castrum icon of stakes over a crenellated wall scored 0.75 against the Walls icon: a central pitched-roof gate tower between stake rows separates them.
- **2026-11-15, Egypt and Rome art, second pass after the designer's realism review:** the first versions were accepted from the prompt and a quick look, and four details were wrong (a Legionary 'pilum' that was a spear with a leaf blade, closed boots, an oversized scabbard, a Medjay with an unattested bronze cuff, strap sandals and an unstrung bow). Zoom into hands, feet and the full length of every held object before passing a painting, and describe only what a crop shows. Both units were also action poses (a mid-throw, a wide crouch) when every existing figure stands upright at rest; an upright figure gets its silhouette from equipment. Props in a finished portrait can be corrected by a Codex image edit of the portrait itself (keep people and setting identical, name only the props), which worked first try for the wax tablets, the four-arm groma and the scribe's palette. Both Rome pieces are now one period, the early Principate (veterans' colonies under Augustus): segmented armour is first century AD, so the portrait is no longer set in 30 BCE. The tilted-at-the-ready pose (weapon 20 degrees off vertical, shield angled out) was the one that cleared the miniature gist check for a spear-and-shield figure, where the dead-upright versions scored 0.77 to 0.79 against the Spartan Hoplite and the Settler.
- **2026-11-15, third pass (shield grip, base, bowstring):** a figure can be corrected in place by a Codex image edit of its magenta-field raster naming only the parts to change (it kept the figure, changed the base to the set's single cream disc, flattened a shield bottom and curved a scutum, with the left arm hidden behind it); try three variants and pick by crop. A thin bowstring vanishes in the key's edge pull-in at 256 px: ask for a cord about 5 px wide at 1254. After the shield edits the upright Legionary and Medjay matched silhouettes again (gist 0.78 to 0.79 for every combination of edit variants); mirroring the Medjay (shield on the left, spear in the right hand) cleared it, and also cleared the earlier borderline Legionary against barbarian Crossbowman pair.

## 2026-11-20: Gold tile buying returns at rules 46 (`claude/buy-tiles`)
- **A rebuild, not a revert.** `2d4304ed4` (rules 20) deleted it with the TypeScript mock engine, speed scaling and a `LegacyBuyTile` stub still in the tree. Today the old Standard prices are plain constants (`data.rs::TILE_GOLD_BASE` 40, `TILE_GOLD_PER_TILE` 20, ring 100/125/150 percent) and the Code of Laws discount is deliberately not restored. `tiles_claimed` counts every tile gained since founding (grown or bought), so a bought tile raises the next price but is never in `grown_tiles`: population loss cannot release it and settling still claims one tile per population above 1.
- **Bumping the version moved no hash.** `GameState::hash` leaves `save_version` out, so rules 46 changed no `pins.json` entry and no scenario hash; `refresh-save-version-fixtures.py 45 46` plus `rules.generated.json` (`saveVersion`) were the whole fixture bump. `LEGATUM_OFFLOAD=1 tools/sim/repin.sh` came back with zero changed files. `rehash_scenarios` over `find scenarios -name '*.json'` panics on a non-scenario file ("unknown field `first`"): pass `scenarios/lab/*.json` and `scenarios/war/*.json`, and it only re-gzips the state with a different header byte, so skip it when the hash is unchanged.
- **Same tile, two orders.** Nothing special is needed: resolution applies orders one at a time against live state, so the second order finds the tile owned and is refused ("That tile is already owned"). Validation of one empire's own list simulates the first order, so it shows the refusal before Ready; a rival's order can only fail at resolution (`OrderFailed`).
- **Check order matters for the wording.** The explored test comes before the claim-candidate test, so a tile one ring past the work radius that is unexplored says "Explore a tile", not "farther tiles come only from growth". A test of the reach rule has to mark the tile explored (the city's own sight does not reach ring 4).
- **Decision views skip the list.** `buyable_tiles` is built only in a `Full` view (the AI never buys tiles yet); `tile_base_price` is cheap and kept in both. An AI that buys tiles later must read it from a `Full` view or the view must grow a priced candidate set for it. The list comes from real ownership of explored tiles, so a fogged but explored tile that was claimed since shows as unbuyable at once: this matches the old behaviour, and the refusal text says only "already owned".
- **UI (designer, 2026-11-20: the first version's always-on price tags were "so cluttered and confusing i couldnt figure out how to buy the tiles").** One button-like "Buy a tile · from N Gold" button in the city panel (inline in the Units heading's row, because any extra row there pushes build cards off the 1000x600 panel and fails `city-density.spec.ts` and `production-picker.spec.ts`; the queued tile is a row of its own) opens the same placement mode the wonders use (`store.place` with `intent: 'tile'`, `building: null`): only then does the map dim everything but the buyable tiles and price them (greyed when unaffordable); a click on an affordable one queues `BuyTile` and leaves the mode. Outside the mode the only tile-buying mark on the map is the queued tile. Prices are drawn in screen space after the unit counters so a unit never hides one.
- **Baseline noise:** at `playtest-8.9.72` itself 31 Playwright tests fail in 17 spec files (build-card-summary, premium-shots, air-wing, era-research, borders-variants, unlock-density, walls, piece-compare, keshig-shots, roads-art, era-takeover, siege-units-shots, city-assault-forecast, benefit-ledger, aftermath-forecast, path-preview, unit-era), the same 31 at this branch's tip; compare a branch's failures against that set, not against green. The tag also does not merge cleanly into master (`plans/ai-war*` and the coach skill conflict), so `verify-branch` stops at its merge step: verify a throwaway branch with master merged in.

## 2026-11-21: Buy a tile, findable in a building city (`claude/buy-tiles-fixes`, 8.9.73.1)
- **Findability is a placement problem, not a styling one.** In a city that is building, the picker (and with it the 8.9.73 button) sat 302 px below the fold at 1000x600. The control now sits right under "Tiles N worked · M owned" in a building city and stays in the picker's head only when the city is idle; exactly one instance is mounted (`CityPanel.tsx`, slots `territory`, `row`, `inline`). The tour's old city fixtures were all idle, which hid it: new shots use a building city (`tour/turn-one.spec.ts`, 11g to 11l).
- **Two purchases share one slot, and the UI used to hide that.** `toggleBuyTile` silently dropped a queued item purchase, while core refuses whichever order comes second. The button is now disabled with "Cancel the X purchase first", and `tilePurchasePreview.funds` no longer frees an item's Gold (only a queued tile's own).
- **Frozen cities show no tile-buying control** (the Occupied banner already says it buys nothing); `tour/occupation-lock.spec.ts` asserts no Build/Buy/Queue button there. A resisting city still shows the disabled button with the reason.
- **`.amt-tight` hides punctuation after the Gold coin on a tile card.** It pulls the next character 0.22em back over the coin, and a red full stop vanished behind the gold disc ("Costs 60 [coin] You have..."). `.tilecard-row .amt-tight > .amt-icon` resets the margin.
- **A tile fixture needs an explored map and partly owned ring 2 to price rings differently.** At founding only ring 2 is a claim candidate (all one price), so a "mixed affordability" shot needs `players[0].explored.fill(1)` and some ring-2 tiles owned. Patching a view in a spec must make new objects down to the city, or the panel's memoised preview does not re-render.
- **Fixtures wrote `legatum.first-game-hints.v1` while the app reads `.v2`** (since `6785baae5`), so first-game hints covered the map in many specs; fixing the key (every spec) cleared about eight spec files from the 31-failure baseline (walls, keshig-shots, era-takeover, siege-units-shots, city-assault-forecast, aftermath-forecast, path-preview, unit-era). `feel.spec.ts` now expects the `queue` feel event for research (`f61290348`).

## 2026-11-21: launch art audit fixes (`art-fixes`)
- **Crests are rendered from the seals, not painted.** `app/src/assets/heraldry/src/render-crests.mjs` lights each seal with an SVG filter in Chromium and writes `raster/crest-<id>-512.png`, so seal, glyph and crest cannot drift apart and no image model is needed for heraldry. It renders one crest per `seal-*.svg` it finds: a leftover seal of a removed faction produces a crest for it, so delete the seal first.
- **Vite inlines small SVGs imported with `?url` as data URIs.** A test cannot assert that a glyph's URL contains its file name. `factions.test.ts` asserts that the eleven glyph URLs and crest URLs are all different instead.
- **"No reference" is not "unused" when names are computed.** The audit listed `building-broodhall` art and `ui-sakoku.svg` as orphans; both are live (`building-${kind}` lookups for a `BuildingKind` that still exists, and `reportIcons.ts`). Search the kind enums and the tests before deleting art. `.claude/hooks/test-merge-gates.sh` also names a real icon as a fixture, so deleting that icon breaks the hook test.
- **Key a black ground by connectivity, not by colour.** The wonder vignettes have black inside the painting (basalt, shadow, deep water). `tools/art/wonder-vignette-key.py` flood-fills near-black from the border only and refuses to run when the keyed area changes by more than 1.2 points between thresholds 8, 14 and 24, which is the sign of a leak into the painting. The measured spread was under that for all eight.
- **Icon READMEs drift from the drawings in both directions.** Four unit icons had been redrawn as historical weapons while their rows still described the retired setting; eleven others had historical names over the old drawings. Read the SVG, not the row.
- **The squashed history has no per-file dates.** Provenance dates come from prompt headers, READMEs and design notes; `app/src/assets/PROVENANCE.md` says "not recorded" where none exists (improvements, siege props, wonders, reward emblems, the app-icon parchment).
- **The audio manifest is the source for credits.** `CREDITS.md` is generated by `node app/scripts/audio-credits.mjs` and the in-game Credits read `manifest.json`; fix a credit in the manifest (and in `tools/audio/build_audio.py`, which writes it) and regenerate.
## 2026-11-21: A free unit closing in on its besieged city walked into it without asking `can_defend_at` (`claude/hc-stepin`, gene `held_shot_stepin`)
- **The `relief` branch of `military.rs::defend` bypassed the defence pricing.** When every safe tile beside an own city is taken or under fire, the branch "closes in" on any free tile nearer the city. For a unit already one hex away the only nearer tile is the city itself, so a 100 HP Horseman was ordered into empty, 0 HP Geneva on T67 of the Mongol game with six Cataphract shooters in range and six melee units in reach, while `can_defend_at`, `exchange_is_sustainable` and `garrison_doomed` all said it died (4 of 4 salts of the M51-geneva tape on the box). The branch never calls them. The unit dies to the volley and the charge and the city falls the same turn; under the held-shot rule of PR #4 (rules 47, not landed in this stack) the volley lands on it before the charge; at rules 46 the charge alone did the same.
- **Fix:** `may_close_into` leaves the city tile out of that branch when the city is at 0 HP and the unit cannot survive there (`can_defend_at` false). Gene `held_shot_stepin`, default 1.
- **It does not move a timing cell.** Hybrid seat, 96 paired runs, champion and rules-47 champion: M51 0.0 to 0.0, S67 5.2 to 5.2, S120 35.4 to 35.4, M72 +1/-0, M07-early +2/-0, S10, M90, S131 unchanged, quiet guardrail unchanged. The unit is saved, Geneva still falls at T67 in 96 of 96. A hygiene fix, not a Sam-score lever. Trace and tables: `~/LegatumRuns/hill-climb/runs/held-shots/README.md`.
- **Tracing tip:** a one-line `eprintln!` in `Planner::unit_order` (own city at 0 HP as destination, print `can_defend_at`, `exchange_is_sustainable`, a backtrace) found the bypass in one box run; `hill_suite --threads 1 --salts 1` writes it to stderr (`suite.py` discards stderr, run the binary directly).
## 2026-11-21: Player text gets its own plain-language reviewer and gate marker (`claude/project-thread-xpb3x3`)
- **What changed:** Sam asked for board-game-style text (the card gives the short rule, the Guide gives the detail) and "a reviewer going forward on PRs that checks for plain, simple, understandable language". `.claude/agents/legatum-plain-language-reviewer.md` (Sonnet) now owns style, length and the card-versus-Guide split; it writes `.progress/plain-review/<branch>.ok`. The docs reviewer's copy pass is now a truth pass only (numbers, claims, editorializing), and the gate no longer reads `copy: clean`. This supersedes the 2026-11-07 call to fold copy review into the docs reviewer: one reviewer doing both kept passing long, true-but-dense lines like the Celts barbarian row.
- **Detection widened:** the copy class used to be a path list (`data.ts`, `factionSheets.ts`, the Guide, `*Text.ts`...), which missed most player text (it lives in `.tsx` components such as `TileCard.tsx`, `GoalActions.tsx`, `format.ts`). `tools/gates/check.py` now also flags any non-test `app/src` source whose added lines carry a string literal or JSX text of five or more words starting with a capital (`PROSE`; comments skipped). A false hit only costs a cheap review. Tests: `.claude/hooks/test-merge-gates.sh` (78 cases, two new).
- **Cloud PRs:** GitHub merges never fire `merge-gates.sh`, so in the cloud flow run `tools/gates/check.py <branch>` and the reviewers it names by hand, and post their verdicts on the PR.
## 2026-11-21: the siege survival clock loses unseen games; default off (`claude/hc-pr5-clock-off`)
- **Held-out result.** Hybrid seat (his recorded orders, Hard everywhere else), 96 paired runs on each of 12 usable cells of Athens 8.9.47 and Rome 8.9.67, games never traced or tuned on: Sam score (higher is better for the AI) 8.6% with `siege_survival_clock` on, 25.6% with it off, which equals the GitHub master engine cell for cell (Emain Macha 0 to 100, Alesia retake 0 to 74, Dyrrachium 1 to 32, Phaleron 0 to 19.8, Bukhara 17.7 to 24.0, Gergovia 31.2 to 6.2). `leader_war` 0 changes nothing on those cells. Tables: `~/LegatumRuns/hill-climb/runs/heldout/genes-table.md`.
- **Training does not show it.** Six training hybrid cells (M51, S67, S120, M72 defence off, M90, S131): clock on 25.3, off 26.0, `leader_war` off 23.3, both off 17.0. The champion's training edge is the S131 hold, which needs both genes (clock only 54.2, `leader_war` only 82.3, both 94.8, neither 0.0); the clock also hurts S120 (35.4 on, 76.0 off). The first measure of the PR's gain (Gergovia falls T32 in 1 of 24 runs) was on training games.
- **Default 0 here is the fallback.** This branch flips the default so the PR can land at master's held-out level; the stronger fix (a clock that does not lose Athens and Rome) is measured separately.
- **Tracing tip:** `hill_suite --arms control,noSC=params.json,...` runs gene toggles on one champion binary in one pass (`{"schema":1,"name":"noSC","decisions":{"siege_survival_clock":0}}`).

## 2026-11-22: The Single Player subtitle claimed a fixed "five AI empires" (`claude/project-thread-5rjdvl`)
- **The count varies.** A solo lobby holds 2 to 8 empires (`MIN_SEATS`, `MAX_PLAYERS`), so one to seven AIs; five is only Standard's default (`MAP_SIZES.Standard.default` 6). The title screen comes before the lobby, so it can't show the live count: it now says "You against 1 to 7 AI empires", built from `MIN_SEATS - 1` and `MAX_PLAYERS - 1` so it follows the constants, and the README says the range with "five by default" (update the README if the constants change). A literal "seven AI empires" in app source trips the `copyRules.ts` ban left from when Standard started eight empires.

## 2026-11-22: Dead-code sweep of the AI tooling (`claude/review-ai-dead-code`)
- **Removed (none of it touches a pin):** `tools/llm-teacher` and its `teacher_render` example (closed experiment), `tools/duel`, the four unreferenced hand-off scripts in `tools/league` (`export_tuned.py`, its test and the tuned-openings JSON stay: `hard_v2` reads the generated table), and 54 one-off `crates/sim/examples`. About 15,700 lines. `pins.json`, the pinned tapes and `RULES_VERSION` are unchanged.
- **How deadness was shown:** every name was grepped against scripts, tools, workflows, `tools/ci`, skills, agents, current docs, `Cargo.toml` and all Rust; an example or tool survived if any non-dated file named it. Dated records (`docs/sim-results`, learnings, status snapshots) do not keep a tool alive.
- **Looked dead, is live:** the `proxy*` brains (reached by the sim runner's `--proxy-version`, the duel matrix, the search rival `a=` setting and `scripts/opponent-assessment-benchmark.py`); `tuned_openings` (`hard_v2`); `training.rs` (the app's training mode calls it through the WASM API and `search` uses `search_turn_with`, so it cannot go behind a cargo feature without cutting both); `tools/evolve` (climb scripts, `climb-r2.json` is read by a params test).
- **Switches:** all 413 AI genes are read by code and `AiParams` uses `deny_unknown_fields`, so deleting one breaks saved parameter files; every `RuleExperiments` field is serialized in saves and tapes. None were removed.

## 2026-11-22: An online game can outlive the host's tab through a save file (`claude/online-save`)
- **Same save as solo.** `BrowserHost`'s save is `Session::save_json`, the format a solo game loads, so the host's "Save to file" writes the normal `legatum-save` envelope with `online: true` (`onlineSaveFile` in `store.ts`) and Load from file can open it solo. The text comes from the engine on demand (`BrowserHost.save_game`, a new wasm export, read through `hostedGameSave()`), not from the `sessionStorage` copy, which can be missing (too large, blocked) or a turn behind.
- **Credentials are not in the save.** A resumed host gives every seat a fresh token (`Host::new`), so a reload resume keeps every seat reserved. Hosting from a chosen file calls `open_guest_seats` (new wasm export) to clear the tokens of seats 1 and up, so guests take their old seats from the list at once, and `hostOnlineFromSave` takes seat 0 for the host. The room id and key are new. Seat names come from the save.
- **A chosen save never falls back.** `startHosting`'s reload path starts a clean game when the kept save cannot resume (better than no tab). With `fromSave` it throws instead, so the player is told the file is unusable rather than hosting an empty game.
- **Tests:** `net/hostSave.wasm.test.ts` (real engine: host save to file to a new host to solo `Game.load`), `store.onlineSave.test.ts` (fake hosting), and `host_save_file_resumes_on_a_fresh_browser_and_loads_solo` in `crates/wasm/tests/net_host.rs`.

## 2026-11-22: Cities vanished under their banners and lone mountains drew as hex stickers (`claude/map-art-cities-mountains`)
- **Banner over the town.** The name plate's bottom sat 0.62 hex radii above the centre and the own-city meters (a fixed 17 px) hung below it, while the painted town's roofs start only about 0.2-0.4 radii above the centre. At zoom 0.9 the meters just touched the roofs; at the smallest normal zoom (about 0.62-0.7, hex radius 22-25 px, the T203 launch still) they covered most of the town. Fix: `BANNER_BOTTOM` (0.5) is now the bottom of the whole block, and the plate moves up by the meters' height (`render.ts::cityBanners`). The block rises about 0.4 radii into the row above for an own city.
- **Town paler than its farms.** Towns are baked into the terrain chunk before the territory (step 11), so the 0.18 seat-colour tint of border variant C washed over them, while improvements are drawn later in screen space at full strength. Towns in sight are now drawn after the territory; remembered towns stay under the fog wash. The sprite is also 92 world px wide (was 84); walls, construction stages and moment effects read the same `CITY_WORLD`, so they stay registered.
- **Lone mountain as a sticker.** Two things drew the hex: every edge touching a painted feature got the cased grid line (light casing plus ink core), so a lone peak was ringed by a full pale-and-ink hexagon, and its hex was filled with the Mountains ground colour (a pale grey-tan) in the soft colour layer. For mountain regions of up to `SMALL_REGION` tiles, edges to open ground now keep the faint line (`casedEdge`) and the hex takes the commonest open neighbour's ground colour (`footTerrain`); edges inside a small group stay cased, so the grid is still countable. Big ranges are unchanged.
- **Shots:** `e2e/map-fixes-shots.spec.ts` (`MAP_FIX_SHOTS=<dir> MAP_FIX_PREFIX=before|after`) stages a pop-15 walled capital among farms, a lone mountain and a two-mountain group; note that zoom 0.6 is already the far tier (`FAR_HEX_PX` 22 = zoom 0.61), so the worst banner case is about 0.66.

## 2026-11-22: Art masters moved out of the tree before the history squash (`claude/review-asset-removal`)
- **What moved.** 540 files (128.8 MB: 54 unit `*-source.png` 60.5 MB, 11 `people-<faction>.png` plus the title key-art PNG 25.4 MB, `docs/design` clips and images 37.0 MB, `docs/playtests` charts 5.9 MB) are listed in `tools/art/sources-manifest.json` with path, size and sha256, and removed from git. The tracked tree went from 329.9 MB to about 201 MB. `tools/art/fetch-sources.py` restores them from `LEGATUM_ART_SOURCES_URL`; `--add` registers a new master, `--pack DIR` stages the archive for upload.
- **Decide "shipped" from the code, not the names.** `import.meta.glob('../assets/construction/*.png')` bundles the full-size 1254 px construction stage masters too (56 MB, drawn when a site is larger than 256 px on screen), so they stay; only files no glob, import or test reads were moved. `tools/release/runtime-assets.py` already encoded the same rule for the public export.
- **Two tests wanted masters.** `unitArt.distinct.test.ts` (every slug has a `-source.png`) and `portraits.test.ts` (`people-<id>.png`) now accept a manifest entry; no other test or gate reads a master.
- **The project file store rewrites images.** A directory under `/mnt/project-files/` stamps PNG and JPEG files with a provenance block a few seconds after they are written (172 of 490 images differed from their manifest hash, which `cmp` showed as an inserted `jumb`/`c2pa` chunk), so a per-file image archive there can never verify. The archive is one uncompressed `art-sources.tar` (verified by extracting from the mount and hashing every member), which the store leaves alone. The tar is only the hand-over format: `fetch-sources.py` requests one file at a time at `<base>/<repository path>`, so the tar is unpacked into that layout on the object store when it is uploaded. `shutil.copyfile` is not the culprit: `cp` and a plain read/write loop give the same bytes.
- **WebP at build only.** `app/vite-plugin-webp.ts` (on when `LEGATUM_WEBP=1`, which `scripts/build-web.sh` sets by default) re-encodes the emitted PNG assets with `sharp` after bundling and rewrites the references (lossless up to 300 px or 4096 colours, else quality-90 lossy with exact alpha; lossy effort 6 cost 20 minutes of build time for under 3% size, so effort 4); source PNGs, `public/`, the dev server and the desktop build are unchanged. Adding `sharp` with npm 10 rewrote unrelated `libc` fields in `package-lock.json`; the lock here keeps only the added packages.

## 2026-11-22: Autosave moved from one localStorage string to rolling slots in IndexedDB (`claude/review-autosave`)
- **Why.** The whole game hung on one `localStorage` string (about 1 MB by turn 160, a site cap near 5 MB), written best-effort with the failure swallowed, one slot, silently overwritten by a new game, and the turn's unsent orders were never kept. `platform/autosaveStore.ts` now keeps 5 turns per game (one record per turn, id `game:turn`), writes the new record before pruning, and removes another game's records only after the new game's first record is in.
- **Fallback and upgrade.** No database (private mode, blocked) or a refused write falls back to the old key, so nothing regresses. A save under the old key is copied into the database the first time it is read; the old key is removed only after a newer database write succeeded. A copy never doubles a save: the old key's record is skipped when a record has the same `savedAt`.
- **Writes are async now.** `readAutosave` returns a promise, the title screen loads its list in an effect (it shows "Looking for your game…" first, and neither New nor Continue is primary until the list is in), and tests await `autosavesSettled()` before asserting on storage. Writes run one at a time in order, so a slow big write cannot land after the next turn's.
- **The crash card keeps its own tiny read.** `CrashScreen.tsx` duplicates the database name, store and skip key and opens the database with no version number and aborts in `onupgradeneeded`: opening an absent database with `indexedDB.open(name)` would otherwise create an empty version-1 database with no stores, and the app's own `open(name, 1)` would then never run its upgrade. A 1.5 s timer keeps a hung database from hiding the card's save button.
- **"Load the previous turn" hides, it does not delete.** The crash card writes the newest slot's id to `legatum.autosave.skipped.v1` and reloads; reading skips those ids until any successful write clears the list. The count that decides when to offer it ("the card shows for the same turn a second time") lives in `sessionStorage` so a Reload keeps it.
- **Orders** are saved as the whole `orders` array 400 ms after each change, keyed by game and turn in a second store, and restored on Continue only for the same game and turn; each seat's list is re-validated and what the engine refuses is dropped. A `beforeunload` warning is armed only after a failed orders write.
- **Online hosts are left as they were.** A hosted game's save is the host engine's own (`net/hosting.ts`, per-tab `sessionStorage`, resumed on a reload of that tab); it never went through the campaign autosave and still does not. Moving it is a separate change (it also needs a rule for which game Continue means).
- **Load Game asks too.** A file chosen on the title screen asks with the same dialog ("The game in the file takes its place...", primary "Load the file") when a saved game exists. A load from inside a game is unchanged (it already replaces the open game). A static check of `app/e2e` found no spec that starts a second game in one browser context (each test gets a fresh context), so none needed a change; not run.

## 2026-11-22: The app now answers the relay's limits (`claude/review-relay-client`)
- **Refusals were swallowed.** `BrowserHost.announce` threw one generic error and `store.hostOnline` replaced every failure with "Check your internet connection", so a 429 (too many rooms or creations) or 503 (list full, kill switch) told the host the wrong thing. Refusals are now a `RelayRefusal` with a kind; the 429 with `Retry-After` is the creation rate, a 429 without it is the live-room cap, and a 503 is told apart only by the body ("paused" is the kill switch), because the Worker gives no machine code. If the Worker ever adds one, switch `refusalFor` to it.
- **1013 is not always "too many from this address".** The Room also closes with 1013 for "host not connected" and "room is full", which must keep reconnecting; the guest stops only when the reason says "too many connections". Browsers do pass `reason` on the close event, so the match works; a test pins both cases.
- **4029 retry budget resets on a real view,** not on socket open: a flooding guest reopens fine and is closed again at once, so open would never stop the loop.
- **Room name = the host's player name.** The title screen's "name in the game list" box is also the player name, so the cap was already 40; the new part is the note that shows the cleaned form (`net/roomName.ts`, copy of the Worker's `sanitizeName` without its blocklist, kept in step by hand).
- **`Retry-After` was unreadable from the browser (UI review of PR #21).** The app calls the relay from another origin, and a cross-origin page can read only CORS-safelisted response headers plus those the server lists in `Access-Control-Expose-Headers`. The Worker sent `Retry-After` on its creation-rate 429 but did not expose it, so `res.headers.get('retry-after')` was always null and the client showed the per-address "You already have games open" for both 429s. Tests passed because the fake response made the header readable. Fix: `withHeaders` exposes it, and the client also tells the two 429s apart by the body text, so an un-redeployed Worker still reads right. A test whose fake hides the header now pins that. Also from the review: a retry button that cannot succeed must not be the primary; and a title menu with Resume, a two-line notice and the name note ran past the window at 1000x600 until the name note replaced its label and the kicker line hid.

## 2026-11-22: The Age score check
- **`planMoments` shows at most three cards, and "skip the story" means jumping the index to 3** (`MomentLane` sets `index: 3` in several places). A card that must never be collapsed (the score check) has to take one of the three slots rather than make a fourth, or an interrupted story lands on it instead of ending. It goes right after the era-up when there is one, else first.
- **`AgeBegan` follows `EraEntered` in the event list** (the era is entered in `eras::learn`, the Age opens in `objectives::update` at turn end), so the opener's era-up and score check already come in the right order by event index.
- **A render-only fixture can add rivals to a view** (`withScoreCheck` copies the first rival with new ids, factions and VP sources): the track, standings and sheet read only `players`, `standings` and `eras`, so a believable eight-empire scoring screen needs no engine game. Map and race panel code never see these rivals because the takeover covers the screen.
- **Rules 50 landed on master the same day and flipped captured capitals from kept to held**: `capitals_taken` now lists the capitals held now, and the Celts' sacks are a separate `capitals_sacked` (kept). A surface that says whether points are kept or held has to be re-read after every rules bump; the UI reviewer caught the score check still saying "kept".
- **"Show card" on a report line used to replay one moment and stop** (`MomentLane.dismiss` jumps to index 3 for any reopen). Replaying an Age (the opener's era-up, then the score check) needs the dismiss to step through the reopened batch before it clears `eraReopen`.
- **`Event` in `MomentLane.tsx` is the DOM event type**; import the game's as `GameEvent`.

## 2026-11-22: A second End Turn failure on the same turn offers a way out (`claude/review-engine-failure-recovery`)
- **The loop was real.** In WebAssembly a trapped engine is unusable until recreated, so `store.resolveTurn` reloads the last good save and keeps the orders. The engine is deterministic, so End Turn with the same orders from the same state fails the same way and the player saw the same one-line error forever.
- **Now:** the first failure is unchanged. The second failure of the same turn from the same state (key: turn plus the restored save text) opens `TurnFailureDialog` with a primary "Save and report" (Save to file plus Export game history, no network) and "End the turn with my orders cleared" (`endTurnWithoutOrders`: the next resolution submits every human's orders empty, the AI seats plan as usual). If that fails too, the dialog says so and keeps only Save and report. A success resets the count; Esc closes the dialog and the next failure opens it again.
- **Tests:** `store.turnFailure.test.ts` makes the fixture engine throw once, twice and three times. There is no e2e that forces an engine trap: the crash specs have no such hook.
- **First-run tour:** `e2e/tour/first-run-hints.spec.ts` starts from empty storage with the hints ON (every other tour sets `enabled: false`), picks a people and plays the first two turns, one shot per hint, at both sizes.
- **Found by the first-run tour (hints ON):** the capital's panel is already open on turn 1, so "Select your capital and choose something to build" was stale (now "Choose something for your capital to build"); "Open Technologies (T)" named a top-bar button that the 1000x600 bar has folded away (now "Press T or click Research at the top, then pick one"). Open: after a production order is queued, the city banner still shows "? Build" until the turn resolves.

## 2026-11-22: The score check's tally animation
- **Animate with the Web Animations API and `fill: 'backwards'`, not CSS keyframes on the resting element.** The resting layout is the real one from the first frame, so e2e fit checks and screenshots measure the finished screen, and `animation.finish()` on any pointerdown ends the tally at once. A second animation on the same element (a landing flare) must use `fill: 'none'` or its first frame overrides the walk during its delay; animate a child instead.
- **happy-dom's `Element.animate` returns animations whose `finished` promise rejects with AbortError on `cancel()`.** Unhandled, vitest reports it as an error after the tests pass; catch `finished` when you create the animation.
- **A Playwright `click()` on a takeover panel scrolled the fixed full-screen dialog in the 1440x900 run** (the header ended up at y -9). Use `dispatchEvent('pointerdown')` to test a click-to-skip.
- **At 1000x600 a track space is about 80 px wide, so two 32 px crests fit per row**; three empires on one VP stack upward (`flex-wrap: wrap-reverse`). A two-row min-height on `.sc-pieces` pushed the header off the top at 1000x600.
- **Never put `background-color` on a selector that can match an inline `<svg>`.** `MaskIcon` colours its icon with `background-color` through a mask, but the same rule on the score check's inline capital star painted a solid box behind it, so an open capital showed as a grey square. DOM tests passed; only a real render (the art reviewer's) showed it.
- **An element animated in from `opacity: 0` with `fill: 'backwards'` leaves a hole until its turn.** To show a placeholder while a box waits to stamp in, put the placeholder outside the animated element: each score box sits in a `.sc-cell` whose `::before` draws a faint dashed outline, and the box covers it at rest.

## 2026-11-22: A city banner must show this turn's production choice at once
- **The banner read only the resolved view, so a new player's first action looked ignored.** After "Build" on turn 1 the capital's banner kept the red "Build" prompt until the turn resolved, while the panel and the dock already counted the order. `bannerMeters` now takes the item chosen this turn (`cityBuilds` in the render input, from `pendingProduction`) and shows its icon and the build card's turn forecast. Found by playing a fresh game for the launch-quality review; the tours never caught it because they shoot the banner after Ready.
- **Anything the map draws about a city must be fed pending orders, as `cityFocuses` already was.** The panel and the dock read orders; the canvas only sees what `RenderInput` carries.

## 2026-11-22: Re-recording the README and launch media
- **`docs/media/src/refresh.sh` built the app with `npm run build`, and every clip failed in the fixture** ("app/dist was not built for the e2e relay"), because the e2e fixtures now refuse a build made without `LEGATUM_RELAY_URL=http://127.0.0.1:8787`. It also reused whatever engine was already in `app/src/wasm-pkg`, which would film old rules. It now runs `scripts/build-web.sh` with the e2e relay address every time.
- **The media went stale without anyone touching it**: the teaser, `loop-era.webp` and the launch kit still showed the era-objectives list ("Meet any one of the three") after rules 50 replaced it with World Age cards. Any change to a screen that a clip in `app/e2e/media/launch.media.ts` films needs `refresh.sh` re-run before a release. A full re-record takes about 20 minutes on four cores in a cloud container; `img2webp` comes from the `webp` apt package.
